## [Unreleased]

### Security
- **`_is_scratch_path()` in `unified_pre_tool.py` treated every absolute path as scratch (write-gate-exempt) when `SCRATCHPAD=/`**: the prefix test `expanded.startswith(scratchpad.rstrip("/") + "/")` reduces to `startswith("/")` when `scratchpad` is `/` (or any all-slash value), which is true for every absolute path — and this predates the fix, since three live gates already called `_is_scratch_path`. `SCRATCHPAD` was also absent from `PROTECTED_ENV_VARS`, so the degenerate value could be set inline (`SCRATCHPAD=/ <cmd>`), via `export`, or via an env-prefixed Bash command. Fixed on both routes: `_is_scratch_path` now rejects degenerate/relative roots (`/`, `//`, `""`, `.`, `..`, `~`) before the prefix test, and `SCRATCHPAD` was added to `PROTECTED_ENV_VARS` alongside `PLAN_CRITIC_VERDICT_PATH` (7 protected variables total, up from 5). Only after both closures was `_is_scratch_path` wired into a fourth call site, `_is_code_file_target()`, so scratch writes stop being misclassified as gated code targets. `docs/HOOKS.md`'s protected-variable list and scratch-scoping paragraph updated to name both new entries and the degenerate-value rejection. 12 new `def test_` functions in `tests/regression/test_scratch_exemption_code_target.py` (verified via `grep -c`).

### Fixed
- **`test_permission_glob_syntax.py` named `.claude/settings.json` "installed" and asserted that project file carried a non-empty GLOBAL deny list** — three different subjects (this repo's project settings, the canonical deny sources the product ships, and a deployed installed profile) had been collapsed into aliases of one another, so `test_installed_settings_deny_rules` failed on a correctly-configured repo whose project deny list is deliberately empty (policy lives in the user-level profile; duplicating it made every hook run twice, measured 2026-09-03), and additionally depended on ambient user state a clean CI runner cannot have. The three subjects are now separately named and cannot re-alias: `PROJECT_SETTINGS_PATH`, `CANONICAL_DENY_SOURCES` (+ `CANONICAL_GLOBAL_DENY_SOURCE`), and an `installed_config_root` fixture that materializes the EXACT canonical bytes into an isolated `tmp_path` configuration root — never `$HOME`, never runner state, never the source checkout read as an installed substitute. The misleading `"installed"` key was corrected to `"project"` in both `SETTINGS_FILES` and `COLON_STAR_SOURCE_FILES`, retaining glob-syntax coverage of project settings (emptiness is expected; the file is not unchecked). Project settings gained a NEGATIVE CONTROL asserting the canonical deny corpus is ABSENT, so restoring the duplicate-policy defect goes red; the canonical source gained one-at-a-time negative arms (malformed rule / empty deny list / missing `deny` key); missing or stale installed bytes now FAIL rather than skip. `docs/TESTING-STRATEGY.md` gains a "Settings Test Subjects" subsection defining the three subjects with their differing deny-list expectations. 14 `def test_` functions in `tests/regression/smoke/test_permission_glob_syntax.py` (verified via `grep -cE "^[[:space:]]*def[[:space:]]+test_"`), up from 6; 15 collected (one parametrized over the two canonical sources). A redundant `test_installed_profile_is_byte_identical_to_canonical` was added then removed after mutation testing proved every mutant it killed was already killed by the `_installed_profile_drift(...) is None` precondition shared by the two negative arms (`test_stale_installed_profile_fails`, `test_missing_installed_profile_fails`); the byte-identity expectation is enforced by that shared precondition, not a standalone test. (#1762)
- **STEP 0's pipeline sentinel write in `implement.md`, and the sentinel-repair path in `pipeline_completion_state.py`'s `ensure_sentinel_heartbeat()`, both used truncate-on-open writes (`open(path, 'w')` + `json.dump`, and `sentinel.write_text(...)` respectively)** — a process killed between the open/write call and completion left a 0-byte sentinel with the prior content already destroyed, and in the heartbeat case that 0-byte file is exactly the failure `ensure_sentinel_heartbeat()` exists to repair, so the repair path could itself introduce the corruption it was meant to fix. Both now write via `atomic_write_json` (temp file in the destination directory, `chmod 0o600` before rename, `os.replace`), making the heartbeat path's separate `os.chmod` call redundant (removed). `docs/LIBRARIES.md`'s `ensure_sentinel_heartbeat()` entry updated with the atomicity fix.
- **`implement.md`'s `PIPELINE_STATE_FILE` default pointed at `/tmp/implement_pipeline_state.json` while every sibling call site resolved `<repo>/.claude/local/implement_pipeline_state.json` via `get_legacy_sentinel_path()`** — two different files. The same stale `/tmp` literal was also found in `implement-batch.md`'s batch-merge cleanup step, where it was worse: that file never sets `PIPELINE_STATE_FILE` at all, so the `/tmp` fallback fired on every batch run, the cleanup `rm` deleted a file that never existed, and the real per-repo sentinel accumulated indefinitely. All defaults now resolve via `get_legacy_sentinel_path()`; the `export PIPELINE_STATE_FILE` line in `implement.md` is deliberately retained. 36 new `def test_` functions in `tests/regression/test_implement_md_state_contract.py` (verified via `grep -c`), covering both the atomic sentinel write and the `get_legacy_sentinel_path()` default correction.
- **Four sites in `implement.md` (remediation-exhausted issue filing, reviewer out-of-scope finding tracking, security-auditor advisory filing) instructed the coordinator to run `gh issue create` directly in Bash — a call the hook's `unified_pre_tool.py` gate blocks outside the `/implement` pipeline's own allow-listed contexts.** They now dispatch the `issue-creator` agent instead, which the hook already permits by agent identity (read from the subagent's own stdin, independent of sentinel state) rather than by the `_is_pipeline_active()` check a direct Bash call depends on — the same check that returns `False` for the remainder of a run once its sentinel has been recovered (#1384), the exact path by which this filing was silently blocked mid-pipeline. `GH_ISSUE_COMMANDS` was deliberately not widened.

### Changed
- **Five duplicated inline `_resolve_session_id()` copies across `implement.md` (STEP 0, Pre-Dispatch, Post-Dispatch) and `implement-batch.md` (per-issue STEP 0, Post-Dispatch) consolidated onto the single canonical `pipeline_completion_state.resolve_session_id()`.** The `implement-batch.md` copy had drifted furthest — it defaulted to the legacy machine-global `/tmp/implement_pipeline_state.json` sentinel literal rather than the per-repo path every other call site read. Every call site now passes `sentinel_path=os.environ.get('PIPELINE_STATE_FILE') or None` explicitly, since `resolve_session_id()` does not itself read the env var. `resolve_session_id()`'s sentinel-read exception handler also now distinguishes an absent sentinel from a corrupt/unreadable one (previously both silently returned `None`) by emitting `[SENTINEL-UNREADABLE] path=... err=...` to stderr, closing the gap that split pipeline completion state across two files with no diagnostic trail. `docs/LIBRARIES.md`'s `pipeline_completion_state.py` entry gains a v1.16.0 note.
- **`scripts/deploy-all.sh`'s global sync (`~/.claude/{hooks,lib,config}`) was copy-only and never deleted, so a module removed from source survived indefinitely and stayed importable via the `sys.path` fallback, while the script still printed `ALL VALIDATIONS PASSED` (follow-up to Issue #1610)**: commit `7c3a527e` deleted five `lib/` modules from source, and this was the only local transport without `--delete` — the per-repo local sync already had it, and purged 5 of 5 across five repos; `deploy_state.py:608-609` had already named this transport by hand as a gap. Fixed by adding `--delete --max-delete=50 --exclude=extensions/ --exclude='.claude/'` to the global `rsync` call in `deploy_global()`, plus a pre-flight `find`-based check that refuses (exit 1, no deletion) when a source subdir (`hooks`/`lib`/`config`) holds zero regular files — an empty or misresolved source subdir combined with `--delete` would otherwise empty the matching subtree inside `$HOME`. `--max-delete=50` is a second line of defence (largest evidenced legitimate deletion: 5 files; measured regular-file counts excluding `__pycache__`: hooks 176, lib 314, config 16), and hitting it is a named refusal rather than a bare `exit 25`: it names the subdir, states the cap, and warns explicitly that rsync does **not** roll back — files already deleted before the cap was reached stay deleted. `--exclude='.claude/'` protects runtime state hooks write under their own cwd (e.g. `~/.claude/hooks/.claude/logs/activity/`, 52,502 bytes of telemetry in no commit) — it is a directory-name match, not a general safety net: a plain file or symlink literally named `.claude` inside a deployed subdir is not protected. **At the time of this fix, deliberately NOT changed**: the Mac Studio remote sync (`--remote`) kept its pre-existing non-deleting behavior — adding `--delete` there would newly remove consumer-local files across five remote repos, which this fix did not ask for — so the remote target retained the original defect (a source-deleted module still survives in the remote `~/.claude`) as a known, explicit gap. **That gap is closed by a follow-up fix below (`scripts/lib/prune_sync.sh`), which arms deletion on both remote transports behind a pre-deletion preview guard rather than the `--max-delete` cap used here.** 7 new `def test_` functions in `tests/regression/regression/test_issue_1610_deploy_state.py` (verified via `git diff --unified=0` on the file), covering the prune (permit), real-file delivery (permit), consumer-local `extensions/` preservation (permit), nested runtime `.claude/` preservation (permit), the empty-source-subdir refusal (refuse), the rsync flag/trailing-slash shape (structural), and the `--max-delete` refusal without rollback (refuse). `docs/SCRIPTS.md`'s `scripts/deploy-all.sh` entry is updated to describe the new delete behavior, its two guards, the `.claude/` protection's directory-typed scope, and the local/remote asymmetry; it also now documents the pre-existing `--dirty` flag, which predates this fix but was never listed.

- **Three of six rsync deploy transports shipped plugin source with no `--delete` at all, so a file removed from source survived indefinitely on those targets and stayed importable through the `sys.path` fallback to `~/.claude/lib`** — measured 2026-09-05, the five modules removed in `7c3a527e` were still live on all six deploy targets and had to be removed by hand over SSH. This closes the two REMOTE transports left open by the fix above: the per-repo remote sync (five consumer repos over SSH) and the remote global-home sync (`~/.claude` on the Mac Studio). Fixed by a new shared guard function, `prune_sync()`, in the newly added `scripts/lib/prune_sync.sh`, inlined into `scripts/deploy-all.sh`'s existing SSH heredoc via command substitution. Rather than a post-hoc `--max-delete` cap (non-transactional — hitting it leaves the target half-pruned, and a misresolved `config/` subdirectory holds only 16 files and would slip under a cap of 50), the guard previews the deletion set with `rsync --delete --delete-after --dry-run --itemize-changes` and refuses BEFORE any deletion on five conditions: an empty exclusion array, an empty or misresolved source directory, more than 50 deletion candidates, a candidate under a protected path (`.claude/` or a directory named `extensions`), or a candidate with no git history under the plugin source path — the last is an ownership check, since a human-authored file that never existed under plugin source has no history there and is treated as consumer-local rather than stale. Each refusal prints a `REQUIRED NEXT ACTION:` line and returns non-zero; nothing is deleted or synced on refusal. `--delete-after` defers deletion until the transfer completes, so a mid-transfer failure leaves nothing deleted. `--delete-excluded` remains deliberately absent everywhere and is now banned by test, comments included. **Also narrowed in the same change**: `scripts/deploy_local.sh`'s per-repo sync was already destructively armed with `rsync -a --delete` and ZERO exclusions — capable of deleting `hooks/extensions/` (consumer-local state Issue #560 exists to preserve) and any runtime `.claude/` tree a hook wrote under its own cwd. It now sources the same shared exclusion set as the other transports (`deploy_state.py excludes`) plus `--exclude='.claude/'`, and refuses outright if that exclusion set comes back empty. **Deliberately left unchanged**: `deploy_local.sh`'s own global `~/.claude/{hooks,lib,config}` sync still has no `--delete`, and the standalone global rsync elsewhere in that script has no caller anywhere in the repo, so arming a destructive flag on an unproven path was judged to buy nothing. 15 new `def test_` functions across `tests/regression/regression/test_issue_1610_deploy_state.py` and `tests/unit/scripts/test_deploy_extensions_preserved.py` (verified via `git diff --unified=0 -- 'tests/**/*.py'`, 0 removed). `docs/SCRIPTS.md` and `docs/RUNBOOK.md` updated: the former's "LOCAL-ONLY" delete-behavior claim is corrected now that both remote transports also delete, and gains a `prune_sync.sh` entry; the latter's one-time manual "Post-deletion consumer-repo sweep" procedure — written for exactly this remote gap — is marked superseded as the required deletion mechanism and retained only as an independent audit.

- **The library-reachability ratchet (`tests/unit/hooks/test_hook_reachability_ratchet.py`) gained a third reachability edge — relative re-export through a package `__init__.py` — closing a false-positive class where seven `agent_tracker/*` modules were pinned as unreachable despite ten grounded surfaces naming the package** (nine `agents/*.md` files carrying `from agent_tracker import AgentTracker` in fenced Python, plus a settings-bound hook). New helpers `_relative_import_targets(node, anchor)` and `_relative_package_targets(path)` walk relative `from . import X` / `from .. import Y` edges back to their package; `_python_referenced_stems` gained a keyword-only `origin: Path | None = None` to thread the anchor through, and `_consumer_nodes` now keys `__init__.py` by `path.parent.name` instead of `path.stem` so a package initialiser is addressable by its package name. `LIBRARY_REACHABILITY_CEILING` and `LIBRARY_CEILING_HIGH_WATER_MARK` both moved 121 → 105 (verified: `frozenset` literal in the module, 105 entries); the `REACHED → UNKNOWN` direction measured empty — nothing was lost. 16 entries LEFT `PINNED_UNREACHED_LIBRARY` (105 remain): 7 `agent_tracker/*`, 4 `sync_dispatcher/*`, plus `file_discovery.py`, `hook_activator.py`, `uninstall_orchestrator.py`, `sync_mode_detector.py`, and `version_detector.py`, credited through the transitive tail; `implement_dispatcher/*` (5) and `ideators/*` (5) correctly remain unreached. Also corrected in the same diff: `_library_corpus`'s docstring previously justified excluding `__init__.py` from the corpus by claiming "classifying **twenty** of them by the same stem would collide" — the measured count is **five** (`find plugins/autonomous-dev/lib -name __init__.py`, confirmed), and the docstring now states that figure with its basis. 6 new `def test_` functions added (both-arms controls for the new edge — verified via `git diff --unified=0 -- 'tests/**/*.py'`). Test file only — no `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/` module was added, changed, or deleted.
- **The library-reachability ratchet (`tests/unit/hooks/test_hook_reachability_ratchet.py`, Issue #1723) had two blind spots that pinned six live modules as unreachable.** It had no `importlib` edge at all (`grep -c spec_from_file_location` on the instrument itself returned 0), so any module loaded only via `importlib.util.spec_from_file_location` — the pattern several hook loaders use instead of a direct `import` — could never be credited as REACHED. Separately, repo-root `scripts/*.py` was not registered as an entry surface, so modules reachable only from a root-level script were pinned too. Fixed by adding a `MODULE_LOADER_CALLEES` edge (`spec_from_file_location`, read via the existing `ast.walk` scan, same first-positional-string-argument contract as the pre-existing `INVOCATION_CALLEES` route) and by adding repo-root `scripts/*.py` to the entry-surface globs. Six previously-mispinned modules were recredited as REACHED; five modules that remained genuinely unreachable after the recrediting were deleted (see Removed, below). `LIBRARY_REACHABILITY_CEILING` and `LIBRARY_CEILING_HIGH_WATER_MARK` both moved 132 → 121 — the first downward move of this ratchet since it was pinned. 4 new `def test_` functions added (2 permitting-arm / 2 refusing-arm controls, one pair per new edge — verified via `git diff --unified=0 -- 'tests/**/*.py'`); `tests/regression/regression/test_enforcement_skills_361.py` lost 2 `def test_` functions (`test_injector_file_exists`, `test_injector_has_mapping`) that asserted on `context_skill_injector.py`, one of the five deleted modules.
- **`docs/MCP-SECURITY.md` and `docs/MCP-ARCHITECTURE.md` described a "Detection Library" (`plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/mcp_server_detector.py`) that gated MCP tool calls by server type — that module was never imported by `unified_pre_tool.py` or any other executing path at any point, confirmed by the reachability audit above before its deletion.** The claim predates this change and was not merely stale on deletion; it described an aspiration as though it were shipped enforcement (`MCP-SECURITY.md:234` even carried a copy-pasteable `from autonomous_dev.lib.mcp_server_detector import detect_mcp_server` import for code that never ran). Both docs corrected to describe what actually gates MCP calls: `unified_pre_tool.py::validate_mcp_security()` and its supporting registries (`NATIVE_TOOLS`, `tool_intent.MCP_READ_TOOLS`, `_MCP_SIDE_EFFECT_TOOLS`) dispatch on the literal `mcp__<server>__<tool>` name prefix directly — there is no runtime server-type classifier in the live path. `MCP-ARCHITECTURE.md`'s "Hook Integration" pseudocode block (`on_pre_tool_use`, `detect_mcp_server`, `.mcp/security_policy.json` via `load_policy()`) is now labeled illustrative of the original design intent, not a transcript of the live function, since it never matched `unified_pre_tool.py`'s actual implementation.

### Removed
- **Six more `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/` modules deleted — the never-executed approval/retry-consent subsystem — after two independent signals confirmed zero executions**: `auto_approval_engine.py` (499 lines), `auto_approval_consent.py` (278), `mcp_permission_validator.py` (885), `tool_approval_audit.py` (440), `batch_retry_manager.py` (716), `batch_retry_consent.py` (405) — 3,223 lines total (verified: `git show HEAD:<path> | wc -l` summed per file). The reachability walk (`tests/unit/hooks/test_hook_reachability_ratchet.py`) pinned all six as UNREACHED, and a separate scan of 541,492 runtime activity-log lines found zero executions of any of them, despite all six shipping to five consumer repositories via `install_manifest.json`. Both `install_manifest.json` files (repo-root and `plugins/autonomous-dev/config/`) lose the corresponding six `lib/` entries each (verified: `git diff --stat` on both files, 6 deletions apiece, no other changes). 12 test files deleted alongside them — 4 archived unit tests, 8 live (`tests/integration/test_batch_retry_security.py`, `test_batch_retry_workflow.py`, `test_mcp_security_end_to_end.py`, `test_tool_auto_approval_end_to_end.py`; `tests/property/test_auto_approval_engine_properties.py`, `test_tool_approval_audit_properties.py`; `tests/security/test_mcp_bypass_prevention.py`; `tests/unit/lib/test_auto_approval_engine.py`) — plus stale imports and skip-guarded test classes removed from `tests/regression/regression/test_approval.py`, `test_batch.py`, and `test_mcp.py`. Net test count: 350 `def test_` functions removed, 1 renamed in place (not a new test) — verified via `git diff --cached --unified=0 -- 'tests/**/*.py' | grep -c '^[-+].*def test_'`. The two live permission layers this subsystem sat alongside — Claude Code's native rules (4 allow, 61 deny) and `plugins/autonomous-dev/hooks/unified_pre_tool.py` (167 refusals in the two days measured) — are untouched by this change. `docs/MCP-SECURITY.md`, `docs/TOOL-AUTO-APPROVAL.md`, and `docs/MCP-ARCHITECTURE.md` gain top-of-file status banners stating the subsystem was deleted and never ran, plus corrected in-body notes (`MCP-SECURITY.md:270-292`) that previously claimed the deleted module "still exists and implements policy-driven filesystem/shell/network validation" — a blind spec-validator had failed the change on that stale claim before it was corrected. `docs/LIBRARIES.md` sections 22/23/35/38 converted to `— REMOVED` tombstones (numbering preserved); `docs/BATCH-PROCESSING.md`'s `covers:` frontmatter drops `batch_retry_manager.py`. `docs/ARCHITECTURE-OVERVIEW.md`'s library count moves 244 → 238 (verified against `scripts/validate_structure.py::_count_libraries()`'s own rule — recursive `.py` glob excluding `__pycache__`, `htmlcov`, `__init__.py` — via `tests/unit/test_documentation_congruence.py::test_library_count`, 1 passed). `docs/RUNBOOK.md` gains a one-time, manual "Post-deletion consumer-repo sweep" procedure, since consumer repos keep an already-installed copy under `~/.claude/lib/` importable until the next deploy. `docs/TOOL-AUTO-APPROVAL.md`'s ~900-line body below the new banner (Security Model, Architecture, File Structure, API Reference) still described the deleted modules with concrete `Location:` paths and import snippets with no per-claim markers; this pass adds REMOVED/design-history notes at the Security Model heading, the Component Diagram, the File Structure tree, and the `ToolApprovalAuditor` API section. **Known, not fixed here**: `README.md:116` still lists `batch_retry_manager.py` as a live "Error Recovery" component with "Automatic retry with consent" — `README.md` is protected infrastructure requiring `/implement` to edit and is not touched by doc-master directly; filed as a finding below.

- **Five `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/` modules deleted as genuinely unreachable, after the reachability-ratchet blind-spot fix above recredited the six that were only mispinned**: `workflow_tracker.py` (526 lines), `mcp_server_detector.py` (369 lines), `context_skill_injector.py` (360 lines), `git_hooks.py` (334 lines), `native_tools.py` (57 lines) — 1,646 lines total. `docs/LIBRARIES.md` lost the corresponding five module sections (717 lines); its `Tracking Libraries` and `MCP Security Libraries` group headers now read `(2)` each, matching the two remaining modules in each group. `docs/ARCHITECTURE-OVERVIEW.md`'s library count updated 249 → 244, matching `scripts/validate_structure.py::_count_libraries()` (verified: `find plugins/autonomous-dev/lib -name "*.py" ! -path "*__pycache__*" ! -path "*htmlcov*" ! -name "__init__.py" | wc -l` → 244). `plugins/autonomous-dev/config/install_manifest.json` regenerated, 454 → 449 entries (five `lib/` paths removed, no other changes).

### Fixed
- **The deployment manifest under-declared `lib/` and `skills/`, and `install.py` silently collapsed the difference into file collisions (Issue #1747).** `install_manifest.json` listed 229 of 254 `lib/` files and mis-declared `skills` (30 entries, including 9 stale `archived/` ones, missing 21 `.py` template/example files) because the manifest generator globbed non-recursively and compared bare filenames instead of full paths — the completeness test that should have caught this used the same shallow method and passed at 228/254 lib files and 1/21 skill files. Separately, `plugins/autonomous-dev/scripts/install.py` mapped every manifest entry to its install destination via `Path(github_path).name` — flattening every path to its basename — so 454 manifest entries produced only 389–425 unique destinations; 29 files silently overwrote each other, including 29 distinct `SKILL.md` files collapsing onto one path. Fixed: the manifest is now generated recursively per component, `install.py` preserves subdirectory structure when mapping source paths to destinations, and `install.py` now raises `ValueError` on a manifest entry containing `..` or `/./` (path-traversal refusal matching the uninstaller's existing check). The manifest schema gained a per-component `"exclude"` array (currently `skills: ["archived/"]`, all others `[]`) so the recursive scan can still skip archived/legacy subtrees. `scripts/validate_manifest.py` — which existed but had no caller anywhere in the repo — is now invoked automatically by `scripts/hooks/pre-commit` after the manifest sync step, and its `critical` hooks check dropped the stale `pre_tool_use.py` entry (archived, no longer shipped). 8 new/updated `def test_` functions across `tests/regression/test_issue_1747_manifest_completeness.py` (7, new: 4 completeness/positive controls + 3 detector controls proving a manifest-only install tree can import its subpackages) and `tests/regression/smoke/test_install_sync_critical.py` (`test_all_skills_in_manifest`, 1). `docs/HOOKS.md`, `docs/HOOK-COMPOSITION.md` and `docs/SCRIPTS.md` updated to describe the new `exclude` key and the now-wired completeness gate; `docs/audits/20260904-structural-picture.md`'s claim that the legacy `plugins/autonomous-dev/install_manifest.json` orphan has no readers is corrected — it has six, all in `tests/`, one a deliberate positive control — and its "delete the orphan" recommendation is withdrawn pending those readers being repointed. **A second, more severe gap in the same manifest-trust surface, found by the security audit of this change and fixed in the same commit (CWE-22)**: the `..`/`/./` refusal above covered only the per-file `github_path` field; it never validated the component-level `target` field. `Path(target) / relative_structure` discards the left operand when the right side is absolute, so an absolute `target` produced an absolute install destination, and `download_to_temp` wrote to it during staging — before `FileManager.validate_path`, the only pre-existing containment gate, ever ran. A working proof-of-concept wrote a payload outside the staging directory; the manifest is fetched over the network at install time with no signature or hash check, so this was reachable by a compromised or MITM'd manifest. Fixed with two new names in `plugins/autonomous-dev/scripts/install.py`: `validate_manifest_target(component, target)` refuses empty, NUL, backslash, `..`, `/./`, leading `./`, `~`, absolute, or non-`.claude/`-normalizing targets, checked against the RAW string (not `Path(...).parts`, which silently drops `.` segments and can never see one); and `assert_contained(root, candidate, *, what)`, called in `download_to_temp` immediately before `mkdir`, which resolves both paths and checks containment via `relative_to` rather than a string-prefix compare. `scripts/hooks/pre-commit`'s new manifest-completeness check (above) was hardened in the same pass: its `if [ -f scripts/validate_manifest.py ]` guard had no `else`, so deleting the validator silently passed the gate; it now falls through to `elif [ -f install_manifest.json ]` and refuses the commit when a manifest exists but its validator does not — two sibling `if [ -f ... ]` checks earlier in the same hook (command validation, the archived manifest-sync step) keep the old fail-open pattern deliberately, filed as a follow-up rather than fixed here. `tests/regression/test_issue_1747_manifest_completeness.py` grew again for this arm, 12 → 15 `def test_` functions (31 collected via `pytest --collect-only`). `docs/HOOKS.md` updated again in this pass to describe `validate_manifest_target`, `assert_contained`, and the pre-commit gate's fail-closed elif branch.
- **`implement.md`'s STEP 8 regression-test HARD GATE — "a bug fix must add at least one new test" — had never been able to refuse anything, for three independent reasons**: (1) scope mismatch — the STEP 1 baseline counted only `tests/unit` + `tests/integration` (11,551 tests) while the STEP 8 after-count rglobbed the entire `tests/` tree via `bugfix_detector.get_test_count()` (19,563), so the after-count always exceeded the baseline regardless of what the fix actually added; (2) under a scope-consistent call, `tests/regression/` — 3,403 tests, and the directory `docs/TESTING-STRATEGY.md` designates for regression tests — was invisible to the baseline, so a correctly-placed regression test read as adding nothing and the gate blocked a *correct* change; (3) dominant — `BASELINE_TEST_COUNT` was a plain shell assignment with no `export`, and each coordinator Bash call in `implement.md` is a separate process, so the variable arrived empty at STEP 8, where `int('' or 0)` coerced it to zero and made the comparison unconditionally true regardless of scope. Fixed in `bugfix_detector.py` with one shared constant, `CANONICAL_TEST_COUNT_DIRS = ["tests/unit", "tests/integration", "tests/regression"]`, now consumed by both `implement.md` count call sites; `BASELINE_TEST_COUNT` is now `export`ed at STEP 1 so it survives into the STEP 8 process; and a new tri-state helper `evaluate_regression_test_gate(baseline_count, current_count, project_root=None, dirs=None) -> (verdict, reason)` replaces the old two-state comparison, returning `BLOCK`, `PASS`, or `UNMEASURED` — `UNMEASURED` fires when no baseline was captured, or when none of the three canonical directories exists (a consumer repo laid out `test/` or `spec/`, which the old logic would have blocked on every bug fix), keying on directory *presence* rather than a zero count so a canonical-layout repo holding zero tests still `BLOCK`s. STEP 8 now emits `REGRESSION_GATE: <verdict>`. The false cross-reference from `implement.md` to `implement-fix.md` lines 166-177 is corrected to `implement-fix.md:319`, where the fix-mode counterpart gate actually lives (confirmed: `implement-fix.md:319` is `### HARD GATE: Regression Test Requirement`) — that counterpart remains prose-only, names no directory scope and no counting function, and is a distinct, weaker enforcement from the mechanical gate fixed here. **A fourth failure mode, found by the security audit of this changeset and fixed here:** the STEP 8 block could print *nothing at all*. `.claude/lib` is the first directory its `sys.path` walk accepts, and in a checkout that has not been re-deployed it holds a stale `bugfix_detector.py` without the two new symbols — the block raised `ImportError`, emitted no `REGRESSION_GATE:` line, and the prose defined no branch for "nothing was printed", which is a silent pass of exactly the shape the gate exists to close. Both blocks are now wrapped in `try/except BaseException` and a fourth verdict, `ERROR`, is documented: it fails **CLOSED** (exit 1, `REQUIRED NEXT ACTION: bash scripts/deploy-all.sh`), the opposite of `UNMEASURED`, which fails open by design because it means "this repo has no measurable test layout", not "the instrument broke". The invariant is now that the block prints **exactly one** `REGRESSION_GATE:` line on every path, crashes included. **A fifth, on the same review pass:** the STEP 1 *scope-record* block called `record_baseline_scope(..., int('' or 0))`, writing `baseline_count=0` into the sentinel whenever the count had not been captured — and since a shell variable does not survive between coordinator Bash calls, STEP 8's sentinel fallback is the DOMINANT path, so it read that fabricated zero and any non-empty repo satisfied `current > 0`. The block now skips the write entirely when `BASELINE_TEST_COUNT` is unset, so `get_baseline_scope()` returns `None` and the verdict is `UNMEASURED` — never `PASS` — on a baseline nobody measured. **Hardening in the same lines:** both blocks truncate the exception message to its first line (`str(_exc).splitlines()[0]`), so an exception whose `__str__` embeds a newline plus `REGRESSION_GATE: PASS` can no longer forge a second verdict after the real one — reproduced by the security auditor before the fix; and the scope-record block reads the count from `os.environ` rather than interpolating `int('$BASELINE_TEST_COUNT')` into embedded Python source, removing the injection class regardless of what the producer prints in future. A third marker pair, `# BEGIN/END BASELINE-SCOPE-RECORD`, was added so the scope-record block is extractable and executable by tests as it ships; the two pre-existing pairs are unmoved. Verified with 12 mutation checks — the 9 from the original change plus 3 added this cycle (the B2b scope-record guard reverted to `int('' or 0)`; the STEP 8 truncation reverted to `{_exc}`; the STEP 1 truncation reverted to `{_exc}`) — all killed as predicted after one was strengthened: a first attempt at the STEP 1 spoofing assertion filtered stderr lines by the `BASELINE_TEST_COUNT: ERROR` prefix and the mutant SURVIVED, because the pre-fix form pushes forged text onto *subsequent* lines that no longer carry that prefix; the assertion now requires the block's entire pre-traceback output to be one line — plus one expected-green control confirming the scope cross-check is order-independent. The three verdicts are each reached end-to-end through the production carrier: three real blocks lifted verbatim from `implement.md`, one shared `PIPELINE_STATE_FILE`, and `BASELINE_TEST_COUNT` *absent* from every subprocess environment (asserted via an environment receipt, not assumed) — `PASS` (6 -> 7), `BLOCK` (6 -> 6) and `UNMEASURED` (nothing recorded, current count 6 > 0), with the pre-fix scope-record block flipping that last arm to `PASS — test count rose from 0 to 6`. 16 `def test_` functions / 18 collected in new `tests/regression/test_regression_test_count_scope_consistency.py` (counted with `grep -cE "^[[:space:]]*def[[:space:]]+test_"` and `pytest --collect-only`; the 2-count gap is one 3-way-parametrized test). `docs/LIBRARIES.md`'s `bugfix_detector.py` entry (#87) is updated to describe the new constant and function and to stop describing the pre-fix STEP 1/STEP 8 scope split as current behavior. `CANONICAL_BASELINE_CMD` in `pipeline_state.py` (a different concern — it governs pytest execution, not this gate's directory scope) is byte-identical; only its docstring changed. **Not yet deployed**: `bash scripts/deploy-all.sh` has not run since this fix landed — `.claude/lib/bugfix_detector.py` and `~/.claude/lib/bugfix_detector.py` still lack `evaluate_regression_test_gate` and `CANONICAL_TEST_COUNT_DIRS`. Against that stale copy the STEP 8 block now reports `REGRESSION_GATE: ERROR — ImportError: ...` and **exits 1**, blocking the pipeline with the deploy command as its REQUIRED NEXT ACTION. It does **not** keep its pre-fix never-refusing behaviour until deploy runs — that earlier claim was written before the `ERROR` verdict existed and is withdrawn; the undeployed state now fails CLOSED, and is loud rather than silent.
- **`scripts/hooks/pre-commit`'s `.claude/` staging gate fell open three separate ways, all pre-existing and all found by the security audit of the prior (2026-09-03) allowlist fix, not by this work.** GAP 1: under git's default `core.quotePath`, `git diff --cached --raw` C-quotes any staged path containing a non-ASCII byte, backslash, double quote or control character, wrapping it in a leading `"` — the gate's domain selector `grep "^\.claude/"` never matched the quoted form, so `.claude/café-évil.json` committed with exit 0 and no hook output, neither allowlisted nor refused. GAP 2: the selector was case-sensitive while macOS APFS folds case, so a mixed-case spelling of the protected directory resolved to the same physical location but bypassed the check — the CVE-2024-32002 bug class. GAP 3: `set -e` was on but `set -o pipefail` was not, and the enumeration ended in `|| true`, so "nothing staged" and "the enumeration itself broke" were indistinguishable and both permitted the commit — the gate failed OPEN rather than closed. Fixed by replacing the `git diff --cached --raw | awk | grep | grep -vxF || true` pipeline with `git diff --cached --raw -z | claude_gate_scan` (`scripts/hooks/pre-commit:119-167`): `-z` emits path bytes verbatim regardless of `core.quotePath`, closing GAP 1 by construction; the domain selector became `.[Cc][Ll][Aa][Uu][Dd][Ee]/*` (case-folded) while the allowlist stayed byte-exact, so a case variant of an allowlisted name is now selected into the gate's domain and then refused, closing GAP 2; the scan's exit status is captured under a tightly scoped `set -o pipefail` with an `if`/`else`, and a new branch refuses with a distinct message when the enumeration cannot be read, closing GAP 3 — the trailing `|| true` was removed, not replaced. Removing it turned the regression suite's live negative control (`test_guard_permits_the_live_legitimate_or_true`) red by design, since that control locates its target dynamically; it was re-pointed onto the `else`-guarded `CLAUDE_SCAN_RC=$?` capture the fix introduces, with the retired line preserved verbatim in a new synthetic sibling (`test_guard_permits_the_original_line_shape_synthetically`) so the re-point did not silently drop the shape it discriminated against. No control was weakened, skipped or deleted. Measured cost: +12 ms per commit on a 494-path index (32 → 44 ms), rising to +70 ms at 5000 staged paths, against a 10 s validation-hook budget. `tests/regression/test_precommit_fail_open_guard.py` grew from 13 to 21 `def test_` definitions (25 collected). Not closed by this change: the unmerged-index-entry case (status `U`, mode `000000`, skipped like a deletion) remains a latent dependency on git's own refusal to commit with unmerged paths.
- **`scripts/hooks/pre-commit`'s `.claude/` allowlist matched by PREFIX with no end anchor, so a deny-by-default gate silently permitted any path that merely started with an allowlisted one, and separately disagreed with `.gitignore` about whether `.claude/settings.json` was committable**: the guard's subtraction was `grep -v "^\.claude/PROJECT.md" | grep -v "^\.claude/logs/cloud-runs.jsonl"`, which matches `.claude/PROJECT.md.bak` and `.claude/logs/cloud-runs.jsonl.old` as if they were the allowlisted files themselves. Separately, `.gitignore` had gained a `!.claude/settings.json` negation making that file committable, but the guard had no matching entry, so `.claude/settings.json` could be un-ignored yet still refused at commit time — the two mechanisms disagreed. Fixed by converting the subtraction to exact whole-line matching, `grep -vxF -e ".claude/PROJECT.md" -e ".claude/settings.json" -e ".claude/logs/cloud-runs.jsonl"`, and adding the missing `.claude/settings.json` entry so the guard's allowlist now matches `.gitignore`'s three negated/tracked paths. The refusal message, which said "Only .claude/PROJECT.md should be tracked" (already inaccurate before this fix, since `cloud-runs.jsonl` was also tracked), now names all three paths and states the match is exact. `docs/HOOKS.md`'s `### pre-commit` section gains a paragraph documenting the exact-match allowlist and the prefix-match class it closes. **A second defect, found by the security audit of that fix and repaired in the same change**: the allowlist matched on the staged PATH STRING only, never the staged git OBJECT TYPE. `git diff --cached --name-only` emits only the link path for a symlink, so `ln -s /etc/hosts \.claude/settings.json && git add -f .claude/settings.json` exact-matched the allowlist and committed clean — a symlink to anywhere on the filesystem (`~/.ssh/id_rsa`, `.claude/.bypass`, `.claude/hooks/*.py`) landing under a name Claude Code loads as live configuration (hooks, `permissions.allow`, MCP allow/deny) and that `.github/workflows/claude-code-validation.yml` parses as JSON. Because this diff is what first made `.claude/settings.json` traversable through the gate at all, it was a newly-enabled exploit, not an inherited one. Fixed by making the allowlist key the PAIR (path, mode): the gate now reads `git diff --cached --raw`, pairs each path with the NEW mode in its second field, and matches `.claude/PROJECT.md 120000`, `.claude/settings.json 100644`, `.claude/logs/cloud-runs.jsonl 100644`. The audit's literal recommendation — refuse any newly-staged symlink — was NOT applied: `.claude/PROJECT.md` is itself a symlink by design (`120000`, to the root `PROJECT.md`) and is the allowlist's own first entry, so a blanket refusal would have broken the alignment source of truth. Deletions (new mode `000000`) are dropped before the allowlist and so remain permitted, deliberately: a deletion stages no object and cannot carry smuggled content, and untracking a `.claude/` path is the remedy this gate asks for. 6 new tests in `tests/regression/test_precommit_fail_open_guard.py` (class `TestClaudeAllowlistIsExactMatch`; module 7 → 13, counted with `grep -cE "^[[:space:]]*def[[:space:]]+test_"`), covering the PERMIT arm (`.claude/settings.json` as a regular file), two string-match REFUSE arms (a `.bak` variant of an allowlisted path; an unrelated `.claude/` file), two object-type REFUSE arms (`.claude/settings.json` and `.claude/logs/cloud-runs.jsonl` staged as symlinks — two members of the class, not one filename), and the over-correction control (`.claude/PROJECT.md` staged as a symlink still commits, and lands in the commit object still at mode `120000`). Mutation-checked: reverting the guard to the name-only form turns both object-type REFUSE arms red (`assert 0 != 0` — the commit succeeds) while all four other arms stay green; restoring it returns 6/6. Not shipped to consumers — this hook is absent from `install_manifest.json` and has no installer.
- **Issue #1641's own fix left the grant decision unaudited on three paths — deferring siblings that honoured a receipt granted the bypass and logged nothing**: the #1641 repair above moved the GRANT decision to a new place (the receipt) but left the audit call wired to the old place (the sentinel) — `_log_write_gate_bypass_consumed(file_path, skip_file)` reads the sentinel's own contents for its reason string, so it can only run where the sentinel still exists; the three receipt-honouring branches in `_consume_write_gate_bypass()` run precisely where the sentinel is already gone (a sibling deleted it), so they could not call the only logger that existed for that event and returned `True` silently. The `..._consumed`-exactly-once assertions in the existing fan-out tests then made the gap look correct: logging on those paths would have broken a green test. Fixed with a second, distinct event: all three receipt-honouring return paths now call new `_log_write_gate_bypass_deferred(file_path, call_key)`, which writes a `write_gate_operator_bypass_deferred` record (file_path, winner_pid, call_key, own pid, receipt_age_seconds, agent, session) to the same sink as its `..._consumed` sibling. **Audit-log shape change**: a single bypassed write with N concurrent hook copies now produces exactly one `write_gate_operator_bypass_consumed` record (unchanged) plus (N-1) `write_gate_operator_bypass_deferred` records (new, previously zero) in `.claude/logs/activity/*.jsonl` — counting `..._consumed` lines still counts bypasses used exactly once each, but any script or runbook counting ALL bypass-related lines will now see more of them than before this fix. Separately (unrelated defect, same function): the receipt-claim staging file switched from `os.O_CREAT | os.O_TRUNC | os.O_WRONLY` to `os.O_CREAT | os.O_EXCL | os.O_WRONLY` (mode `0o600` unchanged), and its `try`/`finally` was restructured so cleanup only ever removes a staging file this process itself created — closes a symlink-plant race on the derivable staging path. One existing test, `test_consume_defers_to_a_sibling_without_logging_a_second_time`, asserted `not log_file.exists()` — that assertion WAS the defect being fixed; it now asserts 0 `_consumed` records and 1 `_deferred` record. The two protected "log-exactly-once" tests from the original #1641 fix are byte-unchanged. `docs/HOOKS.md`'s Issue #1641 paragraph, which said concurrent siblings honour the receipt "without re-consuming or re-logging," is corrected: siblings still never re-consume, but each deferring sibling now logs its own `_deferred` record. 8 new test functions in `tests/regression/test_issue_1641_write_gate_duplicate_hook_invocations.py` (29 → 37; verified via `git diff HEAD --unified=0 -- tests/regression/test_issue_1641_write_gate_duplicate_hook_invocations.py` counting added/removed `def test_` lines). 42/42 tests passing. **Not yet deployed**: `bash scripts/deploy-all.sh` has not run since this fix landed — `.claude/hooks/`, `~/.claude/hooks/`, and `.codex/hooks/` still carry the pre-fix logic and will keep granting deferred bypasses with no log record until deploy runs.
- **Issue #1638's fix was only half the defect — the operator bypass token could still be consumed and the write refused anyway (Issue #1641)**: `unified_pre_tool.py` is registered for `PreToolUse` with matcher `*` on BOTH the project `.claude/settings.json` and the user's `~/.claude/settings.json`; Claude Code merges hook registrations across settings surfaces and runs every match concurrently, so one Write tool call spawned two concurrent hook processes. The one-shot sentinel consumption added by #1638 was idempotent per *function call* but not per *logical tool call* — copy A read the sentinel, consumed it, and allowed; copy B, ~367ms behind, found nothing on disk and denied. Claude Code takes the deny, so the operator's `touch /tmp/skip_write_pipeline_gate` still produced a refusal in this topology. Reproduced in the production activity log (`.claude/logs/activity/`, 2026-09-01): three log lines 367ms apart for one tool call. Fixed by making consumption idempotent per logical tool call rather than per process: a new `_write_gate_bypass_call_key()` hashes the tool payload (tool name, path, old/new content) into a stable key shared byte-for-byte by every sibling of the same fan-out; `_claim_write_gate_bypass_receipt()` atomically claims the right to spend the token via `os.link()` mutual exclusion and publishes a receipt; `_read_write_gate_bypass_receipt()` lets concurrent siblings honour that receipt (discriminated from a later, unrelated tool call by comparing the receipt's write time against each process's own start time, `_HOOK_PROCESS_START_TIME`) without re-logging or re-consuming. `_consume_write_gate_bypass()` gains a keyword-only `call_key` argument; omitting it restores the strict pre-#1641 per-process behavior. The one-shot property is unchanged — a second, later production write is still refused. **Documentation correction**: `README.md`, `docs/HOOKS.md`, and `plugins/autonomous-dev/docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md` described (per #1638) the bypass as reliably "consumed on the first refusal it buys passage past" — that claim was not actually true whenever the hook's two-settings-surface registration produced concurrent copies, which is the normal case in this repository. All three now describe the receipt-based coordination and note that hook processes for one tool call are not guaranteed to be singular. 26 new tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1641_write_gate_duplicate_hook_invocations.py` (verified via `grep -c 'def test_'` against the file). **Known, unfixed**: the underlying duplicate-hook-invocation topology (matcher `*` registered on two settings surfaces) is not itself removed — only the one non-idempotent side effect it exposed (the bypass sentinel) is fixed. Any other hook logic that performs a non-idempotent side effect per process rather than per logical tool call remains exposed to the same class of bug.
- **The write-pipeline gate's one-shot operator bypass was consumed before either gate had established a refusal, so an operator following the gate's own printed instruction could burn the token and still be refused (Issue #1638)**: `touch /tmp/skip_write_pipeline_gate` followed by an unrelated tool call — a bare `ls`, a Write to `README.md` or a test file — silently spent the sentinel before the intended Write/Edit ever reached it, because both `_check_write_pipeline_required` and `_check_bash_code_file_pipeline_required` checked it eagerly at the top of the function, before either had determined the call would be refused. Fixed by introducing a single sanctioned consumption point, `_consume_write_gate_bypass()` (new `WRITE_GATE_BYPASS_SENTINEL` constant), called only from a new Tier 0i in `_check_write_pipeline_required` placed after Tier 0h — the first point at which refusal is certain. The consumer in `_check_bash_code_file_pipeline_required` is removed entirely: that gate has been advisory-only since Issue #1408 and never refuses, so it had nothing for a bypass to buy passage past; its directive text no longer advertises a bypass it did not honor. The one-shot property is unchanged — a second production write is still refused. `README.md`, `docs/HOOKS.md`, and `plugins/autonomous-dev/docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md` updated to describe consumption as happening at the point of refusal rather than "on first check". 10 new tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1638_write_gate_bypass_single_consumer.py` (verified via `grep -c 'def test_'` against the file). **Known, unfixed**: the sibling marker `/tmp/skip_agent_completeness_gate` has the same eager-consumption shape and is NOT touched by this change — existing docs describing it as "consumed on first check" remain accurate to its current (buggy) behavior.
- **The pre-commit documentation gate was born dead and refused nothing for 9.5 months**: `scripts/hooks/pre-commit` ran `pytest tests/integration/test_documentation_references.py -q --tb=line 2>/dev/null || true` and then read `TEST_EXIT=$?`. `$?` after `cmd || true` is the exit status of `true`, so `TEST_EXIT` was unconditionally `0`, the `if [ $TEST_EXIT -ne 0 ]` was unreachable, and its `exit` was dead code — introduced in `0b00185f` (2025-11-11), the same commit that added the check. It was observed printing `10 failed, 5 passed` while allowing the commit. The two duplicated branches are replaced by one binary `PYTEST_BIN` selection feeding a single gate that captures with `if/else` (a bare `cmd; rc=$?` is NOT a safe alternative here: under this file's `set -e` the script aborts AT the failing command, so the message never prints — verified with a control). `2>/dev/null` is removed, since it discarded the diagnostic a blocked committer needs and would hide a collection error as a silent pass; the refusal now carries a `REQUIRED NEXT ACTION` reproduce command and does not advertise `--no-verify`. The eight checks above it are byte-unchanged. Not shipped to consumers — this hook is absent from `install_manifest.json` and has no installer, so the blast radius is this repository plus any hand-symlinked clone.
- **The documentation-reference tests the gate runs were themselves broken**: all 10 failures dispositioned. Two genuinely dead links fixed in `docs/LIBRARIES.md` (`HOOKS.md#lifecycle-constraints` → `LIBRARIES.md#lifecycle-constraints`, `HOOKS.md#pre_commit_gatepy` → `HOOKS.md#precommit`) plus one real leaked absolute path. Three were test bugs: the link checker never stripped a trailing `#fragment`, so `HOOKS.md#exit-code-semantics` was resolved as a literal filename — fixed with the lychee/markdownlint-MD051 two-step (strip fragment → resolve file → **validate the fragment against the target's GitHub-slugified headings**), deliberately *not* by resolving the file alone, which would have turned all five links green while leaving two anchors dead; the absolute-path scan now excludes fenced code blocks but **not** inline code spans, since inline backticks in prose are exactly where a real leaked home directory appears; and a global `[`/`]` count over 801 KB was removed while the per-link malformation checks it was meant to be were kept. Five more asserted that `CLAUDE.md` carries deep reference content, which `docs/development/CONTENT_ALLOCATION.md:10` explicitly denies it — re-pointed to the canonical homes (`docs/ARCHITECTURE-OVERVIEW.md`, `README.md`) and three near-duplicates collapsed into one parametrized test. One whole test deleted: `test_library_counts_consistent_across_docs` passed **vacuously** (its `re.search` returned `None`, so the guarded `if` skipped every assertion — the same fail-open shape as `|| true`); `tests/unit/test_documentation_congruence.py::TestComponentCounts::test_library_count` already covers it better by counting real files on disk. Test count 16 → 13, deliberately.
- **Durable guard added** — `tests/regression/test_precommit_fail_open_guard.py` (7 tests at the time this guard was added; 13 as of the allowlist fix above) fails if a `|| true` is reintroduced before a `$?` capture anywhere in `scripts/hooks/`, reusing the `#1588` `_SHELL_COMMENT` instrument rather than reimplementing it. It ships both controls: a positive control reconstructing the original two lines verbatim, and a negative control on the live, legitimate `|| true` inside the `.claude/` allowlist's command substitution (a different shape from the bug — not at end-of-line, followed by an `if [ -n ... ]`, not a `$?` capture). The control locates that line by content, not a hardcoded number, precisely because the allowlist fix above moved it (was `pre-commit:30`, then `:52`, now `pre-commit:73`) — a fixed line number would have gone stale the same day this entry was written. Two further tests run the real hook through an actual `git commit` in a sandbox repo and assert on the observable outcome — refusing arm: return code non-zero **and** `git rev-parse HEAD` finds no commit object; permitting arm: return code 0 and a commit exists. Tracking issue to be filed post-merge.
- **The agent-completeness gate credited `reviewer`/`security-auditor` as complete even when the artifact `implement.md` instructs the coordinator to write for them was never written**: recording the completion (`record_agent_completion`) and writing the verbatim validator output to `.claude/logs/activity/validators/<run_id>/<agent>.txt` are two independently-forgettable writes by the same party — on 2026-08-29 the artifact write was skipped entirely and a reviewer REQUEST_CHANGES finding that had driven a full remediation cycle existed only in narration, unverifiable by CIA or anyone auditing after the fact. New private `_missing_validator_artifacts()` in `pipeline_completion_state.py` cross-checks: for single-issue runs (`issue_number == 0`) only, when `reviewer` and/or `security-auditor` are both required and recorded complete, their artifact file must exist and be non-empty (zero bytes only — no byte/line-count threshold, since a `>=200 bytes AND >=2 lines` rule tried during development misclassified 6 of 19 genuine artifacts). A missing/empty artifact adds a `<agent>-artifact:<path>(absent-or-empty)` sentinel to the `missing` set `verify_pipeline_agent_completions()` returns, which both existing hook call sites render into their block message unchanged — no file under `plugins/autonomous-dev/hooks/` was touched. The check is deliberately inert (contributes nothing, never refuses) for batch mode — the validators directory name is not derivable from `current_run_id` there, since `implement-batch.md` binds two different values to that name — for state with no `current_run_id`, for a `current_run_id` failing the module's run-id regex (path-traversal guard), for an unresolvable `.claude/logs/activity/` directory, and for any `OSError`; every one of those leaves the verdict byte-identical to pre-change behaviour. Both documented escape hatches (`SKIP_AGENT_COMPLETENESS_GATE`, `touch /tmp/skip_agent_completeness_gate`) still short-circuit before any artifact probing. `docs/LIBRARIES.md`'s `pipeline_completion_state.py` entry and `plugins/autonomous-dev/docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md`'s "Agent completeness gate BLOCKED" row are updated to explain the new `<agent>-artifact:...` sentinel shape, which otherwise reads as a nonsense agent name inside a "missing required agents" message. 19 new tests (2 in `tests/unit/lib/test_pipeline_completion_state_run_id_stamping.py`, 17 in new `tests/regression/test_validator_artifact_receipt_gate.py`; both counts verified via `grep -c 'def test_'` against the diff).
- **The agent-completeness gate credited a second `/implement` run for agents the FIRST run completed — a confused-deputy defect keyed on SESSION rather than RUN (Issue #1045)**: `pipeline_completion_state.py` read completions from a per-session state file with no notion of which RUN produced them, so a second `/implement` invocation inside the same Claude Code session inherited the first run's recorded agents and passed the gate having executed nothing. Reproduced live: a spec-validator was stopped mid-run and the gate still reported satisfied. Fix stamps run identity into the existing session-keyed state file rather than introducing a new one: new public `record_run_start(session_id, run_id) -> bool`, called at `/implement` STEP 0 (`commands/implement.md`) and per-issue in `--batch` mode (`commands/implement-batch.md`, the highest-volume generator since batch creates one run per issue in one session); new private `_record_completion_run_ids`/`_filter_to_current_run`/`_report_lost_run_id`/`_report_run_start_failure` implement a five-state policy — a pre-migration file or non-pipeline session stays fully permissive and silent, a corrupted file (stamps present with no `current_run_id`) refuses loudly and names `SKIP_AGENT_COMPLETENESS_GATE` as recovery, and a stamp belonging to a prior run is excluded. No public function signature changed and no file under `plugins/autonomous-dev/hooks/` was edited, but `unified_pre_tool.py`'s pre-dispatch ordering gate calls the same `get_completed_agents()` in production and is now correctly run-scoped as a result. The `record_run_start` import in both commands is wrapped in `try/except ImportError` so a stale deployment fails loudly (`[RUN-START-FAILED run_id=...]`, pointing at `bash scripts/deploy-all.sh`) instead of crashing every `/implement` invocation. **Known limitation, documented not fixed**: `get_completed_agents(sid, run_id=X)` reads a physically different state file that no production writer populates, so `--resume` has never inherited completions through that path — a characterization test now pins this rather than leaving it implied. `docs/LIBRARIES.md`'s `pipeline_completion_state.py` entry gains the full policy table and a corrected module docstring (the previous claim that omitting `run_id` means "no behavior change" is now false and is replaced). 12 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_pipeline_completion_state_run_id_stamping.py`, 5 in `tests/integration/test_pipeline_run_id_resume.py` (both counts verified via `grep -c 'def test_'` against the files as committed).
- **The same confused deputy survived in the two BATCH aggregate gates, which are the ones wired to the commit block (Issue #1045)**: `verify_batch_cia_completions` and `verify_batch_doc_master_completions` iterate `state["completions"]` directly and never call `get_completed_agents()`, so the run-scoping fix above did not reach them. Both are called from `unified_pre_tool.py` before allowing a batch commit, and a batch retry of one issue inside the same session — a partial failure, a resume, or an ordinary re-run — passed the final batch gate on the previous run's CIA and doc-master credit, silently and with no test coverage. This is the highest-volume shape of the defect: batch mode creates one run per issue inside one session. Fix: `record_run_start()` gains an optional `issue_number=` that records per-issue run OWNERSHIP in a new `issue_run_starts` sibling map; new private `_filter_to_owning_run` scopes each issue's credit to the run that most recently STARTED work on it; new private `_agents_stamped_with` holds the stamp match once, shared with `_filter_to_current_run`; new private `_credited_agents_for_scope` composes the success and run filters for both verifiers; new private `_report_superseded_scope` prints a loud stderr block so the refusal is not misread as "the agent never ran". **`current_run_id` is deliberately NOT the authority for these two gates** — batch overwrites it per issue, so filtering to it refuses a clean 3-issue batch (measured, and pinned by positive-control tests of a different shape than the reproducer). A caller that omits `issue_number` (a stale `implement-batch.md` deployment) degrades to the previous permissive behaviour rather than blocking every batch commit. The doc-master **verdict** is still read raw and unfiltered, because filtering it would turn an invalid `SHALLOW`/`MISSING` verdict into the backward-compatible "no verdict recorded" branch and weaken the gate. Nothing under `plugins/autonomous-dev/hooks/` was edited. 10 regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1045_batch_aggregate_run_scope.py`.

### Changed
- **`WebSearch` restored alongside searxng — the searxng-only migration in `9e7ebd79` is partially reversed on measurement**: that commit rested on decision D1 ("declare searxng ONLY, never both"), whose premise — that searxng could do the researcher's job — was never verified. It does not hold. Measured on the identical query, "Claude Code hooks PreToolUse block decision JSON": self-hosted SearXNG returned **3 relevant results in 10**, no official documentation anywhere in the set, the answer buried at #6 inside a paper abstract; hosted `WebSearch` returned **8 of 8 relevant** with the official docs at `code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks` ranked #1. The cause is not tuning — **`code.claude.com` is absent from searxng's index**, and no engine configuration reaches a host the index does not contain. The container was genuinely repaired first, so this is not a verdict on a broken instrument (`searxng-mcp` commits `8fe2df7`, `107fce8`: categories widened, 8 blocked-by-default engines enabled; a general query went 0 → 35 results and a conceptual query 5 junk pages → 102 results at 72% relevance) and it remains insufficient for documentation lookup. A keyed API was rejected under the $0 rule (Brave killed its free tier for new users; a card on file is now required). `.claude/PROJECT.md` was amended by the user in commit `951fdf1e` to permit the fix; the governing clause reads, verbatim: **"Agents MAY therefore declare a hosted search tool as primary, but ONLY alongside a local one"**. INV-8's binding constraint is therefore **never-alone**, not a prohibition on hosted tools or on hosted-as-primary. Accordingly `WebSearch` is re-added to `tools:`/`allowed-tools:` in `agents/researcher.md`, `agents/plan-critic.md`, `commands/plan.md`, `commands/advise.md`, `commands/implement.md`, `commands/implement-fix.md`, `skills/research-patterns/SKILL.md`, and `skills/planning-workflow/SKILL.md` — in every case *added to*, never replacing, the existing `mcp__searxng__*` grants, which stay REQUIRED because they are the only route that survives on a backend without Anthropic's hosted service. The enforcing rule is **inverted, not deleted**: `check_no_hosted_web_tools` keeps its name and now refuses a hosted tool declared with **no local searxng tool alongside it**, and is watched both refusing (hosted-alone) and permitting (hosted + local, `test_positive_control_hosted_alongside_local_passes_r1` — a fixture the previous rule refused, which is what proves the rule inverted rather than being renamed). `researcher.md` gains a third disclosure marker, `Research: web`, for "the hosted route is what answered"; it does not excuse the required searxng query, and `commands/implement.md` STEP 4 now treats `Research: searxng` and `Research: web` alike as success cases. **`WebFetch` is deliberately NOT restored anywhere**: `mcp__searxng__fetch` is not searxng-specific — it is `client.get(url)` with redirect following in `searxng-mcp/server.py`, a plain arbitrary-URL HTTP fetcher, verified this session by successfully fetching `https://brave.com/search/api/` — so hosted `WebFetch` adds no capability on any backend. Operators who want the hosted route off on a given machine can set `"deny": ["WebSearch"]` under `permissions` in `.claude/settings.local.json` (per-machine and gitignored; currently empty in this checkout). **UNVERIFIED**: whether `permissions.deny` actually blocks a *subagent*'s tool call or only the main thread — that scope was not tested, so the switch should not be relied on as a subagent-level control until it is.
- **Shipped agents, commands, and skills migrated off hosted `WebSearch`/`WebFetch` onto client-side searxng MCP tools (`mcp__searxng__search`, `mcp__searxng__fetch`)**: `agents/researcher.md`, `agents/plan-critic.md`, `commands/advise.md`, `commands/implement.md`, `commands/implement-fix.md`, `commands/plan.md`, `skills/research-patterns/SKILL.md`, and `skills/planning-workflow/SKILL.md` had their `tools:`/`allowed-tools:` declarations and prose repointed to the two searxng tools. searxng was permitted across all 8 settings surfaces (`plugins/autonomous-dev/config/global_settings_template.json` and the six `templates/settings.*.json` files, plus `lib/settings_generator.py`'s generated `standalone_tools` list — the 8th surface, which templates alone cannot reach) and added to `lib/tool_intent.py`'s `MCP_READ_TOOLS` registry so plan-exit passage works for both tools. `config/auto_approve_policy.json`'s `web_tools.whitelist` carried a non-existent entry, `mcp__searxng__web_search`, which never matched any real tool name; it is replaced with the two real names, `mcp__searxng__search` and `mcp__searxng__fetch`. Hosted `WebSearch`/`WebFetch` are deliberately RETAINED in `permissions.allow` (they still function against Anthropic-hosted backends) — only the per-agent/command/skill `tools:` grants were migrated, so this is a routing change, not a removal of the hosted tools from the product. A false sentence in `agents/researcher.md` claiming "the coordinator will check your tool usage count — if WebSearch shows 0 uses, you will be retried" — a check that does not exist anywhere in the coordinator — is deleted and replaced with two disclosure markers the researcher agent now must emit: `Research: searxng` (at least one `mcp__searxng__search` query was issued) or `Research: unavailable (no searxng server)` (searxng was absent or errored, findings marked UNVERIFIED). A second, independent instance of the same false claim lived in the coordinator's own prompt — `commands/implement.md` STEP 4's validation line read "If web researcher shows 0 tool uses, retry" — and is rewritten to match: the coordinator now reads the researcher's returned text for one of the two markers above, re-dispatches the researcher once if neither marker is present, and on a second miss carries the research forward as UNVERIFIED and reports it as a STEP 4.5 source-quality gap. The new STEP 4 text self-labels this as the coordinator reading a returned string, not an enforced gate (INV-1) — nothing in the pipeline counts an agent's tool invocations, so no doc or prompt may claim otherwise. Mechanized regression coverage for the researcher agent's MCP tool declarations was added to `tests/unit/lib/test_agent_registry_consistency.py`, and the now-superseded `test_r3_false_websearch_retry_claim_is_gone` (which asserted only the researcher.md-side claim was gone) was deleted since its assertion is subsumed by the broader declaration check. **User-visible consequence**: on any local-model backend or consumer repo with no searxng MCP server configured, agents that previously fell back to hosted `WebSearch` now disclose `Research: unavailable` rather than silently guessing — a capability regression that is now legible instead of silent, both in the agent's own output and in the coordinator's STEP 4.5 gap report. `docs/AGENTS.md`, `docs/LIBRARIES.md`, and `docs/PLANNING-WORKFLOW.md` are updated in this same change to stop describing the researcher/plan-critic tool set as `WebSearch, WebFetch`.

- **Re-pinned the Issue #1582 integration-tier failure ratchet from a laptop-measured baseline to a CI-measured one** (`scripts/integration_ceiling.py`, follow-up to #1579): `FAILURE_CEILING` and `FAILURE_HIGH_WATER_MARK` move 512 → 521; `MINIMUM_COLLECTED_TESTS` moves 1800 → 1824; `ERROR_CEILING`/`ERROR_HIGH_WATER_MARK` stay at 11. The original 512/1844 pin was measured on a developer laptop carrying `plugins/autonomous_dev`, an uncommitted symlink (`.gitignore:31`, `git ls-files` returns empty) that lets that laptop collect tests no CI checkout can collect — the pin was calibrated against an environment the gate never runs in. Re-measured on GitHub Actions CI across two runs bracketing the #1579 alias fix: run `32639109196` (before #1579) read 1792 collected / 525 failed / 11 errors; run `32650827370` (after #1579) read 1874 collected / 521 failed / 11 errors. Against the first honest CI reading (525) this pin is a **decrease** to 521 — it reads as a raise only against the invalid laptop number. Per the module's two-key design, raising a high-water mark requires editing both `scripts/integration_ceiling.py` and its external witness in `tests/unit/scripts/test_integration_ceiling.py` in the same diff; `REVIEWED_FAILURE_HIGH_WATER_MARK` there moves 512 → 521 accordingly. No new tests were added; two existing baseline-permitting tests (`test_permits_the_measured_baseline`, `test_permits_the_full_measured_run`) were re-pointed from `failed=512`/`collected=1844` to `failed=521`/`collected=1874`. Unlike the original pin, 521/1874 is a single post-fix CI reading, not yet confirmed set-stable across two runs — stated as a caveat in the module docstring rather than hidden.

### Added
- **A shrink-only ratchet pinning the count of append-mode log-writer modules in `plugins/autonomous-dev/{lib,hooks}` at 35 (Issue #1718)**: 35 modules independently `open(..., "a")` (or equivalent — `Path.open`, `io.open`, the append-default `logging` handler family, `os.open` with `O_APPEND`), 13 of them writing into `.claude/logs/activity/`, and nothing in the repository refused another one being added — the gap this closes. New `tests/unit/lib/test_append_writer_ratchet.py` defines an AST-based detector, `append_writer_sites()`, and pins the current 35 sites behind `PINNED_APPEND_WRITERS`/`APPEND_WRITER_CEILING`/`CEILING_HIGH_WATER_MARK`, the same two-key ceiling-plus-mark shape as the five prior ratchets in this family. **Enforced in the `smoke` CI job, not the unit tier**: `tests/unit/` currently carries 78 standing failures (#1719) and the `test` CI job reports `failure` on every recent run, so a new refusal placed there would be indistinguishable from the pre-existing red; `summary` gates merge on `SMOKE_RESULT` independently of `TEST_RESULT`, so the new `Append-mode writer ratchet (Issue #1718)` step in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (no `continue-on-error`) is both merge-blocking and observable today. The module also runs inside the unit tier as a side effect of its file location; its own docstring states that run is not the enforcing copy. 25 test functions, 65 collected test cases (verified via `pytest --collect-only`). Collapsing the 13 `.claude/logs/activity/` writers to one is explicitly out of scope for this issue.

- **A single canonical source for hook execution timeouts — `config/hook_time_budgets.json` — replacing an independently-declared `5` in at least four places (Issue #1704)**: `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/hook_budgets.py` is the new loader (`load_budget_config`, `get_hook_budget`, `has_hook_budget`, `get_library_timeout`, `library_timeout_or`, `check_ceiling`, `check_nesting`, `check_measured_headroom`, `check_declared_hosts_match_derived`, `check_installed_settings_skew`, `record_budget_overrun`, plus the anti-vacuity companions `budgeted_hook_count` / `measured_hook_count` / `inspected_settings_count`). 14 `.hook.json` sidecars had their 16 individually-declared `timeout` fields removed (0 remain); `scripts/generate_hook_config.py`'s hardcoded `reg.get("timeout", 5)` default — the origin of the sprawl — is gone, replaced by `resolve_timeout()` reading the canonical file, and a sidecar that still declares `timeout` is caught by `tests/regression/regression/test_issue_1704_hook_time_budgets.py::test_no_sidecar_declares_a_timeout`. Budgets were sized from measurement over 89,442 `hook_timings_*.jsonl` rows windowed to `ts >= 2026-08-21` (window recorded in the config's `_measurement_window` so the counts are re-derivable): `unified_pre_tool` 5→20s (p99 2,223.4ms, max 13,139.7ms — 23 invocations in the window exceeded the old 5s budget, each discarding all ~51 checks with no record anywhere), `unified_prompt_validator` 5→50s, `stop_quality_gate` stays 60s (already correct; max observed 28,038.1ms), 12 `fast_local` hooks unchanged at 3s or 5s, `auto_fix_docs` newly budgeted at 20s, `genai_prompts.DEFAULT_TIMEOUT` 5→15s, `intent_classifier.timeout_seconds` 5→40s (was equal to its 5s host budget, so the library could never fail cleanly and report — the runtime discarded the hook at the same instant), `semantic_gate.TIMEOUT_S` unchanged at 3s. The 60s ceiling is read from `hook-metadata.schema.json` (`timeout.maximum`), never re-declared; `check_ceiling` additionally refuses any budget below `OVERRUN_FLOOR_SECONDS` (3s), under which an overrun could never be recorded at all. `python3 scripts/generate_hook_config.py --check-timeouts` reports 0 timeout drift across the 7 settings surfaces discovered by content (not by a `settings*.json` glob, which misses `config/global_settings_template.json`), and 2 registrations with no timeout declared at all (both `auto_format`) — reported, deliberately not auto-filled, since bounding black on an arbitrarily large file carries real risk. A timeout-skip is now countable: `HookTimer` calls `maybe_record_budget_overrun()`, which routes one row to the existing refusal sink (`hook_telemetry.log_block_event`) under `decision_shape="budget_overrun"` — deliberately excluded from `BLOCK_SHAPES` so it can never inflate a refusal count — with `HOOK_BUDGET_OVERRUN_DISABLED=1` as a rollback switch and a `MIN_BUDGET_NS` fast path that keeps the check off the hot path (3.28ms → 0.0005ms per hook exit, MEASURED over 15 fresh processes). `host_hooks` is DERIVED from the import graph rather than asserted — including `importlib.spec_from_file_location` edges, this repo's primary hook→lib loader — which caught `auto_fix_docs` sitting as an undeclared host of a 15s library. **120 new tests, MEASURED via `pytest --collect-only`** (25 in `tests/unit/lib/test_hook_budgets.py`, 95 in `tests/regression/regression/test_issue_1704_hook_time_budgets.py`); a `def test_` grep undercounts because parametrized defs expand to multiple collected cases. Three review rounds removed three defects from the fix itself, each now covered by a two-armed test: `--sync-timeouts` silently rewrote an unbudgeted hook's declared bound down to `5` (the sprawl's origin line, reintroduced in the write path); `--check-timeouts` printed installed-settings skew and exited 0, so nothing could gate on it (now exit 1, with `--allow-skew` as the narrow acknowledgement for a known pre-deploy window); and a test claiming to lock the six fail-safe literals compared X to X, because `library_timeout_or` discards its fallback whenever the config is readable — the literals are now parsed out of the source and compared statically. **Not yet deployed**: this is live in source only. `~/.claude/settings.json` still carries the old `timeout=5` and `~/.claude/lib/hook_budgets.py` does not exist yet, which `--check-timeouts` reports as 4 skews and exits 1 on. Deploy with **`bash scripts/deploy-all.sh --global-settings`** — the flag is REQUIRED, because `deploy-all.sh:110` sets `DO_GLOBAL_SETTINGS=false` and a bare run would ship the 15s and 40s library timeouts into `~/.claude/` while settings stayed at 5s, manufacturing the exact nesting violation this change removes. The libraries and the settings land together or not at all. `docs/HOOKS.md` gained a "Hook Time Budgets" section and `docs/LIBRARIES.md` gained the `hook_budgets.py` entry; `docs/HOOK-SIDECAR-SCHEMA.md`'s worked examples and field reference, which still showed `"timeout": 5`/`"timeout": 3` inside sidecar JSON, are corrected in this change to reflect that no sidecar declares the field anymore.

- **A mutation-witness harness that requires a declared test to be OBSERVED failing against a mutant of the code it claims to cover — the DYNAMIC half of Issue #1660, built and NOT wired** (`scripts/mutation_witness_gate.py`, `scripts/mutation_witness.py`): the four `coverage_baseline.py` checks composed by `step5_quality_gate.py` are all COUNTERS — `assert True` satisfies every one of them — and this supplies the property none of them can express. For each `{test, target, anchor, replacement}` claim queued at `.claude/local/mutation_claims.json`, the driver runs the named test twice: once against the unmodified target (the control, which MUST pass) and once with the anchor line replaced (the mutant, which the test MUST fail against). A claim failing either arm — still passing on the mutant (`VACUOUS`), an anchor that cannot be found (`BROKEN_CONTROL`), a tampered target, or an uninterpretable mutation — refuses with `{"decision": "block"}`; `BLOCKING_VERDICTS = {VACUOUS, BROKEN_CONTROL, TAMPERED, INDETERMINATE}` is deliberately narrower than the outcome space, so `SKIPPED_BUDGET` stays queued and named rather than crying wolf about batch size. An 8-shape bypass hunt found 8 defenses, one unanticipated: `pytest file.py::test_nope` exits **4**, not a failure code, for a node ID that does not exist — a harness reading "nonzero means the mutant was caught" would have certified a test that does not exist; the control-must-pass arm refuses it. **NOT SHIPPED, NOT REGISTERED, and deliberately in `scripts/` rather than `hooks/` and `lib/`.** Both halves sit beside their sibling harness `scripts/integration_ceiling.py`. There is no `.hook.json` sidecar, no entry in either `install_manifest.json`, and no registration in any settings surface. Three shipped ratchets measured the earlier placement as a defect and all three were right: `test_no_new_unreachable_refusers` and `test_install_manifest_lists_all_hooks` for the driver in `hooks/`, and `test_no_new_unreached_library_modules` for the library in `lib/` — its only importer was `lib/step5_quality_gate.py`, itself pinned in `PINNED_UNREACHED_LIBRARY` as "named four times across implement.md and implementer.md as the gate that 'blocks', and invoked by neither", and since #1698 the reachability walk is transitive. That `step5_quality_gate` composition has been removed rather than pinned: wiring a harness into a host nothing invokes buys the appearance of a backstop and no enforcement, which is the exact false signal this issue exists to eliminate. Pinning was not an available resolution for any of the three ratchets. **Issue #1660's enforcement loop is therefore OPEN, not closed**: the mechanism is proven refusing AND permitting, the enforcement is not wired, and nothing in the repo writes a claim — so a blocking gate could only produce false refusals today. Landing a producer means editing `agents/test-master.md` and the coordinator wiring, a separate change with its own review; registration instructions are in the driver's module docstring.
