The unwired state is locked executably by `TestDeliberatelyNotShipped` and `TestHonestlyUnwired`, whose negative assertions go red the day a producer or a registration lands. 78 tests collected and passing (38 in `tests/unit/lib/test_mutation_witness.py`, 14 in `tests/unit/lib/test_mutation_witness_safety.py`, 26 in `tests/unit/hooks/test_mutation_witness_gate.py`), MEASURED via `pytest --collect-only` per file, not grep and not from memory. The two instruments disagree in BOTH directions and the disagreement is the point: a `^\s*def\s+test_` grep sums to 82, over-counting the first two files because they embed test source inside triple-quoted fixtures, and under-counting the third because one parametrized def expands to four collected cases. `--collect-only` is authoritative. The STATIC half of #1660 (constant-assertion mutation checks) shipped separately as Issue #1667; this change does not touch it.

- **STEP 4.9 wires `prior_art_search.py` into `/implement` so a mechanical closed-issue search runs before every planner dispatch (Issue #1669)**: the module shipped with its own docstring admitting the wiring was "deferred to a follow-up" — a shipped-but-unwired mechanism with zero production consumers, caught only when a 2026-08-25 `/implement` run planned a hand-rolled mutation engine and the plan-critic, not any mechanism, noticed mutation testing had already shipped and closed under #770. `commands/implement.md` now runs the search inline (no agent) between STEP 4.8 and STEP 5, and STEP 5 requires the resulting `$PRIOR_ART_BLOCK` to be pasted verbatim into the planner prompt — summarizing, truncating, or dropping it reproduces the exact defect #1669 records. The block always exits 0 and reports one of two honest statuses, `HITS (N)` or `UNKNOWN` (never `ABSENT`/`NONE`), distinguishing "searched, found nothing" from "never ran" via an explicit `SEARCH-EXECUTED: yes|no` line. New regression suite `tests/regression/test_issue_1669_prior_art_wiring.py` (15 `def test_` functions, 17 collected via `pytest --collect-only`) executes the extracted Bash block directly rather than grepping for the string `search_prior_art` — a negative control (`test_prose_only_block_fails_the_same_harness`) confirms a block that merely mentions the function without invoking it fails the same harness the real block passes. Three of those tests cover the `FEATURE_DESCRIPTION` path — the bare `/implement "<description>"` invocation, which had no success-path coverage at all — and are proven non-vacuous by mutation: a harness ignoring `FEATURE_DESCRIPTION` reddens the success test, an inverted-precedence harness reddens only the precedence test, and the pre-existing `ISSUE_TITLE` test stays green under both, which is the control on the mutation instrument itself. `docs/PIPELINE-MODES.md`'s Step-by-Step Sequence table is updated to list STEP 4.9. `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/prior_art_search.py`'s module docstring — which said wiring "is deferred to a follow-up — #1669 stays OPEN for that wiring" — is corrected in this same change; it now names STEP 4.9 as the consumer and states the residual honestly: the block is mechanical once invoked, but *invoking* it and *forwarding* `$PRIOR_ART_BLOCK` remain prose obligations, and deployed copies under `.claude/commands/` carry the wiring only after `scripts/deploy-all.sh` runs. **#1669 is NOT closed by this change** — three prose dependencies remain (exporting the query variable, invoking the step, forwarding the block), and the issue is about prose not being enforcement.

- **`find_vacuous_tests()` and a shrink-only ratchet for tests whose assertions can never fail** (`plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/test_pruning_analyzer.py`): existing gates (coverage regression, skip count, test count) are all COUNTERS, so a `test_*` function containing only `assert True` / `assert None` / `assert 1` passes every one of them while asserting nothing. The new module-level `find_vacuous_tests(source, filename)` detector reuses `TestPruningAnalyzer._has_only_placeholder_asserts` rather than duplicating the rule, and `tests/unit/lib/test_vacuous_test_ratchet.py` pins the 29 currently-known vacuous tests behind constants that may only shrink. 17 new test functions — 9 in `tests/unit/lib/test_test_pruning_analyzer.py` (37 → 46 by `grep -cE "^[[:space:]]*def[[:space:]]+test_"` against `git show HEAD:`) and 8 in the new ratchet file; 61 collected across the two (49 + 12, parametrize expands them). An earlier draft of this entry said 27/19, counted from a `git diff` line scan that also matched moved and re-indented lines; corrected here against the two file states directly.

### Fixed
- **`PreToolUseWrite-protect-sensitive.sh` refused nothing in production — registered, wired to the correct payload key and envelope, and its deny list split into deny/ask (Issues #1587, #1588)**: three independent reasons its 0 rows in `.claude/logs/hook-blocks.jsonl` were unreadable rather than clean — it was bound to no lifecycle event in any settings surface despite shipping via `install_manifest.json` to every consumer repo; it read `.parameters.file_path`, a key no real Claude Code `PreToolUse` payload carries, so the pattern match always ran against an empty string; and it emitted a bare top-level `permissionDecision`, an envelope Claude Code honours as neither `hookSpecificOutput.permissionDecision` nor the legacy `{"decision": "block"}`, so any decision it did compute was discarded. All three are fixed: registered on `PreToolUse` (matcher `Write|Edit|MultiEdit|NotebookEdit|mcp__.*`, timeout 5) in every settings template plus `global_settings_template.json`; reads `tool_input.file_path`; emits the `hookSpecificOutput` envelope matching `unified_pre_tool.py` on both allow and refusal paths. A fourth, independent defect was found and fixed in the same change: matching was case-sensitive on a case-insensitive filesystem (macOS/APFS), so `SECRETS.yaml` bypassed a deny already in force on `secrets.yaml` — the same file under a different spelling. Matching now routes through a single `matches_pattern()` helper using `grep -qiE`. Fixing the payload key and envelope turned a hook that refused nothing into a live gate, which made a pre-existing policy question unavoidable: the single deny list is now two decision classes — **deny** (`credentials`, `secrets`, `private.*key`, `.pem`, `.key`, `.git/`) refuses outright; **ask** (`.env`, `.env.*`, `PROJECT.md`) stops the agent and prompts the human, since the hook's own `PROJECT.md` reason text already said "edit PROJECT.md manually" — a request for a human decision, not a blanket refusal. Because the hook never actually ran before, every one of these refusals and prompts is new in practice for consumer repos, including the ones the hook always claimed to make. Side effect of case-insensitivity: the unanchored `credentials`/`secrets`/`private.*key` substrings now also refuse CamelCase filenames such as `AwsCredentialsProvider.java` and `SecretsManagerClient.ts` — their snake_case spellings were already refused. Both `deny` and `ask` decisions record to `hook-blocks.jsonl` with `decision_shape="dict"`, which every existing reader counts as a block; the actual class is in `metadata.decision`, so counting hard refusals requires splitting on that field. Deliberately deferred, not covered by this change: `id_rsa`, `.netrc`, `kubeconfig`, `terraform.tfstate`, `.npmrc`, `service-account.json`. `tests/regression/test_protect_sensitive_regression.py` gains a net 12 new test functions (16 added, 4 removed/rewritten for the new deny/ask shape); `tests/unit/hooks/test_hook_reachability_ratchet.py`'s `PINNED_UNREACHABLE` drops this hook from the 5 gates the #1612 ratchet found unreachable, with `REACHABILITY_CEILING` and `CEILING_HIGH_WATER_MARK` both lowered 5 → 4 in the same diff. `docs/HOOKS.md` gains the hook's first entry in the PreToolUse table — it had none, documented or otherwise, before this change.
- **`docs/ARCHITECTURE-OVERVIEW.md`'s component-count line drifted stale as the mutation-witness files moved into and back out of `hooks/`/`lib/`, failing the live `test_active_hook_count`/`test_library_count` checks in `tests/unit/test_documentation_congruence.py`**: the line was bumped to 29 active hooks and 249 libraries when the harness briefly lived in `hooks/` and `lib/`, then left stale when both halves moved to `scripts/`. On-disk counts are 28 and 248 (per `validate_structure.py`'s `_count_hooks()`/`_count_libraries()` methodology — flat `.py` files in `hooks/`, recursive `.py` files under `lib/`, both excluding `__init__.py`). Corrected; both tests pass again.
- **103 acceptance tests were collected and counted toward coverage but never executed by the standard gate — misfiled in `tests/genai/`, which is flag-gated and skipped by default**: `tests/genai/` conflates a *traceability convention* (acceptance tests live next to each other) with an *execution contract* (the directory is skipped unless `--genai`/`GENAI_TESTS=true` is set); the Test Placement Classification Rule in `commands/implement.md` STEP 6 says static file-inspection tests belong in `tests/unit/`, but the rule lives only in prose (INV-1) and nothing checked "tests in `tests/genai/`" against "tests that actually call `genai.judge()`/reference an LLM." 8 files (103 `def test_` functions, verified by direct count: 35+18+18+13+5+5+5+4) — `test_acceptance_reviewer_improvement.py`, `test_acceptance_runtime_verification.py`, `test_acceptance_runtime_data_aggregator.py`, `test_acceptance_component_classifications.py`, `test_acceptance_validator_session_scoping.py`, `test_acceptance_refactor_excludes.py`, `test_acceptance_hook_test_regression.py`, `test_acceptance_918_read_tool_settings.py` — perform only static inspection (file existence, string/regex matching) with no LLM call, so they are moved to `tests/unit/`, their `@pytest.mark.genai` decorators dropped (19 removed), and their one cross-tier import repointed. Tier reclassified T0 → T3. Verified both arms: before the move, the plain gate reported `1 failed, 102 passed` and required `GENAI_TESTS=true` to run at all; after the move, `pytest tests/unit` runs the same 103 with no flag — `103 passed, 1 warning in 10.15s`. `tests/genai/` collection drops `445 → 342` (445 − 342 = 103, arithmetic checks); the remaining 342 stay correctly flag-gated (`342 skipped` without `--genai`). Regression attribution by set: `tests/unit`'s pre-existing 82 failing node IDs are identical before and after (`before − after` and `after − before` both empty, via `comm` diff). A separately-discovered stale test, `test_ac4_no_production_hook_modification`, asserted on hardcoded line numbers in `unified_pre_tool.py` that Issue #1503 deliberately retired when the gate was broadened to cover MultiEdit/NotebookEdit/MCP editors; rewritten as `test_ac4_write_protect_gate_never_classifies_read_as_a_write`, asserting the mechanism two-armed (refuses `Read`, permits `Write`) and proven strictly stronger by a 5-mutant harness (all killed). `docs/ARCHITECTURE-OVERVIEW.md` and `docs/TESTING-STRATEGY.md` genai test-file counts corrected 52 → 48 — counted from pytest's own collection output (`--collect-only`, distinct source files), which is the authority. An earlier revision of this entry said 45; that figure came from `ls tests/genai/test_*.py`, a non-recursive glob blind to the three files under `tests/genai/skills/`. Two agents reported 45 independently and agreed, because both used that same glob — agreement between checks sharing an instrument is not verification; `docs/LIBRARIES.md` Testing sections for `runtime_data_aggregator.py` and `runtime_verification_classifier.py` repointed to the new `tests/unit/` paths. Anyone who previously ran these 8 files with `--genai` or `GENAI_TESTS=true` no longer needs to — they execute in the default `pytest` invocation.

- **The paid-dependency gate's sidecar disagreed with its own generated settings template, and the naive fix would have silently widened it back to every tool call (follow-up to #1639)**: `validate_paid_dependency.hook.json`'s matcher was `"*"`, registering the PROJECT.md paid-API gate on every tool invocation rather than the write-tools-only scope `docs/HOOKS.md:53` already documented — the sidecar and the five settings templates are two independent registration surfaces for the same gate with no equality check between them, so the disagreement went undetected until `generate_hook_config.py --check` exited 1 (`DRIFT DETECTED`) against `tests/unit/test_ci_hook_enforcement.py:48`. Matcher is now `"Write|Edit|MultiEdit|NotebookEdit|mcp__.*"`, matching what `docs/HOOKS.md` already said; `plugins/autonomous-dev/config/global_settings_template.json` was regenerated via `generate_hook_config.py --write`, not hand-edited. New regression test `tests/unit/hooks/test_paid_dependency_matcher_scope.py` (4 test functions, 8 collected cases) adds an instrument control distinguishing the wildcard from the fix, an instance guard against the sidecar reverting to `"*"`, a class-level guard parametrized over all 5 settings templates, and a both-arms check on the generated surface (admits `Write`, refuses `Bash` and `Read`). Red-before/green-after measured: reverting to `"*"` and regenerating fails 7 of the 8 new tests; the fix passes all 8. `--check` exit code: 0 (was 1), no drift remaining.
- **`proof_of_block.py` reported a repo that deliberately opted out of enforcement as a repo whose guards had broken (Issue #1685)**: the header printed `bypass : present` and every per-guard verdict then ignored it, so in a repo carrying a committed `.claude/.bypass` — the durable per-repo opt-out CLAUDE.md documents — an ordinary inert gate was labelled `FAILS-OPEN` / `FAILS OPEN SILENTLY`, identical to a guard that genuinely broke under fault. Measured in spektiv (committed `.bypass`, `7997575f1`, 17 Jun): `write-pipeline-gate` reported FAILS-OPEN with the hook's own reason reading `Universal bypass active (#969)`, inflating that repo's silent count to 1. The worse consequence was the second one: a genuine fail-open there was indistinguishable from the opt-out, so the failure the harness exists to catch was the one it could no longer see. Bypass is now read through `hook_bypass.is_bypassed()` — the same function the hooks call, not a second `.exists()` — and evaluated PER SCENARIO against the exact cwd each hook subprocess is given, so the five guards that run against temp fixtures outside the repo stay fully measured in an opted-out repo (verified: a synthetic guard that genuinely fails open still reports `FAILS OPEN SILENTLY` while running inside spektiv). A fail-open that IS under an active bypass gets a distinct fourth outcome, `NOT ENFORCED (repo opt-out)`, with its raw classification preserved; `REFUSES` is never rewritten, so the #1435 hard floor keeps its label (six guards still `REFUSES` in spektiv). The two counts are reported separately — `N silent fail-open(s), M not enforced by repo opt-out` — and only N feeds the `FINDING` line, the silent-set ratchet and the goal's abort threshold, which could previously be tripped by a consumer repo using a supported feature rather than by any regression. Committed and uncommitted flags are distinguished (#1601 vs #1434), and the limit is printed rather than papered over: under a file bypass a genuine fail-open in that scenario is NOT distinguishable, and the report says so per guard instead of guessing. Measured after the fix — autonomous-dev 8/8 PROVEN, 0 silent, 0 opt-out, exit 0 (unchanged); realign identical; spektiv 7/8 PROVEN + 1 NOT-ENFORCED, **0** silent, exit 0 (was: FAILS-OPEN, 1 silent, exit 1).
- **A bare `from conftest import X` in two GenAI test files aborted collection of the entire combined `pytest tests/unit tests/integration` run — exactly `pipeline_state.CANONICAL_BASELINE_CMD` (Issue #1666)**: under a combined collection, pytest binds the bare name `conftest` to `tests/integration/conftest.py` rather than `tests/genai/conftest.py`, so both `tests/unit/genai/test_genai_client.py` and `tests/unit/genai/test_soft_failure_thresholds.py` raised `ImportError` and took the whole baseline collection down with them, not just themselves. Fixed by relocating the shared symbols (`CACHE_DIR`, `PROJECT_ROOT`, `OPENROUTER_BASE`, `THRESHOLDS_FILE`, `_load_thresholds()`, `_extract_json_from_response()`, `SoftFailureTracker`, `GenAIClient`) out of `tests/genai/conftest.py` into a new, normally-importable `tests/genai/_genai_support.py` (527 lines), and updating both test files' imports to be package-qualified. `conftest.py` shrinks from 583 to 69 lines and now holds only pytest hooks and fixtures — its actual contract — while re-exporting `PROJECT_ROOT`, `GenAIClient`, and `SoftFailureTracker` for backward compatibility. **A planned deletion was reversed after being disproven by a real collection failure, not by review**: the plan asserted `PROJECT_ROOT` was dead code via Serena's `find_referencing_symbols` ("no referencing symbol anywhere"), but it has 33 live consumers, every one a relative import (`from .conftest import PROJECT_ROOT`) that the LSP did not follow past the relative-import boundary; deleting it would have broken all 33. The conftest re-export preserves every consumer instead. Verified: `pytest tests/unit/genai/ -q --no-cov` → 51 passed, node-ID set identical to the pre-change baseline (empty `diff`, not merely an equal count); `pytest tests/unit tests/integration --collect-only -q` → 12,105 tests collected, 0 errors (previously exited 2 on the two `conftest` `ImportError`s); `pytest tests/genai/ --collect-only` → 445 collected, 0 errors (collection only, no API calls). New regression guard `tests/regression/test_issue_1666_no_bare_conftest_import.py` (3 test functions, mechanically counted via `grep -c "^def test_"`) watches both arms: REFUSES `from conftest import Foo` and `import conftest`, PERMITS a package-qualified import and the differently-shaped near-miss `import conftest_helpers` that a naive prefix match would false-flag. `pytest tests/unit` separately shows 81 failed / 10081 passed; verified pre-existing and unrelated — no file under `tests/unit/` outside `tests/unit/genai/` references `conftest`, `tests.genai`, or `_genai_support` (checked with a live positive-control pattern that matched 373 files, confirming the search itself was working). Left untouched and out of scope: `tests/regression/test_issue_1570_multi_operand_write_targets.py` still fails to collect on an unrelated `AttributeError: module 'tool_intent' has no attribute '_BASH_COMMAND_PREFIXES'`.

- **`test_pruning_analyzer.py`'s zero-assertion scanner crashed on every real file under Python 3.12+, silently abandoning 1,016 of 1,023 test files (99.3%) mid-walk while reporting success**: a dead branch referenced `ast.NameConstant`, an alias removed from the `ast` module in Python 3.12 (this repo runs 3.14.3), so every function containing a real assertion raised `AttributeError`. The caller's `except Exception` swallows the error at `logger.debug`, so the scan returned an apparently-clean, near-empty report instead of failing loudly. CI never caught it because every workflow pins Python 3.11, where the alias still resolves. The dead branch is removed; the swallow-to-DEBUG behavior is left as-is for this fix, with a `DECISION` comment at the call site naming it as a known, stated gap (a scan that partially fails still reports success) rather than fixing it silently in the same change.
- **`collect_cia_findings()` returned zero signals for any non-empty findings directory, silently killing the read half of the continuous-improvement loop (Issue #1658)**: `runtime_data_aggregator.py` contained a bare package-relative import — `from .finding_target_classifier import classify_finding_target` — inside `collect_cia_findings()`, but the module is always loaded flat (`sys.path.insert(0, lib_dir)` then `import runtime_data_aggregator`, no parent package) everywhere in this codebase. `__package__` was therefore empty, the relative import raised `ImportError` on every call that reached it, and the function's own top-level `except Exception` swallowed the failure into `SourceHealth(status="error")` with zero signals — no exception surfaced, so `/improve --auto-file`'s C3 promotion layer saw an empty findings source and never promoted anything. Introduced by commit `7718d3c6` (2026-07-08), which added the new import a few lines below an existing, correctly-guarded `try/except ImportError` flat-fallback for the sibling `issue_triage_analyzer` import but didn't follow the established pattern. Fixed by wrapping the import in the same `try/except ImportError` + flat-fallback pattern. Because the instruction was to close the class of bug rather than the single instance, the same unguarded package-relative import was found and fixed in four further `lib/` modules that share the flat-load pattern: `installation_validator.py`, `migration_planner.py`, `retrofit_executor.py`, `retrofit_verifier.py`. 1 subprocess-based regression test added in `tests/unit/lib/test_cia_finding_store.py` (`TestCollectCIAFindings::test_regression_issue_1658_flat_loaded_module_reads_findings`) that imports the module fresh via the production flat-load pattern in a separate subprocess and asserts on the returned signal count and `SourceHealth.status`, not on import statement text, so a fix that only changes wording without changing behavior cannot pass it. `SourceHealth.status` already distinguished `ok` / `empty` / `error`; no new field was needed — an empty findings directory correctly reports `empty`, distinct from this bug's `error`. **Doc-drift note**: `docs/EVALUATION.md`'s closed-loop diagram describes `collect_cia_findings()` aggregating findings as working, current behavior — that description was aspirational, not true, for the roughly six weeks between the `7718d3c6` regression and this fix; it is accurate again as of this fix and required no edit. The flat-vs-package-relative import convention used across `lib/` (five modules now share the identical guard) is not written down anywhere in `docs/` or the skills — that is a documentation gap, tracked separately rather than fixed here since no doc currently claims otherwise.
- **The context-file guard was a permanently-red check in the one repo it could act on (Issue #1648)**: `hooks/validate_claude_md_size.py` enforced ABSOLUTE size limits, so a repo that *inherited* an oversized context file had every edit to it refused with no edit able to clear the refusal. Measured across the five consumer repos: three carry `.claude/.bypass` and are inert (`main()` short-circuits on `is_bypassed()` before any check runs), autonomous-dev is at 71/127 under every limit, and **realign — 456 lines of `.claude/PROJECT.md` against a 225 ceiling, 167 open issues, actively worked — was the only repo where this guard could refuse anything at all.** Blast radius one repo and one file, which is why the fix is ~35 production lines rather than 400. For the two REPO-TRACKED files the block limit becomes `max(hard_ceiling, line_count_at_git_HEAD)` — *you may not make this file worse than it already is at HEAD*. The mark is the repository's own committed history, so there is **no new artifact**: no baseline file, no format, no writer, no bootstrap step, no stale-detector and no forgery cap, and raising the mark requires committing a bigger file past a live refusal, visibly, in a diff. Three earlier designs were rejected in writing — a committed `.context-budget` TSV plus a CLI (162 prod lines, and nobody creates realign's file so the issue stays unfixed after every step ships), a `.claude/`-resident config (rejected on **derivability**, not on committability — `.gitignore` negates `.claude/PROJECT.md` in both repos), and an in-source constant table on the `scripts/integration_ceiling.py:128` precedent (one shipped hook running in five repos would enumerate its consumers inside deployed source). The fitted `ABSOLUTE_MAXIMUM = target x 4` cap is **deleted rather than retuned** — 456/150 = 3.04, so it had been fitted to the reproducer; under a git-derived mark there is no hand-written integer left to bound. One **real bug fixed on the way in**: `get_repo_root` stops at the first ancestor where `.git` *exists*, but **git does not stop there** — it walks PAST an invalid `.git` into an ancestor repository and returns THAT repo's HEAD (reproduced directly: `git show HEAD:CLAUDE.md` from inside an empty nested `.git` returns the outer repo's content with returncode 0, and `--show-toplevel` returns the outer root). Unpinned, a repo with a malformed `.git` would read a **foreign repository's file as its own size mark**; `--git-dir` pins it, and the earlier evidence that this was safe was environmental — it held only because pytest's basetemp happens to sit outside any repo. Every one of eleven fallback paths lands on the ABSOLUTE ceiling, each arm built inside a fixture where a working ratchet *would* have permitted: git absent, invalid `.git`, **nested ancestor repo**, zero commits, untracked at HEAD, shallow clone, squashed history, non-UTF-8 blob (a `UnicodeDecodeError` is a `ValueError`, **not** an `OSError`, so it is caught by name), `.git` as a FILE (worktree/submodule — unsupported by design), a committed **symlink** (`git show` returns the link target, so the mark is 1 and the ceiling always wins — THIS repo's `.claude/PROJECT.md` is mode `120000` and is that case), and a hung subprocess. Under an in-force mark **both** bands move to it, so the outcome is binary rather than a permanent yellow — "a permanently-red check trains everyone to ignore the whole class" applies identically to warnings. The refusal text is reworked for that case: at 457 the pre-existing message would have rendered *"over the hard ceiling of 456 (target 456)… trim back to 456 or fewer"* — the same integer three times, telling the developer to trim to a size they are one line above — so a ratcheted variant carries four DISTINCT integers (457 / 456 / 225 / 150) and points at its own owner, realign#1681. The non-ratcheted message is **byte-identical** to before, which is why all 59 pre-existing arms pass **unedited**. `lru_cache` is a **correctness** requirement, not a performance one: uncached this is 4 subprocesses x `timeout=2` = 8s against the sidecar's 5s, and a hook killed mid-run emits nothing while PostToolUse reads silence as approval — the guard would fail **OPEN**. Cached it is 2 x 2s = 4s plus the hook's own measured 0.044s. Evidence: **50 new arms**, including four source-level mutations over a copied hook that each flip a NAMED arm against a verified-pristine control (`max`→`min`, a mark forced to `None`, an inverted in-force test, and **dropping the `--git-dir` pin — which flips the nested-repo arm from REFUSE to PERMIT**, so the pin is proven load-bearing rather than assumed), both classify paths asserted to agree including the OK band that `collect_size_findings`' `if not message: continue` hides, and a read-only arm against realign itself with a positive control proving the discriminator it asserts absent is reachable. The hook creates **no** artifact: a permit writes nothing at all and a refusal writes only the sanctioned `.claude/logs/hook-blocks.jsonl` sink from #1639, measured and asserted with its gate metadata rather than excused.
- **A ~150-character Bash command skipped every PreToolUse gate at once (Issue #1620)**: `lib/heredoc_utils.py` stripped heredoc bodies with `<<-?\s*['\"]?(\w+)['\"]?.*?\n(.*?\n)*?[ \t]*\1\b`. `(.*?\n)*?` nests a quantifier inside a lazy repeat, so an **unterminated** heredoc made the engine enumerate every partition of the body — exponential in body-line count. Measured end-to-end through the real hook subprocess: **147 characters at 21 body lines cost 6.65s** against the `"timeout": 5` the deployed `.claude/settings.json` gives `unified_pre_tool.py`; controls from the same run were 0.086s (same-size *terminated* heredoc) and 0.083s (`echo hello`). Exceeding that budget is a **bypass, not latency**: measured in a sandbox, both arms, two runs each, an instant-deny hook BLOCKED while the identical deny behind `sleep 8` **PROCEEDED**, so one over-budget command skips every gate in the hook — including the #1435 protected-infrastructure hard floor. The production timing corpus holds **16 historical over-budget events** for this hook across five days (max 12.719s), predating any probing; this was live, not theoretical. The four prior ReDoS fixes (#1194, #1220, #1221, #1222) all bounded a *character class* with `{1,N}`, and that precedent deliberately does **not** transfer: this is a quantifier over a *group*, so `{0,N}` stays exponential in N **and** silently stops stripping heredocs past N body lines — weakening the 7 security gates the strip protects. The regex is replaced by a **linear line scanner** that emulates it byte-for-byte, including the `(\w+)` prefix backtracking that lets `EO` close a heredoc opened with `EOF`; the reasoning is recorded in the module docstring so the next maintainer does not "restore consistency". Evidence: **50,000 seeded inputs, zero mismatches** on full `sub()` output, with a coverage counter proving 10,661 of those matches actually entered the emulated class (an alphabet without proper-prefix tokens covers it *zero* times and would still be green); a **negative control** scanner without the prefix loop diverges on 9,990/50,000 (0.1998), which is the justification-of-record that the loop is load-bearing; a further 500,000 inputs across three independent alphabets (including exotic whitespace and Unicode word characters) also found zero mismatches. Growth is flat — in-library `t(18)`/`t(21)`/`t(24)` go 0.135s/1.074s/**8.559s** before to 8µs/9µs/**10µs** after (ratio 63.4 → 1.29), and end-to-end the N=21 command goes 6.65s → **0.065s**. All **7 call sites keep their verdicts: 0 disagreements** across 7 gates × 22 commands, with a determinism control (which caught two phantom disagreements caused by the stateful `/tmp/.claude_deny_cache.jsonl`, not by the scanner) and a positive control proving the comparison can report non-zero. Stale in-source claims corrected at the same time: `heredoc_utils`'s "(or EOF)" — an unterminated heredoc is **not** stripped, it produces no match at all — and `unified_pre_tool.py`'s previously-unsourced "Claude Code PROCEEDS on hook timeout", which is now cited to the measurement above. `subprocess_pattern`'s separate cubic behaviour is out of scope and tracked as #1629.
- **The block log was wrong in BOTH directions at once, and is now correctable in neither (Issue #1611)**: `.claude/logs/hook-blocks.jsonl` is the evidence base for whether enforcement works, and measured at 10,966 rows it was **over-counting** (574 rows, 5.2%, carried `decision_shape: "mode_skip"` — enforcement *skipped*, the opposite of a refusal) and **under-counting** (`plan_gate.py`, a real gate, emitted `_output_decision("block", …)` on two paths and recorded nothing; all 287 of its rows were skips) at the same time. An instrument wrong in one direction can be corrected with a filter; one wrong in both, differently per hook, cannot be corrected from the data it produces. Six changes: (1) **`plan_gate.py`'s enforce path now records by construction** — its sole emitter `_output_decision` is decorated with `hook_telemetry.block_event_decorator`, one of the three sanctioned fusing sinks. `block_event_decorator` was chosen over `HookDecision` deliberately because it is the only sink that leaves the **emitted envelope untouched**: driven as a subprocess through both block paths before and after, the stdout envelope is byte-identical (`permissionDecision: "block"`, same reason, same `systemMessage`) and the row count goes 0 → 1; both ALLOW paths still record 0, so the recorder discriminates rather than firing on everything. The decorator gained keyword-only `decision_shape` and `refusal_values`, both defaulting to the pre-#1611 behaviour (`"tuple"`, `{"deny"}`) so no existing caller — including `unified_pre_tool.py`, the repo's largest refuser — changes. `plan_gate.py` leaves `PINNED_OUT_OF_SINK` (6 → 5) and **both** ceilings in `tests/unit/hooks/test_refusal_sink_ratchet.py` drop with it, including the anti-growth literal, since leaving it at 6 would be exactly the slack `TestCeilingIsNotATautology` exists to refuse. (2) **`BLOCK_SHAPES` is exported once**, from `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/hook_telemetry.py` beside the only writer, together with `is_refusal_shape()` / `is_refusal_row()`. It previously lived in `scripts/hook_perf_report.py` and nowhere else — a convention two of three consumers ignored is not a mechanism. All three readers now import it, and none keeps a fallback COPY, because a second definition inside a reader is the defect rather than the fix — `test_no_reader_redefines_the_literal_set` enforces that, and correctly went red when a remediation draft added a local `frozenset` to `pipeline_timing_analyzer.py` as a stale-install fallback. That fallback is now `None` (filter simply not applied, i.e. the pre-#1611 answer) rather than a divergent second rule. (3) **Rows carry an explicit `refused` boolean**, written by `log_block_event` from the shape. A reader that ignores an explicit field is making a visible choice; one that omits a filter is making an invisible omission. `is_refusal_row()` falls back to the shape for pre-#1611 rows, so historical rows classify identically, and a non-bool value in the field is ignored so a hand-edited row cannot launder itself. (4) **Both unfiltered readers fixed**: `scripts/hook_block_summary.py` reports `REFUSALS` and `NON-REFUSAL events` separately and labelled, ranks refusals only in `top_hooks` (so `plan_mode_exit_detector.py`, whose docstring says it cannot block, no longer appears fifth among blockers), gives non-refusals their own ranking, and still reports an unrecognised future shape rather than dropping it; `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/pipeline_timing_analyzer.py` now requires `prompt_integrity_block` rows to be refusal-shaped while deliberately admitting `prompt_integrity_recovery` rows regardless of shape, because `extract_prompt_integrity_recoveries()` cannot join a pair whose second half was filtered away — the asymmetry is a property of that join, not (as an earlier draft claimed) of a live recovery-latency metric going to zero, since the only `recoveries=` call site in the repo is a test. (5) **The two readers no longer disagree about the same rows.** `hook_block_summary.py` counted the 57 `prompt_integrity_recovery` rows in the live log as REFUSALS — they carry `decision_shape: "dict"` because `unified_pre_tool.py` writes them through the refusal recorder *after* its deny branch exits, on the allow path, and #1611's own `refused` boolean is derived from that shape, so it stamped `refused: true` onto them structurally. Under the new `Top hooks by REFUSAL` heading that is a positive assertion that 57 known ALLOWs are refusals — a strengthened claim where the old neutral `Top hooks:` over `N event(s)` was merely imprecise. Fixed in the ONE classifier, not per-reader: `hook_telemetry.NON_REFUSAL_EVENT_TYPES` is consulted by `is_refusal_row()` **before** the boolean and the shape, so rows already on disk are reclassified without the writer changing, and `_normalize_row` delegates instead of inlining its own copy of the rule. Measured end to end over a FROZEN 11,576-row snapshot of the live log (`sha1 ba259511`), so the figure is reproducible rather than drifting with the file: refusals 10,974 → **10,917** (−57), non-refusals 602 → 659 (+57), total unchanged at 11,576 — the partition stays exact, and the delta equals the 57 recovery rows in that snapshot exactly. The REFUSALS heading now names what it excludes. Repairing the *writer* remains `unified_pre_tool.py`'s and is deliberately out of scope here (that file is under separate review). It is NOT yet filed as an issue — `gh issue create` is hook-gated to `/create-issue`, which the remediation pass could not invoke; the three follow-ups are named in the session report instead of being silently claimed as filed. (6) **plan_gate's newly-recorded refusals are separable from honoured ones.** `plan_gate.py` emits the out-of-enum `permissionDecision: "block"` on a `PreToolUse` event, and a recorded row carries `hook_name`/`decision_shape`/`reason`/`refused` and nothing distinguishing it from a genuine honoured `deny`. Recording it without that distinction would convert an unknowable zero (honestly *no evidence*) into a confident positive count that may be counting refusals the client never honoured — a third direction of error in an issue about an instrument wrong in two. `block_event_decorator` gained a `metadata=` passthrough and the call site stamps `permission_decision: "block"`, `protocol_enum_divergence`, `honoured: "unverified"`, `issue: 1589`. No envelope change. A `plan-gate-requires-a-plan` spec was added to `proof_of_block.py` — the first of its eight aimed at a hook other than `unified_pre_tool.py` — watched refusing an oversized planless `Write` and permitting a doc file: **8/8 PROVEN**, fault arm REFUSES, silent set unchanged at 4 so the committed baseline needs no re-record. That spec proves plan_gate *emits* a refusal on both arms; it does **not** prove Claude Code honours the out-of-enum value, and no arm of that harness can — it reads the hook's stdout, not the client's behaviour. That remains #1589's. `scripts/hook_perf_report.py`'s output is **byte-identical** before and after over a frozen 405k-row corpus (`shasum` match on both the text and `--json` forms); byte-identical **yes**; "already correct" **no** — that second claim was in an earlier draft of this entry and is false two independent ways. Measured: across 408,254 rows in 33 `hook_timings_*.jsonl` files the observed `decision_shape` values are exactly `exception` (314,227), `allow` (94,015) and `exit_nonzero` (12) — rows matching `BLOCK_SHAPES`: **0**, with the counting probe controlled (a synthetic `tuple` corpus counts 3/3, a synthetic `allow` corpus counts 0). Independently, `HookTimer.set_decision_shape` has **zero** real call sites across `hooks/`; all 25 grep hits are the no-op fallback stub. So `block_count` and `b_ratio` are structurally 0 for every hook, always, and the reader is better described as *the only one that had the filter* than as *correct*. #1611 corrects the prose and locks the measurement (`TestPerfReportBlockColumnIsDead`, with positive and negative controls on the AST scanner, goes red the day a hook wires the setter up); the dead column itself is a separate change and is NOT yet filed, for the same reason. The synthetic corpus in `test_report_over_a_fixed_corpus_is_stable` is now labelled `SYNTHETIC CORPUS`, because unlabelled it made that column look exercised.
- **A hook that emits a refusal must now have a path that records one (Issue #1611)**: new ratchet at `tests/unit/hooks/test_refusal_recording_guard.py`. This is the arm the #1588 sink ratchet does not cover and the reason `plan_gate` went unnoticed for months — it *was* pinned, it *did* call `log_block_event`, so any "does this file reference a recorder?" check cleared it, but its single recorder call sat on the Phase-E skip path carrying a **non-refusal shape**. The rule: a refusal-capable hook must either route through a sanctioned sink, or call a bare recorder with a `decision_shape` in `BLOCK_SHAPES`. The refusal instruments (`_python_refusal_evidence`, `_shell_refusal_evidence`, `_iter_hook_files`, `_sink_evidence`) are **imported** from the #1588 ratchet, not reimplemented, and a premise test asserts they are the same function objects; the hook list is derived from disk. Both arms are watched, on shapes unlike the reproducer: refusing controls use an `ask` envelope with a `mode_skip` recorder, a `sys.exit(2)` with an `allow`-shaped recorder, and a `return 2` with a computed (non-literal) shape; permitting controls cover the two-act-but-refusal-shaped hook, the fused hook, and the migrated `plan_gate` shape. Driven against the pre-fix tree with `plan_gate.py` as the only isolated variable, the guard fails naming `plan_gate.py` and the other 17 tests stay green. Three hooks remain pinned in `PINNED_UNRECORDED_REFUSERS` — `enforce_tdd.py`, `enforce_prunable_threshold.py`, `enforce_regression_test.py`, all commit gates refusing via a bare `return 2` with zero telemetry calls — under a shrink-only ceiling of 3. That ceiling carries **three** constants, not the #1588 two: an initial draft used the older literal-plus-equality form, and review correctly refused it — that form is green at today's values, green after one legitimate advance (pin 3→2, ceiling 3→2), **and green again when the pin is re-grown to 3**, which is exactly the hole `test_the_residual_headroom_is_zero` was added to close in #1612, the immediately preceding issue. `UNRECORDED_CEILING_HIGH_WATER_MARK` plus a residual-is-zero arm closes it here the same way. The ceiling is also now **watched**: `TestCeilingIsNotATautology` drives the real ceiling test over mutated copies of the module in a subprocess (a constant-versus-constant assertion is unfalsifiable in-process), with the harness's own negative control (unmutated copy must pass, and the `-k` must select exactly one test — a selection matching nothing exits 0 and would read as green on every arm), a refusing arm for growth, a refusing arm for the #1612 re-growth shape, a refusing arm for raising the ceiling alone, and a permitting arm proving the sanctioned advance stays green without a third constant edit. Every mutation anchor is derived from the module's own constants, so the ratchet advancing does not turn the harness red and demand a re-anchor.

### Added
- **A hook that can refuse but fires on nothing is now a ratchet failure, not an unreadable zero (Issue #1612)**: a block-row count cannot distinguish "never invoked" from "invoked, never needed" — both produce **0** rows in `.claude/logs/hook-blocks.jsonl`. Classifying all 33 tracked hook files by the #1588 refusal instruments (imported, not reimplemented) finds **9 gates** (refusal-capable) against **24 observers**; walking the tracked registration surfaces (`plugins/autonomous-dev/templates/*.json`, `plugins/autonomous-dev/config/*.json`) for a lifecycle-event key finds **5 of the 9 gates unreachable**: `PreToolUseWrite-protect-sensitive.sh`, `enforce_orchestrator.py`, `enforce_prunable_threshold.py`, `enforce_regression_test.py`, `enforce_tdd.py` — each registered on no `PreToolUse`/`PostToolUse`/`UserPromptSubmit`/`SessionStart`/`Stop`/`SubagentStop`/`PreCompact`/`Notification` key anywhere in the tracked surfaces, and none invoked by anything else in the corpus either. **A non-zero block count is not evidence of reachability**: `enforce_orchestrator.py` carries 280 rows and reads as a live gate; running only its own unit suite moves the count 280 → 281, so all of it is self-manufactured coverage, not production firing — this hook was not one of the four the issue's title names, because its non-zero count concealed it from the metric that found the other four. A `type: "utility"` sidecar declaration is accepted as a second, legitimate reachability route, but only when backed by a real AST-resolved `import`/`from-import` naming the hook's module, a string constant naming the file passed to an **invocation-shaped** call (`INVOCATION_CALLEES`: `run`, `Popen`, `call`, `check_call`, `check_output`, `run_path`, `execv`, `execvp`, `spawn`, `system`), or a shell line that **executes** the file (interpreter prefix or explicit path) — never a substring, a docstring mention, or a string argument to an unrelated call. That last exclusion was added after a review round: an earlier form accepted a filename constant inside *any* call, so `logging.info("enforce_tdd.py is deprecated")`, `print(...)`, `raise ValueError(...)` and `add_argument(help=...)` each cleared a `utility`-declared gate on one line of prose — measured end to end, a single `lib/` file containing only `import logging` plus one such log call dropped the flagged set from 5 to 4. The importer chain must also be **grounded**: `_utility_route_is_grounded` requires a `utility` hook's importer chain to terminate outside the hook corpus (a `lib/`/`scripts/` consumer) or at a lifecycle-registered hook, so two `utility` hooks that invoke only each other no longer vouch for one another — the `51743c87` defect with two files instead of one sidecar. That distinction is not academic: commit `51743c87` reclassified `enforce_prunable_threshold.py` and `enforce_regression_test.py` as `type: "utility"` to clear a CI drift check that had been red for ~24h, citing imports from `lib/hook_safety.py` and `lib/bugfix_detector.py`; resolved by AST, both citations are a module-docstring line and two comments — nothing imports or invokes either hook. The new `tests/unit/hooks/test_hook_reachability_ratchet.py` (76 tests collected, from 48 `def test_` functions plus parametrization) pins the 5 unreachable gates in `PINNED_UNREACHABLE`, each entry naming which of two conditions it fails (`no-lifecycle-registration`, `utility-declared-without-importer`), under three guarding constants: a literal `REACHABILITY_CEILING <= 5`, an anti-slack `REACHABILITY_CEILING == len(PINNED_UNREACHABLE)` equality, and `CEILING_HIGH_WATER_MARK = 5` (`REACHABILITY_CEILING <= CEILING_HIGH_WATER_MARK`) so a raise costs a second, separately-reviewed constant — `test_the_residual_headroom_is_zero` closes the gap the first two left open (lowering the ceiling alone, without lowering the mark, would have let the pin grow back to the mark with every other assertion green). Same tautology-proofing pattern `test_refusal_sink_ratchet.py` (#1588) already uses, extended by this third constant after review found the two-constant form insufficient. Whether each of the five should be registered or deleted is deliberately **not** decided by this change; it adds only the detection instrument, per the issue's stated split between measurement and remediation.
- **The #1612 reachability ratchet now walks `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/`, closing the fourth item its own docstring named as undetectable — "a dead LIBRARY still vouches" (Issue #1698)**: `_utility_route_is_grounded` treats any importer outside `HOOKS_DIR` as grounded without asking whether that `lib/`/`scripts/` file is itself reached, so a hook invoked only by an orphaned library function still read as reachable — the recursion stopped at the edge of the hook corpus. `tests/unit/hooks/test_hook_reachability_ratchet.py` grounds the `lib/` corpus through its own local walk (`_library_corpus`, `_consumer_nodes`, `_binding_surfaces`, `_bound_stems`, `_references_in` — not delegated to or reusing the #1588 hook-side instruments, which stay scoped to the hook rule) over three routes: an entry surface, a settings-binding surface resolved by CONTENT rather than a `settings*.json` filename glob (two of the seven tracked binding surfaces are invisible to that glob), and — transitively, from anything already grounded — a reference credited under one of three invocation styles: an AST-resolved import, an invocation-shaped call, or an executing shell line. Markdown fences, `python3 -c`, and heredocs are a source-recovery CARRIER (`_embedded_python_sources`) that feeds recovered text into the import/invocation-call style, not a fourth peer route. Modules for which no route resolves are pinned, without touching the hook rule's existing grounding logic (that stays #1612's). The corpus is derived at runtime, never hardcoded: 248 modules, 116 REACHED and 132 UNKNOWN (59,297 lines), with `LIBRARY_REACHABILITY_CEILING = 132` asserted equal to `len(PINNED_UNREACHED_LIBRARY)` so an equal-count swap of which modules are pinned cannot hide behind the same total. The verdict vocabulary is REACHED|UNKNOWN — the instrument never asserts a module is dead, only that no route to it was found. Both arms are observed directly: the unmutated assertion does not raise, and disabling the markdown-fence carrier makes it raise, naming `prior_art_search.py`. 25 modules (14,182 lines) are reached only WITHOUT an import — by script invocation or a settings binding — so an import-only ratchet would have misclassified all 25 as dead. **Markdown NARRATIVE is not a shell program** (review round 1): the bare-path-in-command-position rule was applied to `commands/*.md` and `agents/*.md`, where line-start is a sentence rather than a command position, so four lines of documentation were grounding 24 modules — the largest root being a table cell in `commands/audit.md:56` whose `**/models.py` glob grounded three `models.py` files that then rooted ~20 more through legitimate relative imports. A `.md` line now requires an explicit interpreter token, or must sit inside a fenced block where the full command grammar applies again; no file is special-cased and no stem allowlisted. That re-verdict is why the ceiling is 132 and not the 108 first measured — a sharper instrument made pre-existing orphans visible, the single case the constant's contract allows a raise. Cross-checked: a blunter mutation dropping the bare-path rule for every file type lands on 132 too, with an empty difference set. The 24 include `agent_tracker/*`, `implement_dispatcher/*` and `sync_dispatcher/*` — plainly live packages whose entry point no tracked surface invokes visibly; UNKNOWN is the correct verdict for that state and nothing is to be deleted. The rule that markdown controls must be aimed at the live entry point is itself enforced structurally (`controls_bypassing_the_live_call_path`): an AST pass over this file's own source flags any test that reaches directly for a markdown carrier helper (`_embedded_python_sources`, `_shell_invoked_stems`, `_fenced_code_blocks`) without also reaching — directly or through a helper defined in the file — one of `_references_in`, `library_reachability` or `unreached_library_modules`. It is driven REFUSING over three synthetic sub-helper-aimed controls and PERMITTING over two compliant ones, so its empty result over the live corpus is a measurement rather than an inert probe. The motivating case is `prior_art_search.py` itself (#1669): it shipped manifest-registered and deployed with zero production consumers for a day before being wired in `b5f9e726`, which is exactly the blind spot this closes. Test file only (`tests/unit/hooks/test_hook_reachability_ratchet.py`, 48 → 91 `def test_` functions, 43 net new; 131 pytest node IDs collected, all 131 pass) — no production code changed. `tests/unit/hooks/` as a whole: 2645 passed, 3 skipped (the 3 skips pre-exist at HEAD; this diff adds zero new skip/xfail markers).
- **`proof_of_block` is now portable, shipped and wired — enforcement is observable in consumer repos for the first time (Issue #1586)**: the harness that drives each block-capable guard end-to-end and watches it REFUSE a realistic bad action while PERMITTING the closest legitimate one was the only artifact in this repo demonstrating both control arms, and it ran in **0** CI jobs, **0** hooks and **0** tests, from a top-level `scripts/` directory the install manifest does not deploy. `git mv` to `plugins/autonomous-dev/scripts/proof_of_block.py` plus a manifest entry; the three module-scope path pins (`REPO`, `HOOKS`, `ARTIFACTS` — **three**, not the two the issue stated) are replaced by runtime resolution. `REPO` comes from the canonical `path_utils.find_project_root()` — the sanctioned sink, reached via the verified sibling-bridge idiom (`<script>/../lib`, which resolves in both layouts because the manifest deploys `scripts`→`.claude/scripts` and `lib`→`.claude/lib` as siblings); the two module-private `_detect_project_root` copies in `security_utils.py` and `alignment_gate.py` were deliberately NOT imported and no third copy was written, with `lib/` being unreachable exiting 2 and printing the candidate list rather than falling back. **Acceptance signal, measured not asserted**: run from `~/Dev/realign/.claude/scripts/proof_of_block.py` with cwd `~/Dev/realign` after `deploy-all.sh`, resolving `HOOKS` to realign's *installed* `.claude/hooks` — **7/7 PROVEN in 12.9s**, every guard watched refusing (7 positive arms → `deny`) and permitting (7 negative arms → `allow`), with the same 4-guard silent set the canonical repo reports. The exit floor stays runtime-enumerated (`proven == len(results)`, extracted to `compute_exit_code()` so it is testable) and gained one strengthening the plan's "byte-identical" instruction contradicted but its own acceptance criteria required twice: an **empty** result list now exits 1 rather than passing vacuously on `0 == 0`. New `compare_silent_set()` ratchet compares SILENT membership as a SET, never a count, and **raises** on a baseline carrying no `fault` keys rather than reporting an empty set and passing — the committed baseline had `fault` on 0 of 7 entries and was re-recorded with the fault arm on, pinning the starting silent set at 4. Exit-code polarity is unchanged: fault outcomes never gate. 39 test functions in `tests/unit/scripts/test_proof_of_block_portability.py` (49 cases), including the anti-substitution control (three synthetic all-PROVEN guards exit 0, which a hardcoded floor of 7 cannot do), both ratchet arms authored to different shapes, and a parametrized assertion that all eleven control arms survive the port.
- **`/health-check` runs the proof-of-block harness (Issue #1586)**: the only shipped per-repo invocation point, resolving across the installed and source layouts and printing `PROOF-OF-BLOCK: exit N`, plus resolved `REPO`/`HOOKS`/`ARTIFACTS` and `bypass: present|absent` before any verdict — under a committed durable opt-out every guard legitimately allows, and that must be distinguishable from breakage. Deliberately **not** part of the command's exit OR: a consumer-side check that turned `/health-check` permanently red would train bypass of the whole command. The machine reader is one appended `.claude/logs/activity/` row carrying a top-level `"type": "proof_of_block"` — that sink runs 6,472–23,997 rows/day and none of its existing rows carry a `type` field, so an unmarked row would be unfindable, which is the same defect as never writing it. The row distinguishes "not measured" from "measured, none found" (`fault_arm: false` with `silent: null` under `--no-fault`) rather than emitting a vacuous empty list. `--no-fault` is a **measured** branch: full run 22.9s vs 10.2s, against a command whose other checks total under 1s; both arms are still driven for every guard, so the refusing/permitting evidence is intact. The documented time was corrected from "< 5 seconds" to ~12s to match measurement.
- **`smoke` CI job runs the proof-of-block harness (Issue #1586)**: added `continue-on-error: true` per the plan's measured-branch rule, applied to a measurement taken **in the environment the step actually runs in** rather than on a developer machine. A pristine `git clone` (tracked files only, what `actions/checkout` produces) yields **3/7 PROVEN, exit 1**, against 7/7 on a deployed tree. Root cause, bisected: `unified_pre_tool.py:1750-1751` (`_is_autonomous_dev_repo`) stats the *installed* marker `.claude/commands/implement.md`, and `.gitignore:147` ignores `.claude/*`, so `_is_protected_infrastructure()` returns False and four guards correctly decline to fire — overlaying `.claude/{config,lib,hooks}` alone leaves it at 3/7, adding the marker takes it to 6/7. This **refutes** the claim carried in the plan that the hard floor "matches by path shape without stat-ing the target, so no guard is scope-excluded in a consumer repo". The ratchet flags are deliberately not passed in CI yet: the silent set measured without an installed tree (6) differs from the recorded baseline (4) because guards do not fire, not because they regressed, so ratcheting there would report environment noise as a regression. Installing the plugin in the job and flipping to blocking is tracked as a follow-up. The harness was not weakened to make the step green.

### Fixed
- **A hook could refuse and record nothing — indistinguishable from a hook that never fired (Issue #1588)**: measured across the hooks directory, refusal had four emitter functions (`output_decision` in `unified_pre_tool.py`, `_output_decision` in `plan_gate.py`, `_deny` in `enforce_file_organization.py`, and `plan_mode_exit_detector.py`, which recorded nothing at all) over three vocabularies (`deny`/`ask`/`block`) and two recording paths, one automatic (a decorator wrapping `output_decision`) and the rest manual. The concrete instance: `enforce_file_organization.py`'s `_deny()` had no logging call and no decorator, was registered on `PreToolUse`, and had **0 rows** in `.claude/logs/hook-blocks.jsonl` — a zero that was unknowable, not zero. `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/hook_safety.py` gains `HookDecision` (a dataclass a hook's `main()` RETURNS instead of printing) plus `emit_decision`/`safe_main`, so a hook that never touches stdout cannot refuse without recording — emitting and recording become one indivisible act delegated to the existing `hook_telemetry.deny_and_record`/`log_block_event` sinks rather than a second envelope builder. `enforce_file_organization.py` is migrated: `main()` returns a decision, never calls `print`. Verified by driving the real hook: a disallowed root basename returns `deny` and `.claude/logs/hook-blocks.jsonl` grows by one attributed row; a legitimate path permits and records nothing. **Three review rounds found and fixed real defects in the fix itself.** (1) The sink initially recorded before emitting, and a `json.dumps` failure on a non-`str` `HookDecision.reason` was swallowed by a bare `except: pass` — so a refusal became a silent allow *while the block log still asserted the block happened*, worse than the original 0-row invisibility because it over-counts enforcement (the same fail-open shape as #1471, reproduced inside the mechanism meant to prevent it — the pre-consolidation hook called `json.dumps` directly and hit the same slip). Fixed by `_render`'s coercion chain (`json.dumps` → `default=str` → hand-rebuilt minimal envelope → constant refusal, each tier warning to stderr), a `_payload_carries_decision` post-condition that rejects and rebuilds a sink payload missing `permissionDecision`, a non-fatal `OSError` on stdout that still emits `[hook error]` stating a block row exists for a block that did not ship, and `emit_decision` returning `bool` so `safe_main` raises the implied exit 0 to **1** when a refusal was decided but not emitted. (2) The `test_refusal_sink_ratchet.py` guard matched only `sys.exit(2)`, missing the `return 2` form `hook_safety.py` itself documents and `unified_prompt_validator.py:828` uses in production; adding that instrument immediately surfaced three pre-existing out-of-sink refusers with zero recording calls (`enforce_tdd.py:458`, `enforce_prunable_threshold.py:157`, `enforce_regression_test.py:183`) — pinned (they are commit-gate hooks whose refusal channel genuinely is exit status) rather than migrated, with `PINNED_CEILING` raised 3 → 6 as a baseline correction, not a regression. (3) The ceiling's anti-slack `PINNED_CEILING == len(PINNED_OUT_OF_SINK)` equality was, on its own, a tautology — both operands are constants in the same file, so a diff adding a pinned entry and raising the ceiling together left it green (proven by mutation). The final form keeps the equality *and* restores a literal `PINNED_CEILING <= 6`, which refuses every raise while remaining silent on a lowering. Also fixed: an unsanitised write-target basename could carry a forged directive (newlines) into the model-visible `permissionDecisionReason`; the stale-install fallback path had regressed from recording 1 telemetry row to 0 by routing everything through `HookDecision` (now restored via a direct, independent `hook_telemetry` import); a telemetry failure in the sink could silently zero the audit trail (now reported on stderr, `[hook-telemetry] refusal NOT recorded: ...`); and `_render`'s third coercion tier was unreachable because the tier above it invoked the same hostile `__str__` it existed to catch. `docs/HOOKS.md` (Safe Failure Behavior section) and `docs/LIBRARIES.md` (`hook_safety.py` entry) now document the sanctioned refusal path. 145 tests pass across `tests/unit/hooks/test_enforce_file_organization.py`, `tests/unit/hooks/test_refusal_sink_ratchet.py`, and `tests/unit/lib/test_hook_safety.py`, up from 68 before this work (mechanically counted via `pytest --collect-only` on both the pre- and post-change trees).
- **`proof_of_block` deleted the real pipeline state it was measuring, and its fixtures could destroy the canonical source (Issue #1586, security remediation)**: three defects found by the security audit of the #1586 port, all of which mattered *because* that port ships the harness to consumer repos and wires it into `/health-check`, converting a developer-only hazard into a deployed one. **(1) The probe performed a real deletion of real pipeline state.** `drive_raw` drove `"session_id": "proof-of-block"`, which `unified_pre_tool.py::_is_stale_session()` reads not as a label but as an **ownership claim**: on mismatch it executes `state_path.unlink(missing_ok=True)` against `<repo>/.claude/local/implement_pipeline_state.json`, resolved from the driven cwd — the **real repository** for the four `_real_repo` guards. Measured with four controls before the fix: a sentinel storing a real session id was **DELETED**, while one storing the probe's own tag, one storing `unknown`, and one driven with a matching id all **SURVIVED** — the instrument produced both outcomes, so the deletion was signal and not an artefact of a probe that cannot fail. It was also observed live: this repository's sentinel `recovered_at` advanced mid-session while the probe ran. This is the exact deletion Issue #803's guard hard-blocks during an active pipeline, reached without ever traversing that guard because it is not a Bash `rm` but the hook's own staleness branch, and because `_is_pipeline_active()` gates which decisions fire, a developer running `/health-check` mid-`/implement` silently degraded their own enforcement posture for the rest of the session. Fixed **by construction** rather than by avoidance: `drive_raw` now points the already-sanctioned `PIPELINE_STATE_FILE` override at a throwaway temp path for every run, which also makes the harness **deterministic** — results no longer depend on whether a pipeline happens to be active on the machine, an uncontrolled variable in an instrument whose entire purpose is measurement. A second instance of the same class, found by reading and then **driven rather than assumed**, was confirmed real: `_read_plan_exit_marker()` resolves `.claude/plan_mode_exit.json` via `Path(os.getcwd())` and unlinks it when stale-by-TTL or corrupt — **5 of 8** real-repo guard runs deleted a planted stale marker, while a planted-but-not-driven marker and an in-TTL marker both survived. No environment override exists for that path, so it is neutralised by snapshot/restore in a new `_preserved_plan_exit_marker()` context manager, scoped to `cwd == REPO` because restoring inside temp fixtures would break `run_fault`'s positive control for `FAULT_PLAN_MARKER_CORRUPT`, which proves the fault landed precisely by the hook having consumed the fixture's marker. **The `_real_repo` docstring's safety claim — "This is safe: PreToolUse only RETURNS a decision ... pointing at real protected paths mutates nothing" — was false, and is corrected here**: deciding is not free, and everything downstream that relied on that sentence inherited the error. **(2) A guard scoped to the instance that prompted it.** `verify_injection_instrument()` did `spec["fixture"](REPO)` on `GUARDS[0]`, safe for exactly one accidental reason — `GUARDS[0]`'s fixture happens to be `_real_repo`, which ignores its argument. `_adev_repo` had no `root == REPO` check and unconditionally runs `git init` and overwrites `plugins/autonomous-dev/.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` (7253 bytes, the marker CLAUDE.md documents as the self-maintenance-mode detector) with a 31-byte stub; `_plan_exited` delegates to it and then writes live plan-exit state. This was not hypothetical — running the pre-fix code during this remediation stubbed the real `marketplace.json` and injected a live plan-exit marker into this repository, which then **deadlocked the session's own hook stack for the marker's full 30-minute TTL**; both were restored. The repo already carried the missing refusal at `_apply_state_corruption`, applied at one call site only. It now lives **inside `_adev_repo` itself**, so it covers every present and future caller including `_plan_exited` — the difference between fixing the class and fixing the instance — and the positional coupling is removed outright: `_first_real_repo_guard()` selects by fixture identity, so reordering `GUARDS` can no longer hand the real repository to a temp-dir fixture. **(3) Instrument failure was reported as a guard finding.** `compare_silent_set` caught only `json.JSONDecodeError`. Measured against the live function: a JSON scalar `5` raised `AttributeError: 'int' object has no attribute 'get'`, a JSON list `[]` the same for `'list'`, and `{"results": [1, 2]}` raised `TypeError: argument of type 'int' is not a container` — all three escaped `main()`'s `except (FileNotFoundError, ValueError)` handler and exited **1**, the code meaning "the harness ran and found a guard problem", instead of `EXIT_UNRESOLVABLE` (2), "the harness could not run", sending an operator hunting for a broken guard that does not exist. Not exploitable — it fails closed — but it collapses *instrument broken* into *guard finding*, the precise conflation this harness exists to prevent. `isinstance` checks on the top-level object, on `results`, and per entry now raise `ValueError` in the established message shape so the existing handler catches it; `verify_injection_instrument`'s new `RuntimeError` path is handled the same way rather than introducing a fresh instance of the defect. **Verification**: the harness still reports **7/7 guards PROVEN, exit 0**, with instrument positive/negative controls, both trace-probe arms and all three classifier arms green, and the recorded 4-guard silent set unchanged; the real sentinel's md5 was **identical before and after two consecutive full harness runs**, where previously every run destroyed it. `tests/unit/scripts/test_proof_of_block_portability.py` grew from **39 to 55 test functions** (mechanically counted; 80 cases collected), each defect's regression test observed **failing before the fix and passing after** — 4/11 → 11/11 on a standalone red/green harness driving both copies, with the four control arms passing in *both* states to prove the measurement itself worked. Every fix ships its permitting arm as well as its refusing one: the sentinel and marker tests are each paired with a positive control that reinstates the pre-fix condition and asserts the deletion **does** occur, so survival cannot be confused with an unreachable branch; `_adev_repo` is asserted to still build a legitimate temp fixture; and a well-formed baseline still produces the correct set difference in both directions. Regressions were attributed by SET, not by count: across the change perimeter `after - before` is **empty** (zero new failures) and `before - after` is empty, with 10 pre-existing failures in `test_genai_install_wrapper.py`, `test_sync_settings_hooks.py`, `test_audit_tool_intent_coverage.py` and `test_alignment_delta_precision.py` unchanged and untouched by this work.
- **The #1600 onset gate cleared genuine proposals phrased as hedges — a silent recall hole in the un-overridable layer (Issue #1600, second pass)**: the first pass gated on the segment's FIRST token and stocked `PROPOSAL_ONSET_VERBS` with imperatives only, so `should`, `could`, `want`, `wish`, `suggest`, `consider`, `propose`, `recommend`, `plan` and `need` were all absent — `propose`, the most on-the-nose proposal verb in English, included. Measured against a reconstructed pre-#1600 substring matcher and the live `.claude/PROJECT.md`, four proposals to violate INV-7 escalated BEFORE #1600 and CLEARED after it: *"Proposal to drop the hmac signature to save space."*, *"I want to drop ..."*, *"Suggest we drop ..."*, *"We should drop ..."*. Two further shapes — *"Consider dropping ..."*, *"Propose dropping ..."* — were a pre-existing gap rather than a regression, because a gerund hides the phrase (`"dropping the hmac"` does not contain `"drop the hmac"`); the pre-#1600 matcher cleared those too. All six now escalate on the delta branch. Three changes: the onset set gained the modal, suggestion and proposal-noun openers (repair verbs remain excluded, still asserted by test); the onset is now located by consuming a contiguous run of `PROPOSAL_FRAME_TOKENS` (first/second-person subjects, opinion verbs, hedging adverbs) so `I think we should drop the hmac` reaches its modal while `A reviewer asked whether we should duplicate this content` stops dead at `a` — the closed set, not the length cap, is the protection, and enumerating two-token subject+modal pairs was rejected as a members list after this repo's own smoke test found `I think we should ...` still clearing; and each segment is now matched de-gerunded as well as verbatim, purely additively (`_degerund_variants` is consulted only after the raw segment, so a rewrite can add a match but never remove one). **The previous entry's claim that "every lexical error in a proposal-only list over-escalates, so it cannot silently kill a true positive" was wrong, and is what hid this hole** — errors are not one-directional: a spurious opener over-escalates (cheap, visible), a missing one under-escalates (expensive, invisible), and the onset set is the only route by which a phrase can reach an escalation. The module comment and `docs/LIBRARIES.md` now say so. Re-measured in BOTH directions rather than reasoned about: the 30-row descriptive census stays **0/30** (each row's `reason` branch checked, not just its outcome — census row 23, *"A reviewer asked whether we should duplicate this content into the runbook"*, is the specific `should` precision risk and still clears), the named 12-entry positive population stays **11/12** with `arch-006` the sole injection-precedence exception, and corpus balanced accuracy is byte-identical at **0.9714 tuning / 0.8750 holdout** with identical escalate-class miss SETS (`{out-012, out-018}`, `{out-021}`) and zero clear-class false escalations. Full-suite regressions were attributed by SET rather than by count: `after - before` is **empty** (zero new failures), and the 4 entries in `before - after` are order-dependent flakes in `test_coverage_threshold_isolation.py` and `test_gh_issue_create_regression.py` that still fail in isolation and are NOT claimed as fixes. `tests/regression/test_alignment_delta_precision.py` grew from **27 to 44 test functions** (mechanically counted; 175 → 212 cases across the three alignment modules, 292 across all six modules that import `alignment_classifier`): `TestModalProposalsStillEscalate` (the six rows, each observed failing before this fix and passing after, each labelled regression-vs-gap and that label itself executed against the reconstructed pre-#1600 matcher), `TestHedgedOnsetDoesNotCostPrecision` (the permitting arm — reported speech, third-person modals, the frame-scan bound), `TestProposalFrameIntegrity` (reporting verbs and third-person subjects asserted ABSENT from the frame set), `TestDegerundIsAdditiveOnly`, and a class-level guard, `test_no_named_proposal_is_lost_versus_the_pre_1600_matcher`, which asserts the property rather than enumerating hedge words: anything the unconditional substring matcher escalated on the named proposal population must still escalate, whatever its wording.
- **Alignment Stage 0 escalated on any prose that DESCRIBED an invariant, not only prose proposing to change one (Issue #1600)**: `lib/alignment_classifier.py::detect_architecture_delta()` matched all 30 `ARCHITECTURE_DELTA_PHRASES` as bare substrings over the whole feature text with no context test. Measured against the live module and this repo's `.claude/PROJECT.md`: **30 of 30 phrases admitted a plain descriptive sentence that escalated**. The bias pointed the wrong way — the gate fired hardest on briefs describing an invariant violation, which is exactly the brief needed to repair one — and it fired in production twice on 2026-08-21, on the #1586 brief (`fail open`, a measured fault-injection finding) and on #1600's own repair brief (`prompt-level advisory`); the Stage 1 classifier independently returned `in_scope` at high confidence for both. The fix is universal sentence-onset gating: the text is split into segments on `[\n.;:!?,]` and a phrase escalates only when the segment carrying it OPENS with a `PROPOSAL_ONSET_VERBS` token, matched by exact token equality after trimming surrounding punctuation and truncating at an apostrophe — so `Let's` matches `let` while the third-person `Replaces` does not match `replace`, which is precisely why a brief reporting *"Replaces prompt-level advisory text with a hook"* now clears. Repair verbs (`fix`, `add`, `document`, `test`, `harden`, `strengthen`, `block`, `enforce`, `restore`, `gate`, `tighten`) are deliberately excluded from the onset set, with an inline comment naming this issue; admitting one would recreate the defect. There is no descriptive-marker list and no suppression path. (**Superseded**: this entry originally claimed "every lexical error in a proposal-only list over-escalates, so it cannot silently kill a true positive". That is false — a *missing* opener silently kills a true positive, which is exactly what shipped here and what the second-pass entry above corrects.) Purely punctuational leading tokens are skipped when locating the onset, so a proposal written as a markdown list item (`- **Let's** merge the reviewer ...`) is not exempted by its bullet. Recall is unchanged, verified by SET rather than count: the 11 delta-branch positives (3 proposals stated in #1600 + 8 of the 9 `arch-*` corpus entries) all still escalate; `arch-006` escalates via injection precedence both before and after, since `run_stage0` checks `injection_signals()` first. Corpus balanced accuracy is identical before and after (0.9714 tuning / 0.875 holdout) with zero new escalate-class misses. Stage 0 remains deterministic and offline — `re` only, no LLM (INV-6) — and the consumer-repo `has_invariants` exemption stays the first statement of the function body, asserted verbatim by a test. 66 new tests in `tests/regression/test_alignment_delta_precision.py` (a 30-row descriptive census asserting the `reason` BRANCH rather than the outcome, plus refusing-arm controls and programmatic delimiter safety over the live tuple) and 10 new `in_scope` corpus entries, each observed escalating on the delta branch before the fix and clearing after.
- **`GenAIAnalyzer` never successfully invoked the LLM — the failure was indistinguishable from a model refusal (Issue #1593, Part 1 of 2)**: `hooks/genai_utils.py::GenAIAnalyzer._initialize_client()` called `Anthropic()` with no credential. That call does not raise — it returns a truthy client with `api_key=None` — so the module's own `if not self.client` emptiness guard could never fire, and the resulting request-time `TypeError` was swallowed by a blanket `except Exception` handler. Measured: `llm_used=True` appears zero times across 19,915 telemetry rows spanning 17 days. New module `lib/genai_credentials.py` is now the single sanctioned place in the repository to construct an `anthropic.Anthropic` client: a two-layer check (an environment pre-check so a credential-less client is never built at all, plus an `auth_headers` post-check as defence in depth) resolves `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` first, then `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` (newly honoured by this path), then returns `None` — every consumer degrades to its existing non-LLM path on `None`, preserving INV-8 (no gate requires a paid API to function). `hooks/genai_utils.py` and the two direct-construction sites in `lib/genai_refactor_analyzer.py` (`_submit_batch`, `_poll_batch`) now route through it. A new ratchet test, `tests/unit/lib/test_anthropic_client_ratchet.py`, AST-scans `hooks/`, `lib/`, and `scripts/` and fails the build on any new direct `Anthropic(...)` construction outside the sanctioned site; six pre-existing exceptions (`alignment_gate.py`, `conflict_resolver.py`, `genai_manifest_validator.py`, `genai_validate.py`, and two undeployed `scripts/` sites) are pinned by exact membership with a ceiling asserted by equality, so the exemption list can only change via a deliberate two-line diff. 43 new tests total across `tests/unit/lib/test_anthropic_client_ratchet.py`, `tests/unit/hooks/test_genai_utils_auth_chain.py`, and `tests/unit/hooks/test_genai_analyzer_degradation.py`. **Part 1 deliberately does not close #1593** — no CLI/subprocess transport change, no change to failure-outcome labelling or the telemetry schema, no change to the intent classifier's enablement flags. The headline signal (`llm_used=True` appearing even once) requires Part 2's transport work.
- **A red unit suite silently hid the integration and regression suites in CI (Issue #1580)**: the first `Full Test Suite` run to complete after #1567 immediately exposed the next layer of the same problem — unit failed and integration and regression were both recorded `skipped`, so two-thirds of the test surface stayed invisible. Cause: all three `python -m pytest` steps in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s `test` job carried only `if: steps.route.outputs.skip_all != 'true'`, and GitHub Actions applies an implicit `success()` status check to any `if:` expression that contains no status-check function — so once the unit step failed, its own `if:` was effectively `success() && ...` for the remaining steps and both were suppressed. All three steps now carry `!cancelled()`, so a failure in one suite no longer prevents the others from running and reporting their own results. `!cancelled()` rather than `always()`: GitHub's own documentation names `!cancelled()` the recommended alternative, because `always()` also runs steps after a human deliberately cancels the run. `continue-on-error` was deliberately not used — it would let the job report success while a suite is failing, which is worse than the suppression it would replace. **Adding `!cancelled()` also removes a gate that had to be named back**: the implicit `success()` GitHub applies only to status-function-free expressions was load-bearing — it is what made a failed `Install dependencies` skip all three suites. Without it a broken `pip install` would run three suites' worth of `ModuleNotFoundError`, output indistinguishable in the log from genuine test failures, and #1579 records that 77 of the 237 current unit failures already *are* missing-dependency import errors, so real and fake import noise would be unseparable by eye. `Checkout code`, `Set up Python` and `Install dependencies` therefore gained `id:`s and each suite step's `if:` now names them: `!cancelled() && steps.checkout.outcome != 'failure' && steps.setup_python.outcome != 'failure' && steps.install.outcome != 'failure' && steps.route.outputs.skip_all != 'true'`. `Route tests` is deliberately *absent* from that list, preserving the asymmetry: it is engineered to fail open (`continue-on-error: true`, and a crash writes nothing to `$GITHUB_OUTPUT` so `skip_all` is unset and everything runs) because a broken router must not silence the suites, whereas a broken install must. `outcome` rather than `conclusion` —
