`conclusion` is the value after `continue-on-error` is applied, so a prerequisite that later gains `continue-on-error: true` would read `success` while broken, disarming the gate from an unrelated diff. `!= 'failure'` rather than `== 'success'` — a step's `outcome` is `skipped` when its own `if:` is false, which is exactly what happens to `Install dependencies` whenever the router sets `skip_all`, and treating that skip as grounds to suppress the suites would be silent suppression on a non-failure, i.e. #1580 again through a third door. `tests/regression/test_ci_pytest_timeout.py` gained a class of guards that discover every job with two or more pytest-running steps directly from the parsed workflow YAML (never a hardcoded job list, so a fourth suite added later is covered automatically), assert none of them can be suppressed by an earlier sibling's failure, assert no test step carries `continue-on-error`, and assert every failure-tolerant suite step gates on every prerequisite the parsed job structure says can break it — prerequisites are *derived* (every earlier step that is neither a sibling suite nor `continue-on-error: true`), so no step name appears in the guard and a setup step added later is covered with no test edit. The failure-tolerance classifier was hardened from a substring match to whole-operand matching, because `${{ always() == false }}` contains the token while meaning its opposite and would have been waved through as compliant. `docs/GITHUB-ACTIONS.md` updated to describe the independent-reporting behavior, the prerequisite-gating rule with its `outcome`/`!= 'failure'` reasoning, the deliberate `route` asymmetry, and the warning against `continue-on-error` as the wrong fix.
- **CI's `Full Test Suite` job had not completed since 2026-05-25 — it was hanging, not slow (Issue #1567)**: `patch.object(display, 'should_continue', side_effect=[True, False])` in `tests/unit/test_progress_display.py` patched a boolean *attribute* rather than a method, which installs a `MagicMock` — unconditionally truthy — so `ProgressDisplay.run()`'s poll loop could never see `should_continue` become `False` and ran forever. The `test` job's timeout had already been raised 15 → 60 minutes (misdiagnosed as "the suite is just slow"); raising the ceiling only made each hang more expensive, and the job kept ending as GitHub Actions `cancelled` with **zero test results**, not a passing or a failing run. `ProgressDisplay` gained a real `stop()` method giving `run()`'s poll loop a genuine, externally-settable termination condition, and `pipeline_controller.py`'s post-SIGKILL `wait()` — previously unbounded, so a wedged (D-state) child blocked the caller forever with no error — is now bounded by a new `KILL_REAP_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 5.0` constant; past it, `stop_display()` reports the failure to stderr (pointing to `plugins/autonomous-dev/docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md`, which now documents this case) and returns `False` instead of hanging. `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s five pytest invocations (across the `smoke`, `genai-validation`, and `test` jobs) now all carry a per-test `--timeout` (`smoke` 30s, the rest 300s) with `--timeout-method=signal`, so a future hang fails **by name, with a stack trace**, and the rest of the suite still runs, instead of silently cancelling the whole job. **Requires `pytest-timeout>=2.1.0` for local dev** — now pinned in `plugins/autonomous-dev/requirements-dev.txt` and installed in every CI job that passes `--timeout`; a bare local `pytest tests/` is unaffected (the flag is CI-only), but any future workflow step that adds `--timeout` without also installing the plugin in that same job will hard-error with "unrecognized arguments" — several steps use `-o "addopts="`, which wipes any `pytest.ini`-level `addopts`, so the flag must be passed per-invocation, not configured centrally. A new class-wide regression guard, `tests/regression/test_ci_pytest_timeout.py`, walks every job in every `.github/workflows/*.yml` file and hard-fails a PR that adds an unbounded pytest invocation, uses `--timeout-method=thread` (which kills the whole process and reproduces the "no results" failure), sets a `--timeout` too close to its job's cap to ever fire, or omits the matching `pytest-timeout` install. 13 new/updated tests total across `tests/unit/test_progress_display.py`, `tests/unit/test_pipeline_controller.py`, and the new regression file. Two follow-on issues were filed rather than folded into this fix: **#1576** (`safety-net.yml` is independently and permanently red — `--cov=src` against a repo with no `src/`, 40/40 recent runs failed) and **#1577** (two pre-existing display-rendering defects this fix unmasked — `truncate_message()` has zero callers, and a status banner breaks positional agent-ordering).

### Added
- **Read-only Serena LSP symbol tools granted to four analysis agents (Issue #1546)**: `CLAUDE.md`'s "Code Navigation (LSP > grep when available)" rule previously could only be followed by the coordinator — no agent's `tools:` frontmatter allowlist contained an `mcp__` entry, so specialists doing dependency analysis and dead-code detection fell back to grep even when the Serena LSP MCP server was configured. `planner` (`find_symbol`, `find_referencing_symbols`, `get_symbols_overview`), `researcher-local` (those three plus `find_implementations`), `reviewer` (those three plus `get_diagnostics_for_file`), and `test-coverage-auditor` (`get_symbols_overview` and `find_referencing_symbols` only — no `find_symbol`, a minimal-by-role grant rather than a confidentiality boundary: `find_referencing_symbols` returns literal source in `content_around_reference` and the agent holds `Bash`) now hold these grants. Every write-capable Serena tool is forbidden to every agent, including `implementer`, `doc-master`, `spec-validator`, and `test-master`, which already hold `Write`/`Edit` — closing the class of gap tracked by Issue #1503 (closed) and #1510/#1511 (open) where MCP editing tools evaded the write gates. A new `optional_mcp:` frontmatter key declares MCP tools an agent's prose attempts but does not unconditionally hold (Appium tools on `mobile-tester`, the Playwright navigate tool on `ui-tester`, Playwright runtime-verification tools on `reviewer`), each guarded by an existing availability check. `planner.md`'s Call-Boundary Audit now names `mcp__serena__find_referencing_symbols` as the primary caller-enumeration tool with `Grep` as the documented fallback, removing the reference to `findReferences` — a tool name that never existed. Each granted agent emits a `Navigation: serena` or `Navigation: grep (serena unavailable)` disclosure line and falls back to `Grep` on any Serena error or absence. Mechanically enforced by `tests/unit/lib/test_agent_registry_consistency.py::TestAgentMcpToolDeclarations`, whose negative controls prove the checker refuses prose-cites-undeclared-tool, granted-writer, unregistered-tool, wildcard-for-any-server (write-capable or not), concrete-unclassified-optional-token, concrete-`browser_evaluate`-optional-token, and malformed-frontmatter cases, and whose positive controls prove it still permits the shipped `mobile-tester`/`ui-tester`/`reviewer` declarations. `optional_mcp:` forbids wildcards outright for every server: a wildcard asserts coverage over tools that cannot yet have been classified, and a ban scoped to servers already listed in `MCP_WRITE_TOOLS` would have admitted `mcp__playwright__browser_evaluate` (arbitrary JS execution, in neither registry) through `mcp__playwright__*`. The wildcard ban alone closed only the *instance*: INV-2 iterates `tools:` only, so any **concrete** token from a server with zero `MCP_WRITE_TOOLS` entries still passed every invariant with zero violations regardless of what the tool did — `mcp__appium__tap` and `mcp__appium__type` mutate device state and shipped that way. INV-5 closes the class: every concrete `optional_mcp:` entry must be on a **positive allowlist** — `MCP_READ_TOOLS` or the new `MCP_OPTIONAL_DECLARABLE_TOOLS` registry in `tool_intent.py`, via the canonical `may_be_declared_optional()` predicate — with unclassified tokens refused by construction. The weaker rule "every declared tool must be *classified*" is deliberately not what is enforced, because it does not close the hole: `browser_evaluate` **is** classified today (`MCP_KNOWN_EXEC_TOOLS` in `scripts/audit_tool_intent_coverage.py`) and must still be refused, since no availability check can bound arbitrary JavaScript execution. Declarable is therefore strictly wider than grantable *by design*: `tap` may be declared behind an availability check but never granted, and it is classified into `MCP_OPTIONAL_DECLARABLE_TOOLS` rather than laundered into `MCP_READ_TOOLS`, whose membership carries runtime authority (`classify()` returns `READ`; the plan-exit gate lets members through). `MCP_OPTIONAL_DECLARABLE_TOOLS` is not consulted by `classify()` — a branch ahead of the fail-closed shape test would disable that fallback for members, and one after it would be dead code — so this change is behaviour-preserving at every enforcement site. `docs/AGENTS.md` updated with per-agent tool lines, a new "MCP Tool Declarations" section describing the `tools:`/`optional_mcp:` convention, and a new `test-coverage-auditor` section (previously undocumented). (#1546)
- **Two-stage PROJECT.md alignment gate (Issue #1467)**: `/implement` STEP 2 (and L1/F1/I1.6 in light/fix/batch mode) is converted from coordinator self-attestation to a hard, specialist-classified gate. New `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/alignment_classifier.py` implements: a deterministic **Stage 0** pre-check (`run_stage0()`) that scans untrusted feature text for prompt-injection markers, PROJECT.md OUT-of-scope keyword overlap, and architecture-invariant deltas — its `ESCALATE`/`BLOCK` outcome is final per INV-6 and can never be overridden by an LLM judgment; a **Stage 1** fresh-context classification via the new 17th agent, `alignment-classifier` (Haiku, `plugins/autonomous-dev/agents/alignment-classifier.md`), which must cite a PROJECT.md clause that `verify_citation()` confirms verbatim before the gate can `auto_pass`; and `record_alignment_verdict()`, the sole writer of `alignment_passed`, which persists an audit artifact (`.claude/alignment_verdict.json`) and JSONL telemetry (`.claude/logs/alignment_verdicts.jsonl`) *before* touching pipeline state, downgrading to `escalate` on any write failure (fail-closed, INV-7). The lowercase verdict enum (`auto_pass`, `escalate`, `user_approved`, `block`) is co-signed into the pipeline state's HMAC alongside the existing `alignment_passed` boolean (`pipeline_state.py`). `unified_pre_tool.py` now rejects a present-but-disallowed verdict even when a consumer bypasses `record_alignment_verdict()` directly, and extends the STEP-2 hard gate to block `implementer`/`test-master`/`doc-master` dispatches (not just the coordinator) on an unresolved `escalate`/`block`. Interactive `escalate` routes through `AskUserQuestion`; autonomous/batch contexts (`is_autonomous_context()`, `drain_runner.py` now sets `AUTONOMOUS_DEV_NONINTERACTIVE=1` for driven runs) instead hard-BLOCK and label the issue `needs-scope-decision` (new label registered in `scripts/setup-labels.sh`). PROJECT.md gains a new `### INVARIANTS` section (INV-1..INV-8) documenting the load-bearing properties (hooks-not-nudges, fresh-context specialists, fixed pipeline shape, protected infrastructure, one-topic-one-home, deterministic-before-probabilistic, signed/fail-closed state, local-and-free) that Stage 0 checks proposed changes against; repos with no `INVARIANTS` section are never architecture-blocked. Legacy pipeline states (no `alignment_verdict` field) and consumer installs without the classifier library fall back to the pre-#1467 boolean-only gate for backward compatibility. The single STEP 2 caller is `evaluate_and_record()`, which wires PROJECT.md parsing, Stage 0, the Stage 1 fold (`map_verdict()`), and persistence (`record_alignment_verdict()`) together end to end so `commands/implement.md` cannot drift from the library surface. **Security hardening**: the `escalate` -> `user_approved` upgrade is now REFUSED when no human is present (`is_autonomous_context()`) — the attempt is recorded as `user_approved_refused: "autonomous_context"` rather than silently accepted, and an already-`user_approved` verdict arriving from upstream is downgraded the same way, closing a bypass where a caller could pre-set the flag; an *applied* interactive approval is now recorded as an `approval` evidentiary sub-object (`source`, `approved_at`, `stage0_reason`, `citation_verified`) so a real `AskUserQuestion` round-trip is distinguishable from a bare flag flip. `ALIGNMENT_USER_APPROVED` is added to `unified_pre_tool.py`'s `PROTECTED_ENV_VARS` to block inline Bash spoofing of the approval flag (`ALIGNMENT_USER_APPROVED=1 ...`). Stage 0 text now passes through NFKC + zero-width-character (Unicode category `Cf`) normalization before any detector runs, closing an obfuscation bypass where fullwidth characters or zero-width splices inside a phrase marker ("ign​ore previous instructions") could defeat literal matching. Seeded-corpus regression tests enforce CI floors against `tests/fixtures/alignment_classifier_corpus.json`. `docs/LIBRARIES.md`, `docs/HOOKS.md`, `docs/WORKFLOW-DISCIPLINE.md`, `docs/PIPELINE-MODES.md`, `docs/BATCH-PROCESSING.md`, and `docs/ARCHITECTURE-OVERVIEW.md` updated. 194 new tests across `tests/unit/lib/test_alignment_classifier.py` (89), `tests/unit/lib/test_pipeline_state_alignment_verdict.py` (9), `tests/unit/hooks/test_alignment_verdict_gate.py` (32), `tests/regression/test_alignment_classifier_corpus.py` (20), `tests/regression/test_alignment_gate_backcompat.py` (27), and `tests/unit/lib/test_alignment_integration_wiring.py` (17). (#1467)
- **Eval-metrics primitives library (Issue #1453 CORE)**: New stdlib-only `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/eval_metrics.py` providing dependency-free building blocks for scoring non-deterministic agent/model evaluations: reliability (`pass_at_k` — Chen et al. unbiased pass@k estimator; `pass_hat_k`/`pass_hat_k_dataset` — the pass^k consistency-under-resampling gating metric, distinct from pass@k), statistical gating (`wilson_interval`/`WilsonInterval` — non-flaky score-rate confidence bounds; `gate_decision` — pass/fail gate on the Wilson lower bound vs. a baseline minus margin), judge calibration (`cohens_kappa` — chance-corrected inter-rater agreement; `agreement_report`/`AgreementReport` — reports raw agreement alongside kappa so chance inflation is never the unqualified headline), and contamination detection (`capbencher_binomial_test`/`CapBencherResult` — one-sided exact binomial test flagging accuracy implausibly above a Bayes cap). No file I/O, network, or subprocess calls; pure computation. Judge-panel aggregation, DeepEval integration, and trajectory/span evals are explicitly deferred to #1452 — this module has no command-level integration yet. `docs/LIBRARIES.md` updated with a full API entry. 51 new unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_eval_metrics.py`. (#1453)
- **SessionStart staleness warning for a forgotten `.claude/.bypass` (Issue #1434)**: A new `check_bypass_staleness()` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/hook_bypass.py` emits a non-blocking WARNING when a `.claude/.bypass` file is **both** uncommitted (not git-tracked) **and** older than a configurable threshold (default 24h, override via `AUTONOMOUS_DEV_BYPASS_STALE_HOURS`) — catching the common mistake of `touch .claude/.bypass` for an emergency and forgetting to remove it. A committed `.bypass` (the supported durable per-repo opt-out) and the process-scoped `AUTONOMOUS_DEV_BYPASS=1` env var never warn. `SessionStart-batch-recovery.sh` now calls this check on every session start; its `SessionStart` matcher in `plugins/autonomous-dev/templates/settings.autonomous-dev.json` was broadened from `compact`-only to `*` so the check runs regardless of session source. The check is a separate reaper — `is_bypassed()` and all existing bypass behavior are unchanged. `docs/HOOKS.md`, `docs/HOOK-REGISTRY.md`, `docs/LIBRARIES.md`, `plugins/autonomous-dev/docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md`, and `README.md` updated to document the new env var and warning. 11 new unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_hook_bypass.py` (`TestCheckBypassStaleness`). (#1434)
- **`/align --project --invariants` derives and audits PROJECT.md's `### INVARIANTS` section (Issue #1489)**: New sub-mode lets a brownfield repo opt into the Issue #1467 architecture-delta gate without hand-authoring invariants. Runs entirely inline (no sub-agent, Read/Grep only), mirroring the `--content` mode's AUDIT → PROPOSE → APPROVE → EXECUTE shape. **Derive** (no invariants yet): gathers evidence in priority order (PROJECT.md/README/CLAUDE.md stated guarantees, hooks returning `{"decision": "block"}`, contract tests, CI gates, policy/config files, git-log "never do X" corrections), then proposes 5-8 candidate `- **INV-N — Property.** explanation` bullets plus an evidence table (`Candidate | Property | EVIDENCE | Would-VIOLATE example`); properties with no enforcing hook/test are tagged `(intended, not yet enforced)` rather than padded to hit a count. **Audit** (invariants already exist): idempotent drift check — re-verifies each `INV-N`'s cited evidence still resolves, reports `DRIFT: INV-N — ...`, proposes additive/corrective deltas only, never duplicates; a clean run emits a no-op report. **Approval-gated**: PROJECT.md is a governed alignment source, so any write goes through `AskUserQuestion` (Apply / Edit specific invariants / Save-to-file-only / Cancel) before `parse_project_md` is re-run as a post-write self-check; `--dry-run` emits the proposal only and never writes. Reuses `alignment_classifier.parse_project_md` as-is (no signature changes); deliberately does not reuse `alignment_assessor.py`'s `ProjectMdDraft`/`AlignmentAssessor` (mechanical file/dependency counting) since invariant selection requires semantic judgment about which properties are load-bearing. **Greenfield template genericization**: `plugins/autonomous-dev/templates/PROJECT.md` — previously a symlink to autonomous-dev's own `.claude/PROJECT.md` (so a fresh install's scaffold shipped autonomous-dev's real INV-1..8) — is now a real, generic file with its own placeholder content; both it and `PROJECT.md.template` gained a placeholder `### INVARIANTS` subsection. **Decision-4 parse-safety guard**: the shipped placeholders are written as a *numbered* list (`1. INV-1 — ...`), not `- ` bullets, so `parse_project_md` reports `has_invariants=False` on a fresh, uncustomized repo — the safe opt-in default is preserved until a maintainer runs `/align --project --invariants` or hand-converts the placeholders to real bullets under `## ARCHITECTURE`. `docs/MAINTAINING-PHILOSOPHY.md` and `plugins/autonomous-dev/docs/COMMANDS.md` updated. 3 new regression tests in `tests/regression/test_alignment_gate_backcompat.py` (`TestGreenfieldTemplateInvariants`): both shipped templates parse to `has_invariants=False`, real bullet-format invariants under `## ARCHITECTURE` do parse, and an `INVARIANTS` heading placed outside `## ARCHITECTURE` is correctly ignored. (#1489)

### Security
- **Protected-infrastructure Write/Edit is now a non-bypassable hard floor (Issue #1435)**: The universal hook bypass (`.claude/.bypass` marker / `AUTONOMOUS_DEV_BYPASS=1`, Issue #969) previously granted an unconditional `allow` in `unified_pre_tool.py::main()` *before* the protected-infrastructure Write/Edit deny gate ran — so a session with the universal bypass active could silently rewrite `agents/*.md`, `commands/*.md`, `hooks/*.py`, `lib/*.py`, or `skills/*/SKILL.md` even though `_is_protected_infrastructure` is a registered hard-floor function (`config/hard_floor_hooks.json`) meant to always fire. Fixed by checking, before granting the bypass allow, whether the Write/Edit target is protected infrastructure AND `is_hard_floor("unified_pre_tool.py", "_is_protected_infrastructure")` is true; if so the bypass falls through to the existing deny gate instead of short-circuiting it (fail-closed on any error in that check). This also means the bypass no longer exempts the Issue #1296 coordinator-dispatch-sentinel sub-check, which lives in the same gate. `/implement` remains the sole sanctioned path for protected-infrastructure edits — there is no operator override for this specific gate. Non-protected Write/Edit and Bash commands are unaffected; the bypass still works normally for everything else. `README.md`, `docs/HOOKS.md`, `docs/HOOK-REGISTRY.md`, `docs/LIBRARIES.md`, and `plugins/autonomous-dev/docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md` updated to document the exception. 9 new regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1435_hard_floor_bypass.py` (deny cases for env-var and flag-file bypass across all five protected-path types, positive controls proving the bypass still works for non-protected writes and Bash, and a scope guard proving the protection only applies inside an autonomous-dev repo); 2 existing tests in `tests/regression/test_universal_hook_bypass.py` inverted to assert `deny` (previously asserted `allow`) now that the invariant has flipped. (#1435)

### Fixed
- **`auto_commit_and_push()` discarded the caller's staging area and swept the whole working tree into the commit (Issue #1564)**: the function called `stage_all_changes()` unconditionally, so a caller that had deliberately staged a narrow set got that index thrown away and everything — modified files it had not staged, untracked scratch directories, `.proposal` drafts, and logs — folded into its commit. Reached through `create_commit_with_agent_message()`, which `/implement` STEP 12.7 and STEP L4.7 declare mandatory ("FORBIDDEN: bare `git commit` here"), so no caller on that path could opt out. Measured live: nine deliberately staged files produced a **482-file, 204,325-insertion commit**. Staging is now conditional on a new `stage_all: bool = True` parameter threaded through `auto_commit_and_push()`, `GitOperations.auto_commit_push()`, and `create_commit_with_agent_message()`. **The default is unchanged**: `stage_all=True` still stages the entire working tree, which is the commit-everything semantics batch, worktree, and drain flows depend on, and every existing caller keeps it — this release changes no shipped behaviour. `stage_all=False` commits the caller's index verbatim and adds nothing to it, leaving unstaged and untracked files out; the emptiness precheck follows the mode (working tree when `True`, index only when `False`, with a distinct "staging area is empty" error). The lever is not yet wired to `/implement`: the pipeline has no staging step of its own — the only `git add` in `commands/implement.md` is a conditional one at STEP 12 for doc-master fixes — and STEP 1 hard-blocks on a non-empty index, so the index at STEP 12.7 is empty on any run where doc-master made no fixes and holds *only* the doc fixes when it did; both are documented as a `Precondition:` on the two docstrings. Two supporting helpers added to `git_operations.py`: `get_staged_files()` reads the index through `git status --porcelain -z` rather than `git diff --cached` so it works with no `HEAD` and so `core.quotePath` cannot mangle non-ASCII or space-bearing paths (`café.txt`, `with space.txt`, and a leading-space filename all verified to resolve on disk), excluding unmerged paths including the `AA`/`DD` pairs a `U`-only test misses, and consuming the separate `-z` source record a rename emits from **either** status column — `R ` is "renamed in index", ` R` is "renamed in work tree", and testing only the index column let an ` R` source beginning with `R` or `C` (`README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `Cargo.toml`) swallow the following record and silently drop a genuinely staged file; `count_committed_files()` measures what actually landed via `git diff-tree -r -m --root`, because `git show --name-only` reports a combined diff that suppresses paths shared with a parent and returned `0` for a real merge commit, and it raises `ValueError` on a flag-shaped `ref` rather than answering with the `0` that means "no commit was created". The result dict gained `files_committed`, present on every path and `0` only when no commit was created. Covered by `tests/unit/lib/test_git_operations_staging.py` (34 test functions, 38 collected), with `tests/integration/test_auto_implement_git.py` mock sequences and one `tests/integration/test_auto_implement_step8_agents.py` assertion updated for the new call shape. Documented in `docs/GIT-AUTOMATION.md` (new "Staging scope" section) and `docs/LIBRARIES.md`, which had no entry for `git_operations.py` at all until now. (#1564)
- **CI unblocked after 100 consecutive non-passing runs (two months, since 2026-06-20)**: `.github/workflows/ci.yml:124` makes the `test` job `needs: smoke`, so a failing smoke job caused the full test suite to be **skipped rather than failed** for that entire period — the test gate, coverage, and every regression suite were unobservable, not green. Two measured blocking layers, both in the smoke job: (1) `cloud_drain_telemetry.hook.json` was missing the required `type` and `interpreter` fields and used a shape no sibling sidecar uses (`events`/`enabled`/`priority`/`retry`/`timeout` instead of `registrations`/`active`), every extra key an `additionalProperties: false` violation of `config/hook-metadata.schema.json`, failing the "Validate hook sidecar consistency" step *before* pytest ran; it is now `type: "utility"`, `interpreter: "python3"`, `active: true`, since `CloudSchedule` is not a Claude Code lifecycle event and `lifecycle` would require fabricating a registration. (2) Three assertions in `tests/regression/smoke/test_drain_watchdog_labels.py` still targeted a raw `gh issue create --label` shell block that commit `004b2640` (2026-07-09) replaced with a `python3` heredoc calling `open_or_supersede_daily_aggregate`; they are repointed to that call site, so they survive the next transport change while still failing if the behaviour is actually removed. One of the three was passing vacuously — asserting a CLI string the current code path can no longer emit under any circumstance — and is now a real guard. Twelve permanent control tests added (8 → 20 functions), including negative controls that fail if the label contract regresses. **Review caught the repointed guards reproducing the same defect they were fixing**: the first pass scoped assertions to the call site but read only the *first* `open_or_supersede_daily_aggregate(` match (`str.find`) and captured only single-line kwarg values (`[^\n]*`), so a second call site or a multi-line `labels=[...]` literal carrying `drain-stuck,auto-improvement` passed green with the Issue #1275 self-loop bug live — both reproduced, then closed by enumerating every call site via `re.finditer` and extracting values with a quote-aware balanced-bracket scan. A plain `[^\n,]*` fallback was rejected because it truncates the comma-joined form (`label="drain-stuck,auto-improvement"` → `"drain-stuck`), trading the new blind spot for the original one. Guards watched both ways against the real pending change — migrating the selector-stall alert at `drain-watchdog.yml:285-287` to a second aggregate call: `selector-stall,high-priority` permitted, `selector-stall,auto-improvement` refused. Verified end-to-end by running all four smoke steps under a CI-matching Python 3.11.14 venv with the exact CI install set (`pytest pyyaml anthropic jsonschema`); no third blocking layer exists.
- **`docs/HOOKS.md` claimed an invocation path for `cloud_drain_telemetry.py` that does not exist**: the entry stated the module is "Invoked by the `/drain-queue` workflow." A repo-wide search for `cloud_drain_telemetry`, `should_emit_telemetry_commit`, and `create_telemetry_commit` returns no call site outside the module and its own unit test — the hook ships via `install_manifest.json`, is absent from both settings templates, and is called by nothing. Corrected to state that plainly, so the doc and the new `type: "utility"` sidecar agree. `docs/HOOK-SIDECAR-SCHEMA.md` gained a note that `type` gates deployment (all sidecars reach the manifest; only `lifecycle` reaches the settings template) and that utilities need not be imported by another hook.
- **Pipeline completion ledger silently discarded recorded agent completions under concurrent writes (Issue #1544)**: `pipeline_completion_state.py`'s `_write_state()` called `open(path, "w")` — which truncates — **before** acquiring `fcntl.LOCK_EX`. A concurrent `_read_state()` landing in that truncation window got `JSONDecodeError` on a 0-byte file; the failure was silently treated as "no state file yet," and `_ensure_state()` rebuilt a blank skeleton, discarding every previously-recorded completion with no error surfaced. `unified_pre_tool.py` is registered in both settings tiers and runs twice concurrently per event (#1483), so this fired on every Agent dispatch and was observed six times in a single session, manifesting as agents that had demonstrably completed being blocked as "not yet run." Originally misdiagnosed as a wall-clock TTL problem — the actual Issue #1413 staleness/mtime-refresh path is untouched, with negative-control tests pinning that it is not the cause. Fix: a pre-existing, correct helper (`_locked_rmw`, from #1170/#1188) had only 2 callers; all 8 public state-mutating functions (`record_agent_completion`, `record_agent_launch`, `record_prompt_baseline`, `set_validation_mode`, `record_doc_verdict`, `record_research_skipped`, `record_plan_critic_skipped`, `record_plan_critic_passed`) now route through it, with public signatures, defaults, and return values unchanged. `_write_state()` self-wraps through `_locked_rmw()` via a new thread-local re-entrancy guard when not already inside one, so any future direct caller is serialized by construction. New `_atomic_write_state()` stages via `tempfile.mkstemp()` in the same directory, `chmod 0o600` before `os.replace()`, then `fsync` — atomic on POSIX, so a concurrent reader always sees either the complete old file or the complete new one, never a truncated one; `_gc_stale_states()` gained a staging-file glob so a writer killed mid-write leaves no `/tmp` litter. `_read_state()` no longer equates a parse failure with genuine absence: 3 retries with 10 ms backoff, then a loud stderr report naming path, cause, and effect — it still returns `{}` rather than raising, since these callers gate reads and a hard failure would block the pipeline rather than degrade it. Mid-fix regression: the re-entrancy guard originally opened after the mutator ran, so a mutator calling `_write_state()` directly self-deadlocked on a second `flock` of the same lockfile (reproduced with a 6s timeout); the guard now spans read+mutate+write on all three `_rmw()` call sites. `docs/LIBRARIES.md` and `docs/ARCHITECTURE-OVERVIEW.md` updated to correct the prior "atomic write" / "last-write-wins" claims, which described the intended design rather than the actual (racy) implementation. Measured overhead: write path 0.795 ms/call, read path 0.108 ms/call. 35 new regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1544_state_write_race.py`. (#1544)
- **`create_commit_with_agent_message()` — the commit path `/implement` STEP 12.7/L4.7 declare mandatory — raised `TypeError` and could never complete a commit (Issue #1555)**: Two defects fixed. (1) **Call-signature error**: `read_artifact('manifest')` was called with 1 of `ArtifactManager.read_artifact(workflow_id, artifact_type, validate=True)`'s 2 required positional args at two call sites. Replaced with a new `_load_workflow_manifest()` helper that checks `ArtifactManager.artifact_exists()` first, then calls `read_artifact(workflow_id, 'manifest', validate=False)` — the manifest is now **optional** enrichment context for the commit body rather than a prerequisite, so a workflow that never wrote v2.0 artifacts is still committable. (2) **Archived-agent dependency removed**: the helper invoked `commit-message-generator` and `pr-description-generator`, which exist only under `agents/archived/`, violating the "active code must never reference archived components" rule — and the dispatch was unusable by construction anyway, since `AgentInvoker.invoke()` only returns a Task-tool dispatch descriptor, never an executed `{'success','output'}` result. Commit and PR messages are now generated in-process, deterministically, offline via new public functions `generate_commit_message()`, `generate_pr_description()`, `infer_commit_type()`, `infer_scope()`, `build_commit_subject()` (plus private `_load_workflow_manifest()`, `_collect_changed_files()`, `_recent_commit_subjects()`) in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/auto_implement_git_integration.py`. The existing `invoke_commit_message_agent()` / `invoke_pr_description_agent()` names and their `{'success','output','error'}` contract are preserved for the ~15 test sites and `push_and_create_pr` that depend on them. **Behavior change**: `create_commit_with_agent_message()` previously propagated a clean-tree `success=True, commit_sha=''` result — a silent no-op falsely claiming a commit exists; it now returns a real failure with an actionable message ("No commit created: nothing to commit, working tree clean … Fix: stage your changes"). `docs/GIT-AUTOMATION.md`, `docs/LIBRARIES.md`, and `docs/BATCH-PROCESSING.md` updated to remove references to the archived `commit-message-generator`/`pr-description-generator` agents and document the new in-process generation functions and clean-tree failure behavior. 12 new/changed tests: `tests/regression/regression/test_issue_1555_commit_path.py` (10 new), plus net +1 each in `tests/integration/test_auto_implement_step8_agents.py` and `tests/integration/test_batch_git_edge_cases.py`. (#1555)
- **STEP 1 baseline capture silently reported `Baseline failing tests: 0` when the measurement had actually failed (Issue #1533)**: Two distinct routes both rounded a failed measurement down to a "clean" baseline. **Route A — collection error**: `tests/integration/test_auto_add_to_regression_workflow.py` imported `auto_add_to_regression`, a module that exists only under `hooks/archived/`; pytest aborted during collection (`Interrupted: 1 error during collection`, exit 2), zero tests executed, `parse_failing_tests()` found no `FAILED` lines and returned an empty set, and STEP 8 later classified every real failure as NEW against that phantom-empty baseline. The Issue #1094 `__TIMEOUT__` sentinel never fired because no timeout occurred — the failure mode was novel. **Route B — parser verbosity blind spot**: `pytest.ini` sets `--verbose` in `addopts`, but the canonical baseline command adds `-q`, netting verbosity 0, at which pytest emits failures ONLY in the short test summary (`FAILED <id> - <reason>`); `parse_failing_tests` matched only the verbose per-test shape (`<id> FAILED`), so a real failing run (`1 failed, 5 passed`, exit 1) still parsed to `set()`, and `classify_failures(∅, ∅)` yielded `new_failures = 0`, so the `new_failures > 0` gate could never fire. Fixed in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/fix_forward.py`: `parse_failing_tests` gains a `_SHORT_SUMMARY_FAILED_PATTERN` for the short-summary shape; new `detect_capture_failure()` encodes the invariant **a capture that executed zero tests is a measurement failure, never a baseline**, checking pytest's exit code (`{2, 3, 4, 5}`), abort markers in the output (`Interrupted:`, `error during collection`, `INTERNALERROR`, `no tests ran`), and the executed-test count from the summary line via new helpers `count_executed_tests()` / `count_reported_failures()`; a distinct `COLLECTION_ERROR_SENTINEL` (`__COLLECTION_ERROR__`) is written alongside the existing `TIMEOUT_SENTINEL`, and `is_capture_failure_sentinel()` recognizes either. `commands/implement.md` STEP 1 calls `detect_capture_failure()` after the baseline pytest run and writes the sentinel instead of an empty file; STEP 8 treats `__COLLECTION_ERROR__` exactly like `__TIMEOUT__` — skip fix-forward classification rather than compare against an unknown baseline. `pipeline_state.py`'s `CANONICAL_BASELINE_CMD` deliberately does NOT gain `--continue-on-collection-errors`: surviving a collection error would suppress the loudest signal (`Interrupted:` + exit 2) the new detection depends on, so a comment now records why. The archived-code scanner in `tests/regression/test_no_duplicated_facts.py` gains an AST-based import scan across `tests/` (previously scoped to `plugins/autonomous-dev/**` only) that flags any unguarded import resolving only to an `archived/` module — the exact class of import that caused Route A. The offending dead test file (`tests/integration/test_auto_add_to_regression_workflow.py`, 28 tests, orphaned since the Issue #558-era `auto_add_to_regression.py` hook was archived) is deleted. `docs/LIBRARIES.md` entry 91 (`fix_forward.py`) updated to document the new sentinel/detection API and short-summary parsing fix. 18 new unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_fix_forward_capture_failure.py`, 31 new regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1533_baseline_collection_error.py`, and 4 new regression tests in `tests/regression/test_no_duplicated_facts.py` (archived-import scan). (#1533)
- **Running the unit test suite armed the LIVE Issue #1296 agent-dispatch authorization sentinel (Issue #1535)**: Two measured routes wrote to the real `<repo>/.claude/local/active_agent_dispatch.json` during ordinary test runs — a test calling `agent_dispatch_sentinel.write()` with no `repo_root` (Route 1, resolves via `find_project_root()` to the real repo), and a test driving a hook (`session_activity_logger.main()`) with a Task/Agent `PreToolUse` payload where the HOOK itself calls `write()` (Route 2 — invisible to any grep of `tests/`, since the test file contains no `write()` call at all). While armed, the Issue #1296 gate in `unified_pre_tool.py` attributes a coordinator's Write/Edit to a dispatched agent and permits writes to protected infrastructure (`agents/*.md`, `commands/*.md`, `hooks/*.py`, `lib/*.py`, `skills/*/SKILL.md`); the Issue #1448 sliding TTL then kept a test-armed sentinel alive on ordinary session activity — measured still active 335 seconds after a test run. Fixed with a new autouse fixture `_isolate_agent_dispatch_sentinel` in `tests/conftest.py` that intercepts `agent_dispatch_sentinel._path()` — the shared choke point both routes funnel through — and redirects only the default (no-argument) branch, and only when that branch would resolve to the live repo sentinel; the explicit-`repo_root` branch is passed through verbatim so the 30+ existing `repo_root=tmp_path` tests are unaffected, and tests that legitimately exercise default-branch resolution against a temporary fake repo (`test_issue_1484_path_convergence.py`) still get the real `find_project_root` answer. A per-file scan of all 78 files in `tests/unit/hooks/` confirmed 0 files now mutate the live sentinel (previously 2). No production code changed. 5 new regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1535_sentinel_test_isolation.py`. (#1535)
- **A phantom `SubagentStop` could disarm a still-running dispatch's agent-dispatch sentinel (Issue #1512)**: A `SubagentStop` naming an `agent_transcript_path` that was never written to disk was popping the #1087 subagent-invocation cache before the genuine stop could, collecting the live dispatch's #1484 generation token and using it to satisfy `agent_dispatch_sentinel.clear()`'s compare-and-delete — disarming the sentinel of a dispatch that was still running and letting a coordinator's protected-infrastructure edit through unauthorized. Measured over 102 typed stops: 50 phantoms vs. 52 real completions, zero overlap. Fixed in two parts. **`unified_session_tracker.py`** gains `_is_phantom_subagent_stop()`, which classifies BEFORE the cache pop; a phantom skips both the invocation-cache pop and the sentinel `clear()` call and instead writes a `__phantom_stop__:<agent>` JSONL audit marker (joining `__dedup_skip__`/`__unattributable__`). An empty transcript path, or one resolving outside `~/.claude`, is deliberately classified UNKNOWN (not phantom) and keeps prior behavior — over-broadening would re-break the #1387/#1412 false-negative class. **`agent_dispatch_sentinel.py`**: `clear(expected_generation=None)` now refuses unconditionally instead of falling back to the old unconditional `unlink()` (that "accepted backcompat gap" from #1484 turned out to be the dominant failure path, firing within about a minute of an ordinary dispatch); `force=True` is the new deliberate operator-recovery escape, never used by production code. `is_active()` is now a side-effect-free pure predicate — it previously unlinked a stale sentinel as a side effect of being read, so a diagnostic call could destroy the evidence it was measuring; that reaping moved to a new public `reap_if_stale()`, which `unified_pre_tool.py`'s protected-path gate now calls immediately before `is_active()` to keep that call site's observable behavior unchanged. Sentinel writes also switched to atomic same-directory `os.replace()` (a prior `Path.write_text()` truncate-then-write could interleave two concurrent hook subprocesses and leave a torn-JSON tail). New diagnostic `scripts/measure_phantom_subagent_stops.py` re-runs the phantom/real classification against `.claude/logs/activity/*.jsonl` to confirm the hit/miss inversion post-deployment. `docs/LIBRARIES.md` entry 223, `docs/HOOKS.md`, `docs/HOOK-REGISTRY.md`, `docs/PIPELINE-MODES.md`, and `docs/SCRIPTS.md` updated. 21 new/changed tests: `tests/regression/test_issue_1512_phantom_classifier.py` (20 new), plus 1 net-new test in `tests/unit/lib/test_agent_dispatch_sentinel.py` (`test_reap_if_stale_removes_stale_sentinel` replacing the now-inaccurate `test_is_active_cleans_up_stale_sentinel`, plus new `test_is_active_does_not_mutate`), with supporting updates in `tests/regression/test_issue_1512_atomic_sentinel_write.py`, `tests/regression/test_issue_1479_1480_sentinel_hardening.py`, and `tests/regression/test_issue_1296_coordinator_bypass.py`. (#1512)
- **Write enforcement was transport-dependent — the protected-infrastructure hard floor (INV-4) was bypassable via NotebookEdit with zero MCP servers installed (Issue #1503)**: Every enforcement site decided "is this a write?" with a hard-coded literal tuple such as `("Write", "Edit")`, so any file-mutating tool whose name was absent from the specific tuple at a specific site walked straight through. Measured against the protected target `hooks/plan_gate.py`: `Write`/`Edit` denied, but `NotebookEdit`, `MultiEdit`, `mcp__serena__replace_symbol_body`, `mcp__serena__insert_after_symbol` and `mcp__serena__replace_content` were all ALLOWED — 17 genuine failures across a 54-check proof harness. `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/tool_intent.py` (the module that already knew `MultiEdit` and `NotebookEdit` are writers, but was never consulted by the hooks) gains `MCP_READ_TOOLS` / `MCP_WRITE_TOOLS` registries, `PATH_KEYS` / `CONTENT_KEYS`, and two new public functions — `is_write()` and `changed_content()` — which are now the canonical, transport-independent answers used by every gate. `classify()` resolves registry-FIRST, with a fail-closed shape fallback (a non-empty path key **AND** a non-empty content key) applied only to unregistered tools, so path-carrying reads such as `mcp__serena__find_symbol` stay EXEC and remain unblocked. The registry is load-bearing rather than belt-and-braces: `replace_in_files`, `rename_symbol`, `safe_delete_symbol` and `delete_lines` carry no content argument at all, so the shape fallback provably cannot catch them. In `unified_pre_tool.py`, the protected-infrastructure hard floor was **relocated out of** the native-tool fast path (which terminates in an unconditional allow — the mechanism by which `NotebookEdit` escaped) into `_enforce_protected_infrastructure()`, invoked upstream of the fast-path entry; the `.claude/.bypass` carve-out, `_is_code_file_target()`, and the plan-exit deny-candidate list all switched to `_ti_is_write()`; and the module-local `_PLAN_EXIT_MCP_READONLY` frozenset was deleted in favour of `tool_intent.MCP_READ_TOOLS` (a strict superset that additionally knows serena's read surface, fixing an over-block of `find_symbol` / `get_symbols_overview` / `find_referencing_symbols` at the `plan_exited` stage). `plan_gate.py`, `enforce_file_organization.py` and `enforce_tier_distribution.py` adopt `is_write()`/`write_targets()`, their PreToolUse matchers widen to `Write|Edit|MultiEdit|NotebookEdit|mcp__.*` in all three registration sites (`*.hook.json`, `templates/settings.autonomous-dev.json`, `config/global_settings_template.json`), and `plan_gate`'s simple-edit exemption is rewritten around `changed_content()` so the <100-line carve-out is about the CHANGE, not the TRANSPORT. `scripts/audit_tool_intent_coverage.py` — the CI gate that should have caught this entire class — loses its blanket `if tool_name.startswith("mcp__"): return True`, which had declared every conceivable MCP tool covered by construction; both `_is_covered()` and `_classify_summary()` now require explicit registry membership or a new `MCP_KNOWN_EXEC_TOOLS` allowlist. **Behaviour change**: when both the plan-exit gate and the infrastructure hard floor would fire, the infrastructure message now wins (deny stays deny — only the visible reason changes); locked by a regression test. Every stale-install fallback is the literal FOUR-tuple `("Write", "Edit", "MultiEdit", "NotebookEdit")` — strictly stronger than the two-tuple it replaces, never weaker — and deliberately does NOT fail closed to deny, since denying on a missing library would block `Read`. Proven by `tests/regression/test_issue_1503_transport_parity.py` (69 subprocess-driven tests, no mocking, across 9 groups including negative controls that assert reads are never blocked).
- **Agent-dispatch sentinel ABA race could disarm a sibling dispatch's still-active sentinel mid-edit (Issue #1484)**: `clear()` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/agent_dispatch_sentinel.py` was an unconditional `unlink()` — when two overlapping Task/Agent dispatches ran close together, the first dispatch's `SubagentStop` could delete the sentinel that a second, still-in-flight dispatch had since re-armed, letting a coordinator's protected-infrastructure edit through with no live agent authorizing it (the #1467 ABA race). Fixed with a per-dispatch generation-token compare-and-delete: `session_activity_logger.py` mints a `uuid.uuid4().hex` generation token on `PreToolUse` and stores it both in the sentinel payload (`write(..., generation=...)`) and in the matching `subagent_invocation_cache` entry (`cache_invocation(..., generation=...)`); on `SubagentStop`, `unified_session_tracker.py` recovers the token from the cache and calls `clear(expected_generation=...)`, which reads the sentinel first and only unlinks when the token matches (or the sentinel predates generation tokens / a cache-miss occurred, preserving the pre-#1484 unconditional-clear behavior for those cases) — a mismatched token means a different dispatch owns the sentinel, so the clear is a no-op. The sentinel-clear call was also relocated from before to after the invocation-cache pop so the recovered token is available, and `unified_session_tracker.py`'s `session_id` resolution was made env-first (`CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` before the payload field) to match the writer, so the `SubagentStop` popper reliably looks up the same cache key the writer used. Separately, `_path()`'s default (no-arg) branch now resolves the repo root via `path_utils.find_project_root()` instead of `Path.cwd()`, so the writer/reader/clearer — three independent hook subprocesses — converge on one sentinel path even from a subdirectory or git worktree; the explicit `repo_root=` branch used by existing tests is unchanged. Sentinel/refresh write failures in `session_activity_logger.py` also switched from a silent `except Exception: pass` to a non-blocking stderr warning, so a failed arm/refresh is now visible instead of silently disappearing. `docs/LIBRARIES.md` entry 223 and `plugins/autonomous-dev/docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md`'s "Coordinator Cannot Directly Edit Protected Path" section updated to document the generation-token lifecycle. 15 new tests: `tests/regression/test_issue_1484_generation_token.py` (7), `tests/regression/test_issue_1484_path_convergence.py` (3), `tests/hooks/test_issue_1484_fix4_stderr_warnings.py` (3), `tests/integration/test_issue_1484_worktree_convergence.py` (2). (#1484)
- **Agent-dispatch sentinel TTL was a fixed window, not a sliding one — false BLOCKED denials on long-running multi-file implementer sessions (Issue #1448/#1475)**: The Issue #1296 coordinator-dispatch-sentinel gate's TTL was anchored once at dispatch time (`write()`), so a long-running implementer doing many reads, test runs, and multi-file edits could still outlive the TTL and get structurally denied on a legitimate protected-path edit even after the #1447/#1471 30s→600s raise — the implementer had been working around this by manually rewriting the sentinel before each edit (a BYPASS-tagged workaround). Fixed by making the TTL a sliding window: new `refresh(ttl_seconds=DEFAULT_TTL_SECONDS, repo_root=None) -> bool` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/agent_dispatch_sentinel.py` slides an existing sentinel's timestamp forward to now; it is deliberately conservative — never creates a sentinel that doesn't exist, never resurrects one that has already gone stale, and preserves the existing `agent`/`pid` payload. `plugins/autonomous-dev/hooks/session_activity_logger.py`'s `PostToolUse` branch now calls `refresh()` (wrapped in try/except so it never blocks on failure; module docstring notes the `ACTIVITY_LOGGING=false` fallback to the old fixed-TTL behavior since this hook then exits early). `write()` (dispatch start) and `clear()` (on `SubagentStop`) are unchanged; `DEFAULT_TTL_SECONDS=600` is now purely the idle/crash backstop — how long the sentinel survives with zero tool activity — not the ceiling on a healthy dispatch's total duration. `docs/LIBRARIES.md` entry 223 and `plugins/autonomous-dev/docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md`'s "Coordinator Cannot Directly Edit Protected Path" section updated to document the write→refresh→clear lifecycle. 8 new unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_agent_dispatch_sentinel.py` (`TestRefreshSlidingTTL`) and 6 new regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1448_sentinel_sliding_ttl.py`; `tests/unit/hooks/test_session_activity_logger.py` fixture hardened for marker-dir hermeticity. (#1448, #1475)
- **Prompt-integrity shrinkage HARD GATE was silently fail-open (Issue #1471)**: The baseline-shrinkage gate in `unified_pre_tool.py::validate_prompt_integrity()` stopped enforcing anything after the `PromptIntegrityResult.shrinkage_percent` -> `shrinkage_pct` rename: the deny-message f-string still read `result.shrinkage_pct`, which raised `AttributeError`, and the block's single broad `except Exception: pass` swallowed it and fell through to `return ("allow", ...)`. Every compression-critical agent invocation was waved through. Fixed on three fronts. (1) **Library API restored** — `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/prompt_integrity.py` renames the dataclass field to `shrinkage_pct: float = 0.0`, adds `should_reload: bool = False`, and populates BOTH on all four `validate_prompt_word_count()` return paths (contract: `should_reload == not passed`); the Issue #794 batch-observation API (`record_batch_observation()`, `get_cumulative_shrinkage()`, `clear_batch_observations()`, plus the `_get_observations_path()` helper backing `prompt_batch_observations.json`) is restored after being dropped in d29c3163, which had been silently disabling the cumulative-drift check via `ImportError` and breaking collection of 12 regression/spec-validation modules. (2) **Gate-internal errors now fail CLOSED** — the fail-open handler is split so `ImportError` (module unavailable) and `IOError`/`OSError`/`json.JSONDecodeError` (baseline/observation file I/O) remain fail-open *by design* with a logged warning, while any other exception logs at ERROR with a traceback and returns `deny` with a `/create-issue` directive; the redundant nested `try/except Exception: pass` around the batch-observation record was removed. (3) **Collateral fixes** — `hooks/cloud_drain_telemetry.py` gained the standard Issue #953 `hook_safety`/`_safe_main_953` wrapper it was missing; `config/known_bypass_patterns.json`'s `worktree_path_boundary` entry had two schema violations corrected (`severity: "high"` -> `"critical"`, `detection.type: "activity_log_pattern"` -> `"log_pattern"`); and `tests/unit/hooks/test_classifier_robustness.py::test_classifier_still_matches_actual_cat_redirect` moved its fixture off `/tmp` (`/tmp/real.py` -> `src/real.py`), which had been contradicting the Issue #1408 write-gate temp exemption that deliberately returns `("", "")` for temp paths. New `tests/unit/hooks/test_prompt_integrity_fail_closed.py` (7 tests) locks the fail-closed/fail-open split and includes an anti-regression test that drives the deny path with a REAL `PromptIntegrityResult` rather than a mock, so a future field rename fails the test instead of silently reopening the gate. `docs/HOOKS.md` updated: the Prompt Integrity Gate section now documents the fail-open/fail-closed split (was previously documented as unconditionally fail-open) and the full current `REINVOCATION_CONTEXTS` set (`fix`/`light`/`research-skip` were already live in code but undocumented); the "Safe Failure Behavior" hook count corrected from 24 to 27 (verified against filesystem) and now notes `cloud_drain_telemetry.py` was the last hook missing the `safe_main()` wrapper. `docs/LIBRARIES.md` gained a new entry for `agent_dispatch_sentinel.py` (previously undocumented despite existing since Issue #1296) documenting the TTL 30s→600s change, and entry 73 (`prompt_integrity.py`)'s `REINVOCATION_CONTEXTS`/`MAX_CUMULATIVE_SHRINKAGE` prose was brought in sync with the current module. (#1471)
- **Over-broad force-push deny glob false-matched safe branch names (Issue #1405)**: The force-push deny glob `Bash(*git *push*-f*)` narrowed to `Bash(*git *push* -f*)` + `Bash(*git *push* -f)` — the old glob false-matched any branch containing the substring `-f` (e.g. `git push origin my-feature` was denied); fixed in `settings_generator.py`'s `DEFAULT_DENY_LIST`, `settings.default.json`, and the corresponding smoke test, with new regression tests in `test_cc_2177_permission_migration.py`. The `*` retained between "git" and "push" (security-audit-driven hardening) keeps global-option and whitespace bypass forms covered — `git -C <path> push -f`, `git -c k=v push -f`, `git --no-pager push -f`, and `git  push -f` (double space) are all still denied, so the `-C`/`-c` gap noted in an earlier draft does not exist in the final form; the `--force` long form remains denied by the separate `Bash(*git *--force*)` sibling. Remaining pre-existing gaps, out of scope: a literal tab separator between "git" and "push", and force-via-refspec (`git push origin +branch`); sibling globs `*git *reset*--hard*` / `*git *clean*-fd*` are unchanged (documented same-class, negligible risk).
- **Unattributable SubagentStop firings escaped the phantom-dedup guard and polluted the agent-completeness gate (Issue #1436)**: Firings with `agent_type` empty, whitespace-only, or the literal `"unknown"` could survive the #1396 heartbeat drop (e.g. non-zero duration or substantive output) and then collide inside the #1414 phantom-dedup cache, which keyed on `(session_id, agent_type)` — two genuinely distinct empty-identity firings would collapse onto the same `(session_id, "")` key and silently suppress each other. New `_is_unattributable()` in `unified_session_tracker.py` intercepts these firings BEFORE `phantom_key` is computed: it writes a `__unattributable__:<agent_name>` audit-only JSONL entry and never inserts the event into `_PHANTOM_DEDUP_CACHE`. New `_is_gate_countable_agent()` in `pipeline_completion_state.py` makes this fail-closed on the write side too — `record_agent_completion()` now no-ops for empty/whitespace/"unknown" agent types instead of persisting them, and `get_completed_agents()` filters out any such keys already present in state. `agent_output_health.py`'s `_get_agent_completions()` was broadened from excluding only `__dedup_skip__:*` markers to excluding all `__`-prefixed internal sentinel markers (now also `__unattributable__`), so the new audit entries don't trigger false-positive ghost/zero-word findings. `docs/HOOKS.md`, `docs/PIPELINE-MODES.md`, and `docs/LIBRARIES.md` updated to document the guard. 6 new regression/unit tests: `tests/hooks/test_subagent_phantom_dedup.py` (2: unattributable firing bypasses the cache, two distinct empty-identity firings don't collide) and `tests/unit/lib/test_pipeline_completion_state.py` (4: reject empty agent_type, reject whitespace/None/"unknown", filter pre-existing empty/unknown keys on read, named agents still recorded normally). (#1436)
- **Suppress telemetry commits for no_drainable_cluster (Issue #1437)**: Cloud-drain fires exit with `no_drainable_cluster` 96% of the time while still emitting `FIRE_END` telemetry commits that drown out real activity. Modified drain-queue command to log these no-op exits to JSONL only, suppressing git commits for `no_drainable_cluster`, `queue_empty`, and `all_clusters_high_severity` exit reasons. Real events like `commit_landed` continue to emit telemetry commits.

### Security
- **Batch worktree path boundary enforcement**: Blocks Write/Edit operations targeting paths outside active batch worktree; prevents main-repo writes during batch runs (#1390)
### Fixed
- **Write-pipeline gate false-positives on scratch/out-of-worktree paths + unsound Bash hard-block downgraded to advisory (Issue #1408)**: The default-on production-code Write/Edit gate (`unified_pre_tool.py`) fired on scratchpad and non-repo paths it should never have gated. Fixed with two new scoping checks that run before tier classification: `_is_scratch_path()` (module-level `EPHEMERAL_PREFIXES` shared with the Bash-code gate — `/tmp/`, `/private/tmp/`, `/var/tmp/`, `/var/folders/`, `~/tmp/`, `~/.cache/`, `$SCRATCHPAD` read live per-call, per-session `/private/tmp/claude-*/` roots, `.claude/tmp/`) and `_is_gated_repo_source()` (git-worktree-aware: paths outside any git worktree or covered by `.gitignore`, checked per-invocation via `git -C <dir> rev-parse --is-inside-work-tree` / `check-ignore` with a 1.0s timeout, fail-open to the pre-#1408 `_is_autonomous_dev_repo` scoping on any git error). `edit_tier_classifier._is_temp_path()` (used by the Bash-code gate) gained the same `/private/tmp/`, `/var/folders/`, and `$SCRATCHPAD` recognition it was previously missing, closing a drift between two independently-maintained scratch-path lists. Separately, general production-code Bash writes (`cat > X.py`, `sed -i X.py`, `tee X.py`, heredocs, etc.) are downgraded from a hard `deny` to a non-blocking `[hook advisory]` (Hybrid model) — general Bash-write detection is unsound (`git checkout`, `dd`, `base64 | tee`, and other forms evade the pattern set), so hard-blocking only the detectable subset gave a false sense of security while taxing legitimate one-off scripting; the command now falls through and is logged as `bash_code_file_gate_advisory:<tier>` (allow). Edit/Write to code files, protected-infrastructure Bash writes, and the Issue #803 cross-tool workaround check remain hard gates. The one-shot `/tmp/skip_write_pipeline_gate` bypass now captures a `reason` string in its activity-log entry (sentinel file contents, else `$WRITE_GATE_BYPASS_REASON`, else `"unspecified"`) for auditability. `README.md`, `docs/HOOKS.md`, `plugins/autonomous-dev/docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md`, and `docs/LIBRARIES.md` updated to match. 39 new/updated tests: `tests/regression/test_issue_1408_write_gate_scoping.py` (33 new), plus updates across `tests/regression/test_issue_803_write_bash_workaround.py`, `tests/unit/hooks/test_classifier_robustness.py`, `tests/unit/hooks/test_infrastructure_protection.py`, `tests/unit/hooks/test_native_tool_auto_approval.py`, `tests/unit/hooks/test_unified_b2_activation.py`, `tests/unit/hooks/test_unified_pre_tool_ephemeral_paths.py`, and `tests/unit/hooks/test_write_pipeline_gate.py` (net +6). (#1408)
- **Submodule-safe canonical hook-path form (Issue #1036)**: Hook commands in every settings template (`settings.default.json`, `settings.autonomous-dev.json`, `settings.granular-bash.json`, `settings.permission-batching.json`, `settings.strict-mode.json`) now resolve via `${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR:-$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)}/.claude/hooks/<NAME>` instead of the bare `$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/...` form. The bare form broke hook resolution when autonomous-dev is checked out as a git submodule, since `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` resolves to the *submodule* root rather than the launch project root; `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` (set by Claude Code at launch) is git-independent and survives that case, with the `$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)` fallback preserved for older CLIs that don't set the variable. `hook_path_validator.py`'s `expand_path()` now collapses the whole `${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR:-<default>}` expression to `project_root` before other substitutions run. `sync_settings_hooks.py` repoints per-repo hook sync to read `templates/settings.default.json` (previously a divergent template), and `scripts/deploy-all.sh` resolves both the new wrapped form and the legacy bare form when rewriting hook commands to an absolute repo path. `docs/LIBRARIES.md`'s `hook_path_validator.py` entry updated to document the new canonical form. 5 new/updated tests: `tests/unit/lib/test_hook_path_validator.py` (+1: `test_expand_path_resolves_claude_project_dir_default_form`) and new `tests/regression/regression/test_issue_1036_submodule_hook_paths.py` (4 tests covering template wrapping, extractability, and both resolution branches). (#1036)
- **Pipeline-completion reliability for background agents (Issues #1387, #1412, #1228, #1396)**: Bundled fix addressing false positives/negatives around agent-completion tracking, rooted in the fact that agents launched with `run_in_background=true` never fire the `Stop`/`SubagentStop` hook events (Anthropic #25147, confirmed won't-fix platform limitation) — their completion has always been recorded coordinator-side. (1) `detect_doc_verdict_missing()` in `pipeline_intent_validator.py` no longer emits a false-positive CRITICAL finding when a correlated completion event is truncated/short but a healthy, full-length doc-master completion exists elsewhere in the same event stream — the low-word-count branch now checks `any_healthy_completion` before flagging (#1387/#1412). (2) `_check_pipeline_agent_completions()` in `unified_pre_tool.py` adds a read-side `resolve_session_id()` fallback at the `git commit` completeness gate: when the payload `session_id` yields zero completion records, it re-evaluates under the resolved session id and adopts that result only if strictly better-informed or fully passing (never masks a genuinely partial primary session); the block message now lists all evaluated session id(s) (#1228). (3) `unified_session_tracker.py` gains `_resolve_agent_type_from_transcript()` — a best-effort scan of the subagent transcript's early JSONL entries to recover `agent_type` when both the payload and the #1087 PreToolUse cache miss it — plus a heartbeat-drop that discards internal SubagentStop firings with no usable identity (empty type, zero duration, no cache hit, no transcript match) instead of recording them as noise, eliminating roughly 95 of ~113 SubagentStop events per run (#1396). `docs/PIPELINE-MODES.md` and `docs/HOOKS.md` updated to document the background-agent completion model and all three fixes. 19 new regression tests: `tests/regression/test_regression_issue_1387_1412_short_completion_fallback.py` (3), `tests/regression/test_issue_1228_ordering_gate_sid_fallback.py` (5), `tests/regression/test_regression_issue_1396_heartbeat_and_transcript_resolver.py` (11). (#1387, #1412, #1228, #1396)
- **Pipeline state-file cleanup un-runnable under the shipped `Bash(rm:-f*)`/`Bash(rm:-rf*)` deny rules (Issue #1411)**: The `/implement` pipeline's own state-file cleanup used `rm -f "$STATE_FILE"`, which the shipped safety deny list hard-blocks — the pipeline's cleanup step could not run under its own default config (reproduced live: blocked a spektiv `/implement --fix` cleanup step). Fixed the 4 cleanup sites across `implement.md` (STEP 15 and STEP L5), `implement-fix.md` (STEP F6.5), and `implement-batch.md` (post-batch-merge finalize) by dropping the `-f`/`-rf` force flag: one site now uses `pathlib.Path(...).unlink(missing_ok=True)`, the other three use `rm -- "$FILE" 2>/dev/null || true` (`--` guards dash-prefixed filenames, `2>/dev/null || true` preserves the original force-remove semantics of no error when the file is already gone). `docs/RUNBOOK.md`'s manual perf-smoke procedure had the same denied `rm -f` pattern and was updated to match. 4 new regression tests in `tests/regression/test_regression_issue_1411_no_rm_f_cleanup.py` (parametrized across the 3 command files) lock the non-denied form and confirm cleanup behavior wasn't silently dropped; `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_1048_heredoc_gc.py`'s AC3 assertion updated from the now-denied `rm -f` env-var form to the force-free form. Scope note: unrelated `rm -f`/`rm -rf` invocations elsewhere in these files (temp scratch-file cleanup, worktree deletion) are the same bug class but were deliberately left out of this fix's scope — see the new test's module docstring. (#1411)

- **Plan-critic REVISE re-invocation enforcement (Issue #1417)**: Added hook-level gate `check_plan_critic_revise_gate()` in `unified_pre_tool.py` that blocks implementer dispatch when `plan_critic_verdict.json` contains `verdict=REVISE` and no planner completion has been recorded since the verdict timestamp. Prevents coordinators from silently self-serving plan-critic feedback instead of re-invoking the planner agent. Also added `get_planner_completion_count()` helper to `pipeline_completion_state.py` to count planner completions after a given epoch timestamp, and fixed `detect_doc_verdict_missing()` in `pipeline_intent_validator.py` to key off the LAST SubagentStop per invocation. Updated `implement.md` STEP 5.5b documentation. 7 regression tests added. (#1417)
- **Prevent state file staleness during long-running pipelines (Issue #1413)**: Added `path.touch()` in `pipeline_completion_state.py::_read_state()` to refresh mtime on successful reads, preventing the state file from being incorrectly considered stale during pipelines that exceed the 2-hour TTL. (#1413)

- **Migrate `Write(path)` deny/allow rules to `Edit(path)` and anchor system paths with a doubled leading slash (Issue #1409)**: Claude Code's file-permission matcher only honors `Edit(<path>)` rules for the file-editing tools (Write/Edit/NotebookEdit) — `Write(<path>)` rules are silently ignored, so the shipped `permissions.deny` rules protecting `/etc/**`, `/System/**`, `/usr/**`, `/root/**`, `~/.ssh/**`, and `~/.aws/**` were a no-op in every fresh install (and emitted a "not matched by file permission checks" warning at session start). Fixed in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/settings_generator.py`'s `DEFAULT_DENY_LIST` and all six settings templates (`settings.default.json`, `settings.autonomous-dev.json`, `settings.granular-bash.json`, `settings.local.json`, `settings.permission-batching.json`, `settings.strict-mode.json`) plus `plugins/autonomous-dev/config/global_settings_template.json`: every `Write(<path>)` rule with no existing `Edit(<path>)` twin was rewritten to `Edit(<path>)`; absolute system paths were also changed from a single to a doubled leading slash (e.g. `Write(/etc/**)` → `Edit(//etc/**)`) so the pattern anchors to the filesystem root instead of matching relative to the working directory. `global_settings_template.json`'s `permissions.allow` also dropped four now-redundant `Write(.claude/plans/**)`/`Write(.claude/decisions/**)` entries that already had `Edit(...)` twins. `docs/TOOL-AUTO-APPROVAL.md`'s Quick Start deny-list example and `docs/LIBRARIES.md`'s `build_deny_list()` reference (both taught the stale, non-functional `Write(path)` form) updated to match. 9 new tests: `tests/regression/regression/test_regression_issue_1409_permission_anchor.py` (6) and `tests/unit/test_sync_settings_selfheal.py` (3). (#1409)

- **Four context-blind hook-gate fixes in `unified_pre_tool.py`**: (1) `_get_pipeline_mode_from_state()` (Issue #1027) now honors `state.get("mode")` for a CONFIRMED same-session run (stored `session_id` == current `session_id`, both known) regardless of state-file mtime age — previously a long-running `--light` session could be misclassified as `"full"` after 30 minutes of inactivity (Issue #1173's mtime-TTL fallback). The TTL fallback still applies to indeterminate sessions (unknown/mismatched session_id). (2) New `_is_message_only_amend()` (Issue #1382) automatically short-circuits the agent-completeness gate for a `git commit --amend` whose staged tree is byte-identical to HEAD (e.g. `--amend --no-edit` or a pure reword) — content-changing amends remain gated; logged with reason `bypass: message-only git commit --amend (tree unchanged, #1382)`. (3) `_detect_gh_issue_create()` (Issue #1383) adds Allow-through 4: `gh issue create --repo <owner>/<repo>` (or `-R`) targeting a DIFFERENT owner than the current repo's `origin` remote is now allowed through (legitimate upstream filing); same-owner, missing-flag, bare, or unresolvable targets remain fail-closed (blocked). (4) `_is_pipeline_active()` (Issue #1384) no longer treats a bare `{session_id, recovered, recovered_at}` recovery breadcrumb (written by `pipeline_completion_state.py` on restart) as evidence of a live pipeline — a genuine active-pipeline sentinel always carries at least one of `run_id`/`mode`/`explicitly_invoked`. 41 new regression/unit tests across `tests/regression/test_issue_802_agent_completeness_gate.py`, `tests/regression/test_issue_849_pipeline_mode_from_state.py`, `tests/unit/hooks/test_gh_issue_create_block.py`, and `tests/unit/hooks/test_stale_pipeline_state.py`. `docs/HOOKS.md` updated (agent-completeness bypass list and GitHub Issue Creation Gate Allow-through list). (#1027, #1382, #1383, #1384)

- **Graceful degradation in STEP 5.5 when plan-critic subagent type is not registered (Issue #1377)**: Adds STEP 5.5a.2 to `implement.md` — when the Agent tool invocation for `subagent_type='plan-critic'` returns an error whose message matches any of five case-insensitive patterns (`agent type not available`, `unknown subagent type`, `invalid subagent_type`, `subagent type … not found`, `no such subagent`), the coordinator calls `record_plan_critic_skipped(bypass_reason='agent_type_unavailable: <raw error>')` and falls through to STEP 5.5c (structural validation still runs). Previously, this error produced an unrecoverable pipeline failure. The STEP 5.5d FORBIDDEN #3 is amended with an EXCEPT carve-out referencing 5.5a.2 so the rule and the exception are co-located. No changes to `agent_ordering_gate.py`, `pipeline_completion_state.py`, or any hook file. 4 regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1377_plan_critic_agent_type_unavailable.py`. Note: full operability of the recorded skip depends on Issue #1228 (session-ID namespace split) — the skip IS written to completion state but the ordering-gate hook may not resolve the session_id namespace on read until #1228 lands. (#1377)

- **Align write-side sentinel path with `get_legacy_sentinel_path()` in `--light` mode (Issue #1376)**: The agent-completeness gate in `unified_pre_tool.py` was false-blocking in `--light` mode because the coordinator exported the per-run sentinel path (`/tmp/implement_pipeline_${RUN_ID}.json`) but the hook read the legacy per-repo sentinel (`<repo>/.claude/local/implement_pipeline_state.json` via `get_legacy_sentinel_path()`). Three write sites in `implement.md` now call `get_legacy_sentinel_path()` at export time — the STEP 0 export block (line 347), the STEP 15 cleanup (line 2252), and the STEP L5 light-mode cleanup (line 2520) — so both sides resolve to the same per-repo path. 4 regression tests added in `tests/regression/test_issue_1376_write_side_sentinel_path.py` cover AC1 (STEP 0 writes to per-repo path), AC2 (STEP L0 light-mode writes to per-repo path), AC3 (`--light` mode false-block reproduction), and AC7 (cross-repo path isolation). (#1376)

- **fix(drain-watchdog): route `[drain-stuck]` alerts through `daily_aggregate_manager` for same-UTC-day edit-in-place and prior-day supersede (Issue #1374)**. Replaces 4h creation-window debounce; extends `unified_pre_tool.py` guard to include `"[drain-stuck] watchdog"` prefix. `.github/workflows/drain-watchdog.yml`'s "Open watchdog alert issue" step now calls `open_or_supersede_daily_aggregate(repo, label="drain-stuck", title_prefix="[drain-stuck] watchdog", body, today_utc=now.date())` — same-UTC-day fires edit the existing aggregate in place, prior-day open aggregates are auto-closed with `Superseded by #<new>` before the new day's aggregate is created. Guard-side changes: `_detect_daily_aggregate_direct_filing()`'s `GUARDED` tuple now includes `"[drain-stuck] watchdog"` alongside the two triage prefixes, so any bypass attempt (`gh issue create --title "[drain-stuck] watchdog …"`) outside the aggregate helper is blocked unless the `triage-aggregate` marker is active. Tests: 3 new parametrize rows + 1 boundary near-miss row (`"[drain-stuck] fmarshal"` MUST NOT block — proves the guard is precisely `"[drain-stuck] watchdog"`, not the broader `"[drain-stuck]"`) in `tests/unit/hooks/test_aggregate_title_gate.py`; new `test_drain_stuck_edit_and_supersede_sequence` (3-day scenario) in `tests/unit/lib/test_daily_aggregate_manager.py`. (#1374)
- **NOTE (#1374 transitional)**: Any pre-existing open `[drain-stuck] watchdog:` issue (colon-format legacy title) will not be auto-superseded by the new aggregate flow — verify only #1305 is open before merge and close others manually.

### Changed
- **Plan-exit enforcement polarity flipped to default-ON (Issue #1361)**: Completes the polarity flip started in Issue #1142+ Phase 1 for the general SDLC enforcement. The plan-exit hooks (`plan_mode_exit_detector.py`, `unified_pre_tool.py`) previously silently no-op'd in foreign repos unless `AUTONOMOUS_DEV_GLOBAL_ENFORCEMENT=1` was set (scoped since #938, 2026-04-25). Now enforcement fires in every repo by default. Opt-out mechanisms unchanged: (1) universal Issue #969 bypass via `.claude/.bypass` sentinel file or `AUTONOMOUS_DEV_BYPASS=1` env var (turns off ALL hooks); (2) plan-review-specific escape via `AUTONOMOUS_DEV_SKIP_PLAN_REVIEW=1`, `.claude/SKIP_PLAN_REVIEW` sentinel, or `/implement --skip-review`. `AUTONOMOUS_DEV_GLOBAL_ENFORCEMENT` is now **deprecated** — a stderr deprecation notice is emitted when it is observed; setting the var is otherwise a no-op. Motivating symptom: a session in realign (2026-07-05) called ExitPlanMode after a single Plan agent run, skipped plan-critic, and self-reported the skip; no enforcement fired because the scope check no-op'd immediately. Behavioral tests added to `tests/unit/hooks/test_plan_exit_scope_check.py` (TestForeignProjectDefaultEnforce, TestGlobalEnforcementDeprecated) and `tests/unit/hooks/test_plan_exit_pretool_enforcement.py` (TestScopeCheckIntegration new cases: `test_foreign_project_with_marker_blocks_after_polarity_flip`, `test_foreign_project_with_skip_env_returns_none`, `test_foreign_project_with_sentinel_returns_none`, `test_global_enforcement_emits_deprecation_notice`, `test_mcp_gate_also_emits_deprecation_notice`). New helper `hook_bypass.warn_global_enforcement_deprecated_once()`. (#1361)

### Added
- **General-purpose agent warning** (Issue #1357): Added non-blocking warning in `unified_pre_tool.py` when the main agent invokes a general-purpose subagent outside an active pipeline. Logs to stderr and activity log for observability. 6 regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1357_general_purpose_warning.py` verify the warning behavior.
- **Cross-machine GH Issue claim mutex for `/implement --issues` and `/drain-queue` (Issue #1335)**: New library `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/issue_claim.py` implements a best-effort cross-machine mutex using a GH Issue `in-progress` label and a `🤖 Implementing #N now [host=…, pid=…, ts=…]` marker comment. This prevents a local `/implement --issues` and a cloud-drain `/drain-queue → /implement --issues` from racing on the same cluster simultaneously (race observed 2026-06-28 on cluster #1331+#1334). Three public functions: `is_claimed()` (checks for a live, non-self, non-stale claim), `claim_issue()` (comment-then-label acquire), `release_issue()` (label-remove-then-release-comment, best-effort). Stale claims older than 4 hours are ignored inline — no separate reaper. All `gh` failures degrade to "not claimed" so the pipeline never deadlocks on transient network issues. Integrated into `implement-batch.md` STEP I1.4 (claim acquisition before pipeline start, HARD GATE — aborts with exit 2 on conflict), STEP B3 (release on terminal failure), and STEP B4 (release on success). `cluster_selector.select_next_cluster` extended with `_is_cluster_in_progress()` to skip claimed clusters during drain selection. `install_manifest.json` updated to register the new library. 23 regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_claim_interlock.py`. (#1335)
### Fixed
- **Narrow `Bash(mkdir*)` to `Bash(mkdir -p .claude/*)` in global settings template (Issue #1335, FINDING-1)**: Follow-up to #1334. The broad `Bash(mkdir*)` entry added in #1334 matched any `mkdir` invocation (including `mkdir /etc/foo`, `mkdir ~/.ssh/...`, `mkdir /root/bar`). Currently a no-op because bare `"Bash"` at line 169 of `plugins/autonomous-dev/config/global_settings_template.json` subsumes it, but if `"Bash"` is ever narrowed in a future hardening pass, the entry would resurface as an intentional broad mkdir grant contradicting the `deny` rules protecting `/etc/**`, `~/.ssh/**`, `/root/**`. Removed the broad entry; the narrower `Bash(mkdir -p .claude/*)` sentinel on the next line remains as the forward-compat marker for `.claude/plans` and `.claude/decisions` directory creation. The five other scoped entries (`Write/Edit(.claude/plans/**)`, `Write/Edit(.claude/decisions/**)`) are unchanged. FINDING-3 (whether to ultimately narrow the bare `Bash`/`Write`/`Edit` allows) is deferred per #1336 — the blanket allows stay for now, and the scoped entries serve as forward-compat sentinels. (#1335)

- **CI Route tests step portability and marker documentation (Issue #1337)**: Follow-up to Issue #1332. Three reviewer findings addressed: (1) `tee /dev/tty` replaced with `tee /dev/stderr` in the Route tests step — `/dev/tty` is not available in all GitHub Actions runner contexts (e.g. when stdin is not a terminal), whereas `/dev/stderr` is always writable; no functional behavior change for the `GITHUB_OUTPUT` capture. (2) A YAML comment added directly above the `Route tests` step documenting why `marker=` is emitted to `$GITHUB_OUTPUT` despite having no downstream `pytest -m` consumer yet (wiring is deferred per #1332 pending a marker audit); without the comment, future readers could delete the emission as dead code.
