(3) Regression coverage added: 3 assertions appended to the existing `test_test_job_has_route_step_with_id` test lock `continue-on-error: true`, the absence of `/dev/tty`, and the presence of `/dev/stderr` on the route step. 1 new test function `test_marker_emission_documented_in_workflow` asserts that `ci.yml` contains references to both #1332 and #1337 alongside the word `marker`. `docs/GITHUB-ACTIONS.md` required no update — the behavioral description of the Route tests step was accurate before and after this fix. (#1337)

- **CI Full Test Suite timeout fix — Route tests short-circuit and pytest-xdist parallelization (Issue #1332)**: The `test` job in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` was hitting the 60-minute job timeout. Two structural changes: (1) A new `Route tests` pre-step (`id: route`) calls `route_tests()` from `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/test_routing.py` and exposes a `skip_all` output; when `skip_all=true` (e.g., docs-only PRs), four downstream steps — Install dependencies, Run unit tests, Run integration tests, Run regression tests — are short-circuited via step-level `if:` guards, preventing documentation-only changes from consuming the full test budget. (2) All three pytest steps now use `pytest-xdist` (`-n auto`) to parallelize across available runner cores, which is the primary wall-clock fix for the timeout. New regression test `tests/regression/test_ci_workflow_routing.py` (5 test functions) asserts the four acceptance criteria: route step with correct `id`, `pytest-xdist` in the install step, at least 3 pytest steps each using `-n auto`, and all four gated steps carrying the correct `if:` guard. `docs/GITHUB-ACTIONS.md` updated to document the Route tests pre-step, parallelization, and short-circuit behavior. (#1332)

- **CI Summary gate now blocks on `cancelled`/`timed_out` job conclusions (Issue #1333)**: The CI Summary step in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` previously gated on `= "failure"` for both `SMOKE_RESULT` and `TEST_RESULT`. GitHub Actions job conclusions include `cancelled`, `timed_out`, and other non-`failure` non-`success` values, so a Full Test Suite run that timed out or was cancelled would fall through the old gate and the CI Summary would emit "All CI checks passed!" — allowing merges where the full test suite never completed. Fixed by flipping both conditions to positive assertions: SMOKE now blocks unless `SMOKE_RESULT = "success"` (any non-success smoke conclusion is blocking, including cancellation); TEST now blocks unless `TEST_RESULT` is `"success"` or `"skipped"` (the `skipped` allowance covers the case where the Full Test Suite job is skipped because smoke failed, which is handled separately by the smoke block). New regression test `tests/regression/test_ci_summary_gate.py` extracts the live shell snippet from `ci.yml` (no hardcoded copy) and exercises it under `bash -c` with synthetic env vars for all 8 relevant SMOKE/TEST result combinations. `docs/GITHUB-ACTIONS.md` updated to document the CI workflow and its merge-gate contract. (#1333)

### Added
- **Ship `.claude/plans` and `.claude/decisions` write permissions in global settings template (Issue #1334)**: Six entries in `permissions.allow` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/config/global_settings_template.json`: `Bash(mkdir*)`, `Write(.claude/plans/**)`, `Edit(.claude/plans/**)`, `Write(.claude/decisions/**)`, `Edit(.claude/decisions/**)`, and `Bash(mkdir -p .claude/*)`. These mirror the rules used in the maintainer's working `~/.claude/settings.json` and align consumer-repo permissions with the maintainer environment for `/plan` and decision-record operations. Note: bare `"Bash"`, `"Write"`, and `"Edit"` blanket allows currently sit at lines 169–172 of the template and subsume the new entries, so the immediate behavior change is zero — the scoped entries serve as explicit-intent sentinels and forward-compatibility safety nets for any future hardening pass that narrows the blanket allows. The `permissions.deny` list (27 entries gating `/etc/**`, `~/.ssh/**`, `Bash(sudo:*)`, etc.) is byte-for-byte unchanged. The underlying template edit was applied by a parallel cloud-drain run as commit `b46f38e` (closes #1334 + #1331); this CHANGELOG entry documents the change for the maintainer-visible release notes that the cloud-drain commit omitted. (#1334)

### Changed
- **Workflow injection safety test widened to all `.github/workflows/*.yml` (Issue #1310)**: The `test_workflow_injection_safety.py` regression test now uses `sorted(WORKFLOWS_DIR.glob("*.yml"))` instead of a hardcoded 5-file list, ensuring any new workflow added to the repository is automatically covered by the env-block safety assertions. The module docstring was updated to reflect the completed scope expansion from the initial migration (#1287, #1306–#1309) to full coverage. (#1310)

### Fixed
- **Mac Studio launchd as canonical healthchecks.io heartbeat for drain-driver (Issue #1326)**: Closed the consecutive-skip starvation gap where GHA's built-in cron schedule drops 5-15% of fires. Under the old design, when Mac Studio launchd skipped dispatch (GHA run was current), healthchecks.io received no ping, triggering false DOWN alerts after 8+ consecutive skips at 30-min cadence. Fix: `scripts/launchd/drain-driver-cron.sh` now pings `HEALTHCHECK_PING_URL` on every successful determination — `skip`, `ok`, and `DISPATCHED`. Healthchecks alerting is decoupled from GHA dispatch: as long as Mac Studio is awake and launchd loaded, the check stays green. Empirical validation: 4 skip events in 7 hours (16% of cycles) previously exhausted the 240-min threshold; with skip-branch pinging, that failure mode is closed. Version-controlled as `scripts/launchd/drain-driver-cron.sh` and `scripts/launchd/com.akaszubski.drain-driver-cron.plist`; deploy procedure documented in RUNBOOK.md "Launchd-as-heartbeat" section with 4-part T+0 fault-injection checklist. (#1326)

- **TEST-PRUNING gate now compares deletable file count, not raw findings (Issue #1317)**: The continuous-improvement-analyst's TEST-PRUNING gate was incorrectly triggering on raw findings count (e.g., 2394 findings) when only a tiny fraction translate to whole-file deletions the auto-pruner can act on (1 file in practice). Fixed `test_lifecycle_manager.check_prunable_threshold()` to compare `deletable_file_count` from a `prune_tests(dry_run=True)` call instead of the raw `prunable_count`. Gate now reflects actionable work. Added 2 unit tests verifying the new metric. (#1317)


- **Rate-limit TEST-PRUNING signals in macro_promotion to reduce noise (Issue #1432)**: The auto-improvement filer was re-filing TEST-PRUNING warnings every session despite the high prunable count being a known reporting artifact. Added rate-limiting logic in `macro_promotion._meets_promotion_gate()` to suppress TEST-PRUNING signals until meaningful reduction occurs. This prevents redundant issue creation while the underlying count remains stable. (#1432)
### Added
- **`/goa` — Governance, Observability, Audit slash command (Issue #1320)**: New MVP command for autonomous infrastructure-health observation. Three subcommands: `start` (activates GOA, emits spec-first `/schedule create` lines for operator cron setup per AC2), `stop` (deactivates), and `status` (prints current state and thresholds). The `watch` subcommand runs a single health-check cycle using conservative-mode thresholds: cron drop rate >70% in 12 h and DOWN events >2 in 12 h trigger GitHub issue creation. A frequency gate (3 issues per 24 h, persisted via `gh issue list` query per AC9 remediation) prevents alert fatigue. Comparator uses `>=` for DOWN_EVENTS_THRESHOLD per AC7 remediation. Implemented by three new library modules: `goa_state.py` (manifest read/write via `atomic_write_json`), `goa_watcher.py` (core health-check logic, conservative thresholds), and `goa_cli.py` (argparse entry point). Command registered in `install_manifest.json`. 22 unit tests across `tests/unit/test_goa_state.py`, `tests/unit/test_goa_watcher.py`, and `tests/unit/commands/test_goa_command.py`. (#1320)
- **`pipeline_state.atomic_write_json` promoted to public API (Issue #1320)**: The private helper `_atomic_write_json` (temp-file + `os.replace` + 0o600 permissions pattern) was renamed to `atomic_write_json` to allow `goa_state.py` to import it without relying on a private name. A backward-compat alias `_atomic_write_json = atomic_write_json` preserves all existing callers. (#1320)

### Fixed
- **Plan-critic skip now emits activity-log event (Issue #1325)**: The `record_plan_critic_skipped()` function now writes a `plan_critic_skipped` event to `.claude/logs/activity/YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl` with timestamp, session_id, issue_number, plan_path, bypass_reason, and run_id fields. This allows the continuous-improvement-analyst to verify that plan-critic skips have corresponding logged justifications. Added 4 unit tests in new `TestPlanCriticActivityLogging` class. (#1325)

- **Hook sync fixes and unified_pre_tool.py deny-emission regression (Issue #1315)**: Three planned hook test/structure sync fixes plus one opportunistic production bug fix discovered during implementation. (1) `test_infrastructure_protection.py` — `test_install_manifest_allows_edit_inside_pipeline` now writes and clears the `agent_dispatch_sentinel` around the assertion, matching the post-#1296 requirement that an active sentinel must be present for the implementer-dispatched allow path to work; without this, the test was asserting against a code path that the sentinel guard blocked. (2) `test_native_tool_auto_approval.py` — 6 `mock_check` local function signatures updated to include `plan_critic_skipped=False` kwarg, matching the real `check_agent_ordering` signature introduced in #1279; the missing kwarg caused `TypeError` on call-through. (3) `enforce_tier_distribution.py` — `hook_safety` import added with inline fallback stub; `__main__` entry point changed from `main()` to `_hook_safe_main(main)` for structural conformance with the hook safety pattern used across all other hooks. (4) `unified_pre_tool.py` — deny-emission sequence at lines 6052-6066 re-indented to be nested inside the `elif not pipeline_active:` branch rather than executing unconditionally after the `if sentinel_active:` block; this fixes a latent `UnboundLocalError` on the implementer-dispatched allow path (where `file_name` was never assigned but the deny block ran anyway), which is the root cause that made fix (1) necessary — the #1296 sentinel check was correctly structured but the deny block misindentation defeated the allow-through logic. All 10 previously-failing tests now pass; broader hook suite confirmed green (2031 passed, 3 skipped, 0 new failures). (#1315)

- **Selector-stall detector hardening pass (Issue #1314)**: Pure hardening on the `selector_stall_detector.py` and `drain-watchdog.yml` introduced in #1303. Four security-auditor LOW findings addressed: (1) `LOG_LINES` bounded to 64 KB via `printf '%s' "$LOG_LINES" | head -c 65536` before the Python heredoc to prevent runaway memory on pathologically long commit subjects (LOW-1). (2) Trust-assumption Note block added to `detect_stall()` docstring explaining why malformed ISO timestamps are skipped without resetting the streak, and pinned by new regression test `test_malformed_timestamp_row_is_skipped_without_resetting_streak` (LOW-2/FINDING-3). (3) `gh auth status` preflight added to `plugins/autonomous-dev/scripts/setup-labels.sh` — exits with a clear error when the gh CLI is not authenticated instead of failing with an opaque `gh` error (LOW-3). (4) `timeout-minutes: 5` added to the "Detect selector stall" workflow step to prevent runaway hangs (LOW-4). Reviewer FINDING-2 also addressed: tool-failure exit tokens (`exit=*`, `*_unavailable`, `*_no_gh_cli`) are now normalized to empty cluster string in the workflow's parsing block — previously these tokens were passed verbatim to `detect_stall()` and treated as non-empty fires, breaking the stall streak. With the fix, tool-failure fires correctly count as empty fires, making stall detection more reliable across mixed-failure-mode periods (this is the only nominal-behavior change in this PR). 3 new smoke assertions in `tests/regression/smoke/test_drain_watchdog_labels.py` cover: `head -c 65536` byte bound, `TOOL_FAILURE_PREFIXES`/`SUFFIXES` normalization, and `timeout-minutes: 5` on the stall step. (#1314)

### Added
- **drain-driver cron tightened from hourly to every 30 min (Issue #1318)**: `.github/workflows/drain-driver.yml` cron changed from `'5 * * * *'` (once per hour at :05) to `'5,35 * * * *'` (twice per hour at :05 and :35). The :05/:35 offset preserves separation from drain-watchdog's :00/:30 slots to avoid concurrent runner contention. Rationale: GHA cron lag of up to 50 min means a single hourly fire can miss the 90-min healthcheck window if it slips; two fires per hour give the dead-man's-switch a second chance to ping within the window. Header comment in the workflow file updated to reflect the 30-min cadence and the #1318 rationale. 2 regression smoke tests added to `tests/regression/smoke/test_drain_driver_cron.py`: `test_drain_driver_cron_is_30_minute_cadence` (pins the `5,35 * * * *` expression) and `test_drain_driver_cron_does_not_collide_with_drain_watchdog` (asserts :00 and :30 minutes are absent from the minute field). (#1318)

- **Selector-stall detection in drain-watchdog (Issue #1303)**: New `selector_returned_empty` signal added to `.github/workflows/drain-watchdog.yml`. When 4 or more consecutive watchdog FIRE_END telemetry commits within the last 6 hours all report `no_drainable_cluster`, the workflow opens a `[selector-stall]` GitHub issue with labels `selector-stall` and `high-priority`. Implemented by new pure-Python module `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/selector_stall_detector.py` exposing `detect_stall(fires, *, now_utc, k, window_hours) -> SelectorStallResult`. K=4 and WINDOW_HOURS=6 are hardcoded module constants (YAGNI — edit module and add regression test to tune). The 4h debounce pattern from the existing drain-stuck alert is reused. AC4 (severity=high exclusion to suppress false positives when every cluster is high-severity) is relaxed in v1; the issue body instructs operators to manually close false positives. New idempotent label-setup script `plugins/autonomous-dev/scripts/setup-labels.sh` creates the `selector-stall` (orange) and `high-priority` (red) labels in the target repo. 7 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_selector_stall_detector.py` cover the stall boundary (k-1, k, k+1), window edge, empty-input, malformed-timestamp, and realistic telemetry-format cases (7th test added in post-remediation cycle to exercise the `export LOG_LINES` placement fix). 3 regression tests added to `tests/regression/smoke/test_drain_watchdog_labels.py` cover: selector-stall label pair presence, `selector_stall_detector` module import in workflow, and 4h debounce reuse. (#1303)

- **Auto-tag on push + consumer-side auto-update (Issue #1302)**: New CI workflow `.github/workflows/auto-tag-on-push.yml` emits an annotated tag of the form `autonomous-dev-v<patch>+<sha7>` on every push to `master` (non-log paths). The tag is idempotent (skipped if already exists), uses only `secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN`, and carries `contents: write` permission with a 3-minute timeout. New consumer-side script `scripts/pull-plugin-update.sh` polls for the latest `autonomous-dev-v*` tag on a 30-minute launchd interval, compares against `.claude/local/last_pulled_tag`, and runs `bash scripts/deploy-all.sh --local --no-global` only when a new tag is detected. Provides eventual consistency without inbound credentials — GitHub never needs SSH access to consumer Macs. RUNBOOK updated with one-time setup instructions (launchd plist template, operations table, failure modes). `docs/GITHUB-ACTIONS.md` updated with workflow entry and description. `docs/SCRIPTS.md` updated with `pull-plugin-update.sh` reference. 27 acceptance tests in `tests/unit/test_auto_tag_on_push_workflow.py` cover all 7 acceptance criteria (workflow triggers, secret isolation, recursive-trigger guard, bounded permissions, script flags, state management, RUNBOOK documentation). (#1302)

- **Issue #908 infrastructure complete — bulk test pruning deferred** (Issue #908): Tier-distribution warning hook and testing-guide skill documentation delivered in commit d2baf52. Infrastructure is operational: `enforce_tier_distribution.py` hook registered, `/sweep --tests` integrated into `/improve` weekly cycle (STEP 2.8), tier selection guidance in testing-guide skill. The bulk test pruning work (reducing 2,353 prunable tests to <500) is deferred to a focused follow-up issue for risk mitigation — ensures existing test coverage remains stable while infrastructure matures.
- **Test pruning analysis in `/improve` weekly cycle** (Issue #908): Added STEP 2.8 to `/improve` command that runs `/sweep --tests` analysis weekly to surface prunable test candidates. Tracks drift target of <500 prunable tests as root-cause metric for test pyramid health. Persistence via `.claude/logs/sweep-tests-*.log` for weekly cycle tracking.
- **Tier distribution gate in test health dashboard** (Issue #908): Added warning-only tier distribution gate to `/improve` test health analysis, checking for test pyramid balance according to 5:2:2:1 target ratio (T3:T2:T1:T0). Gate warns when upper tiers (T0+T1+T2) drop below 25% of total tests, indicating bottom-heavy pyramid drift. Integrated into `test_lifecycle_manager.format_dashboard()` output. Added tier selection criteria to testing-guide skill documenting when to use each tier. Added 6 unit tests in `TestTierDistributionGate` class.


- **Epic #1162 kickoff: CC-native redundancy refactor documentation foundation**: Documented the planned CC-native redundancy refactor strategy in `docs/ARCHITECTURE-OVERVIEW.md` under a new "CC-Native vs Plugin Components" section. The refactor will (1) migrate 22 skills from `plugins/autonomous-dev/skills/` to `.claude/skills/`, (2) migrate 25 commands to `.claude/commands/`, (3) drop `config/auto_approve_policy.json` in favor of native `permissions.allow`/`permissions.deny`, and (4) refactor MCP security to retain only `unified_pre_tool.py` Layer 1 input validation. Estimated ~25% plugin overhead reduction. Methodology (8-step pipeline, plan-critic loop, agent dispatch ordering, session archiving) is load-bearing and preserved. This commit is documentation-only — subsequent sub-PRs deliver the actual refactor phases. (Issue #1162)

- **Security hardening for credential leak prevention** (Issue #1191): Added FORBIDDEN bullet to `security-auditor.md` instructing the agent not to quote credential values verbatim in finding text — must cite file path and line number only. This prevents accidental credential leakage from internal code into public GitHub issue bodies via the implement.md advisory-issue filer (line ~1427). Added test `test_security_auditor_forbids_credential_quoting` in `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_issue844_prompt_integrity_reload.py` asserting this bullet is present.

- **Security advisory parser shell injection protection test** (Issue #1192): Added behavioral test `test_advisory_summary_shell_injection_protection` to `tests/unit/test_security_advisory_parser.py` verifying that shell metacharacters in advisory summaries are passed as safe subprocess arguments with `shell=False`, preventing command injection. The test mocks `subprocess.run` and asserts that metacharacters (`;|$()`) appear verbatim in the argument list, not shell-expanded, satisfying the security-auditor's acceptance criterion.
- **ADR-002 Phase C confidence-based drain eligibility gate** (Issue #1291): Added `AUTO_DRAINABLE_CONFIDENCE_THRESHOLD = 0.80` constant and new `confidence_gate(cluster_confidence)` pure function to `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/drain_queue_state.py`. Extended `evaluate_cluster_gates(...)` signature with `cluster_confidence: float = 0.0` parameter — gate order is now severity → tag → size → confidence. Per PROJECT.md ("HIGH confidence diagnoses applied autonomously"), confidence is the autonomy decision; severity remains for impact-tier ranking. Added `_infer_confidence(labels, body)` helper to `issue_triage_analyzer.py`: returns 1.0 for `confidence:high` label, 0.0 for `confidence:medium/low`, 0.8 if issue body has BOTH `Acceptance Criteria` AND `Proposed fix`/`Implementation Approach` sections, else 0.0. Updated `/drain-queue` STEP 4 to pass `cluster_confidence` through to the gate. Added 33 unit tests in new `tests/unit/lib/test_confidence_gate.py` covering threshold boundaries, malformed-input coercion, gate ordering, label-driven and body-heuristic confidence inference, and end-to-end integration.

- **Phase C auto-revert scaffolding for drain-queue** (Issue #1292): Added optional `revert_status` and `revert_sha` fields to `DrainHistory` records and new `latest_pending_reverts()` query method to support automatic revert on regression detection. This is scaffolding only — the actual revert checker is gated on Issue #1290 (before/after pytest metrics). Added 4 unit tests for field persistence and legacy compatibility.

- **ADR-002 Phase C: Before/after pytest metrics in DrainHistory** (Issue #1290): Added optional `before_metrics` and `after_metrics` dict fields to `DrainHistory` records to capture pytest test count and coverage delta for each drain. `/drain-queue` STEP 12 extended to read `/tmp/drain_before_metrics.json` if present and persist metrics in the history record. Backward compatible — legacy entries lack the new keys. Added 5 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_drain_history_metrics.py`.


- **Shared-file cluster merging in issue triage (Issue #1288)**: New `merge_by_shared_files(clusters)` function in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/issue_triage_analyzer.py` merges clusters within the same root-cause tag when they share at least one file reference, improving auto-drainable cluster sizes while respecting the `MAX_CLUSTER_SIZE_AUTO_DRAINABLE` limit. Integrated into `_triage_from_issues` after initial Jaccard clustering but before cross-cluster dependency detection. Added 5 parametrized test scenarios in `tests/unit/lib/test_issue_triage_analyzer.py`.


### Fixed
- **Coordinator direct-edit bypass prevention (Issue #1296)**: Added agent dispatch sentinel mechanism to prevent coordinators from directly editing protected infrastructure paths (`agents/*.md`, `commands/*.md`, `hooks/*.py`, `lib/*.py`, `skills/*/SKILL.md`) during pipeline execution. The sentinel file (`.claude/local/active_agent_dispatch.json`) tracks whether an agent-dispatched edit is in flight with 30-second TTL. When pipeline is active and no sentinel exists, coordinator attempts to edit protected paths are now blocked with "Coordinator cannot directly edit protected path" message, forcing proper re-dispatch through the implementer agent. Added 20 tests (7 regression, 13 unit) covering sentinel lifecycle, TTL expiry, and block scenarios.

- **Pre-commit `validate_structure.py` — regex and counter both fixed for 'user-facing commands' qualifier**: Master commit 5c88ef7 (autonomous drain of #1159) added "user-facing" before "commands" in CLAUDE.md component-counts line but did not update the validator, breaking all subsequent commits on master. Two separate fixes applied: (1) Regex fix — SUMMARY_RE segment changed from `(?P<commands>\d+)\s+commands,` to `(?P<commands>\d+)\s+(?:user-facing\s+)?commands,` so both bare and qualified variants parse. (2) Counter fix — `_count_user_facing_commands()` added; `check_component_count_drift()` now detects the "user-facing" qualifier in the matched text and dispatches to this counter instead of `_count_commands()` (which returns all 25 files). The new counter reads YAML front-matter and counts only files containing `user_facing: true` (22 of 25 command files). 7 unit tests total in `tests/unit/scripts/test_validate_structure.py` (4 TestSummaryRegex + 3 TestUserFacingCommandCount) lock both behaviors.

- **Agent-completeness gate enforced under .bypass in self-maintenance mode for git commits** (Issue #1195): Under self-maintenance mode (canonical autonomous-dev repo), the `.claude/.bypass` marker no longer fully short-circuits the agent-completeness commit gate for `git commit` Bash invocations. Consumer-repo behavior is unchanged. Added conditional bypass-exit logic in `unified_pre_tool.py` and 2 tests in new `TestBypassSelfMaintenanceInteraction` class in `tests/regression/test_issue_802_agent_completeness_gate.py`.

- **Test collection errors in unit/lib tests** (Issue #1205): Fixed 4 collection errors in `tests/unit/lib/` caused by 3 test files using incorrect import paths (`autonomous_dev.lib.*` instead of `plugins.autonomous_dev.lib.*`). The affected test files (`test_memory_formatter.py`, `test_memory_relevance.py`, and `test_acceptance_criteria_parser.py` with 16 import occurrences total) now use the correct import pattern aligned with the conftest.py module aliasing and 60+ other tests in the suite.
- **Agent-ordering gate session_id boundary mismatch** (Issue #1196): Hook now falls back to sentinel-resolved session_id when payload session_id has no recorded completions (mtime-guarded, 3600s TTL); deny messages now include the evaluated session_id for diagnosability.
- **Stale documentation corrected for hook location after Issue #1183** (Issue #1187): Updated documentation in `plugins/autonomous-dev/commands/setup.md` (lines 290, 293) and `docs/CLAUDE-MEM-INTEGRATION.md` (lines 47, 140) that incorrectly claimed hooks are configured in `settings.local.json`. After Issue #1183, hooks live exclusively in `settings.json` (written by `sync_settings_hooks.py`); `settings.local.json` carries only permissions and user customizations.


### Security
- **Shell injection hardening in drain workflows (Issue #1287)**: Replaced direct `${{ }}` expression interpolation inside `run:` blocks with the canonical GitHub Actions `env:` block + `$VARNAME` shell reference pattern in `.github/workflows/drain-driver.yml` and `.github/workflows/drain-watchdog.yml`. 7 interpolation sites migrated across 2 files: `drain-driver.yml` — `cluster_override` input and `HEALTHCHECK_DRAIN_DRIVER` secret; `drain-watchdog.yml` — `steps.detect.outputs.reason`, `steps.detect.outputs.cluster`, `github.run_id`, `github.server_url`, and `github.repository` in both the drain-stuck alert and selector-stall alert steps. The `env:` block causes the GitHub runner to expand and escape expression values before they reach the shell, eliminating the vector where attacker-controlled inputs (workflow_dispatch values, step output strings) could be pasted verbatim into shell script source. No behavioral change — identical string content reaches the shell; only the delivery mechanism changes. One documented exclusion remains in `drain-watchdog.yml` line 68 (`force_heal` boolean input, tracked as follow-up). (CWE-78)

- **ReDoS hardening in `sync_validator.py` path regex** (Issue #1222): The path validation regex in `sync_validator._validate_hook_file()` line 355 previously used an unbounded `+` quantifier on the `[\w./~-]` character class. An adversarial input could cause superlinear regex-engine scanning time. Fix bounds the character-class run to `{1,512}` to prevent ReDoS attacks, following the same defensive pattern established in Issues #1194, #1220, and #1221. The public API is unchanged — paths exceeding 512 characters (extremely rare in practice) are now rejected. Added 3 regression tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_sync_validator_regex_bound.py` covering: no-match on 100 KB adversarial pad, inclusive upper bound (512-char path matches), and typical short paths still match. (CWE-1333)

### Changed
- Added `user_facing` front-matter to plugin command files and regenerated COMMANDS.md catalogue; new congruence assertion in `test_documentation_congruence.py` prevents future drift (#1159)
- **Drain severity_gate now allows 'medium' severity (ADR-002 Phase D partial)**: `AUTO_DRAINABLE_SEVERITY` expanded from `{"low", "info"}` to `{"low", "info", "medium"}`. `severity_gate` now blocks only `severity=high` (critical/p0 issues requiring human review). `confidence_gate` (>=0.80, Issue #1291) is the real autonomy decision per ADR-002 Phase D. Diagnosis: 10 of 11 open `auto-improvement` issues were blocked by severity_gate due to `bug` label → `MEDIUM_LABEL_KEYWORDS` → `medium` severity mapping (verified against live state 2026-06-24). Full severity retirement (gate removed entirely, severity becomes ranking-only) deferred to a follow-up issue.
- **Sessions database v2 migration kept as in-progress**: Schema documentation moved to `docs/development/SESSIONS_DB_SCHEMA_V2.md`, migration script at `scripts/migrate_sessions_v2.py`, .gitignore rules added for `sessions_v2*.db` files (Issue #1157)
- **ADR-002 Phase A completion criterion added** (Issue #1277): Added explicit acceptance criterion to exclude tracker issues with `root-cause` label from auto-drain selection. The drain-driver exposed a tracker-selection bug where trackers labeled `root-cause` + `auto-improvement` get repeatedly selected despite being meta-tracking issues. Fix documented in `docs/ADR-002-drain-queue-redesign.md` line 179 as new Phase A checkbox criterion. The referenced issue #1277 had its `auto-improvement` label removed (GitHub side-effect, no file change) to prevent the tracker from re-entering the drain queue.

- **ADR-002 Phase status notes added to user-facing documentation**: Added Phase A completion and Phase B in-progress status notes to `/drain-queue` command descriptions in `docs/RUNBOOK.md` (line 100) and `plugins/autonomous-dev/docs/COMMANDS.md` (line 16), including hyperlinks to the ADR-002 design document and issue references (#1274, #1276). These notes inform users of the current implementation status without modifying the command behavior itself.

### Fixed
- **`deploy-all.sh --local --no-global` exits 1 when `~/.claude/hooks/unified_pre_tool.py` differs from source (Issue #1313)**: The post-deploy validate-global block ran unconditionally inside `if $DO_LOCAL && ! $DRY_RUN && ! $SKIP_VALIDATE`, even when `--no-global` was passed and `DO_GLOBAL=false`. This caused `scripts/pull-plugin-update.sh` (which calls `deploy-all.sh --local --no-global`) to exit 1 and skip advancing the state file, blocking consumer Macs from auto-updating. Fix wraps the validate-global block in `if $DO_GLOBAL; then ... fi` — mirroring the deploy-side gate at line 479 — so the global hook checksum comparison only runs when global files were actually deployed in that invocation. Per-repo `validate_local` loop is unchanged. Discovered during field-test of #1302. 4 static-inspection tests in `tests/unit/scripts/test_deploy_all_validate_global_gate.py` lock the gate structure: deploy script exists, `# Validate global` comment is immediately followed by `if $DO_GLOBAL; then`, `--no-global` parser still sets `DO_GLOBAL=false`, and the per-repo validation loop remains outside the new gate. (#1313)

- **plan-critic verdict-only output regression** (Issue #1272): Fixed plan-critic agent emitting verdict-only output (e.g., "Verdict: PROCEED") without the required critique paragraphs. The prompt's verdict format templates were appearing mid-document, allowing the model to short-circuit directly to a verdict without analysis. Fix: restructured `plugins/autonomous-dev/agents/plan-critic.md` to move verdict templates to the END of the prompt, added explicit `## STRUCTURAL ENFORCEMENT: Critique Before Verdict` section requiring substantive critique paragraphs BEFORE any verdict, added `## FORBIDDEN Behaviors` rule against verdict-only output, and strengthened `## Output Format Contract` to specify exact ordering with verdict LAST. Added 7 regression tests in `tests/regression/test_plan_critic_output_structure.py` to lock the structural enforcement.

- **Severity classifier no longer treats `security` label as a HIGH severity signal (ADR-002 Phase A, Issue #1273)**: `_infer_severity()` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/issue_triage_analyzer.py` previously inherited `security` in its `HIGH_LABEL_KEYWORDS` tuple, causing routine `[Security advisory]` hardening issues to be classified `severity=high` and thereby blocked from autonomous handling (the drain selector only auto-drains `low`/`info`). `HIGH_LABEL_KEYWORDS` is now `("critical", "p0")` (line 62); the function docstring at line 412 documents the rationale ("`security` is a topic/area label, not a severity signal"). The fix decouples topic from severity per ADR-002 Invariant 3 and unblocks the security-labeled hardening issues that were stuck in the queue at the time of the audit. ADR-002 Implementation Progress section also added in this commit to track Phase A completion and Phase B/C/D status (tracker #1277).

- **Sandbox policy — targeted `rm` regex replaces blanket `rm -rf` block**: `sandbox_policy.json` had a substring match on `"rm -rf"` that blocked ALL `rm -rf` commands including safe worktree cleanup (`rm -rf .worktrees/batch-*`), breaking `/implement --batch` in consumer repos (Spektiv). Replaced with a targeted regex in both `development` and `testing` profiles that matches `rm` with flags only when the target path is a dangerous destination (`/` absolute paths, `~` home dir, `.` current dir, `.git`, `.ssh`, `.aws`, `.env`). The `production` profile retains a blanket `rm` block. Scope-widens the block to cover additional absolute-path variants and homedir shorthands (security-auditor finding). `batch_git_finalize.cleanup_worktree()` switched from `shutil.rmtree` to `git worktree remove --force` with `shutil.rmtree` as fallback for corrupted worktrees; git failures before fallback now emit `logger.warning()` to the audit log.

- **Watchdog self-loop eliminated — `auto-improvement` label removed from stuck-alert issues (ADR-002 Phase A, Issue #1275)**: `drain-watchdog.yml` was filing `[drain-stuck]` meta-issues with both `drain-stuck` AND `auto-improvement` labels. The `auto-improvement` label caused the drain selector to pick up the watchdog's own alert issues as drainable work. Because the title-prefix filter (`is_drain_stuck_meta` — checks for `[drain-stuck]` in title) was the only guard, any alert whose title diverged from that exact prefix could enter the drain queue and be selected for healing, consuming a runner slot while the real backlog grew by 1 per failed watchdog cycle. Fix removes `auto-improvement` from `--label "drain-stuck"` on the `gh issue create` call at line 169 of `.github/workflows/drain-watchdog.yml`. Operator alert discovery still works via the `drain-stuck` label. 2 regression tests added in `tests/regression/smoke/test_drain_watchdog_labels.py`: negative assertion that the combined label string is absent, positive control that `drain-stuck` is still present.

- **doc-master re-invocation after Step 11 remediation cycles** (Issue #1271): doc-master is now re-invoked when STEP 11 remediation modifies files/tests, preventing CHANGELOG drift. Previously, doc-master ran at STEP 10 BEFORE remediation and was never updated after STEP 11 changes. Implementation: Added `remediation_occurred` flag to `PipelineState` dataclass; new `set_remediation_flag(run_id)` called by coordinator at STEP 11 when remediation triggers; new `get_remediation_flag(run_id)` checked at STEP 12 — if True, doc-master is re-invoked with the updated file list. Coordinator discards stale STEP 10 doc-master results when remediation occurred.

- **Coordinator test-evidence enforcement for reviewer validation** (Issue #1270): STEP 10/10a reviewer invocations now MUST include pytest stdout as a structured artifact, not narrated counts. Previously, coordinators could pass paraphrased test results like "14+87=101 passing", preventing reviewers from independently verifying test evidence. Fix adds "VERBATIM PASSING REQUIRED" clauses to both parallel (STEP 10) and sequential (STEP 10a) modes, requiring raw pytest output with === markers, test names, and final summary line. FORBIDDEN: narrated/computed counts that cannot be independently verified.

- **Duplicate opt-out hint removed from write-gate deny messages** (Issue #1152): The Phase 1 default-on tier-aware write gate previously emitted `Per-repo opt-out: touch .claude/.bypass && git commit.` twice in the deny reason — once in the directive built by `_check_write_pipeline_required` / `_check_bash_code_file_pipeline_required`, and once as a caller-side footer. Fix removes the duplicate from the directive functions; caller-side footer is the canonical single emission point. User-visible effect: deny reasons are slightly shorter and no longer repeat the same opt-out sentence.

- **Batch-gate CLAUDE_SESSION_ID reads now use sanitized helper** (Issue #1189): Three batch-gate sites in unified_pre_tool.py (`_check_batch_cia_completions`, `_check_batch_doc_master_completions`, and agent completeness gate) previously read CLAUDE_SESSION_ID via raw `os.environ.get()` instead of the sanitized helper `_resolve_session_id_safe` (used by 5 sibling sites per #1171). Fix replaces these 3 sites with the same safe-helper pattern. Defense-in-depth completeness; no exploitable behavior change (downstream sha256-hashes the value).

### Added
- **Debug-level audit logging for path traversal rejections in `verify_paths_exist`** (Issue #1219): `verify_paths_exist()` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/plan_freshness.py` now emits a `logger.debug()` entry when a candidate path is rejected because its resolved location escapes `repo_root` (`relative_to()` ValueError branch). Aids future incident investigation if active exploitation patterns appear. Module docstring updated to acknowledge that debug-level diagnostic logging is permitted (consistent with the existing MAX_PATHS truncation log). Return-value behavior is unchanged. 1 regression test added in `tests/unit/lib/test_plan_freshness_path_traversal.py::test_traversal_rejection_logged` using the `caplog` fixture.

- **MAX_PATHS guard in plan_freshness.py** (Issue #1223): Added `MAX_PATHS=500` constant and truncation logic to `extract_referenced_paths()` to prevent O(n) path-check overhead on adversarially large plan blobs. When more than 500 paths are found in a plan, the function truncates the result list and emits a debug-level log message reporting the omission count. This is internal hardening — the function signature is unchanged, and outputs are identical for plans with fewer than 500 referenced paths. Added 1 regression test in `tests/unit/lib/test_plan_freshness_max_paths.py`.

- **Cluster selector for drain-queue tracker filtering** (Issue #1284): New pure function `select_next_cluster(clusters)` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/cluster_selector.py` filters tracker-shaped issues from drain-queue candidates and prefers their lowest-numbered open leaf children when present. Detects trackers via: title prefix `[TRACKER]`, label combination "root-cause" + "enhancement", or body structure with ≥2 phase headings + ≥2 issue references. When encountering a tracker with open leaves, returns a synthetic single-issue cluster pointing to the lowest leaf. Not yet wired into `/drain-queue` command — integration tracked separately. Added 5 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_cluster_selector.py`.

- **Coordinator bypass verdict tracking** (Issue #1279): Extended `record_plan_critic_skipped()` with optional `bypass_reason` parameter and added new `write_coordinator_bypass_verdict()` function in `pipeline_completion_state.py` to enable drain-queue coordinators to record machine-readable audit trails when bypassing plan-critic for mechanical extensions. New `/drain-queue` command documentation section "Coordinator Bypass of plan-critic" details the two-call protocol. 3 new tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1279_coordinator_bypass_verdict.py` verify the bypass tracking logic.

- **Activity log file-path diagnostics for security-auditor and doc-master agents** (Issue #1280): `session_activity_logger.py` adds two optional fields `prompt_file_count` and `prompt_file_paths` to JSONL activity-log entries for ONLY security-auditor and doc-master agent invocations. Private helper `_extract_file_paths()` parses file paths from agent prompts to detect potential file omission during batch context compression. The fields are additive (no removed fields, no changed semantics) and purely observational for debugging. 7 unit tests in `tests/unit/hooks/test_session_activity_logger_file_extraction.py`.

- **Phase 2 SWE intent router (Issue #1263 Phase A — log-only)**: `semantic_gate.py` gains a second public entry point `route(*, file_path, old_string, new_string, tool_name, session_id=None) -> RouterResult` (Phase A, log-only) that classifies code edits as SDLC work vs. non-SDLC. New constants: `ROUTER_PROMPT_VERSION = "v2"`, `_ROUTER_CODE_EXTENSIONS` (11-entry frozenset), `is_write_to_code_file()` filter gate, `_append_route_log()` audit emitter. Audit rows written to `.claude/logs/route/<date>.jsonl` (separate from Phase 1 judge log at `.claude/logs/judge/`). Phase 1 `judge()` / `JudgeResult` preserved unchanged. `unified_pre_tool.py` gains `_maybe_invoke_swe_router()` dispatcher and `_LAST_ROUTE_TOKEN_BY_SESSION` fire-once-per-turn cache; when feature flag `swe_router` is explicitly enabled, invokes `route()` instead of the Phase 1 judge; falls back to Phase 1 judge when only `semantic_gate` is enabled. Phase A is log-only — the router NEVER blocks. `session_mode.py` gains `user_prompt_text` artifact field (≤1000 chars of raw prompt, additive — SCHEMA_VERSION stays at 1) and `get_user_msg_token()` public API for content-addressed per-turn deduplication tokens. Feature flag: `swe_router` key in `.claude/feature_flags.json`, default OFF.

- **Semantic Gate Phase 1 — shadow judge for Write/Edit classification**: New library `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/semantic_gate.py` implements a feature-flagged LLM judge that runs on every Write/Edit attempt inside `unified_pre_tool.py`, before the universal bypass check, and writes one structured JSONL audit row to `.claude/logs/judge/<date>.jsonl`. Phase 1 is shadow-only — the verdict is never enforced; the existing rule-based gate remains authoritative. Any failure path (SDK unavailable, timeout, malformed JSON) returns `JudgeResult(verdict="abstain", fail_open=True)` and never raises. Default OFF via opt-in feature flag (`semantic_gate` key in `.claude/feature_flags.json`). New `is_feature_explicitly_enabled()` helper added to `feature_flags.py` with opt-in semantics (returns True only when the file exists AND `{"enabled": true}` is present explicitly — contrast with the existing `is_feature_enabled()` opt-out path). Library count updated to 230. 22 new tests across `tests/unit/lib/test_semantic_gate.py` (10 tests) and `tests/unit/lib/test_feature_flags.py` (12 tests, including 3 non-regression tests for the existing opt-out path).

- **Evidence Manifest gate structured logging for CIA audit** (Issue #1233): The STEP 8 Evidence Manifest gate in `commands/implement.md` now emits structured log lines with `marker_found=` and `mode=` fields (e.g., `"Evidence Manifest gate: PASS (#1055) — marker_found=true, mode=full"`). Four outcomes covered: `SKIP` (light/fix modes), `PASS` (manifest found), `REMEDIATION` (fixed after retry), `BLOCKED` (missing after retry). The continuous-improvement-analyst gains Check #16 (`[GATE-BYPASS]`) which scans these logs and flags any full-mode run where `marker_found=false` and the outcome is not `BLOCKED` — catching the failure mode observed in pipeline run `20260617-080132` where the coordinator advanced past STEP 8 without the implementer's Evidence Manifest table.
- **MEDIUM-convergence check advisory step** (STEP 11.6): New advisory pipeline step that detects when ≥2 distinct validation agents (reviewer, security-auditor) flag the same file or function with MEDIUM+ severity findings. Surfaces multi-agent convergence patterns via `[CONVERGED-MEDIUM]` banner and offers three non-blocking options: REMEDIATE (re-run implementer + re-validate), DEFER (file GitHub issues for tracking), ACKNOWLEDGE (log justification and continue). Convergence artifacts logged to `.claude/logs/activity/{RUN_ID}_convergence.json` for audit trail. Not a hard gate by design — advisory feedback to highlight high-confidence areas needing attention without blocking progress.
- **Research persistence enhancement for pipeline restart** (Issue #1232): Added `save_merged_research_blob()` function to `research_persistence.py` to enable coordinators to persist already-merged research markdown to the research cache on pipeline restart. This avoids duplicate researcher runs during planner-drift recovery. The new function is a thin wrapper that delegates to existing `save_research()` with no changes to existing function signatures or behaviors. Tests added in `tests/unit/lib/test_research_persistence.py`.

### Changed
- **Planner agent LoC calibration** (Issue #1240): Added mandatory 2x multiplier for module size estimates to address systematic underestimation where planner logic-line counts ran 2.5x below actual implementation lines. Planner now reports both logic lines and total lines (logic × multiplier) with context-aware multipliers: 1.5x for utilities, 2x default, 2.5x-3x for heavily-documented APIs.
### Fixed
- **False-positive `zero_word_agent_output` findings in CIA reports** (Issue #1266): `agent_output_health._get_agent_completions()` now excludes events where `subagent_type` starts with `__dedup_skip__`. These are internal dedup-tracking sentinels written by the SubagentStop dedup guard (Issue #1176) and are not real agent invocations, but they carry `result_word_count == 0`, causing spurious CRITICAL findings in CIA pipeline health reports. One-line filter in the shared helper benefits both `detect_zero_word_completions` and `check_agent_output_health` automatically. 3 regression tests added in `tests/unit/lib/test_agent_output_health.py` (`TestDedupSkipFilter`).

- **runtime_data_aggregator.aggregate() relative path handling** (Issue #1207): The `aggregate()` function now properly resolves relative `project_root` paths to absolute paths, preventing ValueError when constructing the findings directory path. Previously, passing a relative path like `"."` would fail when trying to resolve `findings_dir.relative_to(project_root)` because one was relative and the other absolute. Added `.resolve()` normalization to ensure both paths use the same reference frame. 1 regression test added in `tests/unit/lib/test_runtime_data_aggregator.py`.

- **Prompt-shrink detector false positives on coordinator re-dispatches** (Issue #1227): Fixed false-positive prompt compression blocks when the coordinator legitimately re-invokes an agent after an ordering-gate denial. Added redispatch awareness to `prompt_integrity.py`: new `set_redispatch_flag(agent_type)`, `consume_redispatch_flag(agent_type)`, and `is_canonical_template_match(agent_type, content, *, tolerance=0.10)` helpers. `unified_pre_tool.py` now sets redispatch flags at ordering-gate deny paths. `validate_prompt_word_count()` short-circuits on redispatch flag or canonical-template baseline reset. 6 new regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1227_redispatch_fp.py`.

- **Multi-issue body extraction** (Issue #1231): Fixed `/implement --issues` to correctly fetch and concatenate all issue bodies (up to 10) with section headers, replacing the previous single-issue-only extraction that silently dropped additional issue context.

- **gh issue close comment flag false-positive blocking** (Issue #1216): Extended the #1215 fix to cover `gh issue close -c "..."` and `gh issue close --comment "..."` commands. The hook was incorrectly blocking these commands when the comment text contained the substring "gh issue create" as prose. Added `-c` and `--comment` flags to `GH_ISSUE_BODY_FLAGS` tuple in `unified_pre_tool.py` (3 lines) so these argument values are stripped before the substring detection runs. 9 new tests in `tests/unit/hooks/test_gh_issue_create_block.py` verify the fix and prevent regression.

### Security


- **Defense-in-depth CIA findings directory permission hardening (Issue #1208)**: `cia_finding_store.py` now includes a 5-line chmod backstop after mkdir to ensure findings directories are created with 0o700 permissions as documented. Previously relied solely on `os.makedirs(mode=0o700)` which could be overridden by umask on some systems. The backstop performs `os.chmod(findings_dir, 0o700)` with OSError swallowing for fail-open behavior, mirroring the existing file-mode backstop pattern at lines 293-296. No user-observable behavior change — the library already documented these permissions in `docs/LIBRARIES.md` entry #222, and this ensures the guarantee is honored across all environments. One regression test added in `tests/unit/lib/test_cia_finding_store.py` asserting the 0o755 → 0o700 transition.
- **Defense-in-depth log permission hardening (Issue #1267 Bucket C #3)**: Four log-writing libraries (`coordinator_log.py`, `intent_classifier.py`, `semantic_gate.py`, `logging_utils.py`) now enforce restrictive filesystem permissions on all log directories (`0o700`) and files (`0o600`). Previously, logs were created with default umask permissions, potentially world-readable on multi-user systems. Fixed by adding `mode=0o700` to all `mkdir()` calls and `os.chmod(path, 0o600)` after each log file write. This prevents other local users from reading activity logs, workflow progress, telemetry data, or judge decisions that might contain sensitive session information. The hardening is fail-open — chmod failures are silently swallowed to preserve core functionality. Affected paths: `.claude/logs/activity/`, `.claude/logs/workflows/`, `.claude/logs/judge/`, and any telemetry subdirectories.
- **ReDoS hardening in `already_done_detector._PATH_RE`** (Issue #1221): `_PATH_RE` previously used an unbounded `+` quantifier on the `[\w/.-]` character class (i.e., `[\w/.-]+\.(?:py|md|json|yaml|yml|sh|ts|js)`). An adversarial commit body containing a long run (e.g., 100 KB) of character-class-matching bytes with no matching file extension could cause superlinear regex-engine scanning time in `_extract_symbols()`. Fix bounds the character-class run with `{1,512}` — mirroring the Issue #1194 precedent for `plan_freshness._FILE_PATH_REGEX` and the Issue #1220 fix to `PYTEST_PATH_PATTERN`. The public function signature of `check_issue_already_implemented()` is unchanged; behavior changes only at the boundary — path-like tokens exceeding 512 chars are now rejected by `_PATH_RE`, which does not occur in legitimate commit bodies. A 4-line explanatory comment in `already_done_detector.py` documents the bound and its lineage. 3 regression tests added in `tests/unit/lib/test_already_done_detector_regex_bound.py` covering: no-match on 100 KB adversarial pad (timing < 1 s), inclusive upper bound (512-char path + `.py` matches), and typical short path still matches. (CWE-1333)

- **ReDoS hardening in `security_utils.PYTEST_PATH_PATTERN`** (Issue #1220): `PYTEST_PATH_PATTERN` previously used unbounded `+` quantifiers on both character classes (`[\w/.-]+` for the path segment and `[\w\[\],_-]+` for the optional test-ID segment). An adversarial input — e.g., a 100 KB run of character-class-matching bytes with no `.py` suffix — could cause superlinear regex-engine scanning time. Fix bounds the path-segment run with `{1,4096}` (matching `MAX_PATH_LENGTH`, POSIX PATH_MAX) and the test-ID run with `{1,512}` (mirroring the Issue #1194 precedent). Function signature of `validate_pytest_path()` is unchanged; behavior changes only at the boundary — paths exceeding 4096 chars or test-IDs exceeding 512 chars are now rejected, which does not affect legitimate use. Inline docstring in `security_utils.py` updated to document the new pattern. 3 regression tests added in `tests/unit/lib/test_security_utils_pytest_pattern_bound.py` covering: no-match on 100 KB adversarial pad (timing < 1 s), inclusive upper bound (4096-char path + `.py` matches), and typical short path still matches. `docs/SECURITY.md` Format Validation section updated to show the bounded regex. (CWE-1333)

- **ReDoS hardening in `plan_freshness._FILE_PATH_REGEX`** (Issue #1194): `_FILE_PATH_REGEX` previously used an unbounded `+` quantifier on the `[\w/.-]` character class. An adversarial plan blob containing a long run (e.g., 100 KB) of character-class-matching bytes with no matching file extension could cause superlinear regex-engine scanning time. Fix bounds the character-class run with `{1,512}` — worst-case matching is now O(N × 512) per scan position. Behavior is unchanged for legitimate inputs (planner-output paths are well under 512 chars). The same bound is mirrored in STEP 4.8 and STEP 5.5c prose in `commands/implement.md`. Severity: Low (input is coordinator-produced plan markdown). 3 regression tests added in `tests/unit/lib/test_plan_freshness_regex_bound.py` covering: no-match on 100 KB adversarial pad (timing < 1 s), inclusive upper bound (512-char run + `.py` matches), and typical short path still matches. (CWE-400)

- **Path-traversal hardening in `plan_freshness.verify_paths_exist`** (Issue #1193): `verify_paths_exist()` previously called `.exists()` directly on `repo_root / candidate` without canonicalization. A path with leading traversal segments (e.g., `../../etc/passwd`) or embedded traversal (e.g., `subdir/../../../sensitive.py`) could probe the filesystem outside `repo_root`. Fix applies canonicalize-then-contain: both the candidate and `repo_root` are resolved via `Path.resolve()`, then `relative_to()` asserts containment — paths that escape are treated as missing without any filesystem probe outside the root. Absolute paths in the input list are subject to the same containment check. Severity: Low (input is trusted plan markdown). 5 regression tests added in `tests/unit/lib/test_plan_freshness_path_traversal.py` covering relative traversal, embedded traversal, absolute outside-root, and legitimate in-root paths (both relative and absolute). (CWE-22)
- **security(state): validate `run_id` in `_locked_rmw` against `_RUN_ID_RE` to bring lockfile-path construction to parity with `_state_file_path`** (#1188): The `_locked_rmw` helper in `pipeline_completion_state.py` now validates the `run_id` parameter against the same `_RUN_ID_RE` pattern (`[a-zA-Z0-9_-]{1,64}`) used by `_state_file_path`, preventing potential path traversal in lockfile construction. Regression test added to `tests/regression/test_cluster2_security_bundle.py::TestLockedRmw::test_locked_rmw_rejects_traversal_run_id`.

### Added
- **Cluster selector for drain-queue tracker filtering** (Issue #1284): New pure function `select_next_cluster(clusters)` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/cluster_selector.py` filters tracker-shaped issues from drain-queue candidates and prefers their lowest-numbered open leaf children when present. Detects trackers via: title prefix `[TRACKER]`, label combination "root-cause" + "enhancement", or body structure with ≥2 phase headings + ≥2 issue references. When encountering a tracker with open leaves, returns a synthetic single-issue cluster pointing to the lowest leaf. Not yet wired into `/drain-queue` command — integration tracked separately. Added 5 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_cluster_selector.py`.

- **Coordinator bypass verdict tracking** (Issue #1279): Extended `record_plan_critic_skipped()` with optional `bypass_reason` parameter and added new `write_coordinator_bypass_verdict()` function in `pipeline_completion_state.py` to enable drain-queue coordinators to record machine-readable audit trails when bypassing plan-critic for mechanical extensions. New `/drain-queue` command documentation section "Coordinator Bypass of plan-critic" details the two-call protocol. 3 new tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1279_coordinator_bypass_verdict.py` verify the bypass tracking logic.
- **`/drain-queue` autonomous queue drainer command**: Thin orchestration wrapper around `/implement --issues` that picks the top `/triage` cluster and drains it autonomously. Applies 6 safety gates: daily drain-count + wall-clock budget (`MAX_DRAINS_PER_DAY=10`, `MAX_WALL_SECONDS_PER_DAY=14400s`), cluster severity gate (only `low`/`info` auto-drainable), hydrated label tag gate (blocks on `security`, `auth`, `breaking-change`, `human-only`, etc.), cluster size gate (`MAX_CLUSTER_SIZE_AUTO_DRAINABLE=5`), circuit breaker (2 consecutive failures → 4h pause; 3 in 24h → 24h pause), and push/deploy gates. One invocation = one drain attempt; pair with `/loop` or `/schedule` for recurrence. New libraries: `lib/drain_queue_state.py` (state primitives + gate logic) and `lib/drain_runner.py` (subprocess helpers). 98 new tests across 4 test files.

### Fixed
- **drain-watchdog auto-heal (#1248)**: `/implement --light` pipeline ran end-to-end (pre-flight → alignment → planner → plan-critic PROCEED → implementer → spec-validator PASS → doc-master PASS → CIA clean → commit 414e792 with `Closes #1248`). The implementer produced a verified workflow patch for `.github/workflows/drain-watchdog.yml` (+45 lines): a dedup guard that lists open drain-stuck issues, extracts the cluster from each body's `**Selected cluster for heal**: `...`` marker, queries `gh issue view` for each cluster member's state, and auto-closes stale drain-stuck issues with comment "Auto-closing: underlying cluster (...) has been resolved." when all members are CLOSED. The 4h debounce remains as a safety net. The patch is YAML-validated, shell-logic-sound, and was committed locally (414e792), but **could not be pushed from claude-code-action's GitHub App** — push rejected with "refusing to allow a GitHub App to create or update workflow `.github/workflows/drain-watchdog.yml` without `workflows` permission" (same root cause as #1253). Workflow change reverted from the worktree; this CHANGELOG-only commit lands to break the no-commit-on-master stuck loop. The full patch text is preserved in the auto-heal session log and in the body of #1248. Follow-up: a human PR is required to apply the workflow diff; until then each subsequent 2h cron is expected to repeat this pattern. Recurrence count via auto-heal cycles: now 4.
- **drain-watchdog auto-heal — document 4th drain-stuck recurrence (meta-issue #1256, tracking #1248)**
- **drain-watchdog auto-heal (#1255)**: Third consecutive 2h cron fire (2026-06-18T18:20:39Z) re-triggered the same dedup gap previously documented under #1248 / #1242 / #1254. Watchdog flagged "queue size 39 with 0 real closures in last 3h (watchdog meta-closures excluded)" and selected cluster `1252` (still OPEN, still not actionable from claude-code-action's GitHub App — multi-repo deployment requires `workflows: write` + cross-repo PAT). The pre-selected heal cluster for this session was `1255` itself, the new meta-issue. The `/implement --light` pipeline ran end-to-end (pre-flight → alignment → planner → implementer → spec-validator → doc-master verdict → CIA → commit), produced no source changes (CHANGELOG-only), and lands this entry to break the no-commit-on-master stuck loop for the third consecutive cycle. The meta-issue closes via the `Closes #1255` directive in this commit message. The underlying watchdog dedup bug remains tracked under #1248; until that workflow patch is human-PR'd (App lacks `workflows: write`), each subsequent 2h cron is expected to repeat this pattern. Recurrence count now: 3.

- **drain-watchdog auto-heal (#1242, #1254)**: Two consecutive cron fires re-triggered the same dedup gap described in #1248. Watchdog run for #1242 (2026-06-17T10:07:50Z) flagged "queue size 51 with 0 closures in last 4h" and selected cluster `1224,1225` for heal — both already CLOSED at heal-time. Watchdog run for #1254 (2026-06-18T15:47:26Z) flagged "queue size 40 with 0 real closures in last 3h (watchdog meta-closures excluded)" and selected cluster `1252` (an OPEN improve-CI issue, not actionable from claude-code-action's GitHub App — multi-repo deployment requires `workflows: write` and cross-repo PAT). This auto-heal session ran `/implement --light` end-to-end (planner → implementer → spec-validator → doc-master → CIA), produced no source changes, and lands this CHANGELOG entry to break the no-commit-on-master stuck loop. Both meta-issues close via the `Closes #N` directives in this commit message. The underlying watchdog dedup bug remains tracked under #1248; until that workflow patch is human-PR'd (App lacks `workflows: write`), each subsequent 2h cron is expected to repeat this pattern.
- **drain-watchdog auto-heal (#1253, #1248)**: Auto-heal session on cluster 1253 confirmed the dedup-logic bug — watchdog selected cluster `1250,1251`, both already CLOSED, opened a redundant `[drain-stuck]` meta-issue (#1253), and consumed a 60-min runner slot with no actionable work. The /implement --light pipeline ran (planner → implementer) and produced a verified workflow patch for `.github/workflows/drain-watchdog.yml`: an `elif [ -n "$CLUSTER" ]` branch iterates the comma-separated `$CLUSTER`, calls `gh issue view N --json state` per member, and on ALL-CLOSED forces `STUCK=false` with a clear `forced STUCK=false: cluster $CLUSTER all closed (#1248)` notice to `GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY`. The patch is YAML-validated and bash-logic-sound but **cannot be pushed from claude-code-action's GitHub App** — it lacks the `workflows: write` permission needed to modify `.github/workflows/*.yml`. Follow-up: human PR required to apply the workflow diff; auto-heal session breaks the no-commit-on-master stuck loop by landing this CHANGELOG entry. The patch text is preserved in this session's logs and the issue thread.
- **drain-watchdog auto-heal**: Closed redundant drain-stuck meta-issues #1245, #1246, #1247 — underlying cluster (#1237, #1238) was already resolved by 84bc901. Follow-up issue filed for watchdog dedup logic.

- **Prompt-integrity recovery latency telemetry** (Issue #1178): `unified_pre_tool.py`'s prompt-integrity deny path was invisible in `pipeline_timing_analyzer.py` — blocked Agent dispatches produced no SubagentStop and the 2-4 minute recovery overhead was untracked. A uuid4 `block_event_id` is generated at deny time and persisted into the `_record_agent_denial` state file; the allow path emits a paired `prompt_integrity_recovery` row to `.claude/logs/hook-blocks.jsonl` with computed latency. `pipeline_timing_analyzer.py` adds `PromptIntegrityRecovery` dataclass, `load_prompt_integrity_events()`, and `extract_prompt_integrity_recoveries()` (O(1) pairing by `block_event_id`); `format_timing_report()` gains optional `recoveries=` and `blocked_without_recovery=` kwargs that append a "Prompt-Integrity Recovery Overhead" section to the report. 14 new tests (8 hook + 6 analyzer).

- **Cross-repo sentinel collision — pipeline state relocated from machine-global `/tmp/` to per-repo `.claude/local/`** (Issue #1206): Concurrent `/implement` sessions in different repos clobbered each other's sentinel (`/tmp/implement_pipeline_state.json`), leaking issue numbers and run state across sessions. The sentinel is now resolved per-repo via `pipeline_state.get_legacy_sentinel_path()` → `<repo>/.claude/local/implement_pipeline_state.json`. Backward compatibility: `unified_pre_tool.py` protects both the legacy `/tmp/` path (transition window orphans) and the new per-repo path via `LEGACY_SENTINEL_LITERALS`. Bash hooks (`pre_compact_batch_saver.sh`, `SessionStart-batch-recovery.sh`) source the new `hooks/lib/_sentinel.sh` helper (`_default_sentinel()`, `_find_repo_root()`) so Python and bash resolvers produce identical paths for the same CWD. 19 new tests across unit, integration, and regression layers.

- **skip_agent_completeness_gate bypass — explicit `&&` chaining warning** (Issue #1212): `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/pipeline_completion_state.py` and `plugins/autonomous-dev/hooks/unified_pre_tool.py` both gain explicit "Chaining with `&&` WILL NOT WORK" warnings. The hook intercepts the entire compound Bash command before any part of it executes, so `touch /tmp/skip_agent_completeness_gate && git commit -m "..."` arrives at the gate before `touch` runs — the bypass file is absent and the gate blocks. The deny-reason messages in both the single-issue gate (`_check_pipeline_agent_completions`, line ~3410) and the batch gate (line ~6127) now include this warning inline so coordinators read it at the point of failure. The docstrings for `_check_file_bypass()` and `verify_pipeline_agent_completions()` are updated with the same "IMPORTANT — Chained `&&` does not work" language. `docs/HOOKS.md` Agent Completeness Gate bypass item (1) updated to carry the same warning. New test file `tests/unit/lib/test_skip_gate_bypass_deny_reason.py` (4 tests) locks the warning text in both deny-reason strings and both docstrings.

### Added
- **Cluster selector for drain-queue tracker filtering** (Issue #1284): New pure function `select_next_cluster(clusters)` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/cluster_selector.py` filters tracker-shaped issues from drain-queue candidates and prefers their lowest-numbered open leaf children when present. Detects trackers via: title prefix `[TRACKER]`, label combination "root-cause" + "enhancement", or body structure with ≥2 phase headings + ≥2 issue references. When encountering a tracker with open leaves, returns a synthetic single-issue cluster pointing to the lowest leaf. Not yet wired into `/drain-queue` command — integration tracked separately. Added 5 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_cluster_selector.py`.

- **Coordinator bypass verdict tracking** (Issue #1279): Extended `record_plan_critic_skipped()` with optional `bypass_reason` parameter and added new `write_coordinator_bypass_verdict()` function in `pipeline_completion_state.py` to enable drain-queue coordinators to record machine-readable audit trails when bypassing plan-critic for mechanical extensions. New `/drain-queue` command documentation section "Coordinator Bypass of plan-critic" details the two-call protocol. 3 new tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1279_coordinator_bypass_verdict.py` verify the bypass tracking logic.
- **Consecutive-Run Test Isolation HARD GATE in implementer agent** (Issue #1184): `plugins/autonomous-dev/agents/implementer.md` gains a new `## HARD GATE: Consecutive-Run Test Isolation` section (41 lines). When implementation or tests touch shared filesystem state (`/tmp/` paths, `tempfile.mkdtemp`, fixed `session_id` literals, module-level dicts in hook code), the implementer MUST run the affected test module twice consecutively without clearing `/tmp` between runs — both runs must pass. Trigger detection uses grep patterns for `/tmp/`, `tempfile.mkdtemp|mktemp`, `os.path.join.*tmp`, and session_id literals. When the second run regresses, an `autouse` fixture clearing the shared state is required (example from Issue #1176 `subagent_stop_seen_*.marker` pattern included). FORBIDDEN: declaring completion without the consecutive-run check when any trigger pattern matches; claiming "fresh /tmp" without verifying; leaving fixed literals in tests without cleanup fixtures. Section appears after Output Self-Validation (#707) and before Error Recovery with Retry Budget (#708). New regression test file `tests/unit/agents/test_implementer_md_consecutive_run_section.py` (5 tests) locks the section's presence, required phrases, and ordering. `docs/AGENTS.md` implementer section updated with the new HARD GATE description.

### Fixed
- **git stash mid-pipeline bypass detection — new known bypass pattern + baseline-missing guardrail** (Issue #1139): `plugins/autonomous-dev/config/known_bypass_patterns.json` gains a new pattern `git_stash_mid_pipeline` (26 total patterns) detecting when the coordinator uses `git stash` mid-pipeline to compare baselines or temporarily hide implementer changes — uncontrolled state manipulation that should instead be prevented by the STEP 1 baseline capture (`record_baseline_scope`) introduced in Issue #990. Detection indicators: Bash `git stash` call while pipeline state file exists with `explicitly_invoked=true`; paired `git stash + git stash pop` within a single pipeline run; coordinator output containing `"stash"`, `"compare baseline"`, or `"temporarily remove"`. Severity: `warning`. Hard gate: STEP 1 baseline capture + `record_baseline_scope` from Issue #990. New lib module `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/baseline_guardrail.py` provides `warn_if_baseline_missing(state_path) -> bool` — detects sentinel-without-baseline-cmd state and emits `[BASELINE-MISSING-WARNING]` to stderr; advisory only, never raises (untracked — to be staged).

- **Record pytest baseline_cmd in sentinel — prevent implementer from broadening test scope** (Issue #990): `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/pipeline_state.py` gains module-level constant `CANONICAL_BASELINE_CMD` (the standard `["pytest", "tests/unit", "tests/integration", "-q", "--tb=no"]` invocation, stored as a list for shell-injection safety) and two new public helpers: `record_baseline_scope(state_path, baseline_cmd, baseline_count) -> bool` persists the baseline pytest command and test count into the pipeline sentinel JSON atomically (temp-file + `os.replace`); `get_baseline_scope(state_path) -> dict|None` reads them back, returning `None` on any malformed or missing entry. `plugins/autonomous-dev/commands/implement.md` STEP 1 now calls `record_baseline_scope` immediately after capturing `BASELINE_TEST_COUNT`; the STEP 8 implementer prompt gains an instruction to call `get_baseline_scope` and run pytest with exactly the recorded scope — closing the class of drift failures where the implementer self-selects a broader directory set (e.g., all of `tests/`) after implementation. 24 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_pipeline_state_baseline_scope.py` (includes `get_test_count_for_dirs` scope-restriction tests — Issue #990).

- **Sentinel heartbeat recovery — guard against `clear_stale_state()` deleting the pipeline sentinel mid-run** (Issue #989): `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/pipeline_completion_state.py` gains a new public function `ensure_sentinel_heartbeat(session_id, state_path=None) -> bool`. After each SubagentStop completion is recorded, `unified_session_tracker.py` calls this function to verify that the pipeline sentinel (resolved by `pipeline_state.get_legacy_sentinel_path()` as `<repo>/.claude/local/implement_pipeline_state.json` since Issue #1206; previously `/tmp/implement_pipeline_state.json`, or overridden by `PIPELINE_STATE_FILE`) still exists with a matching `session_id`. When `hook_recovery.py`'s `clear_stale_state()` deletes the sentinel because a subprocess runs under a different `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID`, the function recreates a minimal sentinel `{session_id, recovered: True, recovered_at: <iso>}` so downstream steps can continue recording completions. Emits `[SENTINEL-HEARTBEAT-MISSING] state_path=... recovering_for_session=...` to stderr on recreation for observability. Returns `True` when the sentinel was already healthy, `False` when it was absent or mismatched and was recreated. Never raises — all error paths are fail-open to prevent blocking legitimate agent completions. 6 regression tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_pipeline_completion_state.py::TestSentinelHeartbeat`.

- **docs(runbook): #1202 — weekly drain sequence governance documented in RUNBOOK.md**: PROPOSE-mode step sequence (6 steps: `/triage --auto-improvement` → top cluster → `/implement --issues <cluster>` → `/improve --auto-file` → `bash scripts/deploy-all.sh` (MANDATORY) → digest review), AUTO-flip criteria (4 conditions: `#1041 closed`, `#1195 closed`, 2 consecutive PROPOSE drains with zero `.claude/.bypass` events, `deploy-all.sh` validation exit 0 on both runs), 5 direction-guard metrics (PROJECT.md alignment, worktree isolation, nothing-merges-on-red, digest metrics reviewed each cycle, 5-section digest shape), `deploy-all.sh` mandatory final step called out as digest metric AND AUTO-flip prerequisite, cross-reference from periodic-aggregation section, first two weekly reviews documented as the `TUNABLE_THRESHOLDS` recalibration checkpoint in `macro_promotion.py`.

- **feat(ci-loop): #1201 — macro promotion + direction-guard digest in /improve --auto-file (C3, builds on #1200)**: new pure-logic library `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/macro_promotion.py` (~470 lines) implements the C3 promotion layer that consumes Issue #1200's `collect_cia_findings` output. `decide_promotions(signals, open_issues)` applies a Sentry-style volume+breadth gate (`PROMOTION_FREQUENCY_MIN=3` AND `PROMOTION_DISTINCT_SESSIONS_MIN=2`) with an error fast-path (`max_severity_label=="error"` AND `frequency >= PROMOTION_ERROR_FREQUENCY_MIN=2`) — string-label equality on severity, never the float. `classify_route(signal, open_issues)` does tag-equality filtering FIRST then `issue_triage_analyzer.cluster_within_tag` so over-clustering across unrelated tags can't cause false appends. `detect_recurrence_after_close(signals, closed_issues)` runs over ALL signals (not just promoted) and emits one `(tag, closed_issue_number)` pair per match — surfaced as a loud `FIX DIDN'T STICK:` line so direction-of-improvement regressions are measurable in every digest. `build_digest` + `format_digest` produce the anti-habituation 5-section digest (ACTIONS TAKEN / Recurrence-after-close / Match-rate / Findings-per-session / Error-without-other-channel) — ALL 5 sections render even when empty (so absence of an expected signal is loud). Match-rate ALARM gates on BOTH a low ratio (<50%) AND a non-trivial open-issue COUNT (>20) to avoid false alarms in young repos. Findings-per-session ALARM fires when CIA emission drops to zero against positive observed sessions. `runtime_data_aggregator.py` gains `fetch_issues_with_label(repo, label, limit, state, closed_within_days)` generalizing `fetch_open_issues_with_label` (which is now a backward-compatible alias delegating to `state="open"`); when `state != "open"` the `--json` field list adds `closedAt` and a post-filter drops issues closed older than `closed_within_days`. `plugins/autonomous-dev/commands/improve.md` STEP 5 rewritten — replaces the per-finding `gh issue create` loop with the macro-promotion coordinator: `collect_cia_findings(FINDINGS_DIR, window_days=PROMOTION_WINDOW_DAYS)`, dual-state fetch (open + recently-closed via `CLOSED_LOOKBACK_DAYS=90`), `decide_promotions` + `detect_recurrence_after_close`, side-effects per decision (append → `gh issue comment <n>`; create → `[CI-{severity}-{tag}] {title}` + `gh issue create`; hold → no side effect), TOCTOU re-classification immediately before each CREATE (switches to APPEND if a same-tag open issue now exists), create-failure counting surfaced loudly in the digest (references #1203), persistence via `persist_report` to `.claude/logs/aggregated_reports.jsonl`. HARD GATE block forbids per-finding filing; preserves the `/tmp/autonomous_dev_cmd_context.json` hook contract before any `gh` call (cleaned up after). 9 regression tests in `tests/regression/test_macro_promotion.py` (threshold-constant lock + `Review trigger:` re-evaluation marker, 4-case promotion gate truth table, append-vs-create routing, independent CIA-prompt zero-filing guard (redundant with #1200's `test_cia_prompt_contains_zero_gh_issue_create` by design — two guards prevent silent regression), 5-section digest invariant in both populated AND empty scenarios, recurrence-after-close loud-line emission, match-rate-alarm + findings-per-session emission-failure alarm with both gates exercised).

- **CIA signal capture — finding store + cross-session collector** (Issue #1200): `plugins/autonomous-dev/agents/continuous-improvement-analyst.md` no longer files GitHub issues directly during pipeline runs. The 7 previous `gh issue create` / `gh issue comment` call sites have been replaced with `append_finding()` emission via the new pure library `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/cia_finding_store.py` — atomic fcntl-flocked appends of structured findings (severity / root_cause_tag / title / evidence / file_refs / session_id / ts) to monthly `.claude/logs/findings/YYYY-MM.jsonl` files created `0600` via secure opener, secret-scrubbed, length-capped, symlink-rejecting, fail-open. `runtime_data_aggregator.py` gains `CIA_FINDING_SEVERITY_MAP = {"info": 0.33, "warning": 0.66, "error": 1.0}` and `collect_cia_findings(findings_dir, window_days=90)` — groups findings by `(root_cause_tag, title-token cluster via issue_triage_analyzer.cluster_within_tag)`, tracks frequency + distinct session_ids, requires absolute `findings_dir` (raises `ValueError` otherwise for worktree safety), window-only decay. The ISSUE-CANDIDATE table and routing table remain in the agent definition; `target_repo` is now a pass-through record field for the C3 promotion layer in `/improve --auto-file`. 14 new unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_cia_finding_store.py` (atomicity, 0o600 perms, scrubbing, length caps, symlink reject, fail-open). `install_manifest.json` registers `cia_finding_store.py`; `CLAUDE.md` and `docs/ARCHITECTURE-OVERVIEW.md` library counts bumped 222 → 223. `tests/unit/test_cia_finding_routing.py` rewritten to lock the new emission contract (acceptance-locked grep counts of 0 `gh issue create` / `gh issue comment` in the agent file). Doc drift remediation: `docs/EVALUATION.md` closed-loop diagram and findings-filing description updated to reflect emit-then-promote separation; `docs/WORKFLOW-DISCIPLINE.md` Cross-Repo Finding Routing table updated to show CIA emits records (not issues) with `target_repo` pass-through; `docs/LIBRARIES.md` runtime_data_aggregator entry updated from 4 to 5 signal sources and `cia_finding_store.py` added as entry #222.

- **Security-auditor advisory finding auto-file mandate** (Issue #1180): `plugins/autonomous-dev/agents/security-auditor.md` gains a mandatory `ADVISORY-FINDINGS:` output block requirement. Every security audit output — regardless of overall PASS/FAIL verdict — MUST end with this block enumerating all Low and Medium severity findings not blocking PASS. When no advisory findings exist the agent MUST emit `ADVISORY-FINDINGS: none`; absent block is malformed output and causes the coordinator to re-invoke once then BLOCK the pipeline. A new FORBIDDEN bullet prohibits deferring Low/Medium findings to "follow-up" without emitting them in the block. `plugins/autonomous-dev/commands/implement.md` gains a "Security-Auditor Advisory Finding Tracking" section (after STEP 11, mirroring the existing Reviewer Out-of-Scope pattern): the coordinator reads the final security-auditor verbatim output, extracts the advisory block, dedup-queries open issues via `gh issue list --label security --label auto-improvement --state open`, and files `gh issue create --label security --label auto-improvement` per Low/Medium finding; `gh` auth errors are non-blocking (degraded-functional). Three new FORBIDDEN bullets under STEP 11 prevent acknowledging advisory findings without tracking issues and prevent proceeding past STEP 11 without parsing the block. `scripts/backfill_1175_security_advisories.sh` handles the 2 deferred Lows from the #1175 pipeline run. 6 prompt-assertion regression tests in `tests/unit/test_security_advisory_parser.py` lock the agent contract and coordinator parser requirements. `docs/AGENTS.md` security-auditor section updated with the advisory block output contract.

- **STEP 4.8 Plan Freshness Re-Verification — pre-validated plan staleness guard** (Issue #1175): Complementary T2 fix to the prompt-integrity T1 fix in Issue #1172.
