When STEP 4.7 detects a pre-validated plan whose mtime exceeds 86400 seconds (24 hours), the coordinator now runs `extract_referenced_paths()` + `verify_paths_exist()` from the new pure library `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/plan_freshness.py` to confirm every file path cited in the plan still exists on disk. On any missing paths the planner is re-invoked exactly once using `construct_revision_prompt()` (mirrors STEP 5.5b single-revision semantics) with a feedback message listing the missing paths; STEP 5.5c structural validation remains the final gate regardless. Activation is AND-gated (both `PRE_VALIDATED_PLAN_PATH` set AND mtime > 86400s) to avoid over-activating on fresh plans. The path-extraction regex mirrors STEP 5.5c (`[\w/.-]+\.(py|md|json|yaml|sh|ts|js)`) — documented inline to keep the two in sync. `docs/PIPELINE-MODES.md` updated with STEP 4.7 and STEP 4.8 entries in the Full Pipeline Sequence. `docs/LIBRARIES.md` updated with `plan_freshness.py` entry. `docs/ARCHITECTURE-OVERVIEW.md` library count updated to 222. 9 new unit and integration tests added in `tests/unit/commands/test_pre_validated_plan_detection.py` (net +9 over 5 pre-existing STEP 4.7 tests).

### Changed
- **plan-critic Assumption Audit axis — mandatory tool-use for factual-claim verification** (Issue #1172): `plugins/autonomous-dev/agents/plan-critic.md` gains a *Factual-claim verification (REQUIRED)* paragraph under the Assumption Audit axis. Plan-critic must now run at least one Grep/Glob/Read/Bash tool call to verify every factual claim in the plan (file existence, function existence, specific file content, line numbers, counts, prior-art search results) before scoring that axis; unverified factual claims cap the Assumption Audit score at 2 regardless of plausibility reasoning. The score-3 anchor cell in the calibration table is tightened to require verified factual claims with tool-call evidence, reserving score 3 for plans where all factual claims (file contents, line numbers, symbol existence, coverage, prior art) have been verified with at least one Grep/Read/Bash call, while plausibility assumptions about behavior or user intent remain reasoning-graded. 5 regression tests added in `tests/unit/test_plan_critic_prompt.py` covering: REQUIRED keyword presence in the axis block, factual-claim verification mention, score-2 cap for unverified claims, score-3 anchor requiring tool verification, and score-1 anchor grep-phrase preservation.
- **D6/D3 documentation congruence sync** (#1062 closed/superseded by #1075, #1075 umbrella, #1144 closed-with-correction, #1159 D3 implemented): Sync `plugins/autonomous-dev/docs/COMMANDS.md` to disk reality — header now claims 21 user-invocable slash commands (was "9"); single canonical Active Commands table; 13 previously-missing commands (`/advise`, `/audit`, `/autoresearch`, `/improve`, `/mem-search`, `/plan`, `/plan-to-issues`, `/refactor`, `/retrospective`, `/scaffold-genai-uat`, `/skill-eval`, `/sweep`, `/worktree`) added; phantom-command tables removed. `tests/unit/test_documentation_congruence.py`: renamed misleading `claude_md` fixture to `architecture_overview_md` (it reads `docs/ARCHITECTURE-OVERVIEW.md`); added `TestCommandsMdSync` with two regression tests (row-count match, every command documented); added `test_claude_md_component_count_drift` that invokes `scripts/validate_structure.py::check_component_count_drift()` against real CLAUDE.md — closes the per-commit-coverage gap acknowledged in #1144 (the counter functions and `check_component_count_drift()` were already present from #1140; the missing piece was per-commit pytest coverage, which this change adds).
- **Phase 2 — Classifier Robustness** (Issues #1146, #1153, #1154): three orthogonal robustness improvements to the `edit_tier_classifier.py` Bash-to-code-file detector and to `_check_write_pipeline_required`, without changing the gate's polarity, threat-model perimeter, or activation surface.
  1. **Sliding-window cumulative escalation (#1146)** — closes the multi-Edit splitting gap (emergent bypass via tool-call granularity mismatch). New `record_tier1_allow` / `get_recent_tier1_allows` / `clear_tier1_ring_buffer` API in `pipeline_completion_state.py` (per-`(session_id, file_path)` ring buffer, soft FIFO cap = 10, 60-second window). `_check_write_pipeline_required` accepts a new keyword-only `session_id` arg; when a Tier-1 (`fix`) classification's rolling cumulative-lines-added crosses `TIER_LIGHT_LINE_THRESHOLD` (20), the returned tier label is escalated from `fix` to `light` and the directive carries the marker `cumulative_sliding_window: existing_sum=N new=M`. The ring buffer is dropped after an escalation so a single threshold trigger does not keep firing. Backwards compatible: callers that omit `session_id` skip the sliding-window check (Phase 1 contract preserved).
  2. **Heredoc-strip false-positive fix (#1153)** — extracts the previously-private `_strip_heredoc_content` from `unified_pre_tool.py:2034` into a new shared module `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/heredoc_utils.py` (precedent: `hook_bypass.py` extraction). The regex is unified across THREE inline duplicate sites (the original function body + two literal regex duplicates at `_check_state_file_deletion` line 3705 and at `_check_bash_state_deletion` line 3802) and the new `detect_bash_code_file_write` call site. Heredoc bodies are now stripped before code-file detection patterns 1/2/3/4/6/7/8 run — so a `gh issue create --body-file <<HEREDOC_OUTER ... cat > /tmp/x.py <<HEREDOC_INNER ... HEREDOC_INNER ... HEREDOC_OUTER` payload no longer fires a false-positive code-file write block. Pattern 4 (`<<EOF > X.py`) deliberately scans the unstripped command since the redirect is on the heredoc OPENER line, not the body. The regex also gains a `[ \t]*` tolerance before the closing delimiter so the POSIX-style `<<-` indented-heredoc form is now correctly stripped.
  3. **Dynamic-heredoc chained-assignment resolution (#1154)** — new `_resolve_chained_assignments(command)` helper in `edit_tier_classifier.py` resolves `OUT=foo.py; cat > "$OUT"` and the newline-separator variant `OUT=foo.py\ncat > "$OUT"` so the existing Pattern 3 / Pattern 4 regexes see the literal target path. In-scope: `;` and `\n` separators; single-token literal RHS (bareword, single-quoted, or double-quoted without `$`/backtick); LHS dereference forms `"$NAME"`, `${NAME}`, `$NAME`. Out of scope and documented as residual evasion paths: command substitution `$(...)`, backticks, default-value `${VAR:-default}`, array expansion, concatenation. `OUT=$(echo foo).py; cat > "$OUT"` does NOT block — intentional acknowledged residual.

  No new env vars or config knobs. Per-repo opt-out (`.claude/.bypass`) and one-shot operator bypass (`touch /tmp/skip_write_pipeline_gate`) preserved. 24 regression tests added in `tests/unit/hooks/test_classifier_robustness.py` (7 sliding-window cases including TTL eviction + cross-file/session isolation + post-escalation reset; 7 heredoc false-positive cases including nested + indented + quoted-delim heredoc forms; 10 chained-assignment cases). Phase 1 regression suite (`test_write_pipeline_gate.py`, 20 tests) still passes. `docs/HOOKS.md` updated with "Heredoc-aware Bash detection" and "Sliding-window cumulative escalation" paragraphs in the Bash-to-code-file detection section.

- **Default-on tier-aware Write/Edit gate (Phase 1 polarity flip)** (Issue #1142+): `unified_pre_tool.py` `_check_write_pipeline_required` now blocks code-file edits by default, replacing the `.claude/.enforce` opt-in marker. The line-count "significant additions" heuristic is replaced by `classify_edit_tier()` (new module `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/edit_tier_classifier.py`) — Python AST diff produces 3 tiers (`fix` / `light` / `full`) each mapped to the matching `/implement` variant. AST edge cases (comment-only, formatting-only, import reordering, type-hint-only, docstring-only) all classify as `fix`. Non-Python code files return `light` as safe default. Adds Bash-to-code-file detection (`cat > X.py`, `cat >> X.py`, `sed -i X.py`, `tee X.py`, heredocs `<< 'EOF' > X.py`, `python -c "open(X.py,'w')..."`, base64-decoded heredocs, `awk '...' > X.py`); user-driven patch tooling (`git apply`, `patch < diff`) is excluded. Per-repo opt-out is now via the existing `.claude/.bypass` (durable when committed). One-shot operator bypass `touch /tmp/skip_write_pipeline_gate` preserved. **Deletions**: `_has_enforce_marker()` and `ENFORCE_FILE_RELATIVE` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/repo_detector.py`; `_enforce_marker_fn` module-level binding cascade and `_check_enforce_marker()` wrapper in `plugins/autonomous-dev/hooks/unified_pre_tool.py`; `TestEnforceMarker` test class in `tests/unit/lib/test_repo_detector.py`; AC1 and AC9 in `tests/unit/hooks/test_write_pipeline_gate.py`. **Cross-repo deletions (commit separately in those repos)**: `~/Dev/spektiv/.claude/.enforce`, `~/Dev/realign/.claude/.enforce`, `~/Dev/realign/.worktrees/batch-20260604-214210/.claude/.enforce`. Docs updated: `CLAUDE.md`, `README.md`, `docs/HOOKS.md`, `plugins/autonomous-dev/docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md`.

### Added (Issue #1142 — original, superseded by Phase 1 polarity flip)
- **Production-code Write/Edit gate for consumer repos** (Issue #1142): `unified_pre_tool.py` blocks non-trivial direct Write/Edit to production-code files. **Note**: as of Phase 1 (above) the gate is now default-ON subject to `.claude/.bypass` opt-out; the original `.claude/.enforce` opt-IN marker has been removed.

### Security
- **Cluster 2 security bundle — state-file permissions, atomic RMW, session-id sanitization, dead env-var removal** (Issues #1169, #1170, #1171, #1166, #1177): Five targeted hardening fixes applied to `pipeline_completion_state.py` and `unified_pre_tool.py`. (1) **#1169 — state file chmod 0o600**: `_write_state()` now calls `os.chmod(path, 0o600)` before taking `fcntl.LOCK_EX`, preventing pipeline state from being readable by other local users on shared dev machines (CWE-732); chmod failure is non-fatal and non-blocking. (2) **#1170 — atomic RMW for ring-buffer mutations**: new private helper `_locked_rmw(session_id, mutator, *, run_id=None)` wraps `record_tier1_allow()` and `clear_tier1_ring_buffer()` with an external sibling lockfile (`/tmp/pipeline_agent_completions_{key}.lock`, opened `"a+"` to prevent truncation), serializing the full read-modify-write across concurrent hook processes; the prior `fcntl.LOCK_EX` in `_write_state` only protected the write, leaving an unclosed TOCTOU window. (3) **#1171 — sanitized session-id reads via `_resolve_session_id_safe`**: the "read `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` env var → sanitize → fall back to module-level `_session_id`" pattern was duplicated at 8 sites in `unified_pre_tool.py`; consolidated into a single `_resolve_session_id_safe(input_session_id)` helper that sanitizes via `_sanitize_session_id()` and returns `None` for empty or `"unknown"` values — eliminates the risk of an unsanitized env-var value flowing into filesystem paths or HMAC computations. (4) **#1166 — module-level PCS cache**: the `importlib` lookup for `pipeline_completion_state` ring-buffer API is now cached at module level in `_PCS_MODULE_CACHE` / `_PCS_API_CACHE` / `_PCS_RESOLVED`; previously resolved on every `Write/Edit/Bash` gate invocation, measurable overhead in tight loops. (5) **#1177 — removed 2 dead `PIPELINE_MODE` env-var reads**: two `os.environ.get("PIPELINE_MODE")` reads in `unified_pre_tool.py` were unreachable; env vars do not propagate to hook subprocesses (Issue #779), so only the helper `_get_pipeline_mode_from_state()` (which checks the env var first per Issue #1173) is the effective read path; the dead reads carried 3 follow-up NOTE comments surfacing this hazard. 14 regression tests in `tests/regression/test_cluster2_security_bundle.py`.

- **`CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` added to `PROTECTED_ENV_PREFIX_EXCEPTIONS` — legitimate session propagation no longer blocked** (Issue #1137): `export CLAUDE_SESSION_ID=...` in Bash commands was erroneously blocked by the `CLAUDE_` prefix prompt-injection defense (Issue #606) introduced for privileged variables like `CLAUDE_AGENT_NAME`. `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` is a non-privileged framework correlation UUID (Claude Code-generated, not an auth token); tooling for baseline failure capture and activity-log scoping (Issue #904) requires propagating it into nested subshells. Fix: `PROTECTED_ENV_PREFIX_EXCEPTIONS: frozenset({"CLAUDE_SESSION_ID"})` added to `unified_pre_tool.py`, checked before the prefix loop so the exception takes effect without touching the prefix set. Compensating control: downstream sanitization via `_SAFE_SESSION_ID_RE` in `unified_prompt_validator.py` (Issue #1024) guards against log-attribution spoofing. Revocation condition documented inline: remove the exception if `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` ever gains auth or privilege-bearing semantics. `docs/HOOKS.md` updated with an exception callout. 6 regression tests added to `tests/unit/hooks/test_agent_identity_hardening.py` covering inline assignment, `export`, `env` command, `bash -c` propagation, and confirming other `CLAUDE_*` vars remain blocked.

### Fixed
- **Skill `rm` cleanup chained onto consuming commands — eliminates per-flow user permission prompts** (Issue #1204): Skills emitted standalone `rm` cleanup calls after their temp-file-consuming commands (e.g., `gh issue create ... ; <newline> rm -f /tmp/autonomous_dev_cmd_context.json`). Because the `rm` verb is never auto-allowed by Claude Code (destructive, always prompts), every planning/filing flow surfaced a permission prompt to the user (reported as friction on 2026-06-11: "writing and deleting plans keeps asking for permissions"). Fix: the trailing `rm` is now chained onto the consuming command via `;` (POSIX statement separator) so the single create-approval covers cleanup AND the cleanup runs even on failure paths. Applied to 4 in-scope skill files (`plugins/autonomous-dev/commands/create-issue.md`, `plan.md`, `plan-to-issues.md`, `improve.md`) plus 2 defense-in-depth files expanded into scope by the coordinator decision (same root cause across the skill surface): `refactor.md` and `retrospective.md`. The standalone-prior-write contract from Issue #1203 (`docs/HOOKS.md` "Prior-call ordering contract (#1203)") is preserved — only the trailing `rm` rides the consuming `gh issue create`; the context-file WRITE (the `python3 -c` heredoc that creates `/tmp/autonomous_dev_cmd_context.json`) remains a STANDALONE preceding Bash call so the PreToolUse hook sees the context on disk at evaluation time. Temp-file naming convention documented in each skill: `RUN_TS=$(date +%s%N)` unique nanosecond suffix for collision-safety across concurrent runs. Three error-exit paths in `create-issue.md` (research-failure exit at STEP 1 CHECKPOINT 1, issue-creation-failure exit at CHECKPOINT 2, user-cancel exit at STEP 3) keep their standalone `rm` because no preceding consuming Bash call exists to chain onto; each is now annotated with an inline comment marker `# unavoidable: no preceding Bash call to chain onto` documenting the carve-out. `improve.md` STEP 5 cleans up the hook-contract context file inside the python3 driver block via `Path("/tmp/autonomous_dev_cmd_context.json").unlink()` (the `gh issue create` / `gh issue comment` calls are `subprocess.call` invocations inside that same Bash tool call, so no separate Bash boundary is crossed). New regression test `tests/regression/test_issue_1204_no_standalone_rm_in_skills.py` (3 parametrized test functions × 6 files + 1 negative-case test = 13 assertions) scans the 6 in-scope skill files for standalone `rm` in ```bash blocks, fails on any match outside the exclusion-marker contexts (`WRONG:`, `DO NOT:`, `<details>`, `ANTI-PATTERN`, `unavoidable: no preceding`), and includes a co-occurrence guard rejecting any bash block that contains both `gh issue create` AND a standalone `rm` line (catches the `improve.md` `--trends` block pattern). Pre-fix the test fails 7 times across all 6 files; post-fix it passes 13/13. The full #1203 regression suite (140 tests across `tests/unit/hooks/test_gh_issue_create_runtime.py`, `tests/regression/test_gh_issue_create_regression.py`, `tests/unit/hooks/test_gh_issue_create_block.py`, `tests/unit/hooks/test_gh_issue_command_context.py`) still passes — the standalone-prior-write contract is intact.

- **gh-filing allowance four-defect fix — supersedes #987/#1095/#1199** (Issue #1203): four orthogonal defects caused `gh issue create` to be blocked despite whitelist membership. (1) **TOCTOU-inverse (primary)** — PreToolUse evaluates each Bash invocation BEFORE it runs, so the six command markdowns (plan, improve, plan-to-issues, refactor, retrospective, create-issue) that bundled "write `/tmp/autonomous_dev_cmd_context.json` && `gh issue create`" into ONE Bash call left the context absent at hook-evaluation time and the call was denied. All six markdowns split context-write into its own STANDALONE prior Bash tool call with an explicit FORBIDDEN note. (2) **`/plan` double-broken** — `'plan'` was missing from `GH_ISSUE_COMMANDS` AND plan.md STEP 6 wrote `command:'plan'` which `_is_issue_command_active` rejected; whitelisted `'plan'` (the minimal fix vs rerouting). (3) **bypass-detector false-positive** — `_contains_gh_issue_create_bypass` backtick / `$()` regex scanned the RAW command and matched backtick-quoted prose inside `--body` argument VALUES, blocking legitimate `gh issue comment` calls (live-confirmed). Made argv-aware via `shlex.split` (mirrors `_detect_git_bypass` precedent) — strips `--body` / `--title` / `--body-file` / `-m` / `--message` value tokens (separate-value and `--body=VALUE` attached forms), rejoins, then runs the regex; on `shlex.ValueError` falls back to raw-regex behavior (preserves blocking on malformed shell). True bypass forms (subprocess wrappers, `sh -c`, `bash -c`, backtick / `$()` at command position) remain blocked. (4) **dead marker READ allow-through** — `GH_ISSUE_MARKER_PATH` had a 1-hour READ allow-through that nothing has written since #627 (the WRITE has been blocked by `_detect_gh_issue_marker_creation`); removed the READ as dead code while KEEPING the constant and the WRITE blocker as defense-in-depth. Added env-var override `GH_ISSUE_CMD_CONTEXT_PATH` for runtime subprocess test isolation (mirrors `PIPELINE_STATE_FILE` precedent). 13 new subprocess runtime tests in `tests/unit/hooks/test_gh_issue_create_runtime.py` invoke the real hook as a subprocess and assert on its stdout decision JSON (allow-after-prior-write contract, deny-bare/unknown/TTL-expired/empty-context, bundled-same-call denied, gh-issue-comment-with-backticked-body allowed, true bypasses still denied). 14 new unit tests in `test_gh_issue_create_block.py` (TestPlanCommandAllowed + TestBypassFalsePositive) and 1 in `test_gh_issue_command_context.py` (test_returns_true_for_plan_command). Existing marker-allow tests converted to assert presence has NO effect (`test_marker_presence_no_longer_grants_allow`) plus a new `test_marker_write_still_blocked` regression. `docs/research/GH_ISSUE_MARKER_BYPASS_FIX.md` rewritten as the consolidated #1203 root-cause doc. Out-of-scope cross-session `/tmp` state leak (concurrent-session pipeline-sentinel keeping `_is_pipeline_active()` fresh) documented and tracked as #1206.

- **#1201 remediation — TOCTOU pivot symmetry + `distinct_sessions_observed` lower-bound fix in `/improve --auto-file` STEP 5**: (1) The APPEND branch now re-classifies immediately before acting; if the originally-matched open issue was closed in the window between batch-fetch and action, the coordinator pivots to CREATE rather than commenting on a stale closed issue. This completes the TOCTOU symmetry: CREATE already re-classified to APPEND when a new open issue appeared; APPEND now re-classifies to CREATE when the matched issue disappears. (2) `distinct_sessions_observed` in the digest's Findings-per-session section uses `max()` over per-signal distinct_session counts instead of `sum()`, avoiding double-counting sessions that appear in multiple clusters; the `max()` is a documented lower-bound approximation (truthfully conservative) that still correctly triggers the emission-failure alarm when the numerator is zero. 4 unit tests added to `TestFetchIssuesWithLabel` in `tests/unit/lib/test_runtime_data_aggregator.py` covering `state="closed"` filtering, `closed_within_days` cutoff, fail-open on null/missing/malformed `closedAt`, and alias delegation. (#1201)

- **Root-cause fix for SubagentStop double-fire** (Issue #1183, follow-up to #1176): `templates/settings.local.json` previously registered hooks for 9 lifecycle events (UserPromptSubmit, PreToolUse, Stop, PostToolUse, SubagentStop, TaskCompleted, PreCompact, PostCompact), duplicating what `sync_settings_hooks.py` writes to `settings.json`. Both files are project-tier — Claude Code merges them, causing every hook to fire twice. The template's `hooks` block is now `{}` (with a `_comment` field documenting why). Hooks live exclusively in `settings.json`. `strip_duplicate_hooks.py` gains an `--audit <project_root>` mode (read-only, JSON output, exit 1 when duplicates found) that reads both `settings.json` and `settings.local.json` via the reused `extract_hook_refs()` primitive in `settings_merger.py` and reports the intersection of hook basenames. The audit is wired into `scripts/deploy-all.sh` `validate_local()` as check #12 — every deploy now hard-fails on duplicate registrations. `sync_settings_hooks.py` emits a new `stale_local_warnings` field (sorted list of hook basenames present in both the canonical template hooks and an existing `settings.local.json`) and appends a `WARNING: settings.local.json still registers N duplicate hooks` clause to `result["message"]` so upgrades surface the pre-fix state immediately. The in-handler dedup guard from #1176 is preserved as defense-in-depth (the runtime catches misconfigured deployments; the audit and template fix prevent the misconfiguration in the first place). 5 regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1183_no_duplicate_hook_registrations.py` covering AC1 (empty hooks block in template), AC2/AC5 (audit detects duplicates + non-zero exit), AC3 (zero duplicates on fresh deploy), AC4 (dedup guard preserved), AC6 (sync emits stale_local_warnings).

- **SubagentStop duplicate-fire dedup guard in `unified_session_tracker.py`** (Issue #1176): SubagentStop was firing twice per agent due to dual hook registration in both `~/.claude/settings.json` and `.claude/settings.json`. Duplicate firings polluted JSONL activity logs with doubled entries, double-counted agent durations, and triggered downstream consumers (ghost-agent detection, pipeline completion state, plan-critic stage advance) twice. Fix: in-handler file-backed dedup guard using `os.open(O_CREAT|O_EXCL)` on a `/tmp/subagent_stop_seen_<sha256[:16]>.marker` file keyed by `sha256(agent_transcript_path)` (falling back to `sha256(session_id:agent_name)` when the transcript path is empty). Only the first caller successfully claims the marker; the duplicate sees `FileExistsError`, writes a `__dedup_skip__:{agent_name}` JSONL entry for debuggability, and returns 0. Markers expire after 300 seconds with background sweep gated to at most once per 60 seconds. Fails open on unexpected errors to prevent legitimate firings from being dropped. Pattern mirrors `lib/subagent_invocation_cache.py`. Root-cause fix (template audit to remove the duplicate registration) is deferred to a follow-up issue. 5 new tests in `tests/unit/hooks/test_unified_session_tracker.py` with module-level autouse fixture for `/tmp` marker isolation.

- **`spec-validator` registered in `pipeline_intent_validator.py` STEP_ORDER and SEQUENTIAL_REQUIRED — closes audit D4** (Issue #1158): `spec-validator` was invoked at STEP 8.5 by the `/implement` coordinator but was absent from `STEP_ORDER` in `pipeline_intent_validator.py`, causing the intent-validator to treat it as an unknown agent and skip ordering enforcement. Fix: `"spec-validator": 5.7` added to `STEP_ORDER` (between pytest-gate at 5.5 and reviewer at 6.0); three `SEQUENTIAL_REQUIRED` pairs added — `(implementer, spec-validator)`, `(pytest-gate, spec-validator)`, `(spec-validator, reviewer)` — enforcing that spec-validator runs after tests pass and before human-judgment review. 1 regression test in `tests/unit/lib/test_pipeline_intent_validator.py` (`test_spec_validator_registered_in_step_order_and_sequential_pairs`). `docs/LIBRARIES.md` updated with spec-validator entries for both `pipeline_intent_validator.py` and `agent_ordering_gate.py`.

- **Post-Dispatch Completion Recording Protocol — coordinator synchronously records `record_agent_completion()` after every foreground Agent dispatch** (Issue #1174): `commands/implement.md` (line 174) and `commands/implement-batch.md` (line 672) gain a mandatory "Post-Dispatch Completion Recording Protocol" section. After every Agent tool returns, the coordinator MUST call `record_agent_completion()` from `pipeline_completion_state` synchronously before the next pre-dispatch ordering check. This closes a race widened from Issue #852: SubagentStop fires asynchronously, while the next pre-dispatch ordering gate reads state synchronously — the gate would see the just-returned foreground agent as "not yet run" and fail, causing manual `record_agent_completion()` injections scattered across coordinator transcripts as a symptom. Recording at the call site makes the coordinator state-consistent at all times regardless of SubagentStop timing. Exception clause preserved: doc-master sites covered by Issue #852's `record_doc_verdict` + `record_agent_completion` blocks are not double-called. `pipeline_completion_state.record_agent_completion` is idempotent (fcntl-locked, tri-scope, last-write-wins per Issue #1046). `docs/ARCHITECTURE-OVERVIEW.md` updated to document the three completion-write paths for `pipeline_completion_state.py`. 5 regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1174_post_dispatch_recording_protocol.py`.

- **`_get_pipeline_mode_from_state()` — PIPELINE_MODE env-var precedence and mtime-TTL fallback close stale-state leakage** (Issue #1173): `unified_pre_tool._get_pipeline_mode_from_state()` previously read the mode exclusively from `/tmp/implement_pipeline_state.json`, creating two gaps: (1) `PIPELINE_MODE` env-var was honored at only one call-site (line 1108), while all other call-sites went through this function and silently ignored it (Issue #849 call-site asymmetry — e.g., exporting `PIPELINE_MODE=light` from a `--light` invocation didn't prevent the ordering gate from reading a stale `mode=fix` state file); (2) when `session_id='unknown'` (indeterminate boot-time race), `_is_stale_session()` returned `False` and a stale `--fix` state file would leak its mode into a fresh `--light` run, forcing a reviewer agent that `--light` deliberately omits. Fix: function now (a) checks `PIPELINE_MODE` env-var first and returns immediately when set, eliminating the call-site asymmetry; (b) applies an mtime-TTL check (`_PIPELINE_STATE_TTL_SECONDS = 1800`, the same 30-minute constant extracted from `_is_pipeline_active()`) after the session-staleness guard, returning `"full"` for state files whose mtime exceeds the TTL regardless of `session_id`. `_PIPELINE_STATE_TTL_SECONDS` module-level constant centralizes the 30-minute threshold previously hardcoded as a magic number in `_is_pipeline_active()`. 2 regression tests added to `TestGetPipelineModeFromStateStaleness` in `tests/unit/hooks/test_stale_session_state_readers.py`: `test_pipeline_mode_mtime_ttl_returns_full_for_old_state` (state 1801s old with `session_id='unknown'` must return `"full"`) and `test_pipeline_mode_env_var_overrides_stale_state` (fresh state with `mode='fix'` overridden by `PIPELINE_MODE=light` must return `"light"`).

- **`get_changed_files()` `git diff HEAD~1` fallback — smart routing no longer returns `full_suite: True` on committed-but-empty working trees** (Issue #1136): When the implementer commits all changes and the coordinator's cwd differs from the worktree, `git diff --name-only HEAD` and `git ls-files --others` both return empty, causing `route_tests()` to fall back to a full suite run with `changed_files: []`. `get_changed_files()` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/test_routing.py` now attempts `git diff --name-only HEAD~1` as a tertiary fallback when the primary and untracked queries both return empty; the fallback is skipped gracefully if the repo has no prior commit or git is unavailable. 4 regression tests added to `tests/unit/lib/test_test_routing.py` covering: fallback fires when HEAD diff is empty, fallback skipped when HEAD diff is non-empty, graceful empty return when HEAD~1 is also unavailable, and correct `route_tests()` categorization when the fallback resolves the changed file list.

- **Pre-validated plan content now seeded to planner at STEP 5** (Issue #1138): `commands/implement.md` STEP 5.5a correctly detected pre-validated plans (after Issue #1135) but the planner had already run from scratch before detection occurred. New STEP 4.7 "Pre-Validated Plan Detection (inline)" searches `.claude/plans/` before the planner is dispatched. When a PROCEED-verdict plan is found, its path and content are stored in coordinator state (`PRE_VALIDATED_PLAN_PATH`, `PRE_VALIDATED_PLAN_CONTENT`) and injected into the STEP 5 planner prompt with a "refine, DO NOT re-scope" directive. Planner continues to be invoked unconditionally (Option A); skipping the planner entirely is deferred. Regression test in `tests/unit/commands/test_pre_validated_plan_detection.py`.

- **Pre-validated plan skip gate now matches both verdict formats** (Issue #1135): `commands/implement.md` STEP 5.5a previously checked only for `"Verdict: PROCEED"` (prose/header format), but `plan-critic` accumulates Critique History table rows using `**PROCEED**` (bold markdown cell). Plans with table-format verdicts were never recognized as pre-validated. Gate description updated to accept either format. Regression test added. `docs/ARCHITECTURE-OVERVIEW.md` STEP 5.5 description updated to document both accepted verdict formats and corrected budget-mode axis count from 3 to 4 (Operational Integration Test was added in Issue #1067 but the architecture overview was not updated at that time).

### Added
- **Cluster selector for drain-queue tracker filtering** (Issue #1284): New pure function `select_next_cluster(clusters)` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/cluster_selector.py` filters tracker-shaped issues from drain-queue candidates and prefers their lowest-numbered open leaf children when present. Detects trackers via: title prefix `[TRACKER]`, label combination "root-cause" + "enhancement", or body structure with ≥2 phase headings + ≥2 issue references. When encountering a tracker with open leaves, returns a synthetic single-issue cluster pointing to the lowest leaf. Not yet wired into `/drain-queue` command — integration tracked separately. Added 5 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_cluster_selector.py`.

- **Coordinator bypass verdict tracking** (Issue #1279): Extended `record_plan_critic_skipped()` with optional `bypass_reason` parameter and added new `write_coordinator_bypass_verdict()` function in `pipeline_completion_state.py` to enable drain-queue coordinators to record machine-readable audit trails when bypassing plan-critic for mechanical extensions. New `/drain-queue` command documentation section "Coordinator Bypass of plan-critic" details the two-call protocol. 3 new tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1279_coordinator_bypass_verdict.py` verify the bypass tracking logic.
- **`scripts/cleanup-worktrees.sh` — maintainer recovery tool for stale worktree accumulation** (Issue #1130): Standalone Bash script that removes entries under `.worktrees/` whose branch is already merged into `master`. Skips the main worktree, unmerged branches, and detached HEADs that are not reachable from `master`. Supports `--dry-run` (shows `removed=N skipped=M pruned=P` summary without modifying anything) and `-h|--help`. Intended for manual use after many `/implement --batch` or `/implement --issues` sessions leave behind merged worktrees. `docs/SCRIPTS.md` gains a `## Worktree Maintenance` section documenting the script. `plugins/autonomous-dev/docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md` gains a `## Stale worktrees` entry distinguishing this symptom from the worktree-gitignore deadlock (Issue #1133). 8 integration tests in `tests/integration/test_cleanup_worktrees.py` covering: help flag, merged-branch removal, unmerged-branch skip, main-worktree skip, reachable detached removal, unreachable detached skip, dry-run no-op, and non-git-repo exit-1.

- **`check_component_count_drift()` added to `scripts/validate_structure.py` — component count mismatch is now a pre-commit BLOCK** (Issue #1140): The canonical `"Component counts:"` summary line in `CLAUDE.md` is now verified against live filesystem counts at pre-commit time. Previously, count drift was caught only by reviewer agents as a WARNING (not enforced). The new check parses the prose summary via regex, counts agents (`.md` files flat), skills (subdirs with `SKILL.md` sentinel, excluding archived), commands (`.md` files flat), hooks (`.py` files flat, excluding `__init__.py`), and libraries (recursive `.py` files, excluding `__pycache__`, `htmlcov`, `__init__.py`), and emits one error per diverged component. `main()` now calls `check_component_count_drift()` alongside the existing structure checks. `CLAUDE.md` counts corrected to reflect live state: 16 agents, 20 skills, 24 commands, 25 hooks, 219 libraries. `README.md` and `docs/ARCHITECTURE-OVERVIEW.md` updated to match. 4 unit tests added to `tests/unit/scripts/test_validate_structure.py` covering: pass when counts match, error-per-component when drifted, missing summary line, and `main()` inclusion of the new check.

- **`feat(implement): #1133 --no-worktree modifier for --batch/--issues`** — process issue clusters serially in-place when worktrees are unusable (gitignored `.claude/*`). When `--no-worktree` is added to `/implement --batch` or `/implement --issues`, the batch skips `git worktree add` and runs each issue serially on the current branch, producing one commit per issue and a single multi-issue PR. Signaled via `BATCH_NO_WORKTREE=1` env var (parallels `BATCH_AUTO_APPROVE`); `unified_pre_tool._is_batch_context(cwd)` centralizes the worktree-cwd OR env-var check so all 3 batch hook gates (CIA, doc-master, agent-completeness) keep firing. New helpers: `batch_orchestrator.run_batch_no_worktree_mode(flags)`, `batch_git_finalize.batch_git_finalize(..., mode="no_worktree")`, `batch_git_finalize.open_cluster_pr(issue_numbers, issue_titles, base_branch)`. `scripts/triage-and-implement.sh` now uses cluster mode by default (`--no-cluster` preserves the legacy per-issue loop). `scripts/drain-all.sh --cluster-mode` invokes the top triage cluster as a single `/implement --issues ... --no-worktree` call. Manual perf-smoke procedure documented in `docs/RUNBOOK.md` (≥40% wall-clock reduction target). 12 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_batch_orchestrator_no_worktree.py`, 8 unit tests in `tests/unit/hooks/test_no_worktree_batch_gate.py`, 5 integration tests in `tests/integration/test_no_worktree_cluster_3_issues.py`. Solves the worktree+gitignore deadlock for autonomous-dev self-maintenance.

### Changed
- **`docs/HOOKS.md` Project Detection Guard updated** — the "Project Detection Guard" section now accurately describes the two-signal detection introduced by `.claude/.enforce` support: `is_autonomous_dev_repo()` returns `True` for both the autonomous-dev source repo (plugin marker) and consumer repos opting in via `.claude/.enforce` marker; unmanaged repos still get immediate allow. The `matched_via` audit field (`"plugin_marker"` | `"enforce_marker"` | `"none"`) is documented. Previously the section only described the plugin-marker path.

### Added
- **Cluster selector for drain-queue tracker filtering** (Issue #1284): New pure function `select_next_cluster(clusters)` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/cluster_selector.py` filters tracker-shaped issues from drain-queue candidates and prefers their lowest-numbered open leaf children when present. Detects trackers via: title prefix `[TRACKER]`, label combination "root-cause" + "enhancement", or body structure with ≥2 phase headings + ≥2 issue references. When encountering a tracker with open leaves, returns a synthetic single-issue cluster pointing to the lowest leaf. Not yet wired into `/drain-queue` command — integration tracked separately. Added 5 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_cluster_selector.py`.

- **Coordinator bypass verdict tracking** (Issue #1279): Extended `record_plan_critic_skipped()` with optional `bypass_reason` parameter and added new `write_coordinator_bypass_verdict()` function in `pipeline_completion_state.py` to enable drain-queue coordinators to record machine-readable audit trails when bypassing plan-critic for mechanical extensions. New `/drain-queue` command documentation section "Coordinator Bypass of plan-critic" details the two-call protocol. 3 new tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1279_coordinator_bypass_verdict.py` verify the bypass tracking logic.
- **Cross-repo enforcement via `.claude/.enforce` opt-in marker** — consumer repos (spektiv, realign, etc.) can now opt into autonomous-dev's SDLC enforcement gates by creating `.claude/.enforce` and committing it. Symmetric to the existing `.claude/.bypass` opt-out (Issue #969). Extension is in `lib/repo_detector.is_autonomous_dev_repo()` with a new `_has_enforce_marker()` private helper (mirrors `hook_bypass._flag_file_in_chain` — 30-level bounded walk, `OSError`/`PermissionError` guard, filesystem-root termination). The `matched_via` audit field (`"plugin_marker"` | `"enforce_marker"` | `"none"`) records which signal fired for debugging. Zero hook call-site changes — the function extension propagates automatically to all consumers (`enforce_tdd.py`, `stop_quality_gate.py`, `plan_mode_exit_detector.py`, `get_repo_context()`). Intentional asymmetry preserved: the inlined `_is_autonomous_dev_repo` at `unified_pre_tool.py:1087` is NOT updated — it uses a different marker (`.claude/commands/implement.md`) for protected-infrastructure detection; consumer repos have no protected files anyway. 8 new unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_repo_detector.py`.

- **`enforce_file_organization.py` — live PreToolUse Write/Edit guard for repo-root file placement** (Issue #1034): Revives file organization enforcement as a deterministic PreToolUse hook (Write/Edit tools). Blocks file creation at the repo root when the basename is not on the allow-list. Allow-list sources: built-in hardcoded names (README.md, CHANGELOG.md, CLAUDE.md, pyproject.toml, Dockerfile, Makefile, etc.), all hidden files (`.`-prefixed), all top-level config extensions (`.json`, `.yaml`, `.toml`, `.cfg`, `.ini`, `.lock`), plus `plugins/autonomous-dev/templates/project-structure.json` (`Root directory > allowed_files`) falling back to `.claude/templates/project-structure.json` for installed-only repos. Block message includes a REQUIRED NEXT ACTION directive and a suggested destination folder derived from the file extension (`.py`/`.sh` → `scripts/`, `.md` → `docs/`, `.log`/`.jsonl` → `logs/`, `test_*.py` → `tests/unit/`). Uses `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` for worktree-safe root detection. Escape hatch: `AUTONOMOUS_DEV_BYPASS=1` or `.claude/.bypass` marker (universal bypass). Standalone hook — not wired into `unified_pre_tool.py` (mirrors `plan_gate.py` registration pattern); stdlib-only, no GenAI dependency, no `--fix` mode, no CLI surface; fails open. Replaces the archived GenAI-based `enforce_file_organization.py`. 26 tests: 22 unit in `tests/unit/hooks/test_enforce_file_organization.py`, 4 subprocess regression in `tests/regression/test_enforce_file_organization_regression.py`.

- **Mode-gating enforce-mode runbook in `docs/INTENT-CLASSIFICATION.md`** (Issue #1078): New top-level `## Rollout to Enforce Mode` section documents the operational runbook for flipping `INTENT_CLASSIFIER_ENFORCE` from `false` to `true`. Section contains six subsections: `### Phase Prerequisites (Gate Documentation)` (names Phase A Issue #1076 and Phase B Issue #1077 with their completion criteria — Phase A counting query explicitly excludes synthetic `phase-e-test-*` rows); `### Cost-of-FN Derivation` (enumerates the 6 hard-floor entries from `plugins/autonomous-dev/config/hard_floor_hooks.json`, states the 5% acceptable FN rate, and explains the no-chain-reaction property — only 4 hooks consult `mode_skip`); `### Conditional Flip Rule` (4 criteria: `observed_fn_rate < 5%`, `ci_high < 10%`, no hard-floor bypass observed, audit checklist R6 passes); `### Hard-Floor Coverage Audit Checklist` (4-step audit referencing `plugins/autonomous-dev/config/hard_floor_hooks.json` by name, with a concrete `for fn in ...; do grep -c ...; done` shell verification); `### Reversibility Procedure` (concrete `unset INTENT_CLASSIFIER_ENFORCE` + restart + `tail | grep INTENT` verification — documents that env vars are process-level and changes require restarting the Claude Code session); `### Rollback Procedure` (concrete `export INTENT_CLASSIFIER_ENFORCE=false` + restart + `gh issue create` template for catastrophic leak post-flip). Documentation-only change — no code modified. Every step is reviewer-independent — no "ask the plan author" or "see plan" references.

- **`/align --content` mode — content allocation audit for CLAUDE.md, PROJECT.md, and MEMORY.md** (Issue #1123): New sub-flag on the existing `/align` command. Runs a 4-phase workflow (AUDIT → PROPOSE → APPROVE → EXECUTE) to enforce the one-topic-one-home pattern: measures each auto-loaded file against size budgets (CLAUDE.md ≤200 lines, PROJECT.md ≤200 lines, MEMORY.md ≤200 lines), detects duplicated headings and misrouted content, proposes moves or pointer compressions, and applies approved changes. `--dry-run` emits the plan without writing; `--auto` accepts low-risk proposals (compressions, deletions of stubs below 500 bytes) without prompting — cross-tier moves still require explicit approval. New `skills/content-allocation/SKILL.md` packages the methodology (routing table, 9-step decision algorithm, size budgets, periodic hygiene sweep, repo-type adaptation guidance) and is loaded when `/align --content` runs. Two new templates ship via the install: `templates/CONTENT_ALLOCATION.md` (drop-in routing-table for consuming repos) and `templates/CLAUDE.md.template` (starter CLAUDE.md scaffold that references the content-allocation pattern). `templates/claude_md_section.md` gains a 3-line propagation snippet so consuming repos' CLAUDE.md learns about `/align --content` at install time. Version bumped to 3.51.0. 14 integration tests in `tests/integration/test_align_content_mode.py`.

### Changed
- **`validate_claude_md_size.py` extended to guard PROJECT.md and MEMORY.md size thresholds** (Issue #1121): The hook previously only warned when `CLAUDE.md` exceeded 200 lines. It now also warns when `.claude/PROJECT.md` exceeds 150 lines (content-allocation target documented in `docs/development/CONTENT_ALLOCATION.md`) and when `~/.claude/projects/<slug>/memory/MEMORY.md` exceeds 200 lines (Anthropic's auto-load threshold). New module-level constants `MAX_PROJECT_LINES=150` and `MAX_MEMORY_LINES=200`; new functions `derive_memory_path()`, `check_project_md_size(repo_root)`, and `check_memory_md_size()`. Each check runs independently — a missing or unreadable file is silently skipped, and a failure in one check does not block the others. The slug for MEMORY.md path derivation is the absolute CWD with `/` replaced by `-`. Hook remains non-blocking — always exits 0. 16 new tests in `tests/unit/hooks/test_claude_md_size_guard.py`.

### Fixed
- **`already_done_detector` pickaxe fallback incorrectly returned `MatchResult(classification="mention")` causing false-positive batch skips** (Issue #1131): `check_issue_already_implemented()` pickaxe fallback at line 337 returned `MatchResult(classification="mention")` despite the function docstring promising that only `"closes"` classifications ever reach the caller. Any issue whose title contained a token present in a prior commit (even unrelated commits) would silently skip that issue in `/implement --issues` batches — 38 false-positive skips observed in production. Fix: the pickaxe fallback now returns `None`, honouring the existing docstring contract. The strategy docstring comment (Step 6) updated to remove the now-incorrect `(returns classification "mention")` parenthetical. 1 new regression test (`test_pickaxe_only_match_returns_none_per_docstring_contract`) in `tests/unit/lib/test_already_done_detector.py` plus 1 existing test tightened to assert `result is None` unconditionally.
- **CC 2.1.77+ compound-bash auto-approve breakage — per-prefix Bash allow entries added to granular template, `/setup` warns on affected settings** (Issue #895): As of CC 2.1.77, compound commands (`git status && echo done`) are split at separators before permission matching; each subcommand must individually match an allow entry. `plugins/autonomous-dev/templates/settings.granular-bash.json` now includes per-prefix `Bash(<tool>:*)` allow entries for `mkdir`, `git`, `gh`, `pip`, `pkill`, `test`, and `rm` — the seven pipeline-required prefixes that were previously missing. `/setup` Step 1.7 detects CC ≥2.1.77 at setup time and warns when the global `~/.claude/settings.json` has a bare `Bash` allow entry but no per-prefix entries, directing the user to merge the granular template or run `bash scripts/deploy-all.sh --global-settings`. `plugins/autonomous-dev/docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md` gains a "Permission prompts after upgrading Claude Code (Issue #895)" section documenting both the v2.1.77 compound-bash change and the v2.1.101 deny-precedence hardening, the recommended migration path, and a note on why the broad `Bash(*|*sh*)` deny patterns in `settings.default.json` are retained intentionally. `settings.default.json` deny patterns were NOT modified to preserve no-space `curl x|sh` coverage. 4 regression tests in `tests/regression/test_cc_2177_permission_migration.py`.

- **`construct_revision_prompt()` — coordinator revision/remediation re-invocations no longer blocked by prompt-integrity shrinkage detector** (Issue #1116): New helper `construct_revision_prompt(agent_type, baseline_context, feedback)` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/prompt_integrity.py` fixes a recurring CI gap where every plan-critic REVISE and every reviewer/security-auditor BLOCKING re-invocation cost an extra blocked attempt. The coordinator was passing ONLY the new feedback to the second invocation; the prompt-integrity hook detected this as >20% compression vs. the baseline word count and blocked it. The helper combines the full original baseline context with the feedback under a `## REVISION FEEDBACK` header, guaranteeing the combined prompt's word count is `>= len(baseline_context.split())` and thus always passes the shrinkage check. Mandatory use is enforced in `commands/implement.md` via three directives: (1) COORDINATOR FORBIDDEN LIST — constructing revision/remediation prompts without calling `construct_revision_prompt()` is now explicitly FORBIDDEN; (2) STEP 5.5b — plan-critic REVISE path MUST use the helper to construct the planner re-invocation prompt; (3) STEP 11 — reviewer/security-auditor BLOCKING remediation path MUST use the helper. The `agent_type` parameter is reserved for future per-agent prompt shaping and is currently unused (del'd to avoid W0613). 4 regression tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_prompt_integrity.py::TestConstructRevisionPrompt`.

- **`prompt_quality_rules.py` exempts FORBIDDEN/HARD-GATE/REQUIRED sections from bullet-density check** (Issue #1119): `check_constraint_density` and `_section_bullet_counts` now skip counting bullets in `##`-level sections whose headers contain any of `FORBIDDEN`, `HARD GATE`, `HARD-GATE`, `REQUIRED`, or `MUST NOT` (case-insensitive substring match). These enforcement sections are load-bearing policy text, not prose — capping their length forced agents to adopt `❌ ` symbol-prefix workarounds (observed in session #932, `commands/implement.md`) to avoid the 8-bullet threshold, drifting list formatting across files. The new `EXEMPT_HEADER_TOKENS` constant and `_is_exempt_section(header)` helper centralise the exemption logic; the diff-aware path (`_section_bullet_counts` feeding `check_constraint_density_diff`) also omits exempt sections so the write-time gate in `unified_pre_tool.py` Layer 6 is consistent. The threshold for non-exempt prose sections is unchanged. 9 regression tests in `tests/unit/test_prompt_quality_rules.py`.

- **`unified_pre_tool._is_autonomous_dev_repo` walk-up no longer reaches `$HOME` — global install no longer marks every project as autonomous-dev** (Issue #1127): The local fallback detector `_is_autonomous_dev_repo(file_path)` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/hooks/unified_pre_tool.py` walked up the directory tree looking for `.claude/commands/implement.md`. With autonomous-dev installed globally at `~/.claude/commands/implement.md`, the walk-up from ANY file under `~/` would eventually reach the user's home directory and find the global marker — silently classifying every project under `~/` (including projects with no autonomous-dev install) as autonomous-dev managed, which then triggered `_is_protected_infrastructure()` and blocked direct edits to `agents/*.md`, `commands/*.md`, `hooks/*.py`, `lib/*.py`, `skills/*/SKILL.md` in projects that had nothing to do with autonomous-dev. Fix: the walk-up now resolves `Path.home()` once before the loop and breaks when `current == home`, so the GLOBAL install at `~/.claude/commands/implement.md` is never matched by the walk-up. Project-local installs (where the marker exists below `$HOME`) remain detected correctly. Failure on the home-dir resolve is caught and the walk-up degrades to the unbounded behavior — never fail-open by accident. 5 regression tests in `tests/unit/hooks/test_unified_pre_tool_home_dir_walkup.py` cover: (1) file under simulated home with only the global marker returns False; (2) project-local marker below home returns True; (3) project-local marker wins over global marker when both exist; (4) file outside home returns False even with global marker; (5) 10-level cap still bounds deep paths.

- **Implementer HARD GATE for mechanical test counting — memory-counting and anchored-grep undercounting eliminated** (Issues #988, #1117): `plugins/autonomous-dev/agents/implementer.md` gains a `HARD GATE: Mechanical Test Counting` section requiring the canonical `bugfix_detector.get_test_count()` counter (reused from `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/bugfix_detector.py`) before any test-count claim, with a whitespace-tolerant shell fallback (`grep -cE "^[[:space:]]*def[[:space:]]+test_"`) that mirrors the `^\s*def\s+test_` regex inside `get_test_count`. The gate explicitly FORBIDS (a) self-reporting counts from memory (Issue #988 root cause — session #970 reported "38 new tests" when the real delta was 34) and (b) the anchored `grep -cE "^def test_"` variant which silently returns 0 for indented class-method tests (Issue #1117 — the common pattern in `tests/regression/`). 5 regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_988_mechanical_test_counting.py` lock the gate text and prove the canonical counter handles class-method tests by running it against the test file itself as a fixture.

- **`already_done_detector` false-positives on anti-markers and non-ancestor commits** (Issue #1125): The detector previously returned a match when a commit body listed an issue under `Pending: #N` or `Preflight-skipped: #N` (explicit signals that the issue is NOT done), and also matched closure commits from unmerged side branches. Fix: (1) `git log --all` is now used so all branches are searched; (2) a new `_classify_issue_in_body()` helper distinguishes closure markers (`Closes #N`, `Fixes #N`, `Completed: #N`), anti-markers (`Pending: #N`, `Preflight-skipped: #N`, `Deferred: #N`), and bare mentions — anti-marker matches are filtered to `None` before being returned; (3) closure commits are checked via `git merge-base --is-ancestor` and downgraded to `stale_branch` (also filtered to `None`) when the commit is not reachable from HEAD. The return type is now a `MatchResult(sha, subject, classification)` NamedTuple; callers receive `None` for all non-closure cases. `implement-batch.md` STEP I1.3 updated to document the new return shape and classification semantics. 26 tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_already_done_detector.py`.

- **`repo_detector.is_autonomous_dev_repo()` always returned `False` in autonomous-dev worktrees — self-validation silently disabled** (Issue #1126): `_detect_autonomous_dev()` was probing for `plugins/autonomous-dev/manifest.json`, a file that does not exist. The canonical marker is `plugins/autonomous-dev/.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`, already present and already referenced by CLAUDE.md as the self-maintenance mode trigger. With the wrong marker, `is_autonomous_dev_repo()` always returned `False`, silently disabling the self-validation logic in `enforce_tdd.py` and `stop_quality_gate.py` for every autonomous-dev worktree. Fix: the marker path in `_detect_autonomous_dev()` changed to `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`; module-level and function-level docstrings updated to match. The existing regression fixture in `tests/regression/regression/test_self_validation_workflow.py` was patching the wrong marker and is now corrected. 5 new regression tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_repo_detector.py` cover: detection via the canonical marketplace marker, `False` when marker is absent, `False` when only the legacy `manifest.json` exists, detection from inside a worktree, and detection from a nested subdirectory.

### Security
- **#1134** Add `INTENT_CLASSIFIER_ENFORCE` to `PROTECTED_ENV_VARS` (`plugins/autonomous-dev/hooks/unified_pre_tool.py:336`). Closes an out-of-scope security finding from #1058 where the env var controlling classifier enforcement could be inline-spoofed via `INTENT_CLASSIFIER_ENFORCE=false git push`. OWASP A05. Conservative-direction fix (falls back to full enforcement, not bypass).

- **`install_manifest.json` protected as deployment infrastructure — direct edits blocked** (Issue #980): `plugins/autonomous-dev/config/install_manifest.json` is now treated as protected infrastructure parallel to `agents/*.md`, `commands/*.md`, `hooks/*.py`, `lib/*.py`, and `skills/*/SKILL.md`. A new `PROTECTED_INFRA_FILES` constant in `unified_pre_tool.py` holds per-file entries matched via `endswith` (preventing partial-basename false positives such as `foo_install_manifest.json`); `_is_protected_infrastructure()` extended to check this set. Direct Write/Edit to the manifest outside the `/implement` pipeline is now blocked with a `REQUIRED NEXT ACTION` directing the coordinator to re-invoke the implementer in REMEDIATION MODE. `commands/implement.md` STEP 9 gains three explicit FORBIDDEN clauses: direct Edit/Write to `install_manifest.json`, citing "1-line fix / config allowlist" as justification, and proceeding to STEP 10 with manifest entries missing. 6 regression tests in `tests/unit/hooks/test_infrastructure_protection.py::TestInstallManifestProtection`.

- **`reset_global_hooks.py` hardening umbrella — symlinks, permissions, atomicity** (Issue #965): Four security hardening items applied to `plugins/autonomous-dev/scripts/reset_global_hooks.py`. (1) Backup creation now uses `write_bytes`/`read_bytes` + explicit `os.chmod(str(backup_path), 0o600)` instead of `shutil.copy2`, which followed symlinks and inherited the source mode rather than enforcing a fixed 0o600. (2) `_atomic_write_json` now reads the existing file mode and applies a floor of 0o600, stripping any group/other read-write-execute bits; previously, permission inheritance could leave the rewritten `settings.json` world-readable on systems with a permissive umask. (3) Symlink detection added before `os.replace`: if `target` is a symlink, `_atomic_write_json` resolves to the real target and writes there — preserving the dotfiles-manager symlink in the filesystem rather than silently replacing it with a plain file; broken symlinks are handled gracefully with an OS-level fallback. (4) The TROUBLESHOOTING.md Option 2 manual one-liner was a non-atomic `write_text` call; replaced with the same temp-file + `os.replace` pattern so the manual path is atomic under SIGINT. Symlink notes are surfaced through the existing `message` field in the JSON result — no new top-level JSON fields added. 6 new test functions: 5 regression tests in `tests/unit/scripts/test_reset_global_hooks.py` (backup mode 0o600, permission floor on world-readable source, symlink preservation, broken symlink replacement, symlink backup dereferencing) and 1 spec test (AC9) in `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_issue949_reset_global_hooks.py`.

### Added
- **Cluster selector for drain-queue tracker filtering** (Issue #1284): New pure function `select_next_cluster(clusters)` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/cluster_selector.py` filters tracker-shaped issues from drain-queue candidates and prefers their lowest-numbered open leaf children when present. Detects trackers via: title prefix `[TRACKER]`, label combination "root-cause" + "enhancement", or body structure with ≥2 phase headings + ≥2 issue references. When encountering a tracker with open leaves, returns a synthetic single-issue cluster pointing to the lowest leaf. Not yet wired into `/drain-queue` command — integration tracked separately. Added 5 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_cluster_selector.py`.

- **Coordinator bypass verdict tracking** (Issue #1279): Extended `record_plan_critic_skipped()` with optional `bypass_reason` parameter and added new `write_coordinator_bypass_verdict()` function in `pipeline_completion_state.py` to enable drain-queue coordinators to record machine-readable audit trails when bypassing plan-critic for mechanical extensions. New `/drain-queue` command documentation section "Coordinator Bypass of plan-critic" details the two-call protocol. 3 new tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1279_coordinator_bypass_verdict.py` verify the bypass tracking logic.
- **Validator Diversity Score — CIA Check #15** (Issue #991): New `lib/validator_diversity.py` library measures how complementary reviewer and security-auditor findings are by computing `1 − Jaccard similarity` over normalized finding tuples `(severity, category, file, line)`. Low overlap means healthy specialization; high overlap may indicate rubber-stamping. The `continuous-improvement-analyst` full-mode report now includes a `### Validator Diversity` subsection when artifact files are present. Three alert states: `[VALIDATOR-OVERLAP]` (Jaccard > 0.8 AND total findings ≥ 6), `[VALIDATOR-BLIND-SPOT]` (both validators empty with files present), and no alert otherwise. Info severity only — never blocks the pipeline. Artifact files are written by the coordinator to `.claude/logs/activity/validators/{run_id}/` at STEP 10, STEP 10b, and STEP 11 in `implement.md`, and at STEP B3 in `implement-batch.md`. CLI available: `PYTHONPATH="plugins/autonomous-dev/lib" python3 -m validator_diversity --reviewer PATH --security PATH [--json]`. 24 new tests: 12 unit in `tests/unit/lib/test_validator_diversity.py`, 12 spec in `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_991_validator_diversity.py`.

### Changed
- **Planner minimum-scope directive** (Issue #1006): `agents/planner.md` gains a `HARD GATE: Minimum Scope` section requiring every planned component to map to an acceptance criterion. Adding components "in case they're needed" is now explicitly FORBIDDEN. Eliminates the over-build pattern that caused plan-critic REVISE on Issues #995–#999. 2 regression tests added to `tests/unit/agents/test_pipeline_agent_quality_audit.py`.
- **Prompt-quality gate (Layer 6) is now diff-aware for Edit operations** (Issue #1038): Previously, editing any `agents/*.md` or `commands/*.md` file could be blocked if the pre-existing file content (in untouched sections) happened to contain persona openers, casual register phrases, or oversized constraint sections — even when the edit itself introduced no new violations. The gate now subtracts pre-existing violations from the post-edit check for Edit operations: persona and casual-register checks exempt phrases already present before the edit; constraint-density checks use the new `check_constraint_density_diff(old, new)` helper in `prompt_quality_rules.py`, which only flags sections that are newly added or had their bullet count increased past the threshold. Write operations retain the full-content check (everything is considered new). 4 regression tests in `tests/unit/test_prompt_quality_diff_awareness.py`.

### Added
- **Cluster selector for drain-queue tracker filtering** (Issue #1284): New pure function `select_next_cluster(clusters)` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/cluster_selector.py` filters tracker-shaped issues from drain-queue candidates and prefers their lowest-numbered open leaf children when present. Detects trackers via: title prefix `[TRACKER]`, label combination "root-cause" + "enhancement", or body structure with ≥2 phase headings + ≥2 issue references. When encountering a tracker with open leaves, returns a synthetic single-issue cluster pointing to the lowest leaf. Not yet wired into `/drain-queue` command — integration tracked separately. Added 5 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_cluster_selector.py`.

- **Coordinator bypass verdict tracking** (Issue #1279): Extended `record_plan_critic_skipped()` with optional `bypass_reason` parameter and added new `write_coordinator_bypass_verdict()` function in `pipeline_completion_state.py` to enable drain-queue coordinators to record machine-readable audit trails when bypassing plan-critic for mechanical extensions. New `/drain-queue` command documentation section "Coordinator Bypass of plan-critic" details the two-call protocol. 3 new tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1279_coordinator_bypass_verdict.py` verify the bypass tracking logic.
- **Reviewer Write-Path Completeness check** (Issue #1010): `agents/reviewer.md` now includes an explicit "Write-Path Completeness" review dimension. When any Pydantic schema field is added, the reviewer must trace the field from API handler → service method → ORM column; any gap at steps 1–2 is a BLOCKING finding (silent discard — field is validated but never persisted). This catches silent-discard bugs in the API → service → ORM persistence chain that static code reading alone tends to miss. 2 new tests in `tests/unit/agents/test_pipeline_agent_quality_audit.py` (#1010 dimension present, silent-discard rated BLOCKING).

- **Reviewer `missing-pytest-artifact` structured finding category** (Issue #1108): When the reviewer issues `REQUEST_CHANGES` solely because no STEP 8 pytest artifact was provided in context (a process gap, not a code defect), it now emits a structured finding with `Category: missing-pytest-artifact`. The coordinator can inspect this category to distinguish a process-gap denial (re-embed test results and re-dispatch reviewer) from a code-defect denial (route to implementer remediation loop). The gate behavior — `REQUEST_CHANGES` on missing artifact — is unchanged. 1 new test in `tests/unit/agents/test_pipeline_agent_quality_audit.py` (#1108 category format present).

### Fixed
- **STEP 6 no longer ambiguously reports `EXECUTED (0 tests generated)` for all-deterministic acceptance criteria** (Issue #981): When every acceptance criterion is classified as a static/deterministic check (file existence, regex, string matching), STEP 6 has nothing to write to `tests/genai/` — the implementer correctly handles these in STEP 5/8 as unit tests. Previously the coordinator would emit `STEP 6: EXECUTED (0 acceptance tests generated)`, which is visually identical to a ghost invocation. The fix introduces a fourth recognized log-line variant `STEP 6: SKIPPED (all criteria classified deterministic — N/N tests written by implementer in STEP 5/8)` in `commands/implement.md`, with a MUST-output rule so coordinators use the new variant rather than the misleading zero-count executed form. `agents/continuous-improvement-analyst.md` Known False Positives section gains entry #6 documenting that this SKIPPED variant is a valid outcome and must NOT be flagged as a ghost invocation or pipeline skip violation; only the absence of any STEP 6 log line, or a bare `EXECUTED (0 acceptance tests generated)` without the deterministic explanation, warrants a finding. 4 regression tests in `tests/unit/commands/test_implement_step6_deterministic_skip.py`.

- **`get_agent_prompt_template` falls back to `.claude/agents/` in consumer installs** (Issue #1118): When `plugins/autonomous-dev/agents/` does not exist (consumer repos such as spektiv), the function now falls back to `{root}/.claude/agents/` before raising `FileNotFoundError`. Primary path behavior is unchanged when `plugins/autonomous-dev/agents/` is present. 3 regression tests added to `tests/unit/lib/test_prompt_integrity.py::TestGetAgentPromptTemplate`.

- **`detect_issue_research()` allowlist widened for empirical vocabulary** (Issue #1009): `RESEARCH_SECTIONS` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/research_persistence.py` now recognises empirical/scientific section names (`empirical evidence`, `empirical analysis`, `proposed values`, `proposed configuration`, `references`, `findings source`, `data source`, `results`, `experimental results`, `observed behavior`, `measurements`). Eliminates false-negative classification of empirical research issues that previously forced unnecessary STEP 4 web-research dispatches. 13 new regression tests in `TestEmpiricalSections`.

- **`_detect_settings_json_write` tempfile carve-out — false-positive for test fixtures under `/tmp/`** (Issue #958): The settings-write guard (Issue #557) was triggering on Bash writes to `settings.json` under `/tmp/`, `/var/folders/`, and `/private/tmp/` — paths used by `tempfile.mkdtemp()` and pytest fixtures that are physically incapable of affecting real Claude settings. In sessions #944 and #948 the implementer circumvented the block by renaming the fixture file, a bypass now documented in `known_bypass_patterns.json` (`settings_filename_rename_bypass`). Fix: a `_TEMP_PREFIXES` check is inserted at the top of the `for target in write_targets` loop in `_detect_settings_json_write`; targets matching any tempfile prefix are skipped before the `settings_patterns` regex runs. Real writes to `.claude/settings.json`, `~/.claude/settings.json`, and repo-relative `settings.json` remain blocked. 5 regression tests in `tests/unit/hooks/test_settings_write_via_intent.py` (`TestIssue958TempfileCarveout`).

- **`step6_bundling_bypass` pattern severity corrected from `high` to `critical`** (Issue #1114): The `step6_bundling_bypass` detection pattern in `plugins/autonomous-dev/config/known_bypass_patterns.json` had `"severity": "high"`, which is not a valid value in the schema (allowed values: `critical`, `warning`, `info`). The value is corrected to `"severity": "critical"`. No behavioral change — the pattern was already being loaded; this fix makes the config schema-valid. 7 spec-validation tests in `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_1114_step6_bypass_severity.py`.

- **Worktree creation now bootstraps `plugins/__init__.py` and `plugins/autonomous_dev` symlink** (Issue #1115): Fresh worktrees created via `create_worktree()` failed pytest collection with `ImportError` because `git worktree add` cannot propagate gitignored build artifacts. A new internal helper `_bootstrap_worktree_plugin_artifacts()` is called from `create_worktree()` after the git checkout step; it creates `plugins/__init__.py` (if absent) and a `plugins/autonomous_dev → autonomous-dev` relative symlink (if absent). The fix is idempotent — already-bootstrapped worktrees are not affected. On any `OSError` (permissions, filesystem) the helper emits a stderr warning and returns without raising, so `create_worktree()` still returns `(True, path)`. 13 new tests: 5 integration in `tests/integration/test_worktree_plugin_artifacts.py`, 8 spec in `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_1115_worktree_plugin_bootstrap.py`.

- **Regression tests lock the Read-tool-never-blocks-settings fix (Issue #918)**: The `settings_json_write_block` deviation in `unified_pre_tool.py` was gated at Write/Edit operations (line 4457) and Bash writes (line 4685), meaning a `Read` tool call on `.claude/settings.local.json` or `.claude/settings.json` correctly bypasses the guard. No regression test existed to lock this behavior. A new `TestIssue918ReadToolNeverBlocksSettings` class (6 unit tests) added to `tests/unit/hooks/test_unified_pre_tool_false_positives.py` and a new acceptance test module `tests/genai/test_acceptance_918_read_tool_settings.py` (4 tests) verifying the class is present and no production hook code was modified. No production code changed.

- **Three false-positive guards fixed in `unified_pre_tool.py`** (Issues #1001, #1032, #1111): Three independent surgical fixes reduce false-positive blocks without weakening real enforcement. (#1032) `_detect_env_spoofing` now strips heredoc content before running the protected-env-var pattern match, so writing a Markdown or documentation file whose heredoc body mentions `PIPELINE_STATE_FILE` or another protected variable no longer produces a spurious block. (#1001) A new `_is_settings_template_path()` helper short-circuits the Issue #557 settings-file write guard for paths containing a `templates/` directory component — `plugins/*/templates/settings.local.json` is template source, not a runtime settings file, and should not be blocked. (#1111) The settings-file write guard now also short-circuits when self-maintenance mode is active (`_is_self_maintenance_mode()` returns `True`) AND the path is under `plugins/autonomous-dev/` — in the canonical autonomous-dev source tree the maintainer is the runtime settings author and the consumer-side guard does not apply. All three fixes are covered by 7 new regression tests in `tests/unit/hooks/test_unified_pre_tool_false_positives.py`.

### Fixed
- **STEP 6 acceptance-test bundling bypass blocked** (Issue #973): The coordinator could silently skip STEP 6 by citing hook-blockage on its own Write/Edit to `tests/genai/test_acceptance_*.py`, then bundle acceptance test creation into the STEP 8 implementer scope — leaving behavioral acceptance tests absent while a `criteria_registry` artifact gave a false impression of compliance. Three explicit FORBIDDEN prohibitions added to the STEP 6 section of `commands/implement.md`: (1) silent skip when `tests/genai/conftest.py` exists and mode is not `--tdd-first` is forbidden; (2) the bypass reason "STEP 6: SKIPPED (coordinator code-writing is hook-blocked)" or any hook-blockage variant is explicitly named as a NOVEL BYPASS — when hook-blocked, the required path is invoking `test-master` or recording acceptance criteria via `record_acceptance_criteria()`; (3) bundling acceptance tests into the implementer's scope or substituting `save_criteria_registry()` for actual test file creation is forbidden. New detection pattern `step6_bundling_bypass` (severity: critical — corrected from `high` in Issue #1114) added to `plugins/autonomous-dev/config/known_bypass_patterns.json` and `.claude/config/known_bypass_patterns.json` so the continuous-improvement-analyst can flag this pattern in future sessions.

- **CIA ordering enforcement — continuous-improvement-analyst blocked from running before implementer/reviewer/doc-master** (Issue #957): In batch session 2026-04-25 (Issue #948), `continuous-improvement-analyst` was dispatched 10 minutes before `implementer` — a pipeline ordering violation. Root cause: CIA had no entry in `STEP_ORDER` and no entries in `SEQUENTIAL_REQUIRED`, so `agent_ordering_gate.py` did not enforce CIA-last placement. Fix: `continuous-improvement-analyst: 7.0` added to `STEP_ORDER` in both `pipeline_intent_validator.py` and `agent_ordering_gate.py`'s inline fallback; three new `SEQUENTIAL_REQUIRED` pairs added — `(implementer, CIA)`, `(reviewer, CIA)`, `(doc-master, CIA)` — enforcing that CIA runs only after all validation/implementation agents complete. `security-auditor → CIA` intentionally excluded because security-auditor is conditional (only runs in sequential mode with security-sensitive files); requiring it would break `--fix` mode where security-auditor is legitimately skipped. 14 regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_957_cia_ordering.py`.

- **spec-validator narrowed to verdict-only output** (Issue #931): The spec-validator agent was attempting to write test files to `tests/spec_validation/` but was correctly blocked by the workflow enforcement hook twice in the #925 session. The agent's prompt has been updated to prohibit all file writes (`Write`, `Edit`, `NotebookEdit`). Phase 2 is renamed from "Write Binary Pass/Fail Tests" to "Validate Observable Behavior (No File Writes)"; the "Test Placement" subsection that instructed test file creation has been removed. If a criterion cannot be evaluated without writing a file, the spec-validator now emits `SPEC-VALIDATOR-VERDICT: FAIL` and lists it as unverifiable. The PASS/FAIL verdict contract is unchanged. `docs/ARCHITECTURE-OVERVIEW.md` updated to remove the stale "writes behavioral tests" description.

### Added
- **Cluster selector for drain-queue tracker filtering** (Issue #1284): New pure function `select_next_cluster(clusters)` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/cluster_selector.py` filters tracker-shaped issues from drain-queue candidates and prefers their lowest-numbered open leaf children when present. Detects trackers via: title prefix `[TRACKER]`, label combination "root-cause" + "enhancement", or body structure with ≥2 phase headings + ≥2 issue references. When encountering a tracker with open leaves, returns a synthetic single-issue cluster pointing to the lowest leaf. Not yet wired into `/drain-queue` command — integration tracked separately. Added 5 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_cluster_selector.py`.

- **Coordinator bypass verdict tracking** (Issue #1279): Extended `record_plan_critic_skipped()` with optional `bypass_reason` parameter and added new `write_coordinator_bypass_verdict()` function in `pipeline_completion_state.py` to enable drain-queue coordinators to record machine-readable audit trails when bypassing plan-critic for mechanical extensions. New `/drain-queue` command documentation section "Coordinator Bypass of plan-critic" details the two-call protocol. 3 new tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1279_coordinator_bypass_verdict.py` verify the bypass tracking logic.
- **`install.sh --uninstall` — shell-only uninstall path** (Issue #951): Previously, the only documented removal path was `/sync --uninstall` (Python orchestrator), which requires a working Claude CLI. When Claude Code itself is broken or hooks are bricked, `/sync` cannot run. `install.sh` now accepts `--uninstall` (remove hooks and global registration), `--dry-run` (preview plan without mutating anything), and `--repos <list>` (also strip autonomous-dev hooks from named per-repo `.claude/settings.json` files). Two companion scripts — `plugins/autonomous-dev/scripts/uninstall_strip_repo_hooks.py` and `plugins/autonomous-dev/scripts/uninstall_unregister_plugin.py` — handle the per-repo hook stripping and plugin-registry cleanup respectively; both are self-contained (no imports from the plugin's lib tree) so they work even in a partially-broken install. A timestamped backup is created at `~/.claude/backups/uninstall-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS/` before any mutation. `plugins/autonomous-dev/docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md` gains an "Uninstalling autonomous-dev" section with a comparison table. `README.md` Uninstall section updated to document both paths. 15 new tests across `tests/unit/scripts/test_uninstall_strip_repo_hooks.py` (6), `tests/unit/scripts/test_uninstall_unregister_plugin.py` (5), and `tests/integration/test_install_sh_uninstall.py` (4).

### Security
- **`rm -rf` with unresolved variable detection — catastrophe prevention** (Issue #1008): Closes the honest gap documented in Issue #1004, where `_detect_git_bypass` (then `_check_dangerous_bash`) was registered in the hard-floor registry under the description "rm -rf with unresolved variables" but no such detection existed in the codebase. New function `_check_rm_rf_unresolved_vars(command)` in `unified_pre_tool.py` inspects every Bash command for `rm -rf $UNSET_VAR` patterns — unquoted `$VAR` or `${VAR}` expansions as the target of a recursive-force remove. Commands that double-quote the variable (`"$VAR"`), use single quotes (`'$VAR'`), or pass a literal path are allowed through. The function is registered as a hard-floor in both `plugins/autonomous-dev/config/hard_floor_hooks.json` (authoritative JSON, registry now 6 entries) and `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/hard_floor.py` (`_FALLBACK_HARD_FLOOR_HOOKS` fallback constant), so it fires unconditionally regardless of session mode. 16 regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1008_rm_rf_unresolved_vars.py`; `tests/unit/lib/test_hard_floor.py` and `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_997_hard_floor_registry.py` updated to expect 6 entries.

- **`check_doc_links.py` path-traversal guard** (Issue #994): The script previously resolved relative markdown links via `Path.resolve()` and checked `candidate.exists()` without confirming the resolved path stayed inside the project. A crafted link like `../../../../etc/passwd` resolved to a real system file and was silently reported as valid, turning the checker into a filesystem oracle. Fix: a new `_find_project_root()` helper walks upward from the doc's directory to locate a `.git` entry or `pyproject.toml`; `check_link()` now performs a containment check via `Path.is_relative_to()` (with a prefix-comparison fallback for Python < 3.9) BEFORE calling `exists()`. Links escaping the project root are rejected with `"link escapes project root: <target>"` rather than leaking the resolved path. 7 regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_994_doc_links_traversal_guard.py`.

### Fixed
- **Monkeypatch fixture signatures widened to accept `run_id` keyword argument** (Issue #1107): Eight regression and spec-validation test files patched `_state_file_path` with single-positional-argument stubs (`def _patched(s):` / `lambda sid: ...`). Production code in `pipeline_completion_state.py` now calls `_state_file_path(session_id, run_id=run_id)`, causing every affected fixture to raise `TypeError: _patched() got an unexpected keyword argument 'run_id'` at test time. Fixtures updated to `def _patched(s, *, run_id=None):` / `lambda sid, *, run_id=None: ...`. No production code changes; test infrastructure only. Affected: `tests/regression/test_issue_802_agent_completeness_gate.py`, `test_issue_802_file_bypass.py`, `test_issue_849_pipeline_mode_from_state.py`, `test_issue_852_doc_verdict_completion.py`, `test_issue_853_batch_research_skip.py`, `test_issue_906_background_doc_master.py`, `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_issue849_pipeline_mode_from_state.py`, `tests/unit/lib/test_pipeline_completion_state_agent_completeness.py`.

- **`clean_env` fixture CWD isolation — batch-state bleed in worktree test runs** (Issue #956): When pytest was invoked from a worktree path containing `.worktrees/batch-`, the hook's CWD check at `unified_pre_tool.py:4832` routed through the batch-mode agent-completeness branch instead of the non-batch branch that `_check_pipeline_agent_completions` (the function patched by these tests) lives on.
