The batch branch reads real on-disk state and returns `allow` when no pipeline state file exists, causing gate-blocks-without-bypass tests to receive `allow` instead of the expected `deny`. The `clean_env` fixture in `tests/unit/hooks/test_agent_completeness_bypass.py` now accepts `tmp_path` and calls `monkeypatch.chdir(tmp_path)`, isolating the hook's CWD check from the worktree directory for the duration of each test.

- **Settings templates stripped of invalid schema keys — hooks silently disabled on every fresh install** (Issues #1103/1104/1105, Severity: HIGH): Three bugs in the settings template files caused Claude Code to silently skip the entire settings file on load, disabling every hook. (1) `PreCommit` is not a recognized Claude Code hook event; its presence caused the file to fail schema validation and be discarded. Removed from `settings.local.json`, `settings.autonomous-dev.json`, and `settings.strict-mode.json`. The PreCommit hooks (auto_test, security_scan, etc.) now fire only via the git `scripts/hooks/pre-commit` path as intended. (2) The `auto_format` hook matcher was an object `{"tools": ["Write", "Edit"]}` instead of a plain string; corrected to `"Write|Edit"` across all three templates. (3) `Bash(:*)` in the `permissions.allow` list used an unsupported colon-prefixed glob syntax; corrected to `Bash` in `settings.autonomous-dev.json`, `settings.strict-mode.json`, and `settings.permission-batching.json`. Together these three defects meant no hook fired for any user whose `.claude/settings.local.json` was generated from the default template. 5 new regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1103_1104_1105_settings_template_validity.py`; `tests/integration/test_strict_mode_workflow.py` assertion updated to lock in the corrected matcher form.

- **`_check_agent_denial` auto-cleans stale and orphaned deny files** (Issue #1051): `/tmp/adev-agent-deny-*.json` files previously persisted across pipeline runs when their owning session ended (session-id mismatch) or their TTL expired, requiring manual `rm` to unblock subsequent agent invocations. `_check_agent_denial` in `unified_pre_tool.py` now issues a fail-open `os.unlink` at both early-return sites (session mismatch and TTL expiry), removing the dead file immediately. Active deny files (same session, within 300-second TTL) are untouched. 5 new regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1051_deny_ttl_cleanup.py`.

- **`get_cumulative_shrinkage` no longer fires on single-issue remediation loops** (Issue #934): When a batch run revisits the same issue multiple times (e.g., a fix → doc-update-retry cycle), round-2+ prompts are naturally narrower than round-1, causing the cumulative drift detector to report spurious compression. `get_cumulative_shrinkage` now returns `None` when all observations target fewer than 2 distinct issue numbers — a genuine batch drift signal requires at least 2 different issues. Observations missing an issue identifier (legacy or malformed entries) are discarded from the count rather than treated as a pseudo-issue. 5 new regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_934_drift_single_issue_remediation.py`.

- **`test_spec_test_isolation_prompt_integrity.py` scoped to historical commit instead of live working tree** (Issue #933): `test_spec_isolation_3_no_production_code_changes` was originally written as a one-shot acceptance check for PR #784 using `git diff HEAD --name-only`, which caused the test to fire globally on every subsequent PR and block valid hooks/lib/agents changes. The check is now pinned to commit `834ab88` (the PR #784 merge) via `git show --name-only --format= 834ab88`, adding a `PR_784_COMMIT_SHA` constant and a `pytest.skip` fallback when the commit is absent (e.g., shallow clones). The historical regression-lock intent is preserved while future PRs no longer see false positives.

- **`log_block_with_recovery` deprecation now surfaces on stderr instead of silently swallowing** (Issue #993): Python's default warning filter suppresses `DeprecationWarning` raised from non-`__main__` modules, so the legacy shim's `warnings.warn` call was silently dropped when any hook imported `hook_recovery`. The deprecation is now emitted via `sys.stderr.write` directly, bypassing the filter. A module-level `_legacy_warn_emitted` boolean guards the write so each process logs the deprecation exactly once (one-shot semantics). Operators migrating call sites to `hook_telemetry.log_block_event` will now reliably see the `[hook-recovery] DEPRECATION:` line. 3 new regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_993_deprecation_stderr.py` (first-call emits, second-call suppressed, fresh subprocess re-emits); 1 adjusted unit test in `tests/unit/lib/test_hook_telemetry.py` now asserts on stderr instead of warnings.

- **`hook_telemetry.log_block_event` now safe under concurrent hook invocations** (Issue #992): `MAX_REASON_LENGTH=8000` exceeded the POSIX `PIPE_BUF` atomic-append guarantee (4096B on Linux, 512B on macOS), allowing concurrent processes writing telemetry rows larger than `PIPE_BUF` to interleave bytes and produce torn JSONL lines that crashed downstream triage tooling. Fix: `fcntl.flock(LOCK_EX)` is now held around the `write` call for the duration of the append. Works for events of any size. On NFS or filesystems where `flock` is refused, the error is swallowed and the bare append proceeds (telemetry must never break the hook decision path). On Windows where `fcntl` is absent, behavior is unchanged (bare append, concurrent writes are not a realistic concern on this telemetry surface). Lock overhead is ~1ms per write — negligible relative to hook runtime. 3 new regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_992_pipe_buf_concurrency.py`.

- **Baseline failing-tests capture now handles suites longer than 120s** (Issue #1094): STEP 1 and STEP 8 of `commands/implement.md` now wrap `subprocess.run(['pytest', ...])` in a `try/except subprocess.TimeoutExpired` block. The default timeout is 600 seconds (10 minutes, up from the implicit 120s subprocess default), overridable via `BASELINE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` env var. On timeout, a `__TIMEOUT__` sentinel is written to `/tmp/baseline_failing_tests.txt` instead of propagating a traceback; STEP 8 reads this sentinel and skips fix-forward classification gracefully (no garbage IDs injected into the auto-file path). 21 new regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1094_baseline_timeout.py`.

- **`validate_test_categorization.py` now recognizes `tests/structural/` as a valid tier** (Issue #1102): `"structural"` added to `VALID_CATEGORIES` and `tier_order` (position 8). Tests in `tests/structural/` were previously reported as "uncategorized" in distribution reports because the directory was added in #1099 but never registered in the validator. Module docstring updated to document the structural tier (schema/format validation tests: command frontmatter, manifest shape, file structure assertions).

### Added
- **Cluster selector for drain-queue tracker filtering** (Issue #1284): New pure function `select_next_cluster(clusters)` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/cluster_selector.py` filters tracker-shaped issues from drain-queue candidates and prefers their lowest-numbered open leaf children when present. Detects trackers via: title prefix `[TRACKER]`, label combination "root-cause" + "enhancement", or body structure with ≥2 phase headings + ≥2 issue references. When encountering a tracker with open leaves, returns a synthetic single-issue cluster pointing to the lowest leaf. Not yet wired into `/drain-queue` command — integration tracked separately. Added 5 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_cluster_selector.py`.

- **Coordinator bypass verdict tracking** (Issue #1279): Extended `record_plan_critic_skipped()` with optional `bypass_reason` parameter and added new `write_coordinator_bypass_verdict()` function in `pipeline_completion_state.py` to enable drain-queue coordinators to record machine-readable audit trails when bypassing plan-critic for mechanical extensions. New `/drain-queue` command documentation section "Coordinator Bypass of plan-critic" details the two-call protocol. 3 new tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1279_coordinator_bypass_verdict.py` verify the bypass tracking logic.
- **Negative-assertion scope-lock pattern documented across testing guide, TESTING-STRATEGY, and plan-critic** (Issue #1011): When an audit (issue, refactor pass, or migration) produces an "affected files" list with intentionally excluded files (false positives), teams now have a documented pattern for locking those exclusions as regression tests. `docs/TESTING-STRATEGY.md` gains a "Negative-Assertion Scope Locks" subsection under Layer 2 with canonical code template and trigger guidance. `plugins/autonomous-dev/skills/testing-guide/SKILL.md` gains a matching "Negative-assertion scope locks" subsection under Anti-Patterns with GOOD/BAD examples. `plugins/autonomous-dev/agents/plan-critic.md` gains an *Audit-exclusion sub-criterion* under the Assumption Audit axis — plans that inherit an audit's exclusion list without either re-validating or scope-locking are scored 1. Reference implementation: `tests/unit/lib/test_phase3_wrap_adoption.py::test_phase3_false_positive_files_unchanged` (Issue #1007).

- **`/triage --auto-improvement` — periodic-aggregation root-cause triage** (Issue #1099): New slash command and library (`lib/issue_triage_analyzer.py`) that reads all open `auto-improvement` GitHub issues, groups them by bracket tag (`[CI]`, `[HARDENING]`, etc.) and by Jaccard token similarity within each tag (union-find, threshold: 2 shared tokens), ranks clusters by `cluster_size * severity_weight * recency_decay`, and surfaces cross-cluster dependencies via shared file-path references. Filters `fp-acknowledged` issues by default; `--include-fp-acknowledged` restores them. Supports `--repo`, `--limit`, and `--json` flags. Idempotent — byte-identical output on unchanged queue contents (only time-varying input is `now`, pinned in tests). New `fetch_open_issues_with_label()` function extracted from `runtime_data_aggregator.py` as a shared building block; `collect_github_signals()` now delegates to it (external contract preserved). `CLAUDE.md` Periodic Maintenance table updated; `/triage` added to Commands line. 35 new tests: 21 unit (`tests/unit/lib/test_issue_triage_analyzer.py`), 10 integration (`tests/integration/test_triage_command.py`), 4 structural (`tests/structural/test_triage_command_structure.py`).

### Fixed
- **`implementer` Document Output Cleanliness HARD GATE added** (Issue #921): The implementer was producing research notes and ADRs with plan-critic working artifacts (e.g., `## Security Model (Revised: ...)`) in section headers — reviewer flagged this pattern as a recurring WARNING across 3 sessions. A new HARD GATE in `agents/implementer.md` explicitly forbids embedding plan-critic revision annotations, REVISE-cycle notes, or critic-feedback prefixes in section headers of any Markdown artifact emitted during `/implement`. Section headers must be clean prose titles; revision history (if retained) belongs in a footer or metadata block. 12 new regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_921_implementer_header_cleanliness.py`.

### Changed
- **BREAKING: `/refactor --docs` no longer detects markdown redundancy.** Redundancy detection has been renamed to `/refactor --docs-redundancy`. The `--docs` flag now performs narrative-doc drift detection per PROJECT.md Layer 4 (Issue #1098).

### Added
- **Cluster selector for drain-queue tracker filtering** (Issue #1284): New pure function `select_next_cluster(clusters)` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/cluster_selector.py` filters tracker-shaped issues from drain-queue candidates and prefers their lowest-numbered open leaf children when present. Detects trackers via: title prefix `[TRACKER]`, label combination "root-cause" + "enhancement", or body structure with ≥2 phase headings + ≥2 issue references. When encountering a tracker with open leaves, returns a synthetic single-issue cluster pointing to the lowest leaf. Not yet wired into `/drain-queue` command — integration tracked separately. Added 5 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_cluster_selector.py`.

- **Coordinator bypass verdict tracking** (Issue #1279): Extended `record_plan_critic_skipped()` with optional `bypass_reason` parameter and added new `write_coordinator_bypass_verdict()` function in `pipeline_completion_state.py` to enable drain-queue coordinators to record machine-readable audit trails when bypassing plan-critic for mechanical extensions. New `/drain-queue` command documentation section "Coordinator Bypass of plan-critic" details the two-call protocol. 3 new tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1279_coordinator_bypass_verdict.py` verify the bypass tracking logic.
- **`/refactor --docs` — periodic-aggregation drift sweep** (Issue #1098): Sweeps every doc with `covers:` frontmatter against actual code/component state; detects count drift (e.g. "216 libraries" vs actual 219 files) and enumeration drift (list items with no matching source artifact) across the whole repo. Idempotent; repo-relative `doc_path` output for portability. Implemented in new library `lib/doc_drift_detector.py` (`DocDriftFinding` dataclass + `detect_doc_drift()` entry point). First concrete instance of the periodic-aggregation pattern (umbrella #1075). (Issue #1098)

### Added
- **Cluster selector for drain-queue tracker filtering** (Issue #1284): New pure function `select_next_cluster(clusters)` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/cluster_selector.py` filters tracker-shaped issues from drain-queue candidates and prefers their lowest-numbered open leaf children when present. Detects trackers via: title prefix `[TRACKER]`, label combination "root-cause" + "enhancement", or body structure with ≥2 phase headings + ≥2 issue references. When encountering a tracker with open leaves, returns a synthetic single-issue cluster pointing to the lowest leaf. Not yet wired into `/drain-queue` command — integration tracked separately. Added 5 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_cluster_selector.py`.

- **Coordinator bypass verdict tracking** (Issue #1279): Extended `record_plan_critic_skipped()` with optional `bypass_reason` parameter and added new `write_coordinator_bypass_verdict()` function in `pipeline_completion_state.py` to enable drain-queue coordinators to record machine-readable audit trails when bypassing plan-critic for mechanical extensions. New `/drain-queue` command documentation section "Coordinator Bypass of plan-critic" details the two-call protocol. 3 new tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1279_coordinator_bypass_verdict.py` verify the bypass tracking logic.
- **6th critique axis "Operational Integration Test" added to plan-critic** (Issue #1067): `plan-critic.md` now evaluates plans on a 6th axis — whether subprocess, network, or filesystem calls whose correctness depends on runtime context (CWD, env vars, credentials, file permissions) are covered by runtime-context assertions rather than static-shape tests only. Plans that ship such calls with only static-shape tests are flagged per the known failure mode documented in Issue #1064. Budget mode (single-pass) updated from 3 to 4 scored axes to include this new axis (Assumption Audit, Existing Solution Search, Minimalism Pressure, Operational Integration Test). `commands/implement.md` STEP 5.5b updated from 3 to 4 axes in the plan-implementation alignment gate. `skills/testing-guide/SKILL.md` gains a "Runtime Integration Testing Patterns" section documenting the `subprocess.run` kwarg assertion pattern via `monkeypatch`. `docs/PLANNING-WORKFLOW.md` Step 5 axis list updated from 5 to 6 axes. `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_issue880_plan_critic_rubric.py` broadened to accept "2 rounds" or "3 rounds" as valid minimum-round language.

### Added
- **Cluster selector for drain-queue tracker filtering** (Issue #1284): New pure function `select_next_cluster(clusters)` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/cluster_selector.py` filters tracker-shaped issues from drain-queue candidates and prefers their lowest-numbered open leaf children when present. Detects trackers via: title prefix `[TRACKER]`, label combination "root-cause" + "enhancement", or body structure with ≥2 phase headings + ≥2 issue references. When encountering a tracker with open leaves, returns a synthetic single-issue cluster pointing to the lowest leaf. Not yet wired into `/drain-queue` command — integration tracked separately. Added 5 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_cluster_selector.py`.

- **Coordinator bypass verdict tracking** (Issue #1279): Extended `record_plan_critic_skipped()` with optional `bypass_reason` parameter and added new `write_coordinator_bypass_verdict()` function in `pipeline_completion_state.py` to enable drain-queue coordinators to record machine-readable audit trails when bypassing plan-critic for mechanical extensions. New `/drain-queue` command documentation section "Coordinator Bypass of plan-critic" details the two-call protocol. 3 new tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1279_coordinator_bypass_verdict.py` verify the bypass tracking logic.
- **`measure_intent_classifier.py --validate-from-telemetry` — telemetry-driven FN rate measurement** (Issue #1077, Phase B): New flag joins `mode_skip` rows from `.claude/logs/hook-blocks.jsonl` against `~/.claude/archive/sessions.db`, classifies each skipped session as implement-shaped (regex match on first prompt for `/implement`/`/refactor`/`/fix`, or aggregate heuristic: `tool_calls >= 8` AND `transcript_bytes >= 4096`), and computes the observed false-negative rate with a Wilson 95% CI. Synthetic `phase-e-test-*` session IDs are excluded. Output written to `docs/intent_classifier_telemetry_validation.json` (structured JSON with `n_total_skip_rows`, `n_unique_sessions`, `n_implement_sessions`, `observed_fn_rate`, `ci_low`, `ci_high`, `per_skip_class_breakdown`, and diagnostic messaging). Issues a warning when `n_unique_sessions < 50` (insufficient sample). Three new CLI flags: `--validate-from-telemetry` (mode switch), `--telemetry-log` (default `.claude/logs/hook-blocks.jsonl`), `--sessions-db` (default `~/.claude/archive/sessions.db`), `--telemetry-output` (default `docs/intent_classifier_telemetry_validation.json`). Idempotent. New public functions: `validate_from_telemetry()`, `_wilson_ci()`, `_is_implement_shaped()`, `_load_session_metadata()`. `docs/SCRIPTS.md` gains a subsection under Intent Classifier Calibration. `docs/INTENT-CLASSIFICATION.md` gains a "Rollout to Enforce Mode" subsection under `INTENT_CLASSIFIER_ENFORCE`. 35 new tests in `tests/unit/scripts/test_measure_intent_classifier_telemetry.py`.

- `extract_and_label_intent_corpus.py`: `--source {sqlite,transcripts,both}` flag to mine all user-typed messages from transcript JSONLs in addition to `first_user_prompt` from `sessions.db`, closing the class-imbalance gap for refactor/remote_ops/security_critical/test/typo classes (#1072). New function `extract_prompts_from_transcripts(transcripts_dir, *, days, max_prompts)` walks `~/.claude/archive/conversations/{YYYY-MM}/*.jsonl`, applies 7 filters (type=user, role=user, string content, no `<local-command-caveat>` wrapper, no self-traffic judge prompts, length window) plus md5-based dedup and an mtime cutoff. `build_corpus` gains `source` and `transcripts_dir` parameters; routing is sqlite-only by default. Per-entry `source` field is now attached by `build_corpus` (`"sqlite"` | `"transcript"`) instead of hardcoded inside `label_prompts_with_single_judge`. Schema enum extended in `tests/regression/test_intent_classifier_corpus.py` to accept `transcript` alongside `sqlite` and `synthetic`. 13 new regression tests in `tests/regression/test_extract_transcripts.py` (mock-based, no real `~/.claude/archive` reads): one per filter condition, dedup by hash, mtime cutoff, missing dir, malformed JSON lines, valid-message positive control, and CLI flag round-trip.

### Fixed
- **`test_issue_208` no longer raises false-positive failures for legitimate test fixtures** (Issue #928): `test_no_references_to_old_architecture_md` in `tests/regression/progression/test_issue_208_architecture_doc_consolidation.py` added a `fixture_test_files` exclusion set for three spec/unit test files (`test_test_routing.py`, `test_explicit_implement_enforcement.py`, `test_1098_doc_drift_sweep_spec.py`) that use the string literal `"docs/ARCHITECTURE.md"` as an arbitrary generic path fixture — not as a real reference to the archived doc. Each entry carries an inline comment citing Issue #928 and the reason for exclusion. The guard continues to fail on any new real stray reference outside this named set.

- **Agent-completeness gate deny messages reordered to lead with reliable file-based bypass** (Issue #1086): Both the single-issue path (`_check_pipeline_agent_completions()`) and the batch path in `unified_pre_tool.py` now present bypass options in reliability order: (1) `touch /tmp/skip_agent_completeness_gate` as a separate command, then retry — file-based, works mid-session; (2) `export SKIP_AGENT_COMPLETENESS_GATE=1` BEFORE launching claude, with explicit caveat that env vars don't propagate mid-session (Issue #779). Previously the env var was listed first without the "BEFORE launching claude" caveat, causing users to try it mid-session where it silently fails. The `verify_pipeline_agent_completions()` docstring in `pipeline_completion_state.py` is updated to match the same ordering. `plugins/autonomous-dev/docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md` "Agent completeness gate BLOCKED" row updated to present both bypass options in order with the env var caveat. `docs/HOOKS.md`, `docs/HOOK-REGISTRY.md`, and `docs/LIBRARIES.md` updated to reflect the new bypass priority ordering. 6 new regression tests in the `TestBlockMessageOrdering` class in `tests/regression/test_issue_802_file_bypass.py`.

- **PIPELINE_BASE_COMMIT anchoring eliminates spec-validator false-positive FAIL on pre-existing working-tree state** (Issue #1069): Two new public helpers in `lib/pipeline_state.py` — `set_pipeline_base_commit(base_commit, *, state_path=None)` and `get_pipeline_base_commit(*, state_path=None)` — persist and recover the git HEAD SHA captured at pipeline start. The coordinator now calls `set_pipeline_base_commit()` at STEP 0 (full pipeline) and STEP F1 (fix pipeline) immediately after state initialization. At STEP 8.5 and STEP F3.5 / STEP F4, the diff anchor is recovered via `get_pipeline_base_commit()` and all `git diff --name-only` commands use `$PIPELINE_BASE_COMMIT` instead of `HEAD`. Without anchoring, files modified in the working tree before the pipeline starts appear in `git diff HEAD`, causing spec-validator to evaluate acceptance criteria against those pre-existing files and emit FAIL verdicts that require human override. Both helpers are fail-safe: `set_pipeline_base_commit` returns `False` (not an exception) when the state file is absent; `get_pipeline_base_commit` returns `None` and callers fall back to `HEAD` diff. 12 new tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_pipeline_state_base_commit.py`.

- **`resolve_session_id()` activity-log fallback eliminates "unknown" session-id from Bash subprocess contexts** (Issue #1093, Phase 1b): `resolve_session_id()` in `lib/pipeline_completion_state.py` extended from a 3-step (`env → sentinel → "unknown"`) to a 4-step fallback chain (`env → sentinel → activity-log → "unknown"`). Root cause: at pipeline boot, Bash subprocess contexts lack `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` in the environment AND the sentinel file's `session_id` field is the placeholder `"unknown"` (written before the real id is exported at STEP 0), so all 3 prior steps returned `"unknown"`, causing state lookups to miss and requiring a manual `record_agent_completion()` workaround. The new step 3 calls `_resolve_session_id_from_activity_log()` — a private helper that scans today's `.claude/logs/activity/{YYYY-MM-DD}.jsonl` (written by `session_activity_logger.py`) for the most recent entry with a real, non-"unknown" `session_id`. The sentinel step also now skips through when the sentinel value is `"unknown"` rather than returning it prematurely. Two new private helpers: `_find_activity_log_dir(start_dir)` (walks CWD upward to locate `.claude/logs/activity/`) and `_resolve_session_id_from_activity_log()` (JSONL scan). Verified: post-fix `resolve_session_id()` returns the real UUID `0df986e1-81ba-4caa-a524-dfa559f5d2bb` from a Bash subprocess context. 5 new tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_pipeline_completion_state_resolve_session_id.py` (11 total). `docs/PIPELINE-MODES.md` Session-ID Propagation Contract section updated to document the 4-step chain. `docs/LIBRARIES.md` `pipeline_completion_state.py` entry bumped to v1.10.0.

- **SubagentStop `subagent_type` and `duration_ms` now reliably populated** (Issue #1087): New library `subagent_invocation_cache.py` provides a per-session FIFO queue at `/tmp/subagent_invocations_{sha8}.json`. `session_activity_logger.py` gains a PreToolUse registration (matcher: `Task|Agent`) that calls `cache_invocation(session_id, subagent_type, start_time=time.time())` at invocation time. `unified_session_tracker.py` calls `pop_invocation(session_id, preferred_subagent_type=...)` at SubagentStop to recover the cached agent type and compute `duration_ms = (now - start_time) * 1000`. Fixes empty `subagent_type` and `duration_ms=0` in SubagentStop log entries that broke ghost-invocation detection, agent completeness gates, and pipeline timing analysis. Six settings templates updated with the new PreToolUse Task|Agent registration. 15 new tests in `tests/unit/hooks/test_session_activity_logger.py`.

- **`get_prompt_baseline` per-issue isolation restored; `get_cross_issue_baseline` added** (Issue #1082 Phase 1a): Resolves the contradiction between Issue #764 (per-issue baseline isolation) and Issue #867 (silent cross-issue fallback). `get_prompt_baseline(agent_type, issue_number=N)` now returns `None` when no baseline exists for that specific issue — the caller seeds a new baseline from the first observed prompt instead of inheriting a stale prior-issue value. The prior silent fallback (Issue #867) caused every new batch issue's first agent dispatch to compare against the prior issue's baseline, guaranteeing a false-positive 20% shrinkage block on every batch transition. New function `get_cross_issue_baseline(agent_type, *, exclude_issue=None)` provides the explicit cross-issue lookup for callers that need it. Cross-issue drift detection is preserved via the cumulative-drift mechanism (`record_batch_observation` + `get_cumulative_shrinkage` — Issue #794), which tracks trajectory across the full batch rather than triggering on a single first-dispatch comparison. Closes the structural source of first-dispatch shrinkage blocks across all `/implement` batch runs. 4 tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_867_cross_issue_baseline_fallback.py` and `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_issue764_per_issue_baseline.py` rewritten for the new contract (net zero new test functions — rewrites, not additions). `docs/LIBRARIES.md` updated with corrected `get_prompt_baseline` behavior and new `get_cross_issue_baseline` entry.

- **Three framework-friction fixes for fix-mode pipeline enforcement** (Issue #1088): F1 — `implement-fix.md` STEP F3 now includes a `record_pytest_gate_passed()` call after the pytest re-run passes; previously the gate-recording step was absent from fix mode, causing STEP F4 reviewer/doc-master dispatch to hit an ordering violation block. F2 — `detect_missing_security_review()` in `pipeline_intent_validator.py` now excludes test files by checking both `startswith("tests/")` and `"/tests/" in file_path`; the prior bare-prefix check silently missed absolute paths recorded by the JSONL activity logger, generating false-positive `missing_security_review` WARNING findings whenever test files touching hook-related paths were edited. F3 — `implement-fix.md` STEP F1 now calls `clear_prompt_baselines()` before initializing pipeline state; stale baselines from prior full-pipeline sessions were causing false-positive prompt-integrity blocks in fix mode's naturally shorter prompts. 2 regression tests added to `TestMissingSecurityReview` class in `tests/unit/lib/test_pipeline_intent_validator.py`.

- **`test_hook_in_all_settings_templates` assertion corrected to honour `_GLOBAL_FREE_TEMPLATES` invariant** (Issue #1085): `TestHookRegistration.test_hook_in_all_settings_templates` in `tests/unit/hooks/test_conversation_archiver.py` previously asserted that `conversation_archiver` was present in ALL settings templates, contradicting the Issue #944 invariant that `conversation_archiver` is a global hook registered only in `~/.claude/settings.json` and must NOT appear in the four user-facing per-repo templates (`settings.default.json`, `settings.granular-bash.json`, `settings.permission-batching.json`, `settings.strict-mode.json`) to prevent double execution. The fix narrows the positive assertion to dogfooding templates only and adds a companion `test_archiver_absent_from_global_free_templates` that enforces the inverse (archiver absent from the four `_GLOBAL_FREE_TEMPLATES`). No production code or settings templates were changed. 39 tests now pass in `tests/unit/hooks/test_conversation_archiver.py` (was 38 — one new test added).
- **`extract_and_label_intent_corpus.py` — make cost cap configurable and add call-count cap (subscription auth)** (Issue #1070): The `CostTracker.cap_usd=0.50` default was the original two-judge runaway-loop safety net, but became fictional under the #1043 single-judge `claude -p` refactor where Claude Max subscription auth makes per-call dollar cost $0. Real-corpus runs of 130 prompts hit the dollar cap at entry 83 — the runaway-loop safety net was firing when there was no runaway and no cost. Two new CLI flags now decouple the dollar accounting from the actual safety net. `--cost-cap-usd FLOAT` (default `0.50`) — set to `0` to disable the dollar cap entirely under subscription auth where per-call cost is $0. `--max-calls INT` (default `500`) — the actual runaway-loop safety net; set to `0` to disable. `CostTracker.__init__` gains `max_calls` (keyword-only, default `_MAX_CALLS_DEFAULT=500`); `cap_usd<=0` and `max_calls<=0` independently disable each cap. `would_exceed_cap` now returns `True` if either active cap would be exceeded; the label loop emits a cap-specific warning (`Cost cap $X.XX would be exceeded` vs `Call cap (N calls) would be exceeded`) so users see which knob to turn. Backward-compatible: existing `CostTracker(cap_usd=0.5)` callers keep working; defaults preserve today's behavior for API-key users (130 prompts at $0.005/call would still trip the $0.50 dollar cap first). 7 new regression tests in `tests/unit/scripts/test_extract_and_label_intent_corpus.py` (cap_usd=0 disables dollar cap, max_calls=N caps count, max_calls=0 disables call cap, both caps independent, --cost-cap-usd CLI flag flows through, --max-calls CLI flag flows through, backward-compat constructor).
- **`extract_and_label_intent_corpus.py` — strip markdown code fences from `claude -p` judge response** (Issue #1065): `_call_claude_p_judge` now strips ```` ```json ... ``` ```` (and bare ```` ``` ```` ) markdown fences from `envelope["result"]` before `json.loads`. `claude -p` with Haiku frequently wraps structured output in markdown code fences even when the prompt asks for raw JSON, causing every label call to silently fail with `JSONDecodeError` and be dropped as `judge_failure`. Without the fix, the corpus extractor produced zero successful classifications on real `claude -p` runs (after #1064 was already applied). The strip handles ```` ```json ````, bare ```` ``` ````, single-line variants, and trailing whitespace after the closing fence; falls through to the original whitespace-only strip when no fence is present. No CLI flags, schema, or auth changes. +3 regression tests in `tests/unit/scripts/test_extract_and_label_intent_corpus.py`.
- **`extract_and_label_intent_corpus.py` — nested `claude -p` session no longer inherits project context** (Issue #1064): `_call_claude_p_judge` now passes `cwd=str(Path.home())` to `subprocess.run`, so the spawned `claude -p` session starts in `$HOME` instead of the repo root. Previously the subprocess inherited the project CWD, causing Claude Code to load this repo's `CLAUDE.md`, hooks, and skills into the context window — producing an Anthropic API `prompt_too_long` rejection and silently falling back to synthetic labels on every real-labeling run. No change to CLI flags, output corpus schema, or auth model. +1 regression test in `tests/unit/scripts/test_extract_and_label_intent_corpus.py`.

### Changed
- refactor(scripts): #1043 follow-up — `extract_and_label_intent_corpus.py` is
  now single-judge via `claude -p` (was two-judge Anthropic+OpenRouter).
  Removes `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` dependency. Per-entry schema field rename:
  `judge_a` + `judge_b` → `judge`. Committed corpus fixture regenerated.

### Added
- **Cluster selector for drain-queue tracker filtering** (Issue #1284): New pure function `select_next_cluster(clusters)` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/cluster_selector.py` filters tracker-shaped issues from drain-queue candidates and prefers their lowest-numbered open leaf children when present. Detects trackers via: title prefix `[TRACKER]`, label combination "root-cause" + "enhancement", or body structure with ≥2 phase headings + ≥2 issue references. When encountering a tracker with open leaves, returns a synthetic single-issue cluster pointing to the lowest leaf. Not yet wired into `/drain-queue` command — integration tracked separately. Added 5 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_cluster_selector.py`.

- **Coordinator bypass verdict tracking** (Issue #1279): Extended `record_plan_critic_skipped()` with optional `bypass_reason` parameter and added new `write_coordinator_bypass_verdict()` function in `pipeline_completion_state.py` to enable drain-queue coordinators to record machine-readable audit trails when bypassing plan-critic for mechanical extensions. New `/drain-queue` command documentation section "Coordinator Bypass of plan-critic" details the two-call protocol. 3 new tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1279_coordinator_bypass_verdict.py` verify the bypass tracking logic.
- **M2 calibration — intent classifier corpus extraction and per-class accuracy measurement** (Issue #1043): New script `scripts/extract_and_label_intent_corpus.py` extracts real user prompts from `~/.claude/archive/sessions.db` (last 30 days, all projects), applies PII scrubbing, deduplication, and length filtering, then uses two judges (Anthropic + non-Anthropic via OpenRouter) to label each prompt with one of the 13 intent classes — only unanimously-agreed entries are written to the corpus. Falls back to a synthetic-fallback corpus (`tests/fixtures/intent_classifier_real_corpus.json`, 108 entries) when API keys are unavailable. New script `scripts/measure_intent_classifier.py` runs the real `IntentClassifier` against the corpus, computes per-class precision/recall/F1 and a confusion matrix, and writes the baseline to `docs/intent_classifier_calibration.json`. Results are idempotently spliced into `docs/INTENT-CLASSIFICATION.md` via `<!-- BEGIN/END: M2 calibration metrics -->` sentinels. CI regression gate `tests/regression/test_intent_classifier_corpus.py` (gated on `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`) enforces that per-class F1 must not drop >0.05 from baseline and macro F1 must not drop >0.03. Synthetic-fallback run shows `security_critical` F1=0.985 (recall=100%) and all other classes at F1=0.000 with support=0 — this is expected: the synthetic corpus has overwhelming `security_critical` representation; a real-session two-judge run is required for meaningful per-class coverage. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` regression step already passes `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` from secrets. `docs/SCRIPTS.md` gains entries for both new scripts. 33 new tests: 17 in `tests/unit/scripts/test_extract_and_label_intent_corpus.py` (PII scrubbing, dedup, two-judge agreement logic, synthetic-fallback) and 16 in `tests/regression/test_intent_classifier_corpus.py` (corpus schema validation, PII checks, per-class F1 regression gate, macro F1 regression gate).

- **M2 — AskUserQuestion round-trip on AMBIGUOUS classification** (Issue #1024): When the intent classifier returns `AMBIGUOUS` and both `INTENT_CLASSIFIER_ENABLED` and `INTENT_CLASSIFIER_ENFORCE` are `true`, the `UserPromptSubmit` hook now appends an `additionalContext` block instructing Claude to present the user with seven canonical intent options (IMPLEMENT, REFACTOR, TEST, DOC, CONFIG, STATUS_QUERY, EXPLORATION) via `AskUserQuestion`. After the user responds, Claude runs the new CLI script `plugins/autonomous-dev/scripts/persist_intent_answer.py --session-id <id> --intent <choice>`, which calls `session_mode.update_session_mode_partial()` to write `clarified_intent` onto the existing session-mode artifact. The next PreToolUse gate reads `clarified_intent` and `enforcement_decision.py` Priority 4.5 (a new 9th priority inserted between the existing priorities 4 and 5) honors the user's selection, bypassing the pessimistic `fail_open` and `requires_security_audit` short-circuits that AMBIGUOUS normally activates. A loop guard (`clarification_asked` field on the artifact) prevents re-asking within a session. Security boundary: SECURITY_CRITICAL is produced exclusively by the regex pre-gate and can never be AMBIGUOUS, so the round-trip is never offered when a security keyword matched; `security_critical` and `ambiguous` are explicitly rejected by `persist_intent_answer.py`. Session-mode artifact expands from 12 to 14 fields — `clarification_asked` (bool, default `False`) and `clarified_intent` (str or `null`, default `null`) are additive; SCHEMA_VERSION stays at 1. New functions exported from `session_mode.py`: `update_session_mode_partial()` (read-modify-write with closed allowlist), `effective_intent_class()` (resolves clarified vs. original). Session-id values inlined into the template are sanitized through `_SAFE_SESSION_ID_RE` (strip non-`[A-Za-z0-9_-]`) and inserted via `str.replace` on a sentinel (never `str.format`) to prevent injection DoS. 47 new tests: 6 in existing `tests/unit/lib/test_enforcement_decision.py` (clarified-intent overrides + Priority 4.5 security defense-in-depth), 13 in new `tests/unit/lib/test_session_mode_clarification.py`, 11 in new `tests/unit/hooks/test_unified_prompt_validator_clarification.py` (adversarial inputs + brace regression), 11 in new `tests/unit/scripts/test_persist_intent_answer.py`, and 6 in new `tests/integration/test_ambiguous_clarification_round_trip.py`. `docs/INTENT-CLASSIFICATION.md` updated: AskUserQuestion round-trip section, M2 phase roadmap row, 9-priority enforcement_decision description, 14-field artifact description, new related-file entries for `persist_intent_answer.py` and updated test counts.

### Security
- **`hook_timing.py` — three MEDIUM security hardening fixes** (Issue #1056): Security-auditor review of the W0 telemetry library (#1012) flagged three findings. Finding 1: adversarial or malformed `hook_name` values could produce unbounded JSONL rows and break downstream parsers — fixed by capping at `MAX_HOOK_NAME_LENGTH=128` on the write path in `emit_timing_event`. Finding 2: timing log files were created with world-readable permissions on multi-user systems — fixed by adding `LOG_FILE_MODE=0o600` and enforcing it at file-creation time via `os.open` opener plus an `os.chmod` backstop for pre-existing files (CWE-732). Finding 3: `OSError` messages from failed log writes contained absolute paths leaked to stderr — fixed by new `_sanitize_os_error()` helper that rewrites every absolute path in the error string to its basename before writing to stderr, preventing internal directory-tree disclosure. 16 regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1056_hook_timing_hardening.py`; 1 adjusted unit test in `tests/unit/lib/test_hook_timing.py` (monkeypatch target updated for new `open` usage pattern).

- **Phase 3 prompt-injection defense — extend `_wrap_user_input` across GenAI callers** (Issue #1007, follow-up to #960): Adopts the Phase 2 wrapping helper across 8 additional `GenAIAnalyzer` callers (10 sites total), giving the codebase uniform prompt-injection defense at every LLM integration point. New helper `_safe_wrap(text)` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/hooks/genai_utils.py` simplifies adoption (single-line invocation, never raises). Modified callers — Pattern A (kwarg substitution): `complexity_assessor.py` (`feature_description`), `scope_detector.py` (`issue_text`), `issue_scope_detector.py` (`issue_text`), `alignment_assessor.py` (2 sites: `dependencies_sample`/`config_files`/`readme_content`; enum-constrained scalars like `primary_language` left unwrapped), `genai_refactor_analyzer.py` (5 sites covering `doc_content`/`source_content`/`test_source`/`source_under_test`/`function_source`/`references_summary`/`original_analysis`), `security_scan.py` (`line`+`variable_name`; `secret_type` regex-catalog constant unwrapped), `auto_fix_docs.py` (`item_name`; `item_type` constrained literal unwrapped). Pattern B (.format pre-substitution): `feature_completion_detector.py` wraps both `feature` and `evidence` at `.format()` time since `analyzer.analyze()` receives the already-formatted prompt. Audit verified 4 false positives (`error_analyzer.py`, `codebase_analyzer.py`, `enforce_prunable_threshold.py`, `align_project_retrofit.py`) do NOT call `GenAIAnalyzer` and are NOT modified — locked by a parametrized regression test. 26 new test functions: 4 in `tests/unit/hooks/test_genai_utils_safe_wrap.py` (helper guarantees: wraps, escapes, coerces non-string, never raises), 21 in `tests/unit/lib/test_phase3_wrap_adoption.py` (8 wrap-adoption tests parametrized over multi-site files, plus 4 false-positive lock tests), and 1 existing-test adjustment in `tests/unit/lib/test_scope_detector_genai.py` (assertion updated from raw to wrapped form). `docs/INTENT-CLASSIFICATION.md` and `CHANGELOG.md` updated.

### Changed
- **docs: doc-drift fixes for Issue #1022 (M1 baseline publisher)** — `docs/SCRIPTS.md` gains three new entries in the Hook Telemetry section: `scripts/hook_perf_report.py` (p50/p95/p99 latency report, used by capture and publish scripts), `scripts/capture_baseline.py` (synthetic baseline driver), and `scripts/publish_hook_baseline.py` (baseline publisher with `--post --issue N` cross-post support, `synthetic-v0` / `real-workday` data_kind distinction, AC1 deferral note). `docs/research/W0_TELEMETRY.md` gains a new section 7 "Downstream consumers" cross-referencing `hook_perf_report.py`, `capture_baseline.py`, and `publish_hook_baseline.py` — establishing that any schema breaking change must be coordinated with these consumers. (Issue #1022)

### Added
- **Cluster selector for drain-queue tracker filtering** (Issue #1284): New pure function `select_next_cluster(clusters)` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/cluster_selector.py` filters tracker-shaped issues from drain-queue candidates and prefers their lowest-numbered open leaf children when present. Detects trackers via: title prefix `[TRACKER]`, label combination "root-cause" + "enhancement", or body structure with ≥2 phase headings + ≥2 issue references. When encountering a tracker with open leaves, returns a synthetic single-issue cluster pointing to the lowest leaf. Not yet wired into `/drain-queue` command — integration tracked separately. Added 5 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_cluster_selector.py`.

- **Coordinator bypass verdict tracking** (Issue #1279): Extended `record_plan_critic_skipped()` with optional `bypass_reason` parameter and added new `write_coordinator_bypass_verdict()` function in `pipeline_completion_state.py` to enable drain-queue coordinators to record machine-readable audit trails when bypassing plan-critic for mechanical extensions. New `/drain-queue` command documentation section "Coordinator Bypass of plan-critic" details the two-call protocol. 3 new tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1279_coordinator_bypass_verdict.py` verify the bypass tracking logic.
- **M1 hook telemetry baseline publisher** (Issue #1022, milestone #943): New script `scripts/publish_hook_baseline.py` reads a baseline JSONL (produced by `scripts/capture_baseline.py`) and emits two derived artifacts next to it: `<stem>.summary.json` (machine-readable aggregated stats with a metadata block — `captured_at`, `generated_at`, `git_sha`, `platform`, `schema_version`, `source_jsonl`, `row_count`, `data_kind`) and `<stem>.summary.md` (human-readable report with a Top-5 slowest hooks (by p95) table, a Top-5 most-blocked gates (by block ratio) table, and a Baseline policy section). Aggregation reuses `scripts/hook_perf_report.py` (`aggregate`, `stream_rows`) — no recreation. The `data_kind` field distinguishes `synthetic-v0` (row count < 500 OR timespan < 1h) from `real-workday`. Optional `--post --issue N` cross-posts to a GitHub issue idempotently via an embedded sentinel `<!-- hook-timing-baseline:<label> -->`; existing comments are PATCHed instead of duplicated. All `gh` calls use list-arg subprocess invocations with `shell=False` and a 15-second timeout. Issue numbers validated as positive int ≤ 10_000_000 (CWE-20 hardening mirror of `lib/github_issue_closer.py`). Comment bodies truncated to 60_000 chars (under GitHub's 65_536 limit) with a footer linking to the full artifact. Three artifacts now committed under `baselines/`: `2026-05-pre-refactor.jsonl` (existing, untouched), `2026-05-pre-refactor.summary.json` (new), `2026-05-pre-refactor.summary.md` (new). New redirect tombstone at `docs/baselines/README.md` and a `BASELINE_POLICY` section in `baselines/README.md` documenting refresh triggers and the synthetic-vs-real-workday distinction. **AC1 deferred**: this baseline is `synthetic-v0` (~5 invocations × 24 hooks, 119 rows) and does NOT satisfy AC1 of #1022 (real-workday capture, ≥4h active session). The `synthetic-v0` literal is asserted in `tests/integration/test_baseline_summary_artifacts_present.py` and the methodology blockquote is rendered in the .summary.md so the deferral is visible at PR review and on the cross-posted issue. Issue #1022 stays open after merge until a real-workday refresh is performed. 26 new tests: 19 unit (`tests/unit/scripts/test_publish_hook_baseline.py` — metadata extraction, markdown rendering, GitHub comment truncation, gh CLI mock invocation, CLI argument validation) and 7 integration (`tests/integration/test_baseline_summary_artifacts_present.py` — artifact presence, metadata schema, table headers, baseline policy section, AC1 reality lock).

- **W0 per-hook timing observability** (Issue #1012): New library `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/hook_timing.py` provides `HookTimer` — a context manager that wraps each hook's `__main__` block and emits one JSONL row per invocation to `~/.claude/logs/hook_timings_YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl` (daily-rotated). Schema: `{ts, hook, dur_ns, decision_shape, schema_version}`. All 24 active hooks wrapped. Durations recorded via `time.perf_counter_ns` (monotonic, sub-millisecond). Telemetry NEVER raises — read-only filesystem fallback writes to stderr; hook decision path is unaffected. Env vars: `HOOK_TIMING_DISABLED` (rollback switch), `HOOK_TIMING_DIR` (redirect log dir, used by tests and baseline capture). Companion scripts: `scripts/hook_perf_report.py` (p50/p95/p99 report from timing JSONL) and `scripts/capture_baseline.py` (baseline driver). Baseline file `baselines/2026-05-pre-refactor.jsonl` (119 rows). `hook_telemetry.py` extended with `"allow"` shape in `VALID_DECISION_SHAPES`. `pytest.ini` gains `perf` marker. Research notes at `docs/research/W0_TELEMETRY.md`. 62 new tests: 27 unit (`test_hook_timing.py`), 17 unit (`test_hook_perf_report.py`), 6 integration (`test_hook_timing_wraps_all_hooks.py`), 6 integration (`test_baseline_artifact_present.py`), 4 regression (`test_w0_telemetry_doc_present.py`), 2 perf (`test_telemetry_overhead.py`). `docs/LIBRARIES.md` gains entry #98 for `hook_timing.py`. `docs/ARCHITECTURE-OVERVIEW.md` library count updated to 216.

- **M0/C — heredoc env-var migration + stale-state GC** (Issue #1048, M0 umbrella #1041): Final child of the M0 run-isolation track. All 10 heredoc sites and 4 `rm -f` cleanup commands across `commands/implement.md`, `commands/implement-batch.md`, and `commands/implement-fix.md` that previously referenced the bare literal `/tmp/implement_pipeline_state.json` now use the env-var-aware form `${PIPELINE_STATE_FILE:-/tmp/implement_pipeline_state.json}`, making every sentinel reference honor the `PIPELINE_STATE_FILE` override exported at STEP 0. New private helper `_gc_stale_states(max_age_seconds=7200)` in `pipeline_completion_state.py` garbage-collects three glob patterns in `/tmp` older than 7200 seconds (2× `STALE_UNKNOWN_TTL_SECONDS`): `pipeline_agent_completions_*.json` (completion state), `implement_pipeline_*.json` (per-run sentinel files), and `pipeline_*.lock` (orphaned lockfiles); called at STEP 0 immediately before `RUN_ID` generation. Security guards in `unified_pre_tool.py` (`_check_bash_state_deletion`) and the HMAC fail-open mtime check in `pipeline_state.py` (`LEGACY_SENTINEL_PATH`) were explicitly NOT migrated — their hardcoded paths are intentional and pinned by regression tests. `docs/LIBRARIES.md` `pipeline_completion_state.py` entry bumped to v1.9.0 with full `_gc_stale_states` API documentation. `docs/PIPELINE-MODES.md` gains a stale-state GC paragraph in the state-file section. 26 new tests: 10 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_pipeline_state_gc.py` (stale removal, fresh preservation, lockfile cleanup, permission errors, result dict structure) and 16 integration tests in `tests/integration/test_implement_heredoc_honors_env.py` (env-var override verified in all three command files, scope-out pins for security guards and LEGACY_SENTINEL_PATH).

- **M0/B — STEP 0 RUN_ID + PIPELINE_STATE_FILE + lockfile + `--resume` auto-detect** (Issue #1047, M0 umbrella #1041): `RUN_ID` is now generated as a 16-character lowercase hex string via `secrets.token_hex(8)` instead of the legacy `date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S` format. STEP 0 of `commands/implement.md` exports `RUN_ID` and `PIPELINE_STATE_FILE=/tmp/implement_pipeline_<run_id>.json` to the process environment, then acquires an exclusive non-blocking lockfile (`/tmp/pipeline_<run_id>.lock`) via `fcntl.flock(LOCK_EX | LOCK_NB)`. A second concurrent `/implement` in the same process gets a clear "lock held" error message; OS releases the lock automatically on process death (no stale-mtime detection needed). `--resume <id>` now uses `classify_resume_id()` to auto-detect three accepted formats: `batch-*` prefix delegates to `implement-resume.md` (existing batch worktree resume, unchanged); 16-char hex performs the new single-run resume (loads completions via `get_completed_agents(sid, run_id=run_id)`); legacy `YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS` format is accepted for back-compat with in-flight pre-#1047 runs. Any other format is blocked with a message listing all 3 forms. `SessionStart-batch-recovery.sh` now exits early when `RUN_ID` env var is already set (skips implicit batch recovery). Five new public helpers in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/pipeline_state.py`: `generate_run_id()`, `get_lockfile_path(run_id)`, `acquire_run_lock(run_id) -> Optional[int]`, `release_run_lock(fd)`, `classify_resume_id(arg)`. 22 new unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_pipeline_state_run_id_helpers.py`, 3 integration tests in `tests/integration/test_pipeline_run_id_resume.py`, 5 integration tests in `tests/integration/test_pipeline_concurrent_runs.py` (30 total). `docs/LIBRARIES.md` `pipeline_state.py` entry updated with the new public API. `docs/PIPELINE-MODES.md` "How to Resume" section corrected — state file path was wrong (`~/.claude/pipeline_state/{run_id}.json` does not exist; canonical path is `/tmp/implement_pipeline_<run_id>.json`) and now documents all 3 `--resume` forms. `plugins/autonomous-dev/CHANGELOG.md` mirror entry added. **From this PR forward, batch `/implement` is reliable** — distinct run_ids across processes do not collide on shared state.

- **Intent classifier Phase E — enforcement cutover** (Issue #999): Completes the intent classifier track started in Phase 1 (#971). When `INTENT_CLASSIFIER_ENFORCE=true` (default `false`), three pipeline-gating hooks consult the per-session artifact written by Phase D and skip non-floor checks for low-risk intent classes (`doc`, `config`, `typo`, `status_query`, `conversation`). Hard-floor hooks (Phase C registry) always fire regardless of intent class. New libraries: `lib/hook_stdin.py` — one-shot cached stdin reader providing `read_stdin_once()` and `extract_session_id()` so Phase E hooks can read the PreToolUse payload without exhausting the stream across multiple call sites; `lib/enforcement_decision.py` — pure policy layer with 8-rule priority chain (`should_skip_enforcement()`) — zero filesystem side effects, never raises, fail-safe direction is always enforce. Modified hooks: `unified_pre_tool.py` (5 wrap sites via `_phase_e_skip()` helper), `plan_gate.py`, `plan_mode_exit_detector.py`. New telemetry decision shape `"mode_skip"` added to `hook_telemetry.py` `VALID_DECISION_SHAPES` — emitted on skip path to `.claude/logs/hook-blocks.jsonl` for observability. Reader-side API added to `session_mode.py`: `read_session_mode(session_id)` and `should_pipeline_enforce(intent_class)`. Single env var rollback: unset `INTENT_CLASSIFIER_ENFORCE` or set to any non-"true" value to revert all Phase E gating. `docs/INTENT-CLASSIFICATION.md` updated: Phase E roadmap row, Phase E section, `INTENT_CLASSIFIER_ENFORCE` behavior, related files table. `docs/LIBRARIES.md` gains entries for `hook_stdin.py` and `enforcement_decision.py`; `session_mode.py` and `hook_telemetry.py` entries updated. `docs/HOOKS.md` and `docs/HOOK-REGISTRY.md` updated with Phase E notes. 48 new test functions: 13 in `tests/unit/lib/test_enforcement_decision.py`, 10 in `tests/unit/lib/test_hook_stdin.py`, 12 in `tests/unit/lib/test_session_mode_reader.py`, 10 in `tests/unit/hooks/test_phase_e_integration.py`, 3 in `tests/integration/test_enforcement_mode_cutover.py`.

- **Intent classifier Phase 2 — prompt-injection defense** (Issue #960): Hardens `lib/intent_classifier.py` against prompt-injection attacks on the LLM classification path (OWASP LLM01:2025). User prompts are now wrapped in `<user_input>…</user_input>` XML delimiters via a new `_wrap_user_input(text)` helper in `hooks/genai_utils.py` before being substituted into `_LLM_PROMPT_TEMPLATE`; `html.escape(text, quote=False)` encodes `&`, `<`, and `>` so an attacker cannot inject structural tokens that escape the delimiter. Apostrophes and double-quotes are preserved (`quote=False`) because they are common in legitimate prompts. A module-load guard (`_validate_template_integrity`) raises `RuntimeError` (not `assert` — survives `python -O`) at import time if the template no longer contains `<user_input>`; refusing to load is safer than silently degrading to a vulnerable state. `_wrap_user_input` lives in `genai_utils.py` for cross-codebase reuse — other `GenAIAnalyzer` callers can adopt the same defense via follow-up issues. The security regex pre-gate and fail-open contract are unchanged; this fix only affects the LLM path for non-security-keyword prompts. 8 new tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_intent_classifier.py` (`TestPromptInjectionResistance`): injection-escape verification, RuntimeError guard, `_wrap_user_input` round-trip, LLM path uses wrapped form, and fallback behavior when `_wrap_user_input` is unavailable. `docs/INTENT-CLASSIFICATION.md` updated: Phase 2 roadmap row, new Architecture section 5 (prompt-injection defense), test count corrected. `docs/LIBRARIES.md` `intent_classifier.py` entry updated with Phase 2 description and corrected test count. `docs/HOOKS.md` `genai_utils.py` entry updated to document `_wrap_user_input`. Prerequisite before `INTENT_CLASSIFIER_ENFORCE=true` rollout.

- **Intent classifier Phase D — session-mode artifact writer** (Issue #998): New library `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/session_mode.py` wires the semantic intent classifier (Phase 1, Issue #971) to a per-session JSON artifact at `/tmp/session_mode_<sha256(session_id)[:8]>.json`. Written after each `UserPromptSubmit` when `INTENT_CLASSIFIER_ENABLED=true`. The 12-field artifact schema (`schema_version`, `session_id`, `intent_class`, `confidence`, `regex_hit`, `llm_used`, `fail_open`, `requires_security_audit`, `prompt_hash`, `written_at`, `expires_at`, `enforce_mode`) provides O(1) session-mode lookup for downstream Phase E PreToolUse hooks without requiring them to walk the JSONL activity log. Atomic write via `tempfile.mkstemp` + `os.replace()` (same-filesystem rename). Fail-open contract: `write_session_mode()` MUST NEVER raise; any exception is swallowed so hook output is byte-identical on write failure. Artifact TTL: 1 hour (`expires_at`); readers MUST treat expired artifacts as missing. New env var `INTENT_CLASSIFIER_ENFORCE` (default `false`) plumbed in Phase D and captured in the `enforce_mode` field; has no behavioral effect until Phase E. `unified_prompt_validator.py` wired to call `write_session_mode()` when `INTENT_CLASSIFIER_ENABLED=true` and classifier returns a non-None result. `docs/LIBRARIES.md` gains entry for `session_mode.py`. `docs/INTENT-CLASSIFICATION.md` updated with Phase D section, `INTENT_CLASSIFIER_ENFORCE` documentation, and updated phase roadmap. `docs/HOOKS.md` and `docs/HOOK-REGISTRY.md` updated with `INTENT_CLASSIFIER_ENFORCE` env var entries. 17 new test functions: 14 in `tests/unit/lib/test_session_mode.py`, 3 in `tests/integration/test_intent_classifier_observe_mode.py`.

### Fixed
- **Hard-floor registry function names corrected** (Issue #1004): Three entries in `plugins/autonomous-dev/config/hard_floor_hooks.json` and the matching `_FALLBACK_HARD_FLOOR_HOOKS` constant in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/hard_floor.py` used fictional function names that did not match any actual function in `unified_pre_tool.py`. Corrected: `_check_settings_json_writes` → `_detect_settings_json_write`, `_check_protected_infrastructure` → `_is_protected_infrastructure`, `_check_dangerous_bash` → `_detect_git_bypass`. The reason string for `_detect_git_bypass` was also revised from "rm -rf with unresolved variables, force-push to master" to "Detects git bypass attempts: force-push, --no-verify, git reset --hard, git clean -f", which accurately describes what `_detect_git_bypass` actually checks. Honest gap documented: the aspirational "rm -rf with unresolved variables" semantic is NOT implemented anywhere in the codebase and is not claimed by any registry entry. `docs/LIBRARIES.md` entry #97 updated with the correct function names.

### Changed
- **docs: doc-drift fixes for Issue #997 (Phase C hard-floor registry)** — `docs/LIBRARIES.md` gains entry #97 for `hard_floor.py` (public API: `is_hard_floor`, `get_observability_hooks`; dual-source fallback design; full list of hard-floor and observability hooks; 6 test functions). `docs/ARCHITECTURE-OVERVIEW.md` library count updated from 214 to 215; `hard_floor.py` added to Infrastructure key libraries list. (Issue #997)

- **docs: doc-drift fix for Issue #996 Phase B changes** — `docs/LIBRARIES.md` entry #93 (`hook_path_validator.py`) updated: "four issue categories" corrected to "five issue categories"; new `path_style` category and `DISALLOWED_PATH_SUBSTRINGS` registry documented; `_validate_path_style()` behavior described (fires on script-shaped tokens even when file absent; `~/.claude/` and `--git-common-dir` flagged at `error` severity). (Issue #996)

- **BREAKING: standardized hook command paths in settings templates** (Issue #996, Phase B): All 6 settings templates shipped under `plugins/autonomous-dev/templates/` (`settings.autonomous-dev.json`, `settings.default.json`, `settings.granular-bash.json`, `settings.local.json`, `settings.permission-batching.json`, `settings.strict-mode.json`) now use a single canonical pattern for every hook command: `"$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/.claude/hooks/<NAME>.<py|sh>"`. Three legacy patterns are retired: `~/.claude/hooks/...` (hardcoded the global cache path), `"$(dirname \"$(git rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-common-dir)\")/plugins/autonomous-dev/hooks/..."` (resolved through the main repo's git dir), and `"$(dirname \"$(git rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-common-dir)\")/.claude/hooks/..."` (same root cause). **Semantic shift**: hooks now resolve to `<worktree>/.claude/hooks/` instead of `<main-repo>/plugins/autonomous-dev/hooks/`, which is project-local and worktree-aware by design (Phase A's `scripts/deploy-all.sh` + `sync_settings_hooks.py` populate that directory). **User instruction**: run `bash scripts/deploy-all.sh` after creating new worktrees so `<worktree>/.claude/hooks/` is populated; otherwise `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` will resolve to the worktree but the hook scripts will be missing and Claude Code will surface "Hook script does not exist" errors from `hook_path_validator.py`. `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/hook_path_validator.py` gains a new `_validate_path_style` validation pass and a `DISALLOWED_PATH_SUBSTRINGS` registry that flags any `~/.claude/` or `--git-common-dir` substring inside a script-shaped hook command with severity `error`. Personal permission entries (`~/.ssh/**`, `~/.aws/**`) inside the `permissions` block are explicitly preserved by the new test suite — those are NOT hook commands and remain in every template that previously had them. 5 new tests in `tests/unit/test_template_paths_consistent.py` lock in the canonical pattern and the personal-permission-entry preservation invariant.

- **BREAKING: project-local hooks are the default** (Issue #995, Phase A): `scripts/deploy-all.sh` and `install.sh` no longer register autonomous-dev hooks in `~/.claude/settings.json` by default. Per-repo `<repo>/.claude/settings.json` registration is unchanged — autonomous-dev itself still works exactly as before. Hook FILES still cache to `~/.claude/hooks/` via the existing `$GLOBAL_SUBDIRS` rsync (library cache for foreign repos that opt in later); only the **registration** in `~/.claude/settings.json` is now opt-in. Opt-in flag: `--global-settings` on both scripts (`bash scripts/deploy-all.sh --global-settings` or `bash install.sh --global-settings`). Precedence: `--no-global` (existing flag, controls global file rsync) wins over `--global-settings`. `migrate_hooks_format()` in `install.sh` is gated at BOTH the call site AND the function entry (defense-in-depth) so a future code path that calls it without checking `$GLOBAL_SETTINGS` cannot silently mutate the user's `~/.claude/settings.json` hooks block. Migration one-liner for users who previously had global hooks registered:
  ```bash
  cp ~/.claude/settings.json ~/.claude/settings.json.preglobal-hooks-strip
  python3 -c "import json,pathlib; p=pathlib.Path.home()/'.claude/settings.json'; d=json.loads(p.read_text()); d.pop('hooks',None); p.write_text(json.dumps(d,indent=2))"
  ```
  Recovery: `bash install.sh --reset-hooks` continues to work (shipped in #949) for users with bricked global hooks. 3 integration tests in `tests/integration/scripts/test_deploy_all_global_settings.py` lock the new default and the opt-in flag wiring in both scripts.

- **docs: doc-drift fix for Issue #995 changes** — `docs/SCRIPTS.md` `deploy-all.sh` entry updated to document the new default behavior (hooks NOT registered in `~/.claude/settings.json` by default), the `--global-settings` opt-in flag, and the full flags table (`--no-global`, `--local`, `--remote`, `--dry-run`, `--skip-validate`). (Issue #995)

- **docs: doc-drift fixes for Issue #972 changes** — `docs/LIBRARIES.md` gains entry for `hook_telemetry.py` (4 public functions: `log_block_event`, `is_telemetry_disabled`, `block_event_decorator`, `can_user_recover`; `HOOK_TELEMETRY_DISABLED=1` rollback). `docs/LIBRARIES.md` `hook_recovery.py` entry updated to note `log_block_with_recovery()` is now a deprecated shim delegating to `hook_telemetry.log_block_event`. `docs/SCRIPTS.md` gains entries for `scripts/hook_block_summary.py` (hook block triage — `--last/--since/--top/--json` flags, dual-read of `hook-blocks.jsonl` + `hook-recovery.jsonl`) and `scripts/check_doc_links.py` (stdlib Markdown link validator, replaces `markdown-link-check` Node dependency). `docs/ARCHITECTURE-OVERVIEW.md` library count updated from 213 to 214; `hook_recovery.py` Infrastructure entry updated to document the shim and reference the new `hook_telemetry.py`. (Issue #972)

### Added
- **Cluster selector for drain-queue tracker filtering** (Issue #1284): New pure function `select_next_cluster(clusters)` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/cluster_selector.py` filters tracker-shaped issues from drain-queue candidates and prefers their lowest-numbered open leaf children when present. Detects trackers via: title prefix `[TRACKER]`, label combination "root-cause" + "enhancement", or body structure with ≥2 phase headings + ≥2 issue references. When encountering a tracker with open leaves, returns a synthetic single-issue cluster pointing to the lowest leaf. Not yet wired into `/drain-queue` command — integration tracked separately. Added 5 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_cluster_selector.py`.

- **Coordinator bypass verdict tracking** (Issue #1279): Extended `record_plan_critic_skipped()` with optional `bypass_reason` parameter and added new `write_coordinator_bypass_verdict()` function in `pipeline_completion_state.py` to enable drain-queue coordinators to record machine-readable audit trails when bypassing plan-critic for mechanical extensions. New `/drain-queue` command documentation section "Coordinator Bypass of plan-critic" details the two-call protocol. 3 new tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1279_coordinator_bypass_verdict.py` verify the bypass tracking logic.
- **Hard-floor hook registry — Phase C** (Issue #997): New library `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/hard_floor.py` (~155 LOC) and companion config `plugins/autonomous-dev/config/hard_floor_hooks.json` declare an explicit hard floor of hooks and intra-hook functions that MUST always fire regardless of session mode. Catastrophe-prevention hard-floor entries: `security_scan.py` (file-level; blocks secrets in commits), `unified_pre_tool.py::_check_bash_state_deletion` (rm/truncate of pipeline state), `unified_pre_tool.py::_check_settings_json_writes` (writes to settings.json), `unified_pre_tool.py::_check_protected_infrastructure` (writes to hooks/lib/agents/commands), `unified_pre_tool.py::_check_dangerous_bash` (rm -rf with unresolved variables, force-push to master). Always-run observability hooks (non-enforcement, logging only): `session_activity_logger.py`, `conversation_archiver.py`, `unified_session_tracker.py`. Public API: `is_hard_floor(hook_name, function_name=None) -> bool` and `get_observability_hooks() -> list[str]`. Dual-source design: authoritative source is the JSON config; fallback to in-module constants when config is missing or malformed — fallback is conservative and ONLY returns True for explicitly listed entries. NO BEHAVIOR CHANGE in this issue — registry only. Phase D (separate issue) will wire session-mode logic to call `is_hard_floor` before any disable/skip path. `docs/LIBRARIES.md` gains entry #97 for `hard_floor.py`. `docs/ARCHITECTURE-OVERVIEW.md` library count updated from 214 to 215; `hard_floor.py` added to Infrastructure key libraries list. 6 test functions (16 parametrized cases) in `tests/unit/lib/test_hard_floor.py`.

- **Hook composition capstone: unified telemetry + contract documentation** (Issue #972, #942-D, Wave 1 capstone): New library `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/hook_telemetry.py` provides a single canonical surface (`log_block_event`) for recording every "deny" decision across all three deny shapes used in the harness — `("deny", reason)` tuple (used by `unified_pre_tool.py` via `output_decision`), `{"decision": "block", ...}` dict (used by `unified_prompt_validator.py`), and `sys.exit(2)` (used by `enforce_orchestrator.py`). Each block emits one JSONL row to `.claude/logs/hook-blocks.jsonl` with stable schema `{ts, hook_name, decision_shape, reason, metadata, session_id, cwd}`. Telemetry NEVER raises — `OSError` falls back to a `[hook-telemetry]` stderr line. New env var `HOOK_TELEMETRY_DISABLED=1` rolls the telemetry surface back to a no-op without redeploying; `HOOK_RECOVERY_DISABLED=1` is honored as a one-time-warning deprecation alias. New `block_event_decorator(hook_name)` factory wraps `output_decision` to instrument all 13 deny sites in `unified_pre_tool.py` through one decorator (idempotent — double-wrapping is a no-op). `unified_prompt_validator.py` and `enforce_orchestrator.py` use explicit `log_block_event` calls before their respective block-output sites. New documentation `docs/HOOK-COMPOSITION.md` (~150 lines, 6 sections) captures the cross-hook contract from #942: universal bypass (#942-A), recoverability invariant (#942-B), tool-intent classification (#942-C), read/write/delete distinction rules, telemetry contract (#942-D), and a checklist for adding new hooks. Cross-linked from `CONTRIBUTING.md` ("Adding a New Hook" section) and `plugins/autonomous-dev/docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md` ("Hook Block Telemetry" section). New triage script `scripts/hook_block_summary.py` (~280 LOC) reads BOTH `hook-blocks.jsonl` (new) AND `hook-recovery.jsonl` (legacy from #970) for one release cycle and dedups rows by `(timestamp, hook_name, reason)`. Supports `--last 7d`/`--since <ISO>`, `--top N`, and `--json` flags. Reproduces the per-category breakdown from the #942 issue body within ±5% (verified by `tests/regression/test_hook_block_summary_against_942.py` against `tests/regression/fixtures/942_empirical_numbers.json`). New stdlib link checker `scripts/check_doc_links.py` (~125 LOC) replaces the markdown-link-check Node dependency. New CI gate test `tests/integration/test_new_hook_contract.py` AST-walks every active hook and reports any deny path lacking telemetry instrumentation (Phase 1 — warn-only). Three install manifests (`plugins/autonomous-dev/install_manifest.json`, `plugins/autonomous-dev/config/install_manifest.json`, `.claude/config/install_manifest.json`) updated to register `hook_telemetry.py`. 51 new test functions: 28 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_hook_telemetry.py`, 12 regression tests in `tests/regression/test_hook_block_summary.py`, 2 in `tests/regression/test_hook_block_summary_against_942.py`, 6 integration tests in `tests/integration/test_three_hook_shapes_emit_events.py`, 3 in `tests/integration/test_new_hook_contract.py`, 1 in `tests/integration/test_hook_composition_doc_links.py`.

### Deprecated
- **`lib/hook_recovery.log_block_with_recovery`** (Issue #972): Replaced by `lib/hook_telemetry.log_block_event`. The old function is preserved as a thin shim that delegates to the new function (writes redirected to `.claude/logs/hook-blocks.jsonl`, schema mapped via `decision_shape="legacy_recovery"` and legacy fields nested under `metadata`). All 4 existing call sites (3 in `unified_pre_tool.py`, 1 in `unified_session_tracker.py`) continue to work unchanged. New code SHOULD use `log_block_event` directly. Calls now emit a `DeprecationWarning`. Existing `.claude/logs/hook-recovery.jsonl` files are NOT migrated — the summary script reads both for one release cycle.

### Changed
- **docs: doc-drift fixes for Issue #970 changes** — `docs/HOOKS.md` `plan_mode_exit_detector.py` entry updated to document the AC#3 fast-path (marker born `stage="critique_done"` when a PROCEED verdict already exists at ExitPlanMode time); Safe Failure Behavior section gains a Hook Recovery Telemetry paragraph describing `hook_recovery.py`, `HOOK_RECOVERY_DISABLED`, and `.claude/logs/hook-recovery.jsonl`. `docs/HOOK-REGISTRY.md` `plan_mode_exit_detector` row updated to describe the AC#3 fast-path; new "Hook Recovery Telemetry" env var table added with `HOOK_RECOVERY_DISABLED` entry. `docs/SCRIPTS.md` Validation table gains `scripts/audit_hook_recovery.py` entry. `docs/ARCHITECTURE-OVERVIEW.md` library count updated from 212 to 213; `hook_recovery.py` added to Infrastructure key libraries list. (Issue #970)

### Added
- **Cluster selector for drain-queue tracker filtering** (Issue #1284): New pure function `select_next_cluster(clusters)` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/cluster_selector.py` filters tracker-shaped issues from drain-queue candidates and prefers their lowest-numbered open leaf children when present. Detects trackers via: title prefix `[TRACKER]`, label combination "root-cause" + "enhancement", or body structure with ≥2 phase headings + ≥2 issue references. When encountering a tracker with open leaves, returns a synthetic single-issue cluster pointing to the lowest leaf. Not yet wired into `/drain-queue` command — integration tracked separately. Added 5 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_cluster_selector.py`.

- **Coordinator bypass verdict tracking** (Issue #1279): Extended `record_plan_critic_skipped()` with optional `bypass_reason` parameter and added new `write_coordinator_bypass_verdict()` function in `pipeline_completion_state.py` to enable drain-queue coordinators to record machine-readable audit trails when bypassing plan-critic for mechanical extensions. New `/drain-queue` command documentation section "Coordinator Bypass of plan-critic" details the two-call protocol. 3 new tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1279_coordinator_bypass_verdict.py` verify the bypass tracking logic.
- **Hook recovery telemetry + concurrent-session sentinel hardening** (Issue #970, #937, #941): New library `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/hook_recovery.py` provides four public functions (`is_recovery_disabled`, `can_user_recover`, `log_block_with_recovery`, `clear_stale_state`) so deny-emitting hooks can leave a structured JSONL audit trail at `.claude/logs/hook-recovery.jsonl` instead of silently wedging the user. Telemetry is NEVER allowed to raise — `OSError` falls back to a `[hook-recovery]` stderr line and the underlying hook decision is preserved. New env var `HOOK_RECOVERY_DISABLED=1` rolls the telemetry surface back to a no-op without redeploying. New exemption registry at `plugins/autonomous-dev/config/hook_recovery_exemptions.json` (empty seed in Phase 1) allows a future second-pass scan to whitelist deny sites that intentionally do not need a recovery hint. New CI script `scripts/audit_hook_recovery.py` scans `unified_pre_tool.py` for `output_decision("deny", ...)` sites that lack a paired `log_block_with_recovery(` call in the same function; default mode (WARN-ONLY) prints findings and exits 0; `--strict` (also `AUDIT_HOOK_RECOVERY_STRICT=1`) exits 1. `unified_pre_tool.py` instruments three high-traffic deny paths (extension-deny, #528 workflow enforcement, #557/#606 env-spoofing) with `log_block_with_recovery()` calls. `unified_session_tracker.py` `_advance_plan_mode_stage()` records a telemetry row when the verdict-file consumption successfully advances the stage. `plan_mode_exit_detector.py` now reads `.claude/plan_critic_verdict.json` BEFORE writing the marker (AC#3 fix for Issue #937): when a PROCEED verdict already exists at ExitPlanMode time the marker is born `stage="critique_done"`, the verdict file is consumed, and the systemMessage informs the model that the pipeline is ready instead of demanding plan-critic. `_is_pipeline_active()` in `unified_pre_tool.py` now refreshes the pipeline state mtime ONLY when the file's `session_id` matches `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` (AC#4 / Issue #941): preserves Issue #636 (the owning session keeps its own mtime fresh during long pipelines) while preventing concurrent sessions from inadvertently keeping a foreign session's stale state alive. Three install manifests (`plugins/autonomous-dev/install_manifest.json`, `plugins/autonomous-dev/config/install_manifest.json`, `.claude/config/install_manifest.json`) updated to register `hook_recovery.py`. 35 new test functions: 21 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_hook_recovery.py`, 6 audit-script tests in `tests/unit/scripts/test_audit_hook_recovery.py`, 3 CI-audit integration tests in `tests/integration/test_ci_audits.py`, and 8 regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_937_938_941_970.py` covering AC#3, AC#4 (#636 preserved + #941 fixed), AC#5 (audit warn-only), and AC#7 (HOOK_RECOVERY_DISABLED rollback).

- **Tool-intent classifier for settings-write gate** (Issue #971, #942-C): New library `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/tool_intent.py` replaces ad-hoc substring matching in the settings-write protection gate of `unified_pre_tool.py` with a principled tool-intent classifier. The classifier dispatches by tool name for native Claude Code tools (`Read`, `Write`, `Edit`, etc.) and parses Bash commands via `shlex.split` to determine whether a command is READ, WRITE, or EXEC. Python `-c` snippets are analyzed via the existing AST-based `python_write_detector` library. Public API: `classify(tool_name, tool_input) -> Literal["READ","WRITE","EXEC"]`, `write_targets(tool_name, tool_input) -> List[str]`, `has_suspicious_exec(command) -> bool`. New CI gate script `scripts/audit_tool_intent_coverage.py` flags any tool name observed in activity logs that lacks a classification in `tool_intent.py` (exit 1 under `--strict`). `plugins/autonomous-dev/config/install_manifest.json` updated to register `tool_intent.py`. `docs/LIBRARIES.md` updated with `tool_intent.py` API entry. `docs/ARCHITECTURE-OVERVIEW.md` library count updated from 211 to 212. `docs/SCRIPTS.md` updated with `audit_tool_intent_coverage.py` entry. 119 new test functions: 78 in `tests/unit/lib/test_tool_intent.py`, 18 in `tests/unit/hooks/test_settings_write_via_intent.py`, 10 in `tests/unit/scripts/test_audit_tool_intent_coverage.py`, 6 added to `tests/unit/hooks/test_infrastructure_protection.py`, and 7 added to `tests/regression/smoke/test_python_settings_bypass.py`.

### Fixed
- **False-positive block on `python3 -c "json.load(open('settings.json'))"` in settings-write gate** (Issue #971): The settings-write protection gate in `unified_pre_tool.py` previously performed a keyword check against the raw command string. Commands that merely *read* a settings file (e.g., `python3 -c "json.load(open('settings.json'))"`, `cat settings.json | jq .hooks`, `grep hooks settings.json`) were incorrectly blocked because the string "settings" appeared in the command. The fix routes the gate through `tool_intent.py`'s `classify()` and `write_targets()` functions which classify by tool name and input shape rather than substring presence. READ operations on settings files are no longer blocked during `/implement` pipelines.

### Added
- **Cluster selector for drain-queue tracker filtering** (Issue #1284): New pure function `select_next_cluster(clusters)` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/cluster_selector.py` filters tracker-shaped issues from drain-queue candidates and prefers their lowest-numbered open leaf children when present. Detects trackers via: title prefix `[TRACKER]`, label combination "root-cause" + "enhancement", or body structure with ≥2 phase headings + ≥2 issue references. When encountering a tracker with open leaves, returns a synthetic single-issue cluster pointing to the lowest leaf. Not yet wired into `/drain-queue` command — integration tracked separately. Added 5 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_cluster_selector.py`.
