- **Coordinator bypass verdict tracking** (Issue #1279): Extended `record_plan_critic_skipped()` with optional `bypass_reason` parameter and added new `write_coordinator_bypass_verdict()` function in `pipeline_completion_state.py` to enable drain-queue coordinators to record machine-readable audit trails when bypassing plan-critic for mechanical extensions. New `/drain-queue` command documentation section "Coordinator Bypass of plan-critic" details the two-call protocol. 3 new tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1279_coordinator_bypass_verdict.py` verify the bypass tracking logic.
- **Universal hook bypass** (Issue #969): New library `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/hook_bypass.py` provides a single, universal bypass mechanism that every hook honors and that can be set from outside Claude Code so a deadlocked harness cannot prevent recovery. Two equivalent bypass signals: `AUTONOMOUS_DEV_BYPASS=1` (env var, process-scoped) or `.claude/.bypass` (file flag, project-scoped, walks up from cwd through up to 30 ancestor directories, does not follow symlinks). When either signal is active, every hook falls through to `allow` and appends a JSONL event to `.claude/logs/hook-bypass.jsonl` for later audit. Telemetry failures never block the bypass itself. Public API: `is_bypassed(start_dir=None)` and `log_bypass_used(hook_name, tool_name, reason)`. All modified hooks import and check `is_bypassed()` as their first action. `plugins/autonomous-dev/config/install_manifest.json` and `plugins/autonomous-dev/install_manifest.json` updated to register `hook_bypass.py`. `plugins/autonomous-dev/docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md` gains a "Universal Escape" section with usage examples for both signals. `README.md` updated with a "Universal hook bypass" escape hatch entry. `docs/HOOKS.md` updated with a "Universal Hook Bypass" section in the Safe Failure Behavior area. `docs/HOOK-REGISTRY.md` updated with `AUTONOMOUS_DEV_BYPASS` env var entry. `docs/LIBRARIES.md` updated with `hook_bypass.py` API entry. `docs/ARCHITECTURE-OVERVIEW.md` library count updated from 210 to 211. 35 test functions: 14 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_hook_bypass.py` and 21 regression tests in `tests/regression/test_universal_hook_bypass.py`.
- **`install.sh --reset-hooks` recovery path for bricked global hooks** (Issue #949): New standalone flag strips the entire `hooks` block from `~/.claude/settings.json` while preserving all other top-level keys (permissions, mcpServers, env, model, output style). A backup is written to `~/.claude/settings.json.preglobal-hooks-strip` before the in-place rewrite. Invocation: `bash <(curl -sSL .../install.sh) --reset-hooks` (or local clone path) from any terminal OUTSIDE Claude Code — useful when broken hooks block every prompt. The operation is idempotent: if no `hooks` key is present, no backup is created and settings.json is unchanged; re-running overwrites any prior backup. Malformed JSON causes the helper to refuse rather than overwrite. Implemented by new Python helper `plugins/autonomous-dev/scripts/reset_global_hooks.py` (two public symbols: `reset_file()` and `_atomic_write_json()`); `install.sh` dispatches early to this helper when `--reset-hooks` is detected, skipping the normal install flow. `plugins/autonomous-dev/config/install_manifest.json` updated to register the new helper script. `plugins/autonomous-dev/docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md` gains a "Recovery from Broken Hooks" section with all three recovery options (Option 1: install.sh flag, Option 2: manual python3 one-liner, Option 3: `disableAllHooks: true` soft alternative) plus a cross-link from the existing "Hooks not running" section. 23 test functions: 7 unit tests in `tests/unit/scripts/test_reset_global_hooks.py`, 7 integration tests in `tests/integration/test_install_reset_hooks.py`, and 9 spec-validation tests in `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_issue949_reset_global_hooks.py`.
- **Phase 1 semantic intent classifier (shadow mode)** (`plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/intent_classifier.py`): New library classifies user prompts into 9 intent categories (security_critical, implement, refactor, test, doc, config, typo, status_query, conversation) plus an AMBIGUOUS fail-open sentinel. Architecture: regex of 38 security-keyword stems runs BEFORE the LLM — any match short-circuits to `security_critical` without an LLM call, defending against OWASP LLM01:2025 prompt injection. When the regex misses, Claude Haiku 4.5 (pinned model, 5-second timeout) classifies via a strict JSON-only prompt. Any failure path — timeout, SDK unavailable, low confidence (< 0.85), malformed JSON, out-of-enum value — returns `AMBIGUOUS` with `requires_security_audit=True` (fail-open). Every call appends a 9-field JSONL telemetry line to `.claude/logs/activity/`. `unified_prompt_validator.py` wires the classifier under a new `INTENT_CLASSIFIER_ENABLED` env var (default `false`); when disabled, hook output is byte-identical to the pre-classifier version (verified by golden-snapshot test). Config lives at `plugins/autonomous-dev/config/intent_classifier_config.json`. Phase 1 is telemetry/shadow mode only — no routing or blocking changes. 60 test functions in `tests/unit/lib/test_intent_classifier.py`; 61 labeled fixtures in `tests/fixtures/intent_classifier_fixtures.json`; pre-modification golden snapshot at `tests/fixtures/unified_prompt_validator_golden.json`. See `docs/INTENT-CLASSIFICATION.md` for full architecture, fail-open contract, calibration guide, and phase roadmap.
- **Deduplicate global hooks from per-repo settings templates** (Issue #944): Per-repo `settings.json` templates previously duplicated 7 canonical global hook paths already registered in `~/.claude/settings.json`, causing every hook to fire twice and creating a single point of failure (all repos broke when global hooks broke). Fix strips these canonical duplicates from 4 template files (`settings.default.json`, `settings.granular-bash.json`, `settings.permission-batching.json`, `settings.strict-mode.json`). `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/settings_merger.py` gains two new public symbols: `CANONICAL_GLOBAL_HOOKS` (tuple of 7 canonical command strings) and `strip_global_duplicates(settings, *, canonical=None, source_label="")` which returns a filtered settings dict and a list of `ValidationIssue` findings (severity `"info"`, category `"hook-dedup"`). `extract_hook_refs(settings)` extracts hook filenames from a settings dict for downstream inspection. A new CLI script `plugins/autonomous-dev/scripts/strip_duplicate_hooks.py` wraps `strip_global_duplicates()` for use by `install.sh` and `/sync`. `install.sh` now calls `strip_duplicate_global_hooks()` after template rendering to migrate existing user installs. `/sync --all` gains a post-sync step invoking the script and reporting `removed_count` to the user. 1 parametrized smoke test added to `tests/regression/smoke/test_settings_template_integrity.py` verifying no template contains canonical global hook duplicates.
- **Hook path validation in `/health-check`** (Issue #950): New library `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/hook_path_validator.py` validates every `hooks.<event>[].hooks[].command` entry in both `~/.claude/settings.json` (global) and `.claude/settings.local.json` (local) settings files. Detects four issue categories: missing scripts (file does not exist), non-executable shell scripts (`.sh`/`.bash`/`.zsh` missing execute bit), global+local duplicates (same canonical hook path registered in both scopes, causing double-firing), and unresolved environment variables (command references an undefined env var that would expand to empty). `/health-check` now runs `python -m hook_path_validator` after `validate_structure.py` and exits 1 when any error-severity finding is present. Findings reuse the `ValidationIssue` shape from `sync_validator.py` for unified rendering across `/health-check`, `/sync`, and GitHub Actions reports. 32 test functions across `tests/unit/lib/test_hook_path_validator.py` (14 tests), `tests/integration/test_health_check_hook_validation.py` (4 tests), and `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_issue950_health_check_hook_validation.py` (14 tests).
- **Per-repo `.mcp.json` default and migration helper** (Issue #948): Every Claude Code session previously loaded all globally-configured MCP servers from `~/.claude/settings.json` `mcpServers`, injecting 500–2000 tokens of tool definitions per server into every prompt regardless of relevance and exposing secrets (e.g., `GITHUB_TOKEN`) to unrelated repos. Fix introduces per-repo `.mcp.json` as the recommended default. `/setup` Step 1.6 creates `<repo>/.mcp.json` from `.mcp/config.template.json` when the template exists. `install.sh` gains `--migrate-mcp-to-repo <path> --server <name>` standalone mode: copies one named server from the global config into `<repo>/.mcp.json`, detects inline secrets via `lib/secret_patterns.py::COMPILED_SECRET_PATTERNS`, sets file mode `0o600` and appends `.mcp.json` to `.gitignore` when secrets are detected, and preserves existing servers by merging into any pre-existing `.mcp.json`. Pure `${VAR}` indirections are not treated as secrets. New script `plugins/autonomous-dev/scripts/migrate_mcp_to_repo.py` implements the migration logic; `install.sh` delegates to it. `docs/MCP-ARCHITECTURE.md` gains a "Per-repo vs Global Configuration" section explaining the token-bleed cost of global config and the secret leakage risk. `docs/MCP-SECURITY.md` gains a cross-link to the new section. Migration is non-destructive — the global entry is not removed. 39 test functions: 23 unit tests (`tests/unit/scripts/test_migrate_mcp_to_repo.py`), 4 integration tests (`tests/integration/test_mcp_migration_end_to_end.py`), and 12 spec tests (`tests/spec_validation/test_spec_issue948_per_repo_mcp.py`).
- **Hook graceful-degradation safety net** (Issue #953): New library `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/hook_safety.py` provides `safe_main()` and `command_registered()`. `safe_main()` wraps a hook's `main()` so any unhandled exception exits with code 0 plus a `[hook warning]` stderr line rather than blocking Claude Code with an opaque traceback. `command_registered()` allows hooks to verify a slash command is installed before issuing a deny that references it, preventing user deadlocks where a hook demands `/foo` but `/foo` is not deployed; lookup fails CLOSED (returns True) on any error to preserve existing security barriers. All 24 hooks updated to use `safe_main()`. 15 new test functions across `tests/unit/lib/test_hook_safety.py`, `tests/unit/hooks/test_hook_robustness.py`, and `tests/integration/test_missing_hook_resilience.py`.
- **Hook shebang changed from `uv` to `python3`** (Issue #953): All 24 hooks now use `#!/usr/bin/env python3` instead of `#!/usr/bin/env -S uv run --script`. A pinned `uv` interpreter was itself a deadlock risk — if `uv` was absent from PATH, the hook would crash before reaching the `safe_main` safety net. Standard `python3` resolves via PATH without an external tool dependency. The `uv` single-file script metadata blocks (`# /// script ...`) have been removed.

### Security
- **Path-traversal guard in `hook_safety.command_registered`** (Issue #954): `command_registered()` and the internal `_check_command_dir()` helper now reject any command name containing `/`, ``, or `..` before any filesystem or manifest lookup. A secondary `Path.resolve()` backstop verifies the resolved candidate stays inside the expected directory, guarding against symlink-based escape. No current exploit path exists (all callers pass hard-coded slash-command names), but the invariant is now enforced at the function boundary per the M-01 security finding from the #953 security review. Fail-open semantics: an obviously invalid name returns `False` (command not registered) rather than blocking. 9 regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_954_command_registered_traversal_guard.py`.
- **Plan-exit gate: newline/CR injection bypass closed** (Issue #926 remediation): `_PLAN_EXIT_INJECTION_TOKENS` in `unified_pre_tool.py` now includes `\n` and `\r`. A Bash command string such as `ls\nrm -rf .claude/plan_mode_exit.json` would pass the prior rejection check because `str.split()` treats `\n` as whitespace, splitting the string into two tokens neither of which contains an explicit separator character. Both newline and carriage-return are valid Bash command separators and must be treated as injection metacharacters. 5 regression tests added to `tests/unit/hooks/test_plan_exit_pretool_enforcement.py` covering `\n`, `\r`, `\r\n`, the canonical bypass payload, and additional audit-identified payloads.

### Fixed
- **Plan-exit enforcement now fires on tool calls, not user prompts** (Issue #926). Closes a leak where Claude could execute the plan in-turn (e.g., `gh issue create`, `Task(implementer)`) and bypass the plan-critic gate. Also eliminates false-blocks on short user replies ("proceed", "yes", "b") inside skill conversation flows. The marker file format and writer (`plan_mode_exit_detector.py`) are unchanged; only the enforcement event boundary moved from `UserPromptSubmit` (`unified_prompt_validator.py`) to `PreToolUse` (`unified_pre_tool.py`). The new gate uses an explicit Bash read-only allowlist (1-/2-/3-token forms with raw-substring injection metacharacter rejection) and an explicit MCP read-only allowlist (Playwright snapshot/screenshot/console/network, HuggingFace search/fetch, Gmail list/get, Calendar list/get, Drive list/search/read/metadata) — no regex heuristics. `mcp__playwright__browser_evaluate` is explicitly NOT on the MCP allowlist (executes arbitrary JS). At `critique_done` stage, `Bash(gh issue create ...)` and `Task(implementer|issue-creator|continuous-improvement-analyst)` are allowed and consume the marker. 10ms race-mitigation retry catches writer-hook stage advances during a tool call.
- Fix(#925): progressive-compression detector false positives — fix _add_result_word_count to handle content list-of-blocks (root cause), disable cross-issue baseline check from validate_pipeline_intent (false-positive source). MAX_PROMPT_SHRINKAGE_RATIO and detect_progressive_compression retained but deprecated.
- **XML-wrapped slash-command support in plan_mode_enforcement gates** (Issue #922): Both enforcement gates in `unified_prompt_validator.py` now recognise Claude Code's XML-wrapped slash-command form (`<command-name>/implement</command-name><command-args>...</command-args>`) in addition to the existing bare-string form. The `/implement --skip-review` escape hatch at `plan_exited` stage previously only matched bare `/implement --skip-review ...` strings; when a user types `/implement --skip-review` normally via the Claude Code UI, the hook receives the XML-wrapped form and the escape hatch silently failed to fire, leaving the `plan_mode_exit.json` marker unconsumed and the user permanently blocked. Similarly, the `critique_done` gate that allows `/implement`, `/create-issue`, and `/plan-to-issues` to consume the marker and proceed was unreachable via the UI. Fixed by: (1) adding a private `_extract_wrapped_command()` helper that parses the XML form and returns `(command_name, args_str)` or `None`; (2) rewriting both gate `if` checks as `bare-regex OR wrapped-form` disjunctions; (3) using `"--skip-review" in args.split()` (whole-token matching) instead of `\b` regex boundary, which does not match at `-` boundaries in Python. Both bare-string code paths are preserved as the first OR branch so existing tests at lines 226 and 342 continue to pass unmodified. 6 new tests in `TestWrappedCommandForm` class in `tests/unit/hooks/test_plan_mode_enforcement.py` cover: skip-review at plan_exited, skip-review at critique_done, implement at critique_done (happy-path regression lock), create-issue at critique_done, plan-to-issues at critique_done, and wrapped implement WITHOUT skip-review blocked at plan_exited (no accidental bypass).
- **Logging fidelity + contract drift consolidation** (Issue #907, consolidates #871 #872 #881 #899): ROOT-CAUSE consolidation of four related logging and contract-drift defects. `unified_session_tracker.py` gains a `_count_words_in_transcript()` helper that aggregates word counts across all assistant turns in a JSONL transcript, handling both str-content (legacy) and list-of-blocks (modern) content shapes; the call site at line ~801 uses this helper with a fallback to the old single-message count when the transcript path is absent, preserving backward compatibility (#872). `implement-batch.md` agent list updated to include `plan-critic` alongside the other batch-context agents so coordinator prompts correctly attribute plan-critic invocations to the right agent in batch mode (#881). `implement.md` STEP 5.5b plan-critic invocation line now notes that the BATCH CONTEXT block is required when running under `--batch` (#881). Two new contract-lock tests added to `tests/unit/lib/test_prompt_integrity.py`: strict-equality check for `COMPRESSION_CRITICAL_AGENTS` (#871) and arity+name check for `save_merged_research` (#899). Three new regression tests added to `tests/unit/hooks/test_unified_session_tracker.py` for the new helper: multi-turn aggregation, legacy str-content, and missing-file fallback (#872).

- **Background doc-master verdict reliability** (Issue #906, consolidates #874 #882 #897): ROOT-CAUSE consolidation addressing three related reliability gaps in doc-master verdict recording and ordering enforcement. `pipeline_intent_validator.py` `PipelineEvent` dataclass gains `is_background: bool = False` field (backward-compatible default); `_parse_single_log()` reads `is_background` from `input_summary.is_background`; `_validate_step_ordering_for_group()` skips `is_background=True` events from sequential ordering checks so background doc-master invocations do not produce spurious `step_ordering` findings when their log timestamp precedes foreground agents (#882). `session_activity_logger.py` adds `is_background: true` to `input_summary` when an Agent/Task tool call includes `run_in_background=true`, providing the field that the validator reads. `implement.md` STEP 12 retry-on-empty path now explicitly sets `verdict = "MISSING"` and calls `record_doc_verdict()` + `record_agent_completion()` when both the initial invocation and retry return no output, ensuring the audit trail is never silently lost (#897). The existing unconditional STEP 12 recording block (added in #852) covers the success path (#874). 8 regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_906_background_doc_master.py`.
- **PIPELINE-MODES.md matrix corrected for --fix mode** (Issue #905): ROOT-CAUSE consolidation of three stale doc cells (#876 security-auditor silently skipped in --fix; #877 spec-validator absent from --fix batch mode; #885 spec-validator absent from matrix doc). The code in `implement-fix.md` was already correct — STEP F3.5 (spec-validator as HARD GATE) and Security-Sensitivity Detection (conditional security-auditor at F4) both existed. `docs/PIPELINE-MODES.md` now reflects that reality: the spec-validator column under `--fix` changed from `✗` to `✓`; the security-auditor column changed from `✗` to `✓ (conditional)`; the "Minimum agents per mode" bullet for `--fix` updated from "4" to "5 (6 if security-sensitive)"; the Fix Pipeline Sequence code block updated to include F3.5 between F3 and F4 and annotate F4 with the conditional security-auditor.
- **Session-ID propagation contract, remediation false-positives, and cross-pipeline ordering isolation** (Issue #904): ROOT-CAUSE consolidation of three related defects (#898 subshell session-id propagation, #902 remediation false-positive duplicate-agent findings, #875 cross-pipeline step-ordering false positives). `pipeline_completion_state.py` gains a module constant `STALE_UNKNOWN_TTL_SECONDS=3600` that gates the existing 'unknown'-session merge in `get_completed_agents()` — 'unknown' state files whose mtime exceeds one hour are no longer merged into a fresh pipeline session, preventing cross-pipeline contamination from crashed prior runs. A new kwarg `is_remediation=False` is added to `record_agent_completion()`: when `True`, the stored entry uses a dict shape `{"success": bool, "remediation": True}` instead of the legacy plain-bool; the new `_completion_is_success()` helper reads both shapes for backward compatibility. A new public function `is_remediation_completion()` exposes the remediation flag for callers. `pipeline_intent_validator.py` extends the `PipelineEvent` dataclass with a `remediation: bool = False` field and changes `validate_step_ordering()` to group events by the tuple `(session_id, batch_issue_number)` instead of `session_id` alone, so independent parallel batch pipelines sharing a daily JSONL file are fully isolated from each other's ordering checks. The `_validate_step_ordering_for_group()` helper filters out remediation-flagged events before applying duplicate-agent sequential-pair checks, eliminating the "reviewer ran before reviewer" false CRITICAL that #902 reported. `implement.md` at each `python3 -c "..."` heredoc that reads `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` now uses an inline `_resolve_session_id()` helper implementing the three-step fallback `env → sentinel → 'unknown'`, with the sentinel read applying the 3600s TTL guard. `docs/PIPELINE-MODES.md` gains a "Session-ID Propagation Contract" section documenting the full fallback chain, TTL guard rationale, tuple grouping key, and remediation flag JSON shape.

### Removed
- `_check_plan_mode_enforcement()` and `_extract_wrapped_command()` from `unified_prompt_validator.py` (Issue #926). The plan-exit enforcement logic relocated to `unified_pre_tool.py`; the marker writer (`plan_mode_exit_detector.py`) and stage advancer (`unified_session_tracker.py`) are unchanged.
- `tests/unit/hooks/test_plan_mode_enforcement.py` (Issue #926). Superseded by `tests/unit/hooks/test_plan_exit_pretool_enforcement.py`. A 1:1 invariant mapping documents which old tests have direct equivalents (9), which were retargeted to test the underlying tool calls (11), and which are obsolete because the new gate observes tool calls instead of user prompts (13 tests covering question-shape, plain-text, slash-command-flag, and XML-wrapping cases that no longer apply at PreToolUse).

### Added
- **Cluster selector for drain-queue tracker filtering** (Issue #1284): New pure function `select_next_cluster(clusters)` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/cluster_selector.py` filters tracker-shaped issues from drain-queue candidates and prefers their lowest-numbered open leaf children when present. Detects trackers via: title prefix `[TRACKER]`, label combination "root-cause" + "enhancement", or body structure with ≥2 phase headings + ≥2 issue references. When encountering a tracker with open leaves, returns a synthetic single-issue cluster pointing to the lowest leaf. Not yet wired into `/drain-queue` command — integration tracked separately. Added 5 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_cluster_selector.py`.

- **Coordinator bypass verdict tracking** (Issue #1279): Extended `record_plan_critic_skipped()` with optional `bypass_reason` parameter and added new `write_coordinator_bypass_verdict()` function in `pipeline_completion_state.py` to enable drain-queue coordinators to record machine-readable audit trails when bypassing plan-critic for mechanical extensions. New `/drain-queue` command documentation section "Coordinator Bypass of plan-critic" details the two-call protocol. 3 new tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1279_coordinator_bypass_verdict.py` verify the bypass tracking logic.
- **Plan-critic PROCEED verdict gate for stage advance** (Issue #927): `_advance_plan_mode_stage()` in `unified_session_tracker.py` now requires `.claude/plan_critic_verdict.json` to exist with `"verdict": "PROCEED"` and a timestamp at least as recent as the plan-mode-exit marker before advancing the stage from `plan_exited` to `critique_done`. REVISE and BLOCKED verdicts leave the gate closed and the verdict file is retained; the file is consumed (deleted) only on successful PROCEED advance to prevent replay. `plan-critic.md` gains a mandatory `## Verdict Persistence` section requiring the agent to write the verdict file at the end of every critique round (including REVISE/BLOCKED) using a Bash heredoc with substituted `VERDICT` and `COMPOSITE_SCORE` variables. 11 tests: 7 unit tests in `tests/unit/hooks/test_plan_critic_stage_advance.py` covering no-verdict-file, REVISE verdict, BLOCKED verdict, stale verdict, malformed verdict, `critique_done` short-circuit, and PROCEED-consumes-file cases; 4 spec-validation tests added to `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_staged_plan_exit_pipeline.py` covering stage-advance-on-proceed and the three `critique_done`-allows paths. `docs/HOOKS.md`, `docs/HOOK-REGISTRY.md`, and `docs/AGENTS.md` updated to reflect the verdict-gated behavior.

- **`/plan` auto-runs issue creation in quick mode after plan-critic PROCEED** (Issue #884): `/plan` Step 6 now automatically creates GitHub issues via `gh issue create` (quick mode: Summary + Implementation Approach + Acceptance Criteria) immediately after a PROCEED verdict from the plan-critic, when the Minimal Path contains >=2 independent work items. A `--no-issues` flag suppresses issue creation for plans that don't warrant decomposition; the plan file records `Issues not created — --no-issues flag was set` in its `## Linked Issues` section. Issue creation is non-blocking: `gh` failures log a warning and continue to Step 7 (plan file creation) without halting the workflow. `/plan-to-issues` is now positioned as the thorough-mode alternative (full section templates) and fallback when auto-creation is skipped or fails. `plan-to-issues.md` gains a documentation note describing its relationship to `/plan`'s auto-creation. `docs/PLANNING-WORKFLOW.md` updated to reflect the PROCEED-gated, non-blocking Step 6 behavior and the `--no-issues` flag. 17 spec-validation tests in `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_issue884_plan_auto_issues.py`.

- **Post-PROCEED systemMessage suggestion in unified_session_tracker** (Issue #879): When the plan-critic SubagentStop fires and `_advance_plan_mode_stage()` successfully advances the `.claude/plan_mode_exit.json` marker from `plan_exited` to `critique_done`, `unified_session_tracker.py` now emits a `systemMessage` to the user suggesting next steps: `/plan-to-issues --quick` to create GitHub issues from the approved plan, or `/implement` to begin implementation directly. This is a non-blocking informational nudge only — it does not alter enforcement behavior. `docs/HOOKS.md` and `docs/HOOK-REGISTRY.md` updated to document the new `systemMessage` output. 7 unit tests in `tests/unit/hooks/test_plan_critic_stage_advance.py` covering return-value semantics, suggestion content, and None-path behavior.

- **Objective scoring rubric added to plan-critic agent** (Issue #880): `plan-critic.md` gains a `## Scoring Rubric` section with a 1-5 Likert scale (1 = Critical gap / Blocking, 5 = Exemplary), applied per-axis across all five critique axes. A `## Verdict-Score Mapping` table makes the composite-to-verdict conversion deterministic: composite mean >=3.0 with no axis below 2 → PROCEED; <3.0 or any axis at 1 → REVISE; <2.0 or 2+ axes at 1 → BLOCKED. The composite-to-verdict mapping is fixed — overriding it is FORBIDDEN. All three verdict output templates (REVISE, PROCEED, BLOCKED) now include a required `### Scores` table with per-axis score and notes columns. `## Delta Tracking` section added: from round 2 onward a Delta column is appended to the Scores table to make axis-level improvement across rounds visible. Budget mode (single-pass invocation) computes composite over three axes only (Assumption Audit, Existing Solution Search, Minimalism Pressure). `docs/AGENTS.md` updated to document the new scoring and composite threshold behavior for the plan-critic entry.

### Fixed
- **conversation_archiver metadata extraction reads nested `entry["message"]` schema**: `_extract_metadata()` was silently returning zeroes for token counts, model, tool_calls, and first_user_prompt because it read `entry.get("model")`, `entry.get("content")`, and `entry.get("usage")` at the top level of each JSONL line. The real Claude Code transcript schema nests all payload fields under `entry["message"]`. Fixed by reading `msg = entry.get("message")` and then extracting `msg.get("model")`, `msg.get("content")`, and `msg.get("usage")` from the nested dict. The code now also correctly filters to only `user`/`assistant` entry types (skipping noise types like `attachment`, `permission-mode`, `summary`) before incrementing message_count. `sessions.db` schema extended with two new columns — `total_cache_read_tokens` and `total_cache_creation_tokens` (INTEGER) — populated from `cache_read_input_tokens` and `cache_creation_input_tokens` in the usage block; existing databases are auto-migrated via idempotent `PRAGMA table_info` + `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN` guards. The prior CHANGELOG entry for Issue #773 described a "15-column" sessions table; the table is now 17 columns. 21 regression tests in `tests/unit/hooks/test_conversation_archiver_extraction.py` (`TestMetadataExtraction` class) cover the real transcript format fixture and edge cases; `test_conversation_archiver.py` fixture migrated to match the real schema.

- **Broken list-comprehension lib path resolver does not short-circuit** (Issue #896): A subset of the Issue #894 resolver sites used a one-liner list-comprehension form `[sys.path.insert(0,p) for p in (...) if os.path.isdir(p)][:1]` that does NOT short-circuit — the `[:1]` only trims the returned list, but every matching candidate has already been appended to `sys.path`. In a dev repo where both `.claude/lib` and `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib` exist, this inserted both (plus the home candidate when present), with `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib` ending up at `sys.path[0]` and reversing the intended priority. The spec test for #894 grep-checked for candidate strings only and never executed the resolver, so CI missed the defect. Fixed by replacing all 9 occurrences across 6 command files (`implement.md:1220`, `improve.md:160,265`, `create-issue.md:92,169`, `refactor.md:160,166`, `retrospective.md:194`, `plan-to-issues.md:155`) with `next((sys.path.insert(0,p) for p in (...) if os.path.isdir(p)),None)` — a generator-over-`next()` form that is lazy, short-circuits, and inserts exactly one path. The correct for-loop-with-break blocks from #894 (in other sites) are unchanged. 5 new `test_spec_issue896_*` functions (14 pytest instances including parametrized cases) in `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_issue894_lib_path_resolver.py`: `test_spec_issue896_1_no_broken_list_comprehension` (parametrized across 10 command files), behavioral `..._2_resolver_inserts_exactly_one_path` / `..._3_resolver_fallback_plugins_lib` / `..._4_resolver_fallback_home` that execute the replacement expression via `exec()` and assert exactly one insertion in priority order, and `..._5_shell_string_snippets_parse` that runs each of the 9 extracted `python3 -c "..."` snippets under `subprocess.run` and asserts returncode 0. A `sys_path_guard` fixture snapshots and restores `sys.path` around each behavioral test to prevent cross-test pollution. 9 additional spec-blind behavioral tests in `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_issue896_lib_resolver_behavioral.py` (written by the spec-validator agent without access to the implementation) independently verify AC1-AC6, AC9, and AC10 with their own fixtures and sys.path restoration checks.

- **Multi-candidate lib path resolver in command files** (Issue #894): `/implement`, `/sweep`, and 8 other command files contained hardcoded `sys.path.insert(0, 'plugins/autonomous-dev/lib')` that only resolves in the dev repo layout. In consumer repos installed via `install.sh` (where libs live at `.claude/lib/`), every command invocation failed with `ModuleNotFoundError`. Fixed by replacing all ~39 hardcoded insertions with a multi-candidate resolver that probes `.claude/lib` → `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib` → `~/.claude/lib` in priority order, stopping at the first valid directory. `plugins/autonomous-dev/docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md` updated: Quick Fixes table entry revised (symlink no longer listed as the solution for command-side `ModuleNotFoundError`) and a resolver note already present under "Lib files not found" confirms the symlink workaround is now optional. 6 unit tests in `tests/unit/commands/test_lib_path_resolver.py`; 10 spec-validation tests in `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_issue894_lib_path_resolver.py`.

- **Command-string bypass for agent completeness gate** (Issue #802 follow-up): The `SKIP_AGENT_COMPLETENESS_GATE=1` env var was unreachable when written as an inline prefix on the `git commit` command (e.g., `SKIP_AGENT_COMPLETENESS_GATE=1 git commit -m "..."`) because the hook runs in a separate process and inline assignments affect only the child process, not the hook's `os.environ`. Added command-string parsing so the hook checks the Bash command string directly for `SKIP_AGENT_COMPLETENESS_GATE=1` or `skip_agent_completeness_gate=true` (case-insensitive for the `=true` variant). Block messages in both the single-issue and batch-mode paths updated to direct users to `touch /tmp/skip_agent_completeness_gate` as a SEPARATE command first, then retry the commit (clarifying the required two-step sequence). `docs/HOOKS.md` and `docs/HOOK-REGISTRY.md` updated to document the third bypass path. 6 regression tests in `tests/unit/hooks/test_agent_completeness_bypass.py`.

- **plan-critic added to SEQUENTIAL_REQUIRED ordering gate with conditional bypass** (Issue #878): `pipeline_intent_validator.py` and `agent_ordering_gate.py` now include `("planner", "plan-critic")` and `("plan-critic", "implementer")` in `SEQUENTIAL_REQUIRED`, enforcing plan-critic ordering at both the hook level and the log-analysis level. When STEP 5.5a finds a pre-validated plan in `.claude/plans/` and skips plan-critic invocation, the coordinator calls `record_plan_critic_skipped(session_id, issue_number=ISSUE_NUM)` from `pipeline_completion_state.py`. `verify_pipeline_agent_completions()` passes `plan_critic_skipped=True` to `get_required_agents()`, which excludes `plan-critic` from the required set so the agent completeness gate does not block the commit. `get_required_agents()` gains a `plan_critic_skipped` parameter mirroring the existing `research_skipped` parameter. `pipeline_completion_state.py` gains `record_plan_critic_skipped()` and `get_plan_critic_skipped()` convenience functions. `implement.md` STEP 5.5a updated with explicit instructions to call `record_plan_critic_skipped()` after skipping plan-critic. 14 new tests across 6 test files covering ordering enforcement, bypass recording, and completeness gate interactions.

### Added
- **Cluster selector for drain-queue tracker filtering** (Issue #1284): New pure function `select_next_cluster(clusters)` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/cluster_selector.py` filters tracker-shaped issues from drain-queue candidates and prefers their lowest-numbered open leaf children when present. Detects trackers via: title prefix `[TRACKER]`, label combination "root-cause" + "enhancement", or body structure with ≥2 phase headings + ≥2 issue references. When encountering a tracker with open leaves, returns a synthetic single-issue cluster pointing to the lowest leaf. Not yet wired into `/drain-queue` command — integration tracked separately. Added 5 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_cluster_selector.py`.

- **Coordinator bypass verdict tracking** (Issue #1279): Extended `record_plan_critic_skipped()` with optional `bypass_reason` parameter and added new `write_coordinator_bypass_verdict()` function in `pipeline_completion_state.py` to enable drain-queue coordinators to record machine-readable audit trails when bypassing plan-critic for mechanical extensions. New `/drain-queue` command documentation section "Coordinator Bypass of plan-critic" details the two-call protocol. 3 new tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1279_coordinator_bypass_verdict.py` verify the bypass tracking logic.
- **Staged Plan-Exit Pipeline** — `plan_mode_exit_detector.py` now writes a `stage: "plan_exited"` field into `.claude/plan_mode_exit.json` and emits a `systemMessage` directing the model to invoke the plan-critic agent before proceeding. `unified_prompt_validator.py` enforces a 2-state machine: in `plan_exited` state every non-question prompt is blocked with a `REQUIRED NEXT ACTION: Invoke the plan-critic agent` directive; in `critique_done` state `/implement`, `/create-issue`, and `/plan-to-issues` are allowed and consume the marker; everything else remains blocked. `unified_session_tracker.py` advances the marker from `plan_exited` → `critique_done` automatically via `_advance_plan_mode_stage()` when the plan-critic SubagentStop fires. Old markers without a `stage` field are treated as `critique_done` for backward compatibility; unknown `stage` values are treated as `plan_exited` for safety. Escape hatch: `/implement --skip-review` consumes the marker at any stage. 22 unit tests across `tests/unit/hooks/test_plan_mode_exit_detector.py` (2 new), `tests/unit/hooks/test_plan_mode_enforcement.py` (14 new), and `tests/unit/hooks/test_plan_critic_stage_advance.py` (6 new, new file). `docs/HOOKS.md` and `docs/HOOK-REGISTRY.md` updated to document the staged behavior.

- **Opportunistic fix-forward classification** (Issue #860): The `/implement` pipeline now captures a baseline of pre-existing failing tests at STEP 1 (before the implementer runs) using `fix_forward.parse_failing_tests()`. At STEP 8, after the implementer completes, failures are classified into three categories: `fixed` (was failing, now passes), `pre_existing_remaining` (still failing, was already broken before this feature), and `new_failures` (newly introduced). The gate blocks on any `new_failures > 0` (unchanged behavior). When `new_failures == 0` but pre-existing failures remain, GitHub issues are auto-filed via `gh issue create` with label `pre-existing-failure` and the pipeline proceeds. When failures are opportunistically fixed (`fixed > 0`), this is noted in the commit context. `implementer.md` gains a `## Pre-Existing Failure Awareness` section guiding the implementer to attempt fixing pre-existing failures when they are within reach of the current changeset. New library `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/fix_forward.py` provides `parse_failing_tests()`, `classify_failures()`, and `format_issue_body()`. 13 unit tests in `tests/unit/test_fix_forward_classification.py` and 31 spec tests in `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_issue860_fix_forward.py`.

- **PreCommit hook: enforce prunable test threshold** (Issue #863): New `enforce_prunable_threshold.py` PreCommit hook blocks commits when the count of prunable test candidates in the codebase exceeds `PRUNABLE_THRESHOLD` (100). Uses `TestPruningAnalyzer` for local-only AST scanning — no network calls. Registered in `settings.strict-mode.json` only (opt-in; not active in the default settings). Bypass via `SKIP_PRUNABLE_GATE=1`. Fails open when `test_lifecycle_manager` or `test_pruning_analyzer` libraries are unavailable. Block message directs the user to run `/sweep --tests --prune`. `docs/HOOK-REGISTRY.md` updated to list the new hook. 29 tests: 15 unit tests in `tests/unit/hooks/test_enforce_prunable_threshold.py` and 14 spec-validation tests in `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_issue863_prunable_threshold_hook.py`.

- **Proactive template reload HARD GATE in batch mode** (Issue #851): `implement-batch.md` STEP B3 gains a `HARD GATE: Proactive Template Reload` step that requires the coordinator to reload all critical agent templates fresh from disk at the start of each batch issue, before dispatching any agent. The reload iterates `COMPRESSION_CRITICAL_AGENTS` and calls `get_agent_prompt_template(agent_type)` — which reads unconditionally with no caching — so in-memory template representations never drift across issues. The coordinator MUST use these fresh-from-disk templates as the basis for agent prompt construction; constructing prompts from context memory is explicitly FORBIDDEN. The existing `HARD GATE: Prompt Integrity Across Issues` section gains a cross-reference note identifying this proactive step as its complementary prevention layer. No new Python library functions were added; the gate reuses existing `get_agent_prompt_template()` and `COMPRESSION_CRITICAL_AGENTS` from `prompt_integrity.py`. `docs/BATCH-PROCESSING.md` updated to document the two-layer architecture (proactive reload + reactive detection) and the new HARD GATE. 31 tests: 18 regression tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_prompt_integrity_proactive.py` and 13 spec-validation tests in `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_issue851_proactive_template_reload.py`.

- **Agent completeness gate made batch-aware** (Issue #853): The agent completeness gate in `unified_pre_tool.py` now distinguishes batch and non-batch execution contexts. In batch mode (detected by `.worktrees/batch-` in the current working directory), the gate iterates ALL issues tracked in the pipeline completion state file and calls `verify_pipeline_agent_completions()` per issue, respecting each issue's `research_skipped` flag so researcher agents are correctly excluded when STEP 3.5 skipped research for that issue. If any issue has missing agents, the gate produces a per-issue failure list (`#N: missing agent1, agent2`) and blocks `git commit` with a REQUIRED NEXT ACTION directive. In non-batch mode the existing single-issue behavior is unchanged. Bypass via `SKIP_AGENT_COMPLETENESS_GATE=1`. Fails open on state errors. `docs/HOOKS.md` and `docs/HOOK-REGISTRY.md` updated to document the batch-aware behavior. 22 tests: 8 regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_853_batch_research_skip.py` and 14 spec-validation tests in `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_batch_completeness_gate.py`.
- **Pre-Dispatch Ordering Protocol added to coordinator** (Issue #850): Before every Agent tool dispatch in the `/implement` pipeline, the coordinator now calls `check_ordering_with_session_fallback()` from `agent_ordering_gate` as a first-line defense against ordering violations. If the check returns `passed=False`, dispatching the agent is FORBIDDEN — the coordinator must resolve missing prerequisites first. The `enforce_pipeline_order` hook continues to act as a backstop. `implement-batch.md` updated with a cross-reference to the protocol. `docs/WORKFLOW-DISCIPLINE.md` Layer 3 section updated to document the two-tier enforcement model. 10 tests across `tests/unit/commands/test_implement_ordering_protocol.py` (3 tests) and `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_issue850_predispatch_ordering.py` (7 tests).
- **`/plan` Step 6 issue decomposition is now conditional-mandatory** (Issue #854): When the Minimal Path contains >=2 independent work items, GitHub issue creation is now a HARD GATE rather than optional. Issues are created directly via `gh issue create` with temp-file bodies. The plan file template gains a required `## Linked Issues` section listing created issue numbers or a skip reason. When the plan is a single coherent unit, Step 6 is skipped with an explicit log message. If `gh` is unavailable, a warning is logged and `/plan-to-issues` remains available as a manual fallback. The output block now surfaces `/implement --issues #N1,#N2,...` as the recommended next step when issues were created. `docs/PLANNING-WORKFLOW.md` updated to reflect the mandatory gate, the correct `gh issue create` mechanism, the updated plan file format with `## Linked Issues`, and the revised role of `/plan-to-issues` as a fallback.
- **STEP L2.5 Plan Structural Validation added as HARD GATE in the light pipeline** (Issue #858): After the planner runs in STEP L2, the coordinator now validates the planner output against three structural criteria before proceeding to implementation: (1) at least one specific file path (containing `/` and a `.` extension — vague phrases like "update relevant files" do not count), (2) an explicit testing statement or explicit opt-out, and (3) the exact phrase `Recommended implementer model:` followed by `sonnet` or `opus`. On first failure the coordinator re-invokes the planner once with a structured rejection message listing the missing elements. On second failure the pipeline is BLOCKED with a resolution directive. The light pipeline header now accurately states 6 steps. FORBIDDEN: accepting planner output with 0 specific file paths, proceeding to STEP L3 when the plan contains only vague language, or skipping structural validation for any reason. 21 tests: 7 regression tests in `tests/regression/progression/test_issue_858_light_plan_validation_gate.py` and 14 spec-validation tests in `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_issue858_light_plan_validation.py`.
- **Plan-Implementation Alignment Gate added between STEP 8 test gate and STEP 8.5** (Issue #857): After the STEP 8 test gate passes, the coordinator now runs an OUTPUT VALIDATION GATE that compares the planned files from the STEP 5 planner output (files marked CREATE or MODIFY) against `git diff --name-only HEAD`. Test files (`tests/**`) and documentation files (`docs/**`, root `*.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`) are excluded from the comparison. Files present in the plan but absent in implementation produce a WARNING; files implemented but not planned also produce a WARNING. If more than 50% of non-excluded implemented files are unplanned, the pipeline is BLOCKED. The gate outputs a structured alignment report showing planned vs. implemented file counts and the verdict (PASS, WARNING, or BLOCKED). FORBIDDEN: skipping the gate, treating >50% divergence as acceptable. `docs/ARCHITECTURE-OVERVIEW.md` updated to document the new step 7.25 in the workflow pipeline.

- **Root Cause Analysis HARD GATE in /implement --fix pipeline** (Issue #856): `implementer.md` gains a `## HARD GATE: Root Cause Analysis for Bug Fixes` section requiring the implementer to produce a `## Root Cause Analysis` section in its fix-mode output before declaring the fix complete. The section MUST include: root cause statement (1 sentence, underlying cause not symptom), mechanism chain (A → B → C → failure propagation), 5 Whys (minimum 3 levels, each introducing new information), and root cause category (one of: Wrong type / Wrong state / Race condition / Missing check / Stale data / Wrong assumption). `implementer.md` also gains the `debugging-workflow` skill, aligning the agent's declared skill set with its actual capabilities. `implement-fix.md` STEP F3 gains a new `### HARD GATE: Root Cause Analysis Output Gate` section: after implementer completion, the coordinator checks the output for the literal string `## Root Cause Analysis`; if absent on first completion, the implementer is re-invoked once with a targeted violation prompt; if still absent after retry, the pipeline is BLOCKED with a clear error directing the user to re-invoke with a more specific bug description. FORBIDDEN at coordinator level: proceeding to STEP F3.5 without verifying presence of `## Root Cause Analysis`, treating a symptom description as a root cause analysis, skipping the gate when the implementer output is long. `docs/AGENTS.md` implementer entry updated to reflect corrected skills list and new Root Cause Analysis HARD GATE behavior.
- **STEP 5.5 Plan Validation Gate added to /implement pipeline** (Issue #855): A new HARD GATE (STEP 5.5) is inserted between the planner (STEP 5) and acceptance-test generation (STEP 6). Before proceeding to implementation, the coordinator checks `.claude/plans/` for a pre-validated plan containing "Verdict: PROCEED" in its Critique History — if found, plan-critic invocation is skipped. When no pre-validated plan exists, plan-critic is invoked with a constrained budget: 1 round, 3 axes only (Assumption Audit, Existing Solution Search, Minimalism Pressure). PROCEED continues the pipeline; REVISE re-invokes the planner once with feedback then proceeds; BLOCKED halts the pipeline with a clear resolution message. Structural validation always runs regardless of the 5.5a/5.5b path: the plan must contain at least one file path, an acceptance-criteria section, and a testing-strategy section — if any are missing, the planner is re-invoked once before a final block. `docs/ARCHITECTURE-OVERVIEW.md` updated to reflect STEP 5.5 in the workflow pipeline.
- **Iterative refinement loops (Self-Refine pattern) in /advise, /implement, and /refactor** (Issue #840): Three commands now include an inline FEEDBACK pass implementing the Self-Refine pattern (GENERATE → FEEDBACK → REFINE), providing approximately 20% quality improvement per NeurIPS 2023 research. `/implement` gains STEP 4.5 (Research Completeness Critique): after merging researcher output but before passing to planner, the coordinator performs one FEEDBACK pass checking for unresolved ambiguities, missing edge cases, and conflicting findings — the refined output replaces the raw merged research before planner invocation. `/advise` gains STEP 4.5 (Self-Critique): after generating the three-alternative analysis in STEPs 3–4, the coordinator performs one FEEDBACK pass checking that each alternative actually achieves the stated goal, that trade-offs are specific rather than generic, and that PROJECT.md compatibility claims are grounded — revised analysis replaces the draft before final output. `/refactor` gains STEP 1.5 (Findings Self-Critique, `--deep` mode only): after GenAIRefactorAnalyzer produces raw findings, the coordinator performs one FEEDBACK pass checking for false positives, missing context, and severity mis-rating before presenting results to the user. The STEP 1.5 is skipped silently in `--quick` mode or when `--deep` was not active. All three are inline (no agent invoked) and apply only one pass to avoid over-refinement (two-pass optimum per Self-Refine paper). `docs/ARCHITECTURE-OVERVIEW.md` updated to document the new Research Self-Critique step in the workflow pipeline.

### Fixed
- **File-based bypass for agent completeness gate** (Issue #802): The `SKIP_AGENT_COMPLETENESS_GATE=1` env var escape hatch was unreachable from Bash `touch` commands because the hook runs in a separate process spawned by the harness — env vars set in a Bash subshell do not propagate to the hook process. Added a file-based one-shot bypass: `touch /tmp/skip_agent_completeness_gate` before committing; the file is consumed (deleted) on first check so the bypass applies only to the next `git commit`. Implementation lives in `pipeline_completion_state.py` (`SKIP_GATE_FILE`, `_check_file_bypass()`); `verify_pipeline_agent_completions()` checks the file after the env var check. Block message in `unified_pre_tool.py` updated to advertise both bypass methods. `docs/HOOKS.md` and `docs/HOOK-REGISTRY.md` updated to document the file-based bypass. 9 regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_802_file_bypass.py`.

- **run_id fallback in `_get_current_issue_number()` for batch mode ordering** (Issue #869): In batch mode, the pipeline state file may be written with a `run_id` field (pattern: `issue-{N}-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS`) but without an `issue_number` field, causing `_get_current_issue_number()` to return `0` and triggering ordering violations. Fixed by adding a third resolution step that extracts the issue number from `run_id` using a regex match on `issue-(\d+)-` when the `issue_number` field is absent or zero. Resolution order is now: (1) `PIPELINE_ISSUE_NUMBER` env var, (2) `issue_number` field in pipeline state file, (3) issue number parsed from `run_id` field, (4) `0` as safe default. `docs/BATCH-PROCESSING.md` updated to document the 4-step resolution order.

- **Coordinator-side fallback logging for background agent completions** (Issue #868): When agents run with `run_in_background=true`, the `SubagentStop` hook (`unified_session_tracker.py`) often fails to fire, causing completion events to go unrecorded in the JSONL activity log. New library `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/coordinator_log.py` provides `log_background_agent_completion()` — a reusable coordinator-callable function that writes a JSONL entry to `.claude/logs/activity/` tagged `"source": "coordinator_fallback"` so downstream consumers can deduplicate if the SubagentStop hook also fires. Convenience wrapper `ensure_doc_master_logged()` targets the most common missing case. The activity log directory is located by walking ancestor directories for `.claude/`; if none is found, the call is a safe no-op with no exceptions raised. 14 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_coordinator_log.py`.

- **Cross-issue baseline fallback in `get_prompt_baseline()` to detect security-auditor prompt shrinkage across batch issues** (Issue #867): When `get_prompt_baseline()` was called with a specific `issue_number` that had no recorded baseline yet (i.e. the current batch issue is the first to run for that agent in a new batch cycle), it previously returned `None` — causing the prompt integrity gate to skip validation and allowing cross-issue prompt shrinkage to go undetected. Fixed by adding a cross-issue fallback: when `issue_number` is provided but no per-issue baseline exists, the function now falls back to the lowest-numbered issue's baseline instead of returning `None`. Per-issue baselines still take priority when present (Issue #764 behavior preserved). `docs/LIBRARIES.md` updated to document the two-tier lookup: exact-issue match first, lowest-issue fallback second. 6 regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_867_cross_issue_baseline_fallback.py`. 2 existing spec tests in `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_issue764_per_issue_baseline.py` updated to account for the new fallback behavior.

- **Fix pipeline state file deletion guard false positive on heredoc body text** (Issue #866): `_check_bash_state_deletion()` in `unified_pre_tool.py` was matching pipeline state file path patterns against the full raw Bash command, including heredoc bodies and `--body`/`--message` argument values. When a `git commit -m` or `gh issue create` heredoc contained prose that mentioned state file paths (e.g., referencing `/tmp/implement_pipeline_state.json` in a commit message), the guard would block the command with a false positive. Fixed by stripping heredoc bodies (`<<'EOF'...EOF`, `<<EOF...EOF`, `<<"EOF"...EOF`, `<<-EOF...EOF`) and `--body`/`--message` quoted argument values from the command before the pattern scan runs; genuine bare `rm`/`unlink`/truncate/redirect-to-empty commands targeting state files remain fully blocked. `docs/HOOKS.md` updated to document the stripping behavior. 6 regression tests added to `tests/unit/hooks/test_unified_pre_tool.py`: `test_heredoc_body_not_detected`, `test_actual_rm_still_detected`, `test_body_flag_not_detected`, `test_compound_command_rm_still_detected`, `test_double_quoted_body_not_detected`, `test_message_flag_not_detected`.

- **PIPELINE_CLEANUP_PHASE escape hatch for state file deletion guard** (Issue #865): The pipeline state file deletion guard (Issue #803) was blocking STEP 15 / STEP B4 coordinator-authorized cleanup of the state file after batch completion, leaving a stale state file that caused 6 hook tests to fail when run against a live pipeline state. Added `PIPELINE_CLEANUP_PHASE` env var escape hatch: when set to `1` or `true`, the guard allows `rm`/`unlink`/truncate of pipeline state files. Added test isolation to `tests/unit/hooks/test_unified_pre_tool.py` so tests are not influenced by any state file present on disk during the test run. 4 new tests: `test_cleanup_phase_allows_state_deletion`, `test_check_bash_state_deletion_detects_rm`, `test_check_bash_state_deletion_ignores_safe_commands`, `test_agent_auth_isolated_from_stale_state_file`.

- **Session staleness guards extended to `_get_current_issue_number` and `_get_pipeline_mode_from_state`** (Issue #862): When a stale pipeline state file from a prior session remained on disk, `_get_current_issue_number()` would return the prior session's issue number and `_get_pipeline_mode_from_state()` would return the prior session's mode — causing the spec-test deletion scope guard (Issue #790) and the agent completeness gate (Issue #802) to enforce against the wrong issue context. Both helpers now call `_is_stale_session()` immediately after reading the state file; when the stored `session_id` differs from `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` and both are known, the stale file is removed and the helper returns its safe default: `0` for `_get_current_issue_number()` and `"full"` for `_get_pipeline_mode_from_state()`. The `_is_stale_session()` helper itself was introduced in Issue #592; this fix extends its coverage to two previously unguarded call sites. Indeterminate cases (either session_id "unknown" or empty) continue to fall through, with the 30-minute mtime TTL in `_is_pipeline_active()` acting as the secondary guard.

- **Cumulative prompt drift threshold calibrated from 15% to 30%** (Issue #870): `MAX_CUMULATIVE_SHRINKAGE` in `prompt_integrity.py` is raised from `0.15` to `0.30` (30%) to reduce false positives caused by normal inter-issue prompt variance. The 15% threshold (set by Issue #812) proved too tight in practice: legitimate task-specific prompt variation between issues was triggering cumulative drift blocks even when no actual compression was occurring. The 30% threshold retains protection against progressive multi-issue compression (3–5% per iteration adds up) while eliminating noise from normal variance. The per-invocation `max_shrinkage` default (15%) and the hook-level per-issue limit (20%) are unchanged. `docs/BATCH-PROCESSING.md`, `docs/HOOKS.md`, and `docs/LIBRARIES.md` updated to reflect the new threshold. 5 updated test methods across 4 test files (`tests/regression/test_issue812_prompt_shrinkage_boundary.py`, `tests/regression/test_issue_794_progressive_batch_compression.py`, `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_issue764_per_issue_baseline.py`, `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_issue812_shrinkage_thresholds.py`) cover the new 30% boundary value and confirm 29% still passes.

- **Fix environment-sensitive prompt integrity test failures with mock isolation** (Issue #861): Five tests in `tests/unit/hooks/test_prompt_integrity_enforcement.py` and `tests/unit/hooks/test_validate_prompt_integrity_shrinkage.py` were failing in CI and on developer machines where a stale `.claude/logs/prompt_batch_observations.json` file held cumulative shrinkage above the 15% gate. When `get_cumulative_shrinkage` returned a real value from disk, tests that expected `allow` received `deny`. Fixed by adding `patch("prompt_integrity.record_batch_observation")` and `patch("prompt_integrity.get_cumulative_shrinkage", return_value=None)` to all affected tests so cumulative drift state is isolated from disk. One regression test added (`test_stale_observations_file_does_not_cause_false_denial`) documenting the failure mode: when `get_cumulative_shrinkage` returns >15% (simulating stale data), the hook correctly denies — the test verifies that mock isolation is the correct fix. No production code changed.

- **Fix prompt integrity regression test boundary value to match 20% threshold** (Issue #864): One existing test in `tests/unit/hooks/test_prompt_integrity_enforcement.py` was asserting that a 300-word reviewer prompt is allowed against a 396-word baseline. Under the 25% threshold (Issue #747) that was correct (300 > 396 × 0.75 = 297). After Issue #812 tightened the threshold to 20%, 300 words falls below the new gate (300 < 396 × 0.80 = 316.8) — meaning the test would incorrectly expect `allow` but the hook would return `deny`. Updated the test to use `_make_prompt(320)` (320 > 316.8, passes the 20% gate) so the assertion matches the post-#812 behavior. No test methods were added or removed; this is a boundary-value correction only.

- **Prompt integrity thresholds tightened to catch 25–27% batch prompt compression** (Issue #812): The hook-level per-issue shrinkage limit was 25% with a strict `>` comparison, allowing exactly-at-boundary compression to slip through. The cumulative batch drift threshold was 20%, also with a strict `>`. Both are now tightened: `unified_pre_tool.py` calls `validate_prompt_word_count` with `max_shrinkage=0.20` (20%, down from 25%); `MAX_CUMULATIVE_SHRINKAGE` in `prompt_integrity.py` is reduced from `0.20` to `0.15` (15%); both comparison operators changed from `>` to `>=` so the boundary value itself blocks. The reported scenario (implementer prompt 491→357 words, ~27.3% shrinkage) that previously evaded detection is now caught cleanly. Reinvocation-context relaxation doubles the per-issue threshold as before (now 20% → 40% for remediation/re-review contexts). `docs/HOOKS.md` and `docs/BATCH-PROCESSING.md` updated to reflect the new thresholds. 11 regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue812_prompt_shrinkage_boundary.py` cover exact boundary values, the reported 491→357 scenario, cumulative 15% gate, and reinvocation-context relaxation.


- **Security-auditor absent in --fix mode despite security-sensitive file changes** (Issue #845): `implement-fix.md` was invoking only reviewer and doc-master in STEP F4, skipping security-auditor even when changed files touched hooks, auth, crypto, payment, or other security-sensitive paths. Added deterministic security-sensitivity detection as a HARD GATE before STEP F4 agents: the coordinator runs `git diff --name-only HEAD`, matches each path against `SECURITY_SENSITIVE_PATTERNS`, and outputs `Security-auditor: REQUIRED` or `SKIP` before proceeding. When REQUIRED, security-auditor is mandatory and all three agents are invoked in parallel. `SECURITY_SENSITIVE_PATTERNS` in `pipeline_intent_validator.py` expanded from 5 to 29 patterns covering four domain groups: Infrastructure (`hooks/`, `lib/auto_approval_engine`, `lib/tool_validator`, `config/auto_approve_policy`, `lib/security`), Auth/access control (`auth`, `crypto`, `permission`, `session`, `token`, `secret`, `credential`, `password`, `oauth`, `sso`, `jwt`, `rbac`), Financial (`trading`, `payment`, `billing`, `financial`, `transaction`, `wallet`), and Schema/environment (`migration`, `alembic`, `.env`). `implement.md` STEP 10 security-sensitive pattern list updated to match. `unified_pre_tool.py` updated to use the expanded detection. 52 tests: 43 in `tests/unit/test_pipeline_intent_validator_security_patterns.py` and 9 in `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_issue845_security_auditor_fix_mode.py`. `docs/LIBRARIES.md` updated to document the expanded pattern set.

- **Coordinator direct-edit bypass in implement-fix pipeline** (Issue #846): After a fix-mode pipeline completed, the coordinator was falling through to direct Write/Edit tool calls instead of delegating all file modifications to specialist agents. Added a `COORDINATOR FORBIDDEN LIST` section to `implement-fix.md` as a top-level HARD GATE (before any step definitions) that explicitly prohibits the coordinator from writing, editing, or modifying project files directly at any pipeline stage. The section is structured identically to the equivalent gate in `implement.md`, covering three constraint groups: Agent Management (coordinator must not substitute for crashed agents, must not do specialist work directly), Pipeline Integrity (no step skipping, no parallelizing cross-phase agents, no post-completion file edits), and Output Fidelity (no paraphrasing or condensing agent output below 50% word count). 27 tests: 16 unit tests in `tests/unit/commands/test_implement_fix_coordinator_forbidden.py` and 11 spec-validation tests in `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_issue846_coordinator_forbidden.py`.

- **Fix agent completeness gate deadlocking Bash commands on stale/cross-repo pipeline state** (Issue #849): The agent completeness gate in `unified_pre_tool.py` was reading pipeline mode exclusively from `os.environ.get("PIPELINE_MODE", "full")`, causing it to always assume `"full"` mode when `PIPELINE_MODE` was not set. In stale or cross-repo pipeline state scenarios — where the state file contained a non-full mode such as `"fix"` but the env var was absent — the gate required the full-mode agent set, blocking `git commit` because agents like `planner` (not part of fix-mode) appeared missing. Fixed by changing the lookup to `os.environ.get("PIPELINE_MODE") or _get_pipeline_mode_from_state()`, which correctly falls back to reading the mode from the pipeline state file when the environment variable is absent. The function `_get_pipeline_mode_from_state()` already existed and was already used at the ordering-gate call site (line 722); this fix brings the completeness-gate call site (line 2073) into alignment. External behavior is unchanged when `PIPELINE_MODE` is set. 19 tests: 6 regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_849_pipeline_mode_from_state.py` and 13 spec-validation tests in `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_issue849_pipeline_mode_from_state.py`.

- **Prompt integrity: post-reload validation and content slot checking** (Issue #844): Two gaps in the prompt integrity system are closed. (1) After a prompt integrity block triggered a reload, the reloaded agent template was used without re-validation — if the template itself was below threshold it would trigger another block on the next invocation. `validate_and_reload(prompt, agent_type, baseline_word_count, ...)` wraps the validate+reload cycle, re-validating each reload attempt and bounding retries via `max_reload_attempts` (default: 2), so the coordinator always receives a validated prompt or a clear escalation signal. Returns `ValidateAndReloadResult` with `prompt`, `validation`, `reload_count`, and `reload_succeeded`. (2) The security-auditor and reviewer agents were receiving prompts missing required content sections (implementer output, changed files, test results) — word count alone could not catch a structurally empty but non-trivially long prompt. `validate_prompt_slots(agent_type, prompt)` checks for required content markers via case-insensitive substring matching before invocation; `REQUIRED_PROMPT_SLOTS` defines the required slots for `security-auditor` and `reviewer`. Returns `PromptSlotResult` with `present_slots`, `missing_slots`, and `passed`. 16 regression tests added to `tests/unit/lib/test_prompt_integrity.py`.

### Added
- **Cluster selector for drain-queue tracker filtering** (Issue #1284): New pure function `select_next_cluster(clusters)` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/cluster_selector.py` filters tracker-shaped issues from drain-queue candidates and prefers their lowest-numbered open leaf children when present. Detects trackers via: title prefix `[TRACKER]`, label combination "root-cause" + "enhancement", or body structure with ≥2 phase headings + ≥2 issue references. When encountering a tracker with open leaves, returns a synthetic single-issue cluster pointing to the lowest leaf. Not yet wired into `/drain-queue` command — integration tracked separately. Added 5 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_cluster_selector.py`.

- **Coordinator bypass verdict tracking** (Issue #1279): Extended `record_plan_critic_skipped()` with optional `bypass_reason` parameter and added new `write_coordinator_bypass_verdict()` function in `pipeline_completion_state.py` to enable drain-queue coordinators to record machine-readable audit trails when bypassing plan-critic for mechanical extensions. New `/drain-queue` command documentation section "Coordinator Bypass of plan-critic" details the two-call protocol. 3 new tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_1279_coordinator_bypass_verdict.py` verify the bypass tracking logic.
- **Prompt quality gate: anti-pattern detection library and Layer 6 write-time enforcement** (Issue #842): New shared library `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/prompt_quality_rules.py` centralizes anti-pattern detection for agent and command prompt files. Three rule functions: `check_persona()` blocks banned expert-qualifier openers (`You are an expert/senior/world-class`), while allowing legitimate role assignments; `check_casual_register()` flags six weakening phrases (`check for`, `look for`, `make sure`, `try to`, `you should`, `feel free`); `check_constraint_density()` flags `##`-level sections exceeding 8 bullet items. `check_all()` orchestrates all three. `unified_pre_tool.py` gains Layer 6 (Prompt Quality Gate) in the Write/Edit enforcement stack: during active pipeline sessions, any Write/Edit targeting `agents/*.md` or `commands/*.md` is checked via dynamic import of `prompt_quality_rules.py`; violations produce a hard block with a `REQUIRED NEXT ACTION` directive listing up to three violations and directing use of formal directive language (`MUST`, `REQUIRED`, `FORBIDDEN`). For Edit tool calls the gate applies the replacement in memory before checking, so post-edit content is validated — not the original file. Gate fails open on import errors and when pipeline is not active. Test routing config (`plugins/autonomous-dev/config/test_routing_config.json` and `.claude/config/test_routing_config.json`) updated to route `agent_prompt` category to the `unit` tier. 57 tests total: 27 unit tests in `tests/unit/test_prompt_quality.py` (rule unit tests + static inspection of all existing agent `.md` files) and 30 spec-validation tests in `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_issue842_prompt_quality_gate.py`. `docs/LIBRARIES.md`, `docs/HOOKS.md`, and `docs/HOOK-REGISTRY.md` updated to document the new library and Layer 6 architecture.

- **Prompt engineering documentation and skill** (Issue #841): Created `docs/PROMPT-ENGINEERING.md` — a standalone, research-backed reference for writing agent prompts and skill files. Covers constraint budgets (MOSAIC arxiv:2601.18554: 1–6 reliable, 7–15 unpredictable, >15 degraded), register shifting tier taxonomy (Tier 1 audit/verify/enumerate for hard gates; Tier 3 minimal verbs advisory-only), persona anti-pattern avoidance (PRISM arxiv:2603.18507: expert persona prefixes reduce analytical accuracy), Self-Refine loop structure (NeurIPS 2023: ~20% quality improvement, 2–3 pass optimal), XML sectioning rationale, minimal high-signal token rules, HARD GATE pattern template with stick+carrot enforcement, and complete anti-pattern catalog. Created `plugins/autonomous-dev/skills/prompt-engineering/SKILL.md` — compact, actionable companion skill for context injection when writing or reviewing agents/*.md or skills/*/SKILL.md files; includes register shifting checklist, HARD GATE template, model-tier compensation template, and anti-pattern summary table. Updated `docs/model-behavior-notes.md` to add a "Prompt Word Selection" section documenting register tier taxonomy and persona anti-pattern with cross-reference to the new reference doc. Updated `docs/ARCHITECTURE-OVERVIEW.md` to increment skill count from 18 to 19, list prompt-engineering in the Active Skills section, and add a "Prompt Engineering" note under the Model Tier Strategy section. 14 spec-validation tests in `tests/spec_validation/test_spec_issue841_prompt_engineering_docs.py` covering section presence, citations, frontmatter triggers, architecture listing, and cross-reference resolution.

- **Pipeline step ordering enforcement: pytest-gate prerequisite + reviewer→security-auditor always enforced** (Issue #838): `agent_ordering_gate.py` is updated so that `pytest-gate` acts as a virtual prerequisite (step 5.5) for STEP 10 agents — reviewer, security-auditor, and doc-master are blocked until the pytest gate has been recorded as passed. The `reviewer → security-auditor` ordering pair is moved from `MODE_DEPENDENT_PAIRS` into `SEQUENTIAL_REQUIRED`, meaning it is now always enforced regardless of `validation_mode` (sequential or parallel). `MODE_DEPENDENT_PAIRS` is emptied (set kept for backward compatibility). `FULL_PIPELINE_AGENTS`, `LIGHT_PIPELINE_AGENTS`, and `FIX_PIPELINE_AGENTS` sets updated to include `"pytest-gate"`. `STEP_ORDER` updated with `"pytest-gate": 5.5`. `pipeline_completion_state.py` gains two new convenience functions: `record_pytest_gate_passed(session_id, *, issue_number=0, passed=True)` records the pytest gate result as a virtual agent completion using `agent_type="pytest-gate"`, and `get_pytest_gate_passed(session_id, *, issue_number=0) -> bool` checks whether the gate has passed. `unified_pre_tool.py` gains a `SKIP_PYTEST_GATE=1` escape hatch that injects `"pytest-gate"` into the completed agent set before the ordering check runs. `pipeline_intent_validator.py` mirrors the same constants (`SEQUENTIAL_REQUIRED`, `STEP_ORDER`, `TDD_FIRST_PAIRS`) so both the hook-level and log-analysis enforcement stay in sync. 11 new tests across `tests/unit/lib/test_agent_ordering_gate.py` and `tests/unit/hooks/test_pipeline_ordering_enforcement.py` covering pytest-gate ordering, reviewer→security-auditor always-blocked behavior in parallel mode, and mode-dependent pairs removal. `docs/LIBRARIES.md` and `docs/HOOKS.md` updated to document the new behavior and escape hatch. `pipeline_completion_state.py` version bumped to v1.5.0; `agent_ordering_gate.py` version bumped to v1.3.0.

- **Fix doc-master completion missing from agent set when SubagentStop doesn't fire** (Issue #852): `implement.md` and `implement-batch.md` coordinators now call `record_agent_completion(session_id, 'doc-master', issue_number=issue_number, success=...)` explicitly alongside the existing `record_doc_verdict()` call. SubagentStop does not fire reliably for background agents — in practice 11 of 14 doc-master invocations produced no completion event, causing the batch commit gate to block even when doc-master ran and produced a valid verdict. The dual-call pattern (verdict + completion) is idempotent: if SubagentStop does fire, the second call is a no-op. `docs/LIBRARIES.md` updated to document the dual-caller contract for `record_agent_completion`. 4 regression tests in `tests/regression/test_issue_852_doc_verdict_completion.py` cover: pre-fix gap (verdict-only does not register completion), post-fix dual-call behavior, idempotency under double-recording, and static verification that both calls are co-located in command files.
- **Doc-master batch reliability: invocation guarantee + verdict enforcement** (Issue #837): `pipeline_completion_state.py` gains `record_doc_verdict(session_id, issue_number, verdict)` which persists the verdict string under `"doc-master-verdict"` in the per-issue completions dict; valid verdict values are `"PASS"`, `"FAIL"`, `"DOCS-UPDATED"`, `"NO-UPDATE-NEEDED"`, `"DOCS-DRIFT-FOUND"`. `_VALID_DOC_VERDICTS` module-level constant enumerates the accepted values. `verify_batch_doc_master_completions()` is enhanced to check verdict presence in addition to completion: issues where doc-master ran but recorded a sentinel value (`"MISSING"` or `"SHALLOW"`) or no verdict field at all are treated as incomplete (backward compatible — old state entries without a `"doc-master-verdict"` field but with doc-master completion pass through as valid). `unified_pre_tool.py` `_check_batch_doc_master_completions()` now differentiates two failure modes in its block message: "doc-master never ran: #N" vs "doc-master ran but produced no valid verdict: #N", aiding coordinator diagnosis. `implement.md` gains a "Doc-Drift Collection Point" instruction to call `record_doc_verdict()` after parsing the doc-master verdict. `implement-batch.md` gains the same REQUIRED instruction for each batch issue. 17 new tests: 13 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_pipeline_completion_state_verdict.py` and 4 regression tests added to `tests/unit/hooks/test_batch_doc_master_gate.py`. `docs/LIBRARIES.md`, `docs/HOOKS.md`, and `docs/HOOK-REGISTRY.md` updated to document the new function and enhanced gate behavior. `pipeline_completion_state.py` version bumped to v1.4.0.

### Changed
- **Replace casual-register "check for" with verification language across all agents** (Issue #839): Nine agent prompts updated with active verification verbs replacing passive "check for" phrasing. `reviewer.md`: section renamed from `## What to Check` to `## Review Dimensions`; review dimensions expanded from 4 (Code Quality, Tests, Documentation, Observability) to 6 named dimensions (Correctness, Test Coverage, Security, Performance, Maintainability, Observability); "check if changed files include" replaced with "verify whether changed files include". `security-auditor.md`: "Check for hardcoded secrets" → "Detect hardcoded secrets"; "Check for SQL injection risks" → "Detect SQL injection risks"; "check whether security-related tests are affected" → "verify whether security-related tests are affected"; "Check for OWASP Top 10" → "Verify OWASP Top 10". `researcher.md`, `implementer.md`, `doc-master.md`, `test-master.md`, `planner.md`, `continuous-improvement-analyst.md`, `ui-tester.md`: analogous word-level substitutions replacing "check for" with cross-referencing, decomposition, classification, and detection vocabulary throughout. `docs/ARCHITECTURE-OVERVIEW.md` updated to reflect the expanded reviewer dimensions (correctness, test coverage, security, performance, maintainability, observability).

- **Add verification criteria to /advise alternative generation** (Issue #828): STEP 4 of the `/advise` command now includes an explicit post-generation verification pass for all three alternatives (simpler, more robust, hybrid). Each alternative is checked against: does it actually achieve the stated goal; what specifically does it sacrifice vs. the original proposal; and is the trade-off worth it for THIS project given PROJECT.md constraints (e.g., a cloud alternative fails if PROJECT.md requires local-first). The STEP 5 output template gains a `Verified:` line under each alternative — `Verified: [✓/✗ achieves goal] | Sacrifices: [what] | PROJECT.md: [compatible/incompatible]` — making the verification outcome visible in every analysis report. No behavioral change to alignment scoring, decision criteria, or risk assessment.

- **Split coordinator FORBIDDEN LIST into thematic groups** (Issue #825): The 14-constraint COORDINATOR FORBIDDEN LIST in `implement.md` was reorganized from a single flat block into three named thematic groups — "Agent Management" (5 constraints), "Pipeline Integrity — Step Ordering and Gates" (5 constraints), and "Pipeline Integrity — Output Fidelity and Isolation" (4 constraints) — to improve constraint compliance. Research on the MOSAIC benchmark shows LLM compliance degrades when more than 5-7 constraints appear consecutively in a single block; grouping semantically related constraints reduces cognitive load and keeps each group under the 8-item threshold. No constraints were added or removed; the full set of 14 is preserved. 3 density regression tests added in `tests/unit/test_implement_command_structure.py`: max-consecutive-items gate (≤8), 14-constraint preservation check, and thematic group header presence check.

### Added
- **Cluster selector for drain-queue tracker filtering** (Issue #1284): New pure function `select_next_cluster(clusters)` in `plugins/autonomous-dev/lib/cluster_selector.py` filters tracker-shaped issues from drain-queue candidates and prefers their lowest-numbered open leaf children when present. Detects trackers via: title prefix `[TRACKER]`, label combination "root-cause" + "enhancement", or body structure with ≥2 phase headings + ≥2 issue references. When encountering a tracker with open leaves, returns a synthetic single-issue cluster pointing to the lowest leaf. Not yet wired into `/drain-queue` command — integration tracked separately. Added 5 unit tests in `tests/unit/lib/test_cluster_selector.py`.
